- **Code**: `packages/core/src/downloadqueue.ts` (queue, states, return screen and fallback, texts), `packages/core/src/downloadmanager.ts` (scheduling, resuming, writing the queue) and `packages/core/src/resumable.ts` (HTTP resume); sources in `app/src/download-sources.ts` (friend, direct URL) and `app/src/acquire-window.ts` (`siteTransport`); screen in `app/src/download-center.ts`, pill in `app/renderer/frontend.js`.

## Tables menu notifications

On the tables menu, left of the gear, what happens on the network and in downloads shows without covering anything: the **friends online pill** (the friends icon, a green dot, the number; absent when no friend is online; a click opens "Mes amis" (My friends)) left of the downloads pill, and **one notification at a time**, discreet, that fades away on its own. On the desktop it sits on the brand's line, left of the pills, and stops before the playfield and backglass frames; on a cabinet the area is narrow, so it moves below the pills, aligned with them on the right, away from the wheel and the title. It never hides the table's media or the wheel.

- **What is announced**, most urgent first: a friend request received ("Demande d'ami : Zoé" (Friend request: Zoé), amber; a click opens "Demandes d'amis" (Friend requests)), a request accepted ("Zoé a accepté votre demande" (Zoé accepted your request)), a blocked download (the cause first: "Plus de place sur le disque · …" (No space left on the disk · …), red), a finished download ("Téléchargement fini" (Download finished), the table and where it came from, green; a click opens Downloads), a friend coming online ("Bruno vient de se connecter" (Bruno just came online), blue), a friend's record or badge (green), a friend starting a game ("Bruno lance une partie" (Bruno is starting a game), the table, blue). The words always say what happens; the colour only repeats it (blue for information, green for success).
- **Never a stack**: a single notification on screen, about 6 seconds, then a pause before the next. What arrives together, or while another one is showing, is grouped by kind ("3 amis viennent de se connecter" (3 friends just came online), the names as detail); three kinds or more waiting together, back from a game for instance, make a single summary ("4 nouveautés" (4 new notifications), "1 demande d'ami · 1 téléchargement fini · …"); a click opens [Recent activity](#recent-activity), which says each one in full.
- **On every screen**: a single rule says, for each Cyber Pinball screen, where the notification area sits, whether the number of friends online shows there and which notifications appear (`noticeZone`, [noticezone.ts](../packages/core/src/noticezone.ts)); a single place names the current screen (`zoneScreen` in `app/src/frontend.ts`), and the queue, the drawn corner and the line laid over VPX follow it. The wheel: the corner, left of the gear. The system menu and each of its sub-screens (settings, details pages, lists, wizard, friends…), the controls help, a message, "Quitter Cyber Pinball ?" (Quit Cyber Pinball?), attract mode: the number of friends and every notification, on the gear's line, right of the brand, above the screen's header. The built-in browser, when it covers the playfield screen: every notification, as a line laid over its page, bottom left (never on its bar); the number stays on the menu below. During a game and its loading: no counter, only what matters (an invitation, a friend coming online, the game with friends), over VPX; nothing at all with "Masquer les notifications pendant les parties" (Hide notifications during games). Only the startup opening (a few seconds), the black shutdown screen and the Windows desktop have no area; a short notice under the wheel's brand and the shutdown warning make the notification wait, never the counter. Nothing is lost: what waits appears on the next screen that allows it. Guest mode changes nothing: what a notification opens goes through the padlock. An invitation that arrives opens by itself on the wheel, in the system menu and on the help, over the open screen, the cursor on "Répondre plus tard" (Answer later); "Répondre plus tard" or "Retour" (Back) gives that screen back as it was, cursor included (`library.interrupt`; never during a long operation or typing, where only the notification announces it). Test: [notifications-partout.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/notifications-partout.test.ts) walks every known screen (`ZONE_SCREENS`), every menu phase, and the real queue screen by screen.
- **Never at the wrong moment**: what a screen does not show (see above) waits for the next screen that allows it, then expires (10 minutes for a connection, 30 for a finished download, one hour for a friend request) or drops if it no longer applies (the friend left again, the request was answered, the download was opened). The same event is not repeated before its delay (a friend whose connection comes and goes: once every quarter of an hour). At startup, what is already there (friends, requests, finished downloads) is not news, nor are friends connecting in the first ten seconds: it is the cabinet arriving; the pill counts them. A friend just accepted is not announced as "just came online", nor are old records (more than a quarter of an hour) a friend sends again when reconnecting.
- **A single mechanism**: a finished download its screen did not take back is announced by the notification on the tables menu; on a full screen, by the usual short banner, and the notification does not repeat it afterwards.
- **Game with friends**: its news comes before everything else, one at a time, 10 seconds, never in a summary. An invitation ("Bruno vous invite à rejoindre une partie" (Bruno invites you to join a game), the table; it shows with the chime, without waiting), a guest accepts ("Bruno a accepté votre invitation" (Bruno accepted your invitation), "Pour lancer : Menu système > Amis > Mes amis" (To start: System menu > Friends > My friends); together: "Bruno et Léa ont accepté" (Bruno and Léa accepted), or "3 invités ont accepté" (3 guests accepted) when the whole names do not fit; green), declines, with the reason ("David a décliné votre invitation" (David declined your invitation) · "N'a pas cette table" (Does not have this table), "N'a pas la même version" (Does not have the same version), "Occupé pour l'instant" (Busy for now); "Pas de réponse de David" (No answer from David); blue), a player leaves the game or does not start it, the host cancels it (amber), the game is over for everyone while we were already back from ours ("Partie entre amis : Bruno gagne" (Game with friends: Bruno wins), or "vous gagnez !" (you win!), then "Résultats : Menu système > Amis · vous : place 2 sur 3" (Results: System menu > Friends · you: rank 2 of 3); green). It shows even with notifications off or "counter only"; never on the game's screen (room, start, results) nor on the invitation's, which already say it, nor what the band of the game in progress already shows (a player leaving, the end). A click opens the game's screen; with the buttons, its entry is at the top of System menu > Friends > "Mes amis" (My friends) (red dot up to the gear). Results not yet seen open by themselves back from a game on another table. The survey compares the game from one moment to the next, whatever message changed it (`packages/core/src/livenotices.ts`: `liveSnapshot`, `liveNotices`, `liveOutcome`, and `inviteNotices` for invitations); the `live` rules of `NOTICE_RULES` do the rest. Tests: `packages/core/src/test/notifications-partie.test.ts`.
- **Everywhere, even in a game**: the game with friends, an invitation and a friend coming online show on the tables menu, **in the system menu** (same place: on the gear's line, right of the brand) and **during games**, over VPX, in the playfield's top-left corner for the player, under an invitation's card if there is one, in the direction VPX really draws the table (see "Game news" in [Friends](#friends-the-cabinet-network)); without focus: VPX keeps control (`NoticeRule.away`, `NoticeContext.away`). The others (downloads, records, badges, friend requests) show on every Cyber Pinball screen, not during a game. Each has its sound, played as it comes in, once, in the menu as in a game: the **friend signal** when a friend comes online, the **ding** of the game with friends, and for an invitation, the usual chime, when it arrives (see [Interface sounds](#interface-sounds)). A friend who comes and goes does not ring all the time: a reconnection within the presence tolerance (15 s, `PRESENCE_GRACE_MS`) is not a connection, and the same friend is not announced again, nor rings, for a quarter of an hour. **System menu > Friends > "Masquer les notifications pendant les parties"** (Hide notifications during games; off by default; Start toggles; kept in `<données>\amis\notifications-en-partie.json`): on, nothing shows or rings during a game, neither line, nor card, nor chime; what matters waits in the menu afterwards (an invitation still valid opens on return, the friends pill counts them, the news waits its delay).
- **Setting**: **Menu système (System menu) > Médias (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract)** > "Notifications du menu" (Menu notifications): "affichées" (shown, the default), "compteur seul" (counter only: the friends pill, no messages) or "coupées" (off: neither pill nor messages; the downloads pill stays); in the last two, news of a game with friends still shows. Kept in `<données>\medias.json`, field `notices`.
- **Texts**: a 24 px title and a 20 px detail, each on one line: names and titles shortened at the source (40 and 56 characters at most), then to the width really left (the detail first loses its last segments, then its last words, with "…"; the start, which carries the meaning, stays).
- **Code**: the queue, rules, texts and snapshots in `packages/core/src/notices.ts` (`NOTICE_RULES`, `createNoticeCenter`, `socialNotices`, `downloadNotices`); the wiring in `app/src/menu-notices.ts` (network and download-queue snapshots, clock, redraw only when the notification changes) and `app/src/frontend.ts` (`zoneScreen`, `currentZone`, the rule being `noticeZone` in `packages/core/src/noticezone.ts`); the rendering in `app/renderer/frontend.js` (`cornerBox`, `noticeToast`, `fitNotice`) and `frontend.css` (`.status-corner`, `.friends-pill`, `.notice-toast`). A new source only adds a kind to `NoticeKind`, its rule and texts, and its snapshot.
- **Tests**: `packages/core/src/test/notices.test.ts` (one at a time and on time, grouping, summary, priority, expiry, nothing when the screen does not allow it, delay before repeating, snapshots without the startup state, every kind translated and fitting its line with long names, the screen's area read in `frontend.ts`, banner and notification without repetition, texts at the 20 px floor).

## Recent activity

A notification stays only a few seconds, is shortened to fit its line, waits for the end of a game ("Masquer les notifications pendant les parties" (Hide notifications during games)) or melts into a summary ("4 nouveautés" (4 new notifications)): seen too late, it was lost. **Menu système (System menu) > Activité récente (Recent activity)**, at the top of the system menu root, keeps them all, in full. It is also filed in the Cyber Pinball section, since it covers everything, not only friends.

- **What goes in**: every notification event, as soon as it arrives, whether it showed or not (a friend coming online or starting a game, their record, their badge, a friend request received or accepted, an invitation and its end, a guest accepting, declining or leaving, a game with friends finished or cancelled, a download finished or blocked); a newer version of Cyber Pinball found in the background (once per version), the update done or failed at startup; the startup notices that name a screen (backup restored, cabinet configuration recognized, DMD screen to check, Windows firewall). The immediate answer to a gesture (orientation handed back, simulated sleep) does not go in.
- **The screen**: grouped by day ("Aujourd'hui" (Today), "Hier" (Yesterday), then the date), the most recent at the top. Each line has its notification's icon, or the badge of the player it is about, its full text, nothing shortened, its time, the friend's state ("En ligne" (Online)) and, below, what Start does ("↩ Start : voir sa fiche" (see their page), "répondre à l'invitation" (answer the invitation), "voir les résultats" (see the results), "ouvrir les téléchargements" (open downloads), "voir la mise à jour" (see the update)). An action that no longer holds stays visible, the line greyed and its reason instead: "✕ Invitation expirée" (Invitation expired), "Partie terminée" (Game over), "Retiré de la liste des téléchargements" (Removed from the download list), "N'est plus dans vos amis" (No longer among your friends). A blocked download has the red cross of blocking states. "Retour" (Back) returns to the screen you came from; back from an action, the line you left is chosen.
- **Grouped**: what repeats during the day is a single line: "Chloé : 3 connexions" (Chloé: 3 sign-ins), with the times ("À 21:31, 19:02 et 14:10" (At 21:31, 19:02 and 14:10)), "Bruno : 2 parties lancées" (Bruno: 2 games started) on the same table, "Téléchargement bloqué 2 fois" (Download blocked 2 times). The same event said again (a reading that comes back, a record sent again by a friend reconnecting) does not count twice.
- **Kept**: the last 50 lines, from one start to the next (`<données>\activite.json`); beyond that, the oldest already read go first. An unreadable file is set aside, never overwritten. "Effacer l'activité récente" (Clear recent activity) empties the list after a question (Annuler (Cancel) chosen first); friends, downloads and games stay.
- **Unread**: what the notification did not say in full: held during a game or in the system menu, expired, melted into a summary. What a notification or a banner said is read: a sign-in, a finished download, "Cyber Pinball 0.1.9 est installé" (Cyber Pinball 0.1.9 is installed) on restart. Only errors stay unread even when said, until they are seen here. A line is seen when the cursor rests on it (not everything at once on opening); its marker, the red dot and the word "Non lu" (Unread), stays until you leave the screen, and the subtitle counts the lines unread on opening. Menu notifications set to "compteur seul" (counter only) or "coupées" (off): what no longer shows is recorded as already read, except games with friends and errors.
- **Never a red dot**: unread is marked in the screen only, and the entry says "<n> non lues · …" (<n> unread · …). What awaits an answer already has its dot where you answer it: friend requests and invitation in Amis (Friends), finished or blocked download in Téléchargements (Downloads), available version or update that did not go through in "Mise à jour de Cyber Pinball" (Cyber Pinball update). Doubling it here made two dots for one thing, or a dot for nothing (an installed update, a friend signing in), and, the entry then being filed under Amis (Friends), an update dot that rose through the Friends section.
- **From the tables menu**: the "4 nouveautés" (4 new notifications) summary opens Recent activity, which says each one in full; "Retour" (Back) returns to the tables menu. A single notification keeps its direct action (My friends, Friend requests, Downloads, the game with friends).
- **Guest mode**: the screen is closed, like the whole system menu; the summary does not lead there.
- **Code**: the log in `packages/core/src/activity.ts` (`addActivity`, `trimActivity`, `markActivityShown`, `markActivityRead`, `activityText`, `activityAct`, `loadActivity`); the screen and what each line does in `app/src/activity-menu.ts`; each event recorded by `app/src/menu-notices.ts` (`activity`), the notification that says it in full marking it read (`NoticeTick.entered`).
- **Tests**: `packages/core/src/test/activite-recente.test.ts` (full text, grouping within the day and repeats, the 50-line limit, file kept and set aside, unread and summary, every kind in French and English, the real queue on every screen, the screen with its greyed lines and its actions, the line seen under the cursor).

## Downloads and file picker

Everything Cyber Pinball downloads ends up under `<racine>\downloads`, sorted by origin: `acquisition\` (the "Récupérer" (Get) window, waiting list), `sites\<site>-<entry>\fichiers\` (community site media, recorded in `sites\recus.json`), `sites\web\…` (web search from a media item, `sites\web.json`), `vpinmediadb\` (VPinMediaDB files named after their MD5, deleted after a day), `cyberpinball\` (update installers) and, at the top, component archives (VPX), kept to reinstall offline. Indexes (`vpsdb.json`, `vpinmdb.json`…), records, site thumbnails and downloads in progress are internal: neither listed nor emptied.

- **"Fichiers téléchargés" (Downloaded files) screen** (Menu système (System menu) > Téléchargements (Downloads) > "Fichiers téléchargés"): each file, newest first (then by name or by size), with its kind (table, backglass, ROM, image, video, archive, installer), where it came from (site, "Récupérer", site media, web search, VPinMediaDB, update), its date, its size, and what it is for, with a word and a sign: **✓ Installed with** <table>, **! To install** (received by the waiting list, not installed yet), **○ Kept aside**, **i Received for** <media> (sites, web, VPinMediaDB), **✓** what was done with it when it was reused through the picker (log `downloads\journal.json`), **○ No known use**. A file opens (Start or click): its facts, its address, **what it contains** (archive listed on opening) and **the game** it most likely belongs to (the waiting list game that received it or one of whose items comes from the same page, otherwise the installed table whose title is in its name). Actions: **"Ajouter à <table>"** (Add to <table>: add plan targeting this table, the file stays where it is; a waiting list file "to install" is then marked installed), **"Ajouter à un autre jeu…"** (Add to another game…: any installed table, or any waiting list game, in the part the content says; unreadable content: "Quelle partie ?", Which part?), **"Installer comme nouvelle table"** (Install as a new table: when it contains a `.vpx`, or when we cannot tell) and "Mettre à la corbeille" (Move to the trash). "Afficher Téléchargements de Windows" (Show Windows Downloads) adds the last month's files from that folder, without opening File Explorer. **"Tout vider" (Empty all)** moves the whole area (never Windows Downloads) to the Cyber Pinball trash (`<racine>\trash\<date> Téléchargements`), after a confirmation whose cursor is on "Annuler" (Cancel) and which says what the waiting list loses: an item received but not installed is awaited again, a file kept aside is forgotten; an installed table no longer needs its file.
- **File picker**: wherever a file or folder is chosen on the PC (the "Ajouter une table" funnel, the path's "J'ai déjà le fichier" (I already have the file), the ROM library's "Ajouter" (Add), a media item's "Choisir un fichier…" (Choose a file…), "Chercher ailleurs…" (Look elsewhere…) to replace a waiting-list file, another frontend's folder for the media takeover), Cyber Pinball opens its own screen instead of the Windows dialog box, which opened on the main screen and could not be driven with the buttons. It shows where you are, with the flippers (choose), Start (open a folder, tick a file, or take it when only one is expected), Exit (parent folder, then cancel), the mouse (click, drag a file onto the window) and the keyboard (Tab, Enter, Esc). **Places**: Cyber Pinball's downloads (opened by default when they have what is wanted, with each file's origin and use), Windows Downloads, the import folder, the Desktop, then USB drives and disks (`Get-CimInstance Win32_LogicalDisk`, read again every 15 seconds: a plugged-in drive appears at the next opening). The content is **filtered** on what the screen expects ("Filtre : Fronton (.mp4, .png…)"); other files are counted, and "Afficher tous les fichiers" (Show all files) shows them, marked "Ne convient pas" (Does not fit). Several files can be ticked across folders ("Prendre les 2 fichiers choisis", Take the 2 chosen files); "Prendre ce dossier entier" (Take this whole folder) takes the open folder. Distinct states: empty folder, no fitting file, drive removed or access denied ("Réessayer", Try again). A file reused from the downloads area is noted in the log. On a desktop, three columns (places, content, moves); on the cabinet (portrait, rotated playfield), places as chips three across, then the content, then the moves in two columns. Cancelling brings back the screen you came from, cursor included.
- **Invariant**: a test (`packages/core/src/test/downloads.test.ts`) refuses any `showOpenDialog` or `showSaveDialog` in `app/src`.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/downloads.ts` (inventory, origin, link to the waiting list, to site records and to the VPinMediaDB index, log, trash) and `packages/core/src/filepicker.ts` (places, drives, filters, folder content, going up), tests in `packages/core/src/test/downloads.test.ts`; screens in `app/src/downloads-screen.ts` and `app/src/file-picker.ts` (`lib-picker` and `lib-downloads` layout, `app/renderer/library.css`). Mock-ups: design system, "Téléchargements, borne" and "Sélecteur de fichiers, bureau / borne".

## What's missing to play: guided path

A table that will not start almost always has a cause Cyber Pinball already knows (missing ROM, VPX not installed…), but the doctor often stopped at the finding ("ROM « t2_l8 » absente: put the file in the import folder") without saying where to find the file. The **"Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What's missing to play)** screen leads all the way to the game: each step says where it stands and offers the next move, one press of Start away (or one click).

- **What it takes to play**: the `.vpx` file and, if its script asks for one, the ROM, when it is missing or unreadable for PinMAME (not linked, misplaced, scattered, old name without an alias). A ROM found through PinMAME's shared folder, or provided by an alias to a neighbouring revision, is enough. Nothing else holds "Jouer" (Play) back: DMD content, `.directb2s` backglass, PuP-Pack, media, DMD colours, alternative sounds, tidying the table are offered, never required. A single rule, in the core: [packages/core/src/playable.ts](../packages/core/src/playable.ts) (`blocksPlay`, `playBlockers`, `requiredForPlay`). The doctor follows it (only ROM findings have the "error" severity; `playSeverity`, applied by `diagnose`, brings any other finding down to "warning"), and so do the path, the catalog's "Ce qu'il faut" ("indispensable", essential: the table and the ROM), the quick install, the details screen and the "Fichiers de la table" (Table files) inventory (`romBlockers`, which no longer say "ROM missing" when PinMAME finds it through its shared folder). Tests: `playable.test.ts` (every doctor rule, even marked "error") and the invariant checked on every trapped table (`doctor-traps.test.ts`).
- **Where to find it**: at the top of the details screen when the ROM is missing, or when a path is under way ("Reprendre : Étape 3 sur 4", Resume: Step 3 of 4); after a game that failed ("Start : voir ce qu'il manque pour jouer", Start: see what's missing to play); in the diagnosis, as soon as a finding keeps the table from playing; in the waiting list ("Reprendre : ce qu'il manque pour jouer <table>", Resume: what's missing to play <table>). Missing DMD content is offered on the details screen after "Jouer" ("Pour aller plus loin", Going further), never at the top.
- **Steps**: first the path up to "Jouer" (Play), numbered: "Borne et VPX" (Cabinet and VPX), "Table" (the .vpx file, and the ROM its script asks for), "ROM <name>" (if the script uses PinMAME), then "Jouer". At the top, "Étape n sur N" (Step n of N, the path only), a bar with one segment per step and "2 étapes réglées · 1 indispensable à faire · 1 amélioration proposée" (2 steps settled · 1 essential to do · 1 suggested improvement). Then **"Pour aller plus loin" (Going further)**: optional, without a number (a "+" sign) or a place in the count, never essential: "Rangement de la table" (Tidying the table: table outside its folder, script replaced by a `.vbs`, crash report), "Contenu du DMD" (DMD content, if the script loads a FlexDMD or UltraDMD folder), "Fronton" (Backglass, if the cabinet has a backglass screen), "PuP-Pack" (if the script loads one), "Affichage" (Display, if a table-specific setting needs fixing). Each step has its state, a word and a sign: "Réglé" (Settled), "À faire" (To do), "Conseillé" (Advised), "Attendu" (Waiting), "À installer" (To install), "Facultatif" (Optional), "En attente" (Pending), "Non vérifié" (Not checked). The current one is the first essential step not settled, otherwise "Jouer" ("Tout est prêt pour jouer", All set to play). Its moves come first, the advised one lit; then the first move of every other open step, the path then the improvements (a backglass, the DMD content), "Revérifier" (Check again) and "Diagnostic complet" (Full diagnosis).
- **DMD content**: the folder a FlexDMD or UltraDMD script loads (`.ProjectFolder = "./TheGooniesDMD/"`) is optional. Without it, the DMD's place keeps a default DMD: during a game, the ROM's, drawn by VPX's PinMAME and ScoreView plugins (§ "VPX plugins"); in the menu, Cyber Pinball's dot-matrix display (`fallback.js`). VPX 10.8.1's FlexDMD plugin hands `Nothing` back to the script for a missing image (`plugins/flexdmd/actors/Image.cpp`, commit af26b2d93): the DMD the script draws stays empty, and a script that does not expect it stops on "Type mismatch" (VPW's Goonies). The failed game then says so ("Le script s'est arrêté sur le DMD qu'il dessine…", The script stopped on the DMD it draws…), and the step offers "Récupérer la table d'origine" (Get the original table) or "J'ai l'archive de la table" (I have the table's archive); a folder emptied loose at the root of the table is put back in place.
- **Missing ROM**: Cyber Pinball looks in the VPS database (cached, no network) for ROM files of that name, the table's own game first, then the other revisions of the same game (`romSources`). "Récupérer la ROM t2_l8" (Get the t2_l8 ROM) adds it to the waiting list, attached to the table, and opens its page in the "Récupérer" window: you sign in and download yourself there. Cyber Pinball never downloads on the user's behalf from a forum that needs an account (§ "Finding a table" above). While waiting: "Rouvrir la page" (Reopen the page) and "Chercher dans Téléchargements" (Search Downloads). The received file ("Récupérer" window, watched Downloads folder) turns the step into "À installer" (To install): "Installer le fichier reçu" (Install the received file) shows the add plan (ROM library, link in the table), then comes back to the path, which reads the table again. A neighbouring revision already on the cabinet is offered second ("Jouer avec t2_l6, révision voisine", Play with t2_l6, neighbouring revision, with the warning from § "ROM library"), or first if the VPS database gives no link; a neighbouring revision given by the database comes last. Without a link: "Chercher t2_l8 dans le catalogue" (Search the catalog for t2_l8); always: "J'ai déjà le fichier" (I already have the file: drop it on the screen, or pick it on this PC), which also goes through the plan and comes back to the path.
- **Resuming later**: the state is read again at every opening (doctor's findings, waiting list, VPS database). The steps already shown are remembered in `<données>\parcours.json`, with the last position: a step settled since stays in the list, ticked, and "Étape n sur N" keeps its meaning. The path is forgotten when the game is started from it.
- **Code**: plan in [packages/core/src/journey.ts](../packages/core/src/journey.ts) (`planJourney`, `romSources`, `journeyChoices`, remembered paths), tests in `packages/core/src/test/journey.test.ts` (trapped tables analysed by the doctor, from a missing ROM up to "Jouer"); screen in `app/src/journey.ts`, rendering `renderJourney` (`app/renderer/library.js`); catalog entry points (`fetchFor`, `reopen`, `scanDownloads`, `openSearch`) in `app/src/catalog.ts`. Mockups: `EcranParcours` and `EcranParcoursBureau` in the design system.

**What keeps a game from working**, what the doctor said about it, and what the path does:

| Cause | Doctor's finding | Before the path | Step and move in the path |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VPX not installed | none: the script is not read, the ROM not checked | "Aucune table" (No table) message, VPX installation | "Borne et VPX": "Installer Visual Pinball X" (Install Visual Pinball X); the ROM waits ("En attente", Pending) |
| Cabinet not set up | none | wizard on first start | "Borne et VPX": "Configurer la borne" (Set up the cabinet) |
| Table loose in the tables folder | `outsideFolder` | move it | "Rangement de la table" (Tidying the table), going further: the same fix |
| Table folder, or one of its subfolders, whose name was damaged by a copy under Node 22 ("Ã‰tÃ©" for "Été") | `folderMangled` | none: the table showed up twice, or its pack could not be found | "Rangement de la table" (Tidying the table): move the contents under the real name |
| Unreadable script, replaced by a `.vbs`, or adapted for an older version | `scriptUnreadable`, `scriptOverride`, `scriptPatchStale` | `.vbs` to the recycle bin; Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` rewritten from the new script | "ROM" not checked; the `.vbs` under "Rangement de la table" |
| Script stopped by the VPX 10.8 core.vbs (`vpmInit` called twice: "cvpmFlips2.Init is called twice") | `scriptCoreCompat` | none: the game stopped at every launch | repaired by itself before the game (Cyber Pinball's `.vbs`); protected script: "Rangement de la table" says what to remove |
| Missing ROM | `romMissing`, without a fix | "put the file in the import folder" | "ROM": its page in the VPS database, "Récupérer" window, awaited then received for the table, plan, back to the path |
| Neighbouring revision on the cabinet | `romAlias` (blocking) | write the alias | offered second, after the exact ROM |
| Only another revision in the VPS database | none | nothing | "Récupérer t2_l6, révision voisine" (Get t2_l6, neighbouring revision), then the alias |
| Old VPinMAME name | `romAliasHistoric` | write the alias, or "bond.zip is needed too" | the fix, or the real ROM to get |
| ROM in the library, not linked | `romNotLinked` | link it | the same fix |
| ROM elsewhere in the table folder | `romMisplaced` | put it away | the same fix |
| Alias no longer needed | `romAliasObsolete` | remove the line | "Conseillé" (Advised) |
| Missing DMD folder | `dmdFolderMissing` (warning) | gather the loose files, otherwise "add it"; it used to be blocking | "Contenu du DMD", going further: the fix, or the original table to get ("J'ai l'archive de la table", I have the table's archive); "Jouer" right away |
| Missing or misnamed backglass | `backglassMissing`, `backglassMisnamed` | information, or rename | "Fronton", going further: rename, or the backglass that suits the screens |
| PuP-Pack not found, one folder too deep, switched off by the script, option | `pupMissing`, `pupNested`, `pupDisabledByScript`, `pupOptionNotApplied`, `pupOptionUnsuited` | rename, move up, switch the pack on in a `.vbs`, apply the option | "PuP-Pack", going further: the fix, or the pack to get |
| Framing or screens forced by the table | `manualView`, `tableWindows` | remove the setting | "Affichage", going further |
| Screens changed, configuration to choose | none (seen at launch) | message before the game | outside the path: the launch opens the configuration choice |
| Another cabinet menu open | none (seen at launch) | "PinUP Popper est ouvert" (PinUP Popper is open) | outside the path |
| Joystick buttons VPX did not see | none (seen after the game) | redo the "Boutons" (Buttons) step | outside the path |

## Game page: its files

**Its actions**: the game page holds a few choices. At the top, "Jouer" (Play), "Jouer avec un ami" (Play with a friend) and "Prospectus et règles" (Flyer and rules): a single booklet, the flyer (or the generated sheet) then the rules (cards, texts and video found automatically, and always the game controls last); the flippers move from page to page, from one to the other, Start turns the page then goes back to the game page after the last one, and the mouse jumps to the other part. Everything else is under **"Gérer la table…"** (Manage the table…): "Pour aller plus loin" (Going further), "Médias du jeu" (Game media), "Parties du jeu" (Game parts), "Fichiers de la table" (Table files), "PuP-Pack", "Réglages de cette table" (This table's settings), "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game), "Jouer et enregistrer" (Play and record), "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and repair), "Modifier les informations" (Edit info), "Supprimer la table" (Delete the table). Its detail says, from the game page, what needs a look ("À vérifier : Fichiers de la table, PuP-Pack" (To check: Table files, PuP-Pack)), in orange. The screens opened from there come back to it (`sheetManage`, `app/src/library.ts`); Exit on "Gérer la table" goes back to the game page. "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What is missing to play) stays at the top of the game page, before "Jouer". In guest mode, the `sheet.manage` door is closed: the game page keeps "Jouer", "Prospectus et règles" and the back action. Demonstrations name an action under "Gérer la table…" by its name, and the autopilot goes through it, as for a system menu entry (`activateByLabel`).

A table's game page (Start on a table in the menu) shows its statistics on one line and a **bounded summary** of its files: one line per kind present (table, backglass, ROM, DMD content, PuP videos, DMD colours, alternative sounds, music, own settings, media), three names at most per line ("t2_l3, t2_l4 and 4 more"), a "Absents" (Missing) line for the missing kinds and a "À vérifier" (To check) line (misnamed backglass, script replaced by a `.vbs`, crash report). Upright (cabinet with a turned playfield, or portrait screen), the information keeps its place and the actions go on two columns; the action list scrolls if it overflows. On a cabinet, the action list used to take the whole height: with many files, only the first statistics line was still visible.

**"Fichiers de la table"** (Table files) (game page action, detail "66 files · 940 KB · 3 to check"): the whole table folder, grouped by kind, the table and its backglasses first. Each file is a line to choose there, with its full name (it wraps), its folder inside the table's, its state and its size: "cette table" (this table) or "autre version du dossier" (other version in the folder), backglass "chargé par VPX avec cette table" (loaded by VPX with this table) or "mal nommé : VPX ne le chargera pas" (misnamed: VPX will not load it), ROM "lien vers la bibliothèque de ROM" (link to the ROM library), "copie à part : la bibliothèque a aussi cette ROM" (separate copy: the library also has this ROM) or "propre à la table" (own to the table), "demandée par le script" (asked for by the script), PinMAME save and settings, override script, crash report. Folders filed as one block (a PuP-Pack, alternative sounds, DMD colours, FlexDMD content, menu media) are one line, with their number of files; music is listed file by file. What the script expects and is missing (ROM, DMD folder) appears at the top of its kind, "absente" (missing). With the buttons, the flippers go through the list, which scrolls to keep the chosen file visible; Start opens the file's **detail**: type, full folder, size, date, state, and for a linked ROM the library file it shares; "Bibliothèque de ROM" (ROM library) and "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and fix) lead there when useful. In the common tables folder, only the table's companion files (same name) belong to it. The inventory is read when the game page opens, not on each button press.

Code: [packages/core/src/tablecontents.ts](../packages/core/src/tablecontents.ts) (inventory, tests `tablecontents.test.ts`), screens in [app/src/table-files.ts](../app/src/table-files.ts), summary in `buildSheet` (`app/src/library.ts`), layout `.lib-sheet`, `.lib-files` and `.lib-facts` in `app/renderer/library.css`; lines to choose (`Row.pick`) do not repeat their action in the list.

## Game parts

A game is made of **parts**: the table (`.vpx`), the backglass (`.directb2s`), the ROM, the PuP-Pack, the DMD colours (Serum, VNI), the remastered sounds, the music, the point of view (`.pov`), its own settings (`.ini` named like the table), the topper and the menu media. The same parts are used everywhere: the waiting list (`NeedKind`, `PART_KINDS` in `packages/core/src/acquisition.ts`), the page of an installed table (`GAME_PARTS`, `installedParts` in `packages/core/src/gameparts.ts`) and Downloads.

- **On reception**: each file goes to the part its content says, added to the game if needed, several parts at once for a complete archive; a file of another nature never blocks installing what is ready (see [Adding a table](#adding-a-table-the-funnel-the-catalog-récupérer-waiting-list)).
- **"Parties du jeu" (Game parts)** (page of a table, after "Médias du jeu", Game media; closed to guests, door `sheet.parts`): one line per part, **Présent** (Present: files and size), **Reçu, à installer** (Received, to install), **Attendu** (Expected: waiting list), **Inutile** (Not needed: ROM of a table that uses none) or missing ("2 sur VPS", 2 on VPS, when the database knows some); then "Chercher sur le web…" (Search the web…) and **"Jeu VPS : …"** (VPS game). The VPS game is the one the table points to (`tableGame`, `packages/core/src/gameparts.ts`): its origin or its `.info`, otherwise the only game its media come from, otherwise the media's deep search (name written in the table, file, folder, ROM, PuP-Pack, author), kept when it is certain (0.8 at least, well ahead of the next one, or confirmed by the media). Not certain: "Jeu VPS : à choisir" (VPS game: to choose) shows the leads and "Chercher un titre…" (Search a title…); the choice is kept in the `.info` (`Info.VPSId`). Without a game, everything still works: the sites and the web search by the table's title.
- **A part: its proposals, from every source, at once**: opening a part starts the pre-search on all its sources (`partSources`) and each one redraws the screen when it arrives: the VPS database files ("Fichiers de VPS : voir les N au choix", VPS files: see the N to choose from, the file gallery: preview, popularity, sort), the already downloaded files that fit, **VPForums** (signed in: otherwise "Se connecter à VPForums…", Sign in to VPForums…) and **VPUniverse** (the part's categories, `PART_SITE_NODES`: PuP-Packs 120, backglasses 33 and 189, colours 173, 101 and 174, altsound 113, ROM 15, POV 68, tables 82, music 112, topper 160 and 93, media 72). The proposals are tiles of the media gallery (thumbnail, site, popularity, "Autre jeu", Other game, last); one line per source says what it gave ("recherche…", searching…, "3 propositions", "rien pour ce jeu", nothing for this game, "connectez-vous…", sign in…, wait imposed by the site). Start on a proposal: its page opens in "Récupérer" (Get: VPS or site), the received file is filed for this table, then "Installer ce qui est reçu" (Install what was received); an already downloaded file opens the add plan. **"Chercher sur le web…"** (Search the web…) is always there. Then: "Tous les fichiers téléchargés…" (All downloaded files…, those that do not fit included), **"Chercher un fichier ou un dossier…"** (Look for a file or a folder…: Cyber Pinball's file picker, a file or a whole folder through "Prendre ce dossier entier" (Take this whole folder), since a PuP-Pack, sounds or colours are a folder; a folder that holds several, the whole `PUPVideos` or `VPinMAME\altsound`, is narrowed to the table's own from the names its script expects, otherwise "<partie> : lequel ?" (<part>: which one?) lists its subfolders, `partFolderPick`, `packages/core/src/installation.ts`), "Installer ce qui est reçu" (Install what was received), "Options du PuP-Pack" (PuP-Pack options), "Médias du jeu" (Game media) for the media, **"Réaffecter à une autre table"** (Reassign to another table: its files are moved there, named like it for a backglass, a point of view or own settings; what it had instead goes to the trash) and **"Retirer de la table"** (Remove from the table: confirmation, cursor on "Annuler", Cancel; Cyber Pinball trash, `<root>\trash\<date> Parties du jeu`; a library ROM stays there; removing the PuP-Pack also removes Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` that turned it on). Every addition goes through the add plan, **targeting this table**: a pack whose name does not say the game, or a `.vpx` (another version), goes to it; "Annuler" (Cancel) goes back to the part's screen.
- **Code**: [app/src/game-parts.ts](../app/src/game-parts.ts) (screens), `routeReceived`, `repairWaitList`, `contentParts`, `partFits` in [packages/core/src/acquisition.ts](../packages/core/src/acquisition.ts), `target` of `planImport` in [packages/core/src/tableimport.ts](../packages/core/src/tableimport.ts). Tests: `packages/core/src/test/parties.test.ts` (two files from one page, each nature in its part, complete archive, nothing blocking, The Matrix case filed by itself, targeted plan, present parts).

## Never stuck: the web from every adding screen

Wherever something is added or chosen, a way out to the web: **"Chercher sur le web…"** (Search the web…) opens the "Récupérer" (Get) window on a pre-filled free search (DuckDuckGo; engines to choose in the banner; "vpinball" and the part's word added to the title, `partWebQuery`). What is downloaded there goes to the screen's game, in the part its content says, added if needed, then "Installer ce qui est reçu" (Install what was received); without a game (funnel, catalog, waiting list, Downloads, ROM library), the file opens the add plan of a new table.

- **Screens** (`ADD_SCREENS`, `packages/core/src/addscreens.ts`): the "Ajouter une table" (Add a table) funnel, the catalog, the page of a VPS database game, "Ce qu'il faut" (What it takes), "… : au choix" (… : choose one), "Récupérés et attendus" (Received and expected), a waiting list item and its replacement, "Parties du jeu" (Game parts), a part and its downloaded files, Downloads and "Ajouter à un jeu" (Add to a game), "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What's missing to play), "Médias du jeu" (Game media), a medium's screen ("Chercher moi-même sur le web…", Search the web myself…), its proposals, VPinMediaDB and the ROM library. The action carries its screen (`Action.web`); in a screen without icons, it has none either.
- **VPUniverse**: its search ignores words of three letters or fewer, and an "and" search then returns nothing ("goonies never say die"): they are removed from the address (`siteSearchText`), for media as for parts. A visitor gets only one search every 90 s or so: the "Please wait 88 seconds…" page is recognised (`reason: 'flood'`), never taken for "no results"; a site's searches run one after another, and the refused one starts again after the announced wait (150 s at most), the site's line saying so.
- **Invariants**: `packages/core/src/test/addscreens.test.ts` refuses an adding or choice screen without "Chercher sur le web…" (registry, and adding screen titles in the code), checks that each part is searched everywhere (VPS, Downloads, VPForums, VPUniverse, the web), that The Goonies is recognised with no identifier in its `.info`, and reads two VPUniverse witness pages (`packages/core/test-pages/vpuniverse-parties.html`, `vpuniverse-attente.html`).

## The phone as a remote for the built-in browser

A cabinet often has neither keyboard nor mouse, yet "Récupérer" (Get) asks you to sign in to community sites and click around them. The banner shows a QR code, **"Télécommande" (Remote)**: the phone, on the same Wi-Fi, opens a page served by the cabinet itself (no external server, nothing to install). It offers a **touchpad** (swipe: the pointer; tap: a click; two taps close together: a double click; two fingers: scrolling; long press or two-finger tap: right-click), **the phone's keyboard** as soon as a field of the page has the keyboard focus, and **Back, Forward, Keyboard, More, Close**. First milestone: the built-in browser only.

- **Pairing** (`packages/core/src/remote.ts`, `createRemotePairing`): the QR carries the page address and a 256-bit key **in the fragment** (`http://<cabinet>:<port>/#k=…`), which a browser never sends over the network. It is valid for three minutes (`REMOTE_PAIRING_MS`), then renews itself, and serves a single phone. The phone that read it shows a four-digit code, the banner the same one ("Même code ?", Same code?): **Start or "Accepter" (Accept) accepts it, Exit or "Refuser" (Refuse) refuses it**. The banner then takes the keyboard focus, with "Accepter" selected: Enter confirms the selected button, the arrow keys move from one to the other (real test on 06/11/2026: the page kept the keyboard focus, the mouse was needed). With no answer within a minute (`REMOTE_ACCEPT_MS`), it is refused. Once accepted, it receives a session key, which lets it come back after a break (page reloaded, screen locked, Wi-Fi lost for a moment) without being accepted again. **One phone at a time**: the QR disappears while a phone waits or drives; "Déconnecter" (Disconnect, banner or phone) shows a new one, with another key.
- **Encryption**: the page is served over HTTP (a certificate from the cabinet would make the phone show a security warning), where the browser offers no WebCrypto. Each message is therefore sealed with ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439), written in JavaScript for the phone (`app/renderer/telephone/telephone-core.js`) and in TypeScript for the cabinet (`packages/core/src/chacha.ts`): Electron ships BoringSSL, whose `createCipheriv` does not know this algorithm. The associated data bind each message to its connection (the other side's challenge) and an increasing number to its rank: a message replayed, from another connection or under another key cuts the link. Nobody on the Wi-Fi reads what is typed or drives the cabinet without the QR's key. Limit: the page itself travels in the clear; someone able to alter the Wi-Fi traffic at the moment it is opened could change it.
- **Server** (`app/src/phone-remote.ts`): it starts when a QR code is shown ("Récupérer"'s banner, the remote screen, the on-screen keyboard: see [The phone as a remote for the menu](#the-phone-as-a-remote-for-the-menu)) and stops when no QR is shown and no phone is connected any more (listening, link, keys). It serves only the phone page, its style sheets, its scripts, the font and the language catalogue (`/catalogue.js`), with a strict security policy (`default-src 'none'`), no cache and no referrer; it refuses any address outside the local network (`isLocalNetworkAddress`: 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, link-local, the machine itself), any host name other than the cabinet's addresses (DNS rebinding), any origin other than its own, more than four connections, a frame over 4 KB. Link: a minimal WebSocket with no dependency (`packages/core/src/websocket.ts`), a ping every ten seconds.
- **Network and firewall**: the QR's address is the private IPv4 of a real adapter (Wi-Fi, Ethernet; 192.168 first, never a virtual adapter, `pickLanAddress`). The "Cyber Pinball" rule of the Windows firewall targets `CyberPinball.exe` for every port (`firewall.ts`): the server, in the same process, benefits from it, with no extra rule and no prompt. **Without it, nothing listens**: Windows would show its prompt, which nobody can confirm at the cabinet, and would add blocking rules that would also cut the friends network. The block then stays visible, greyed out with its reason ("pare-feu de Windows à autoriser" (allow it in the Windows firewall), "aucun réseau local" (no local network), "lancé depuis les sources" (run from source)). During a test (simulated or disabled network, unattended test: `phoneRemoteMode`), 127.0.0.1 only; `CYBERPINBALL_RESEAU=non` turns it off.
- **Injection**: the phone drives the whole window, the site page **and the banner** (Web, Images, YouTube, Video engines, search field, buttons: real test on 06/11/2026, the engine could not be changed). Its gestures arrive where the pointer is, like those of a mouse and keyboard: `sendInputEvent` (moves, clicks, Enter, Tab, Backspace and arrow keys), and text one character at a time, as `char` events (accents, emojis: `typeChars`, `app/src/page-input.ts`); text and keys go where the phone last clicked (page or banner). Never `insertText`: it crashes the page's renderer (access violation) as soon as no element has focus in it, after Tab or when the page moves focus between two steps, as Google's does; that was the crash of 10/11/2026 on the PinCab, and its promise never settled, which blocked the queue of gestures. A gesture gives control back after 3 s at most, a gesture that fails is logged in `erreurs.txt` and dropped, and a page that crashes anyway is logged, reloaded once, the keyboard going to the banner where Esc and Exit always close. Safeguard and test bench: `packages/core/src/test/saisie-robuste.test.ts` (no `insertText` in the application; an off-screen bench, on local pages that mimic an email field with passkeys, a field replaced while typing, a navigation to another site in the middle of a burst, a cross-origin frame removed, a suggestion list, a page that crashes, a window closed during a burst). Two-finger scrolling always goes to the page (the banner does not scroll), preceded by a move on the spot. A phone that drives accepts nobody: while it drives, another phone is refused outright and the banner has neither "Accepter" nor "Refuser". What the phone may ask for is closed (`parsePhoneMessage`): never Escape, nor a function key, nor anything but the planned gestures.
- **Pointer**: the Windows pointer does not move; the phone's pointer is drawn in a transparent window laid over the whole "Récupérer" window (`app/renderer/remote-pointer.*`, `overlayContent` in `cover-display.ts`): without focus, without a taskbar button, attached to the window (above it, minimised and closed with it), and let through by clicks and by the cabinet's mouse. It glides at each screen frame towards its new place (damped pursuit, 26 ms time constant): Wi-Fi delivers the gestures in bursts, and a small view moved at each gesture advanced in jerks (real test on 06/11/2026). It follows the finger with acceleration (`movePointer`: slowly, a swipe from one edge of the pad to the other crosses a third of the screen; quickly, the whole screen), computed on a smoothed speed of the latest gestures (`smoothSpeed`), and a ring leaves the tip on each click. Frames go out without waiting (`setNoDelay`).
- **Keyboard**: the `REMOTE_FOCUS_PROBE` probe, run in every frame of the page (or of the banner) after a click, a key, a page load and every 0.7 s, tells whether an input field has the keyboard focus, its kind (text, password, email, number, address…), its label and **its text, which the phone takes over** so it can be corrected (real test on 06/11/2026: a filled field arrived empty). **Never the text of a password**, nor of a field the site marks as secret (`autocomplete`: card `cc-…`, one-time code, password shown in clear), nor of a rich area (`contentEditable`), nor a text over 512 characters. The phone then opens its panel: the field's label, a field of the same type (hidden for a password, without autocorrect), Backspace, "Champ suivant" (Next field, Tab), Enter. Field taken over: each change sends the whole text (`set` message), which the cabinet puts in place of the field's text (Ctrl+A, then the text), wherever it was corrected; the text is only sent back to the phone when the field changes (click, Tab, Enter, another field), never while typing in it. Field not taken over: what changes is sent as deletions then additions at the end (`textDiff`), the keyboard's corrections replace the last word. Nothing typed or taken over goes into the log; Cyber Pinball writes nothing into passwords or cookies: the user types, into the site's page.
- **Right-click**: the Windows menu could not be reached from the phone; a right-click from the phone therefore sends the page menu's actions to the phone ("Ouvrir le lien" (Open the link), "Télécharger cette image" (Download this image) in free search). The **"Plus" (More)** sheet offers what the banner offers, easier with a finger than with the pointer: "Retour à la fiche" (Back to the file page, or to the search), "Recharger la page" (Reload the page), "Télécharger cette vidéo" (Download this video), "Récupérer le son / la vidéo" (Get the sound / the video), "Annuler : <file>" (Cancel: <file>), the free search with its engines (Web, Images, YouTube, Video: another engine reruns the same text at once, as in the banner), "Déconnecter ce téléphone" (Disconnect this phone). **Close** asks for confirmation, then closes the built-in browser: the phone goes back to the menu buttons.
- **Phone page** (`app/renderer/telephone/*`): the cabinet's theme, text of at least 15 px, 48 px targets; the page follows the visible area when the phone's keyboard opens (`visualViewport`, `interactive-widget=resizes-content`); each state has its screen (connecting, codes to compare, reconnecting, refused, QR expired or already used, another phone connected, disconnected, browser closed).
- **Log** (`data\logs\journal.txt`): listening and address, phone reading the QR (its name, "Android · Chrome"), accepted, refused, disconnected, link cut and why (unreadable or replayed frame, refused request), QR expired or already used; never a typed text, a key or a page address.
- **Tests**: `packages/core/src/test/remote.test.ts` (encryption on both sides compared with `node:crypto` and the RFC vectors, pairing, addresses, messages, pointer, typing, WebSocket, QR decoded by jsQR); `packages/core/src/test/telecommande.test.ts` runs the remote control scenario end to end in a real off-screen Chromium (simulated phone: the real phone page, which reads the QR, is accepted by Start, drives, types, takes over and corrects a filled field but never a password, opens a link by a long press, clicks in the banner, disconnects; the drawn arrow catches up with the tip; then another host, another origin, QR already used, Exit, "Accepter" with Enter and the arrow keys, forbidden key, replayed frame, unknown key, Close) and pins the invariants (only `social-net.ts` and `phone-remote.ts` listen; no listening on the network without the firewall rule; no `createCipheriv` on the cabinet side).
- **Second milestone**: the same link for the whole menu, next section.

## The phone as a remote for the menu

Second milestone: the built-in browser's link serves the whole menu. The phone holds the cabinet's buttons, types instead of the on-screen keyboard and sends a file to the import folder. What the phone drives follows what the cabinet shows (`RemoteMode`): the site page when "Récupérer" (Get) is open (touchpad, previous section), **the menu** otherwise, nothing during a game.

- **Where the QR code lives**: **System menu > Phone remote**, at the root of the system menu and in the Cabinet section (`app/src/phone-remote-menu.ts`), a panel with the QR on the left and the state on the right ("Connecter un téléphone" (Connect a phone), then the four-digit code and "Comparez les codes" (Compare the codes), then "Téléphone connecté" (Phone connected) and "Déconnecter le téléphone" (Disconnect the phone)); **under the on-screen keyboard**, a "Taper au téléphone" (Type on your phone) strip with a small QR, then the code, then "Le téléphone connecté tape ici" (The connected phone types here) (its height does not change from one state to another: the keys do not move); "Récupérer"'s banner, as before. The same pairing everywhere: three-minute single-use QR, code on both sides, **Start accepts, Exit refuses**. On these screens, while a phone is waiting, Start and Exit belong to it, never to the screen behind (Start does not type a key on the on-screen keyboard). When the screen that showed the code closes, the waiting phone is refused. Unavailable (firewall, no local network), the block stays, greyed out with the reason, with no readable QR.
- **How long the link lasts**: it lives as long as Cyber Pinball's menu runs, games included. Opening "Récupérer" switches the phone to the touchpad, closing it brings it back to the buttons, without a new acceptance. It ends when the phone disconnects ("Plus" (More) > "Déconnecter ce téléphone" (Disconnect this phone)), when it is disconnected at the cabinet, **after 20 minutes without a gesture in the menu** (`REMOTE_IDLE_MS`, game time does not count), or when Cyber Pinball closes (the phone says "Cyber Pinball fermé" (Cyber Pinball closed)). With no QR shown and no phone, nothing listens.
- **Buttons**: Start (confirm), left flipper (previous), right flipper (next), Exit (back), MagnaSave (on the wheel, the next collection; elsewhere, a screen's list or actions; the phone's button says which). Each is sent pressed then released; the cabinet hands it to the **cabinet's key path** (`frontend.ts`, `remoteButton` → `handleKey`), with that button's key on this cabinet (`interfaceKeys`, `zoneSwitchKeys`): whatever a cabinet button does, it does. A held flipper repeats after 0.4 s, every 70 ms (`isAutoRepeat`): the menu speeds up as under a real button, the phone's button says "Défilement rapide" (Fast scroll). A held button is released when the finger lifts or leaves the button, when the page goes to the background, when the link is cut, after 20 s at most. Top of the page: the screen shown and what is selected on it.
- **Typing**: when the menu opens its on-screen keyboard (title search, player name, configuration name, Art-Net node address, LED count…), the phone opens its keyboard panel **with the cabinet's text** (never a password: the menu asks for none); what is typed replaces that text (`library.ts`, `remoteSet`), a number keeps its digits. "Valider" (Confirm) is Enter, "Annuler" (Cancel) is Exit. If the text changes at the cabinet meanwhile, the phone takes it over. Nothing typed goes into the log.
- **Sending a file**: "Envoyer" (Send) picks a file on the phone. The cabinet checks before any transfer: **2 GB at most** (`REMOTE_UPLOAD_MAX`), a non-empty file, disk space (512 MB left free), guest mode (`phone.files` door, closed). The name is cleaned (`cleanUploadName`): the last element only, nothing of a path, no character Windows forbids, no dot at the start or end, never a device name (`CON`, `NUL`…), 120 characters at most; a name already taken becomes "(2)". The 256 KiB chunks go one after the other over HTTP (`POST /envoi/<transfer>/<rank>`), sealed under the link's key, bound to the connection, the transfer and their rank (`uploadAad`): a chunk replayed, moved, from another connection or addressed to another host name is refused and the transfer dropped. The file is written aside (`.envoi-<transfer>.part`, which the import folder ignores), then takes its name in the import folder, never anywhere else; dropped (Cancel, link cut, a minute without a chunk), it is deleted. Progress on the phone ("34 % · 12,4 Mo sur 36,2 Mo", "Envoi 34 %" on the button) and on the cabinet ("Réception depuis le téléphone · 34 %" (Receiving from the phone) banner), then "Reçu du téléphone : <name>, dans le dossier d'import" (Received from the phone); it then shows up in System menu > Tables > Add a table.
- **Faster encryption**: ChaCha20 with no allocation per block, Poly1305 in ten 13-bit limbs (poly1305-donna 16-bit) instead of BigInt, on both sides: about 70 MB/s instead of 9, compared with `node:crypto` by the tests. A 256 KiB chunk opens in a few milliseconds in the main process.
- **Game**: VPX has the buttons; the page says "Partie en cours" (Game in progress), Keyboard and Send are greyed out ("Après la partie", After the game), the buttons do nothing. On return, the phone drives the menu again.
- **Guest mode**: the phone's buttons go through the same doors as the cabinet's (`GUEST_DOORS`); the phone **never unlocks** guest mode (its presses do not count in the combination); sending files is closed (`phone.files`); the remote screen is in the system menu, closed to guests.
- **Phone page, menu mode**: `body[data-mode]`, one detailed panel at a time (the keyboard wins, the transfer goes on and its percentage stays on "Envoyer"); when the visible height drops below 680 px (phone keyboard open), a typing view replaces the buttons; Start turns dark again when "Valider" is on screen or a flipper is held (a single green); an unavailable tool stays, greyed out with its reason ("Aucun champ" (No field), "Après la partie").
- **Tests**: `remote.test.ts` (buttons, text, transfer: closed messages, cleaned names, sealed chunks refused from elsewhere, large chunks correct and fast); `telecommande.test.ts` replays the scenario end to end: the real table management off screen, the QR read **from the screenshot of the screen itself**, the real phone page accepted by Start, Exit and a held flipper on the phone, typing and confirming, a file sent byte for byte, then the refusals (over 2 GB, guest mode, name climbing folders, chunk from another connection, replayed chunk, other host), a game, "Récupérer" taking and giving back the phone, the on-screen keyboard's QR, inactivity, Disconnect, closing; the layout guard rereads every cabinet screen. `guestmode.test.ts` pins the path through `handleKey` and the `phone.files` door.

## ROM library

A ROM is often used by several tables (versions, mods of the same machine). Each ROM is stored **only once**, in `<racine>\pinmame\roms`, and each table that plays it gets a **hard link** to it in its own `pinmame\roms` (where VPX 10.8.1 looks first): the same file on disk, taking no extra space. The folder was not invented: `<racine>\pinmame` is already the shared PinMAME folder that the sync suggests for `[Plugin.PinMAME] PinMAMEPath` as soon as it contains `roms`; a table without a link therefore still finds its ROM there, as a fallback, **provided it has no `pinmame\roms` folder**: as soon as that folder exists, the PinMAME plugin looks only there (`PinMAMEPlugin.cpp`, lines 436-477, commit af26b2d93), and the doctor reports it as blocking when the ROM is missing from it. Code: [packages/core/src/romlibrary.ts](../packages/core/src/romlibrary.ts).

**Per-table ROM aliases** ([packages/core/src/romalias.ts](../packages/core/src/romalias.ts)): the plugin also reads `alias.txt` in the table's PinMAME folder (`<table>\pinmame`), one line "name requested by the script,game loaded", even before the ROM. The doctor uses it in three ways:

- **Neighboring revision**: the requested ROM is missing, but another revision of the same game (`t2_l6` for `t2_l8`) is in the table or the library; on request, it links it and writes the alias. The warning says so: lamps, coils or switches may differ, settings and high scores (NVRAM) are not carried over, and remastered sounds and DMD colors stored under the other name are no longer found. As long as the alias is in use, a reminder stays in the diagnosis.
- **Old VPinMAME names** ("goldfinger" for "bond", 119 names taken from PinMAME's `release/VPMAlias.txt`, in [packages/core/src/vpmalias.ts](../packages/core/src/vpmalias.ts)): VPinMAME translated them on its own, but the VPX 10.8.1 plugin no longer knows them; the doctor writes the line, and says whether the real ROM is missing too.
- **Alias no longer needed**: the requested ROM has arrived, but the alias takes precedence over it; "Revenir à la ROM demandée" (Back to the requested ROM) removes the line (and the file, if no line is left).

- **Same volume required**: a hard link only connects two folders on the same disk (NTFS). A table on a different volume from the root (junction to another disk, FAT32 or exFAT drive) gets a **copy**, and the plan says so.
- **Identity**: two files with the same name are the same ROM if their content is the same: the fingerprint covers the chips in the zip (path, CRC and size of each file), so the same ROM zipped by two tools (different compression, different dates) remains a single version; a file that is not a readable zip is compared byte for byte (SHA-1). Two different contents under the same name: the most recent (file date) is kept, or the one chosen in the plan ("Garder plutôt…" (Keep instead…)); the other goes to the recycle bin. An identical copy replaced by a link is deleted (its content stays in the library, byte for byte: this is checked just before); any other replaced file goes to the recycle bin.
- **Adding**: an imported ROM enters the library, then is linked into **every installed table that plays it**: those whose script requests it without having any copy, those that already have a copy, and the table the import is meant for. A new table whose ROM is already in the library gets it without importing anything more. A ROM that no table is expecting yet is stored anyway, for the table to come. Scripts of tables not yet read are read before the plan (VPX `-ExtractVBS`, about one second each, then cached; if VPX does not return the script, the one written in the `.vpx` is read without it, unless the script is protected).
- **ROM recognised by its content** ([packages/core/src/romzip.ts](../packages/core/src/romzip.ts)): PinMAME (VPinMAME as well as the VPX 10.8.1 plugin) only reads a ROM as `<nom>.zip`, **its chips at the root of the zip**. An archive is a ROM when it contains nothing but chips (no table, image, video, sound or other archive; `.txt` notes are tolerated), all in one folder: at the root, or in a subfolder (`t2_l8.zip › t2_l8 › t2_l8.rom, t2_u14.l3…`, like many downloaded ROMs). It is recognised everywhere: a zip on its own, a zip inside a table's package (zip, 7z, rar), a ROM as 7z or rar, a folder of unpacked chips (`roms\t2_l8\`). Its name comes, in this order, from a ROM requested by a table's script and carried by the archive or a folder (case included: `T2_L8.ZIP` becomes `t2_l8.zip`), from a known ROM name (VPS database, old VPinMAME names), from a chip named like a requested ROM (`t2_l8.rom` in "Terminator 2 ROM.zip"), from the only requested ROM of the same family as the chips (`t2_u14.l3` → `t2_l8`), and finally from the archive name. A ROM whose chips are in a folder is **rebuilt as a flat zip** (chips copied as they are, without recompression, dated like the original archive) before entering the library; the plan says so ("ROM t2_l8 reconnue dans le dossier « t2_l8 »" (ROM t2_l8 recognised in the "t2_l8" folder)), as it does for a rename. Its files are never stored one by one in a table's folder.
- **Tidying up** (system menu, Exit in the menu: "Ranger les ROM dans la bibliothèque" (Move ROMs into the library)): for ROMs from before the library. The plan shows, ROM by ROM, the one that enters the library, the copies replaced by a link, the links added, the versions set aside and the space freed; nothing moves until "Ranger" (Tidy up). The summary gives the space taken by ROMs measured before and after.
- **Overall view** (system menu: "Bibliothèque de ROM" (ROM library)), with the cabinet buttons: each ROM, the tables that use it and its size; the ROMs to tidy up; **unused** ROMs (no table has it or expects it), with the recycle bin offered one by one or for all; ROMs **expected but missing**, with "Ajouter" (Add). Disk space counts each physical file once and states what the links save. Only ROMs in `pinmame\roms` folders are concerned: `.zip` files stored elsewhere (FlexDMD fonts, for example) are not ROMs.
- **Doctor**: "ROM pas liée dans la table" (ROM not linked in the table) (it is in the library, not in `pinmame\roms`: link it) and "Copie de ROM au lieu d'un lien" (ROM copy instead of a link) (replace it with a link; for a different version, use the library one, the other goes to the recycle bin). The table's details screen marks a linked ROM with "(bibliothèque)" ((library)).
- **Scattered ROM, ROM in a subfolder** (repairing what versions up to 0.1.3-essai.1 left behind: they unpacked a ROM zip with a subfolder and stored its chips at the root of the table): "ROM « t2_l8 » en fichiers éparpillés dans la table" (ROM "t2_l8" scattered as loose files in the table) spots the chips of the requested ROM left at the root of the table (same name as the ROM or same family: `t2_l8.rom`, `t2_u14.l3`…; never `.vgm`, `.ini` or `.log`) or in a folder named after it; "Refaire t2_l8.zip dans la bibliothèque et le lier" (Rebuild t2_l8.zip in the library and link it) rebuilds the flat zip, stores it in the library, links it in `pinmame\roms` and moves the chips to the recycle bin. If the ROM is already there (library, link, alias), all that is left is moving these files to the recycle bin. "ROM illisible : ses fichiers sont dans un sous-dossier du zip" (ROM unreadable: its files are in a subfolder of the zip) spots such a zip put by hand in `pinmame\roms`, and "ROM mal rangée" (Misplaced ROM) a subfolder zip elsewhere in the table: both rebuild it flat. The "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What's missing to play) journey offers these fixes on the spot, before any search.

Deleting a table only sends its link to the recycle bin: the ROM stays in the library, and the overall view then offers it as unused if no table plays it anymore.

## PuP-Packs

Cyber Pinball targets **VPX 10.8.1 plugin mode**: the PUP plugin shipped with VPX replaces PinUP Player, so there is nothing to install or register (classic mode will come later, DOF goes through the VPX DOF plugin: see [LED and feedback (DOF)](#led-and-feedback-dof)). The plugin looks for the pack in `<dossier de la table>\pupvideos\<nom>`, case-insensitively, where `<nom>` is the value the script passes to `PuPlayer.B2SInit` or `Init`, or failing that the ROM being played.

**When adding** ([packages/core/src/puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts), [tableimport.ts](../packages/core/src/tableimport.ts)), a pack (a folder containing `screens.pup`, `playlists.pup`, `triggers.pup`, or options that contain them) is:

- **named** the way the script loads it: name passed to `B2SInit` (a literal, or a variable such as `pGameName`, `cPuPPack`), then the ROM, then the name passed to `Init`. A pack already correctly named keeps its name; a pack named otherwise ("Monster Bash PuP-Pack v2") takes the expected name. Without a readable script (VPX missing), it keeps its folder name and the plan points this out;
- **flattened**: `rom\rom\…`, or an archive folder that contains only the pack (a readme next to it does not get in the way);
- **cleaned**: `._*`, `.DS_Store`, `__MACOSX` and 0-byte MP4 files are not installed;
- **replaced entirely** if it already exists: the old folder goes to the recycle bin, never a mix;
- **configured**: the screen layout option is applied by copying its files, **without ever running a `.bat`**.

**Options.** Published packs store their variants in `PuP-Pack_Options\<option>\` and ship one `.bat` per option at the root, for example `Option 2 - FullDMD on Backglass.bat`:

```bat
rem echo off
xcopy /y "PuP-Pack_Options\Option 2 - FullDMD on Backglass\*"
del /Q PuPDesktopPunch.exe
del /Q PuPInit.bat
```

Every `.bat` whose name mentions an option (or that copies files) is **read, not run**. Recognized: `copy` and `xcopy` of a folder, a pattern (`*`, `*.pup`) or a file of the pack to its root or one of its folders (`/s`, `/e` to recurse), `del`/`erase` of files at the root (they go to the recycle bin), and anything with no effect: `rem`, `::`, `echo`, `@echo off`, `pause`, `cls`, `exit`, `title`, `cd`/`pushd` to the `.bat` folder (`%~dp0`, `.`), `>nul`. Anything else makes the analysis fail: absolute path, `..`, variable (`%PUPDIR%`), `start`, `if`, `for`, `call`, `set`, `move`, `ren`, `rd`, chaining (`&`, `|`), redirection to a file, copy with no matching file. Option folders with no readable `.bat` (packs shipped with only `PuP-Pack_Options`, like those applied by hand on Linux) also become options. Classic-mode tool `.bat` files (`PuPInit.bat`, `configdisplays.bat`) are ignored.

**Choosing the option**, automatically, based on the cabinet model: the option name ("Topper", "FullDMD on Backglass", "2 Screen", "Desktop", "16x9", "4x3", "AIO", "Stream"…) and then its `screens.pup` (screen 0 on: topper; screen 5 or 1 inside the backglass through CustomPos: DMD in the backglass) are compared with the assigned roles (backglass, DMD, topper) and with the aspect ratios of the DMD (real 4:1 or FullDMD 16:9) and of the backglass. A desktop or all-in-one cabinet option is never picked on a cabinet. If the choice is not clear-cut, if the cabinet is not configured, or if a `.bat` could not be read, the "Quelle option du PuP-Pack ?" (Which PuP-Pack option?) screen opens before the plan, showing the rejected `.bat` and the reason; from the plan, "Changer l'option du PuP-Pack" (Change the PuP-Pack option) reopens it. The chosen option and the pack's original name are kept in the table's `.info` (`CyberPinball.pup.<nom>`, other fields are preserved): when the pack is updated, the same option is reused without asking.

**DMD frame on the backglass.** Many packs have neither a DMD screen (1) nor a FullDMD screen (5), but a screen placed on the backglass whose name or playlist mentions DMD (*Star Trek LE*: screen 13 "BGdmdText", at 26 %, 79 %, over 48 % × 21 % of the backglass). When it is on, the pack plays its own animations there: the real DMD stays on its own screen (option for a cabinet with a DMD). When it is off, the frame stays empty so the DMD can be placed there (option for a two-screen cabinet). With no screen 1, no screen 5 and no frame, nothing about the DMD is inferred from `screens.pup`: a pack without a DMD screen does not mean "cabinet without a DMD". The ROM's DMD cannot show inside a pack's frame: the PUP plugin, which then draws the backglass window, never draws the DMD.

**Changing the option after installation**: table details screen > "PuP-Pack", offered as soon as one of the table's packs has options (`tablePupPacks`, `packages/core/src/doctor.ts`). The screen shows the applied option, the one that suits the cabinet, and every option with what it targets (topper, DMD on its own screen or in the backglass, FullDMD, aspect ratios). Start applies the chosen option: its files are copied, the files they replace and those its `.bat` deletes go to the trash (`trash\<date> PuP-Pack <folder>`: the previous state, to go back to it), and no `.bat` is ever run (`pupOptionOps`). If one of the option's files is missing from the pack, nothing is touched and the message names it (`missingSources`). After the copy, the pack is read again: "appliquée" (applied) is only shown if the option is recognised in place, otherwise the message says what is missing (`checkAppliedOption`). The choice is remembered in the `.info` (`option`, and `chosen: "user"`: the doctor no longer questions it) and applies from the next game; "Jouer" (Play) is offered on the same screen to see it. On the details screen, the "PuP-Pack" line turns orange when another option suits the cabinet better. "Pour cette borne" (For this cabinet) names the recommended option and the cabinet as configured (backglass, FullDMD or 4:1 DMD, topper); when two options are equally good, it names both, to be tried. A list taller than the screen shows a rule on the edge behind which entries remain, at the top as at the bottom, and the selected entry is brought whole into view when the selection changes (`markOverflow`, `app/renderer/library.js`, every list of the management screens). **A list's position only follows the user's gestures** (flippers, Tab, click, mouse wheel): a screen redrawn by an update (download progress, "Attente du site · 27 s" (Waiting for the site · 27 s), state of a received file) puts every list back where it was, without bringing the selected item back into view; the proposals can be browsed with the wheel while a card downloads. Only `app/renderer/scroll-keeper.js` scrolls a list (menu and VPX settings): it records every scrolled list before the render and restores it after, and only brings the selection into view if it changed or another screen opens (which starts from the top); a list whose content changes in nature carries its own key (`data-scroll-key`, details of a VPX setting) and starts from the top. What is on screen does not move either: the selected item, if it was in view, stays at the same place in its list when an update inserts something above it (a site's "Non connecté" (Not signed in) banner at the top of the proposals), and if it was whole and grows ("Choisi, à enregistrer" (Chosen, to be saved)), the list moves just enough for it to stay whole. A screen opened directly on an item further down (media received from a friend) keeps it whole as long as no gesture has followed, even when the cards' images load afterwards. These positions are measured in the list's layout (`offsetTop`), never on screen: a cabinet's playfield is rotated and `getBoundingClientRect` measures the other axis there (a selection that overflowed passed for whole). Never a fade: it made the last visible line look disabled; the layout guard reports a fade on a scrolling list and a selection that is not whole in its list, `defilement.test.ts` rejects a fade in the stylesheets. A list on its own (system menu, sections) starts just below the title, at the same height from one screen to the next, centred horizontally only.

**Recognising the option in place** (`appliedOption`, [puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)). Everything the option copies must be at the root, identical, and what its `.bat` deletes must be absent; only the preview images placed at the root (`Option 2.JPG`, for PinUP Popper) do not count. The `.pup` files alone are not enough: on *Guardians of the Galaxy* (`gotg_2020`), options 0 "3 Screen Desktop" and 2 "3 Screen FullDMD" have the same `screens.pup`, `playlists.pup` and `triggers.pup`; only `Option Selected.txt` and the desktop tools (`PuPDesktopPunch.exe`, `PuPInit.bat`) tell them apart. A complete option comes before an option whose `.pup` files alone are there ("en partie seulement", partly only, with what is missing); between options with identical files, the one remembered in the `.info` wins, and the screen says so ("Mêmes fichiers", same files). The `.bat` is read in order: `del Option*.jpg` then `xcopy … Option 2.JPG` leaves the preview in place (before, it went to the trash right after being copied). An option's folder is the folder of its `.pup` files; an options folder that a `.bat` copies does not become a second option, and two options never share a name: every GOTG option appeared twice, and the tie between twins hid the recommendation ("pas de choix net", no clear choice). A backup folder (`PuP-Pack_Options - Copy`, `_backup`, `….orig`, `….bak`) is never an option (`isBackupName`). "NoDMD" written as one word reads as "No DMD".

**PuP-Pack switched off by the script.** Some scripts only start the pack when a variable allows it, and ship it switched off: *2001 - A Space Odyssey (Original 2025)* writes `usePUP = False` ("enable Pinup Player functions for this table").
