However well the pack is placed and its option applied, the script never calls `PuPlayer.B2SInit`: the PUP plugin loads nothing (`vpinball.log` says "Plugin PUP loaded", never "PUP path") and the game only shows the usual backglass and DMD. `pupScriptSwitch` ([puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)) follows the conditions around `CreateObject("PinUpPlayer.PinDisplay")`, in its Sub and then where that Sub is called, and keeps the variable whose starting value, a literal never switched back on elsewhere, prevents reaching it. It stays silent when the value comes from a table option, a file read or another variable (*Guardians of the Galaxy*: `DMDMode`). To switch the pack on without modifying the table, Cyber Pinball writes `<table>.vbs` next to the `.vpx`: VPX 10.8.1 plays that file instead of the table's script (`PinTable::LoadScriptOverride`; the log says "Loading script: …"). It is the script read from the `.vpx` (`readVpxScript`, [vpxinfo.ts](../packages/core/src/vpxinfo.ts), without VPX), byte for byte, with only the value changed, plus a last line `' CyberPinball-script {…}` that keeps the fingerprint of the original script ([scriptpatch.ts](../packages/core/src/scriptpatch.ts)); script error line numbers do not move. That `.vbs` is not a "replaced script": the details screen says "Script adapté" (Adapted script). When a table is added with its pack, or a pack for a table already installed, the plan writes it automatically; when the table is updated, the plan rewrites it from the new script, or moves it to the recycle bin if the new version starts the pack by itself. A `.vbs` from elsewhere is never replaced: the doctor offers to switch the pack on in it. A protected (encrypted) script cannot be read without VPX: the doctor then says which value to change.

**Relayed switch, and PuP-DMD.** *The Goonies Never Say Die Pinball (VPW 2021)* ships `Const enablePupPack = False`, then `Dim HasPuP : HasPup = enablePupPack`, and only starts the pack `If HasPup Then`. You could hear the table's music, never the pack. The log said "Plugin PUP loaded", with no "PUP path". `pupScriptSwitch` now follows a variable that only copies another one (two relays at most, never changed in a Sub) back to the literal value: that is the one switched on. The PuP-DMD (the score the script writes on the pack's DMD screen, `LabelInit`) often has its own switch (`Const enablePupDMD = False`), found the same way by `pupDmdSwitch`. Switched off, the pack lays its empty frame (`PuPFrames\default.png`) in the DMD window, on top of the table's DMD, and the script asks PinUP Player to hide that screen (`SendMSG` FN 12), which the PUP plugin does not do. `pupSwitchesToEnable` therefore switches both on when the option in place takes the DMD window (`packDrawsDmd`) of a cabinet that has one, otherwise the pack alone. The `.vbs` keeps them all in its last line (`kind: 'dmd'` for the PuP-DMD). The doctor reads the script again from the `.vpx` rather than trusting the cached reading, which might predate this (readings at version 6: an older reading of the same table is redone without VPX, `cachedScript`). Adding a table or a pack does the same.

**Pack nobody starts.** *Game of Thrones LE (VPW, Stern 2015)* is an original table with FlexDMD and no ROM. Its script never creates `PinUpPlayer.PinDisplay` ("`Dim PuPlayer ' Not currently used`"); mikeleonheart's pack was made for PinUP Player hooked to B2S Server, which looked for it under the game name given to B2S (`cGameName = "gameofthrones"`). The VPX 10.8.1 PUP plugin only loads a pack by itself for a PinMAME game (`OnControllersChanged` only keeps a `pinmame::…` game, never `b2s::…`): the log said "Plugin PUP loaded", never "PUP path". The doctor said nothing: with no PuP call and no ROM, it expected no name, and none of its rules applied. It was the third pack in a row installed with no video on the backglass. Now `pupLoaderNeeded` ([puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)) recognises this case; the expected name is the game name for B2S (`b2sName`, read from `.B2SName = "…"` or `cGameName`), failing that the name of the pack's only folder, and the pack is renamed to it (the option kept in the `.info` follows, `renamePupMemory`). Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` then starts the pack the way the table would (`addPupLoader`): `Sub Table1_Init() : CyberPinball_PuPStart` on the Sub's line (the table object's name is read from the `.vpx`, `readVpxTableName`), and, after the last line, a Sub that creates `PinUpPlayer.PinDisplay` and calls `B2SInit "", "<pack>"`, under `On Error Resume Next` then `Err.Clear`. Line numbers do not move; the last line keeps `"loader": {…}`, and the `.vbs` is rebuilt when the table is updated as long as the new script does not start the pack itself. It is done before each game ("PuP-Pack jamais lancé" (PuP-Pack never started), in `PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`). Safeguard: a pack present that no name points to (several folders, no game name) gives "PuP-Pack jamais chargé" (PuP-Pack never loaded) instead of silence. The test `puplancement.test.ts` simulates the plugin (name given by the played script, or ROM, then `pupvideos\<name>\screens.pup`) on each kind of table: no ROM and no PuP, `B2SInit` under the right or another name, `usePUP = False`, PinMAME game; after the pre-game repairs, each one loads its pack.

**DMD image triggers without PinMAME.** The *Game of Thrones* pack has 185 active triggers, 183 of them `D<n>` with `PupCapture\<n>.bmp`: PinUP Player recognised the table's DMD images. The PUP plugin only receives these images from PinMAME ROMs (`GetIdentifyFrame`); FlexDMD does not provide them (`FlexDMDPlugin.cpp`, "TODO … pup events"). The script, however, can read the FlexDMD frame: `FlexDMD.DmdPixels` returns 128 × 32 bytes at the exact 16 levels of the captures (0, 17 … 255 in `GRAY_4`), as an array VBScript reads dot by dot. So Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` redoes the plugin's matching ([pupcapture.ts](../packages/core/src/pupcapture.ts)): `Sub FrameTimer_Timer() : CyberPinball_PuPCapture` on the line of the Sub of the table's frame timer (the one whose script sets `Interval = -1`), and, after the last line, a block that reads the frame at most every 20 ms of `GameTime`, compares 16 probe dots then the hash of the area of the candidate captures only, and sends `B2SData "D<n>", 1` then `0` to the object that started the pack (`CyberPinball_PuP`, or the script's own), like libpupdmd: the first capture by increasing number, never the same one twice in a row. The block's first line carries its settings (pack, objects, timer, depth, hash of the `PupCapture` files), and so does the `.vbs`'s last line (`"capture": {…}`): when the captures or the script change, the block is rebuilt in place; when the table is updated, it follows the rebuilt `.vbs`. The doctor offers it ("Vidéos du PuP-Pack déclenchées par le DMD : sans effet" (PuP-Pack videos triggered by the DMD: no effect), fix "Reconnaître les images du DMD" (Match the DMD frames)) and the menu does it by itself before the game (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`). Impossible without a `FlexDMD.FlexDMD` object, with a colour DMD, without a frame timer, without the pack's object in a variable, or with a protected script: the finding says so, with no fix. Nothing is done when the script sends `D` triggers itself (`PuPlayer.B2SData "D…"`, `dmdTriggers`). On *Game of Thrones*, 120 of the 183 active captures are, dot for dot, a frame of a FlexDMD asset; the others mix an asset with the script's text, to be checked on the cabinet. Cost measured in `cscript`: 0.28 ms per read of an ordinary frame. Tests: `pupcapture.test.ts` runs the generated VBScript in `cscript` (simulated FlexDMD and PinDisplay) and compares it with libpupdmd's matching; `pupcapture-medecin.test.ts` checks the repair, updated captures and table, a `.vbs` from elsewhere, `usePUP = False`. Script readings at version 7.

**Background already in place.** The PUP plugin restarts a screen's background video on every `SetBG` trigger, even when it is the one already playing (`PUPMediaManager::Play`); PinUP Player let it play on. On *Game of Thrones*, the DMD's "choose your house" screen alternates with a filler frame: D11 (Stark, `SetBG`) restarted on every return, and the backglass showed almost only the video's black start, frame included, with flashes of Stark. `backgroundTriggers` ([pupcapture.ts](../packages/core/src/pupcapture.ts)) reads from `triggers.pup` the `D<n>` triggers whose active lines all set a file as background; the matching only sends them when they change at least one screen's background (`CyberPinball_PuPCapChanges`). A trigger that does anything else goes out as before. `triggers.pup` is part of the block's fingerprint (`captureSignature`): a `.vbs` already written is rebuilt before the next game. A background changed by a trigger the matching does not send (`E…`, sent by the table) is not tracked.

**Files the plugin reads differently.** The background fix was not enough: the *Game of Thrones* backglass still flickered. The real cause was in the pack's files. The house sigils (`Choose your House\Stark.mp4`…) are PNG or JPEG images, the backglass background (`Backglass\Backglass.mp4`) a JPEG, all named `.mp4`. PinUP Player showed them as images; the PUP plugin decides by extension (`PUPScreen::Play`: only `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg` and `.bmp` are images) and hands them to FFmpeg, which plays them as one-frame videos, in a loop: an almost black backglass, with flashes of the sigil. The same pack names `9y10perro y montaña.mp4` in a UTF-8 `triggers.pup`; the plugin converts that name with the Windows code page (`std::filesystem::path` from a `std::string`, `PUPTrigger::CreateFromCSV`), does not find the file and drops the trigger ("PlayFile not found" in `vpinball.log`). `packFileFixes` ([pupfiles.ts](../packages/core/src/pupfiles.ts)) reads the nature of each playlist file from its first bytes (`mediaKind`): an image named like a video gets its true extension back, a name with accents (file or playlist folder) becomes ASCII (`asciiName`), never overwriting another file, and every `.pup` in the pack, options and backups included, is rewritten byte for byte except the changed names: the option in place is still recognised. A name the table's script mentions is never changed. This is the finding "Fichiers du PuP-Pack que le plugin PUP lirait mal" (PuP-Pack files the PUP plugin would misread), repaired before each game (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`) and now also as soon as a table or a pack is installed (`install`, [library.ts](../app/src/library.ts), which applies all the `PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS` repairs). What remains missing, a file named by an active trigger or a lit screen and absent from the pack, is reported ("Fichiers du PuP-Pack absents" (PuP-Pack files missing), `missingPackFiles`, no fix): on the cabinet, bietekwiet's FullDMD video for *Game of Thrones*, and one file in the *2001*, *Star Trek* and *The Matrix* packs. The test `pupfichiers.test.ts` simulates the plugin's reading (names read with the Windows code page, case-insensitive) and checks that after the repairs every named file is found and no image carries a video extension.

**Backglass left to the `.directb2s`, and repairs before the game.** An option may place the Backglass screen (2) inside the FullDMD (5): on *2001*, "(3 Screens) B2S Users, …, Pup on Fulldmd" writes `2,Backglass,…,"5,12.7,13.5,74.8,74.6"`. The plugin then draws nothing in the backglass window: a window is only filled if its root (topper: screen 0; backglass: 2, otherwise 6; DMD: 5, otherwise 1) is not a screen placed inside another one with no children, and a visible screen descends from it (`PUPManager::Render`, reproduced by `pupWindows` in [puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)). These "B2S Users" options rely on the table's `.directb2s`; without it, the backglass stays on a still image. The automatic choice therefore reads, in each option's `screens.pup`, which windows it fills: a backglass left empty costs a lot if the table has no `.directb2s`, a little if that is unknown, almost nothing otherwise (the pack on the backglass stays preferred). Whether the topper window is filled wins over the name ("Default Locale Topper Off" puts the topper in the main screen). The doctor then offers the option that puts the pack on the backglass ("Option du PuP-Pack inadaptée à la borne", with "fronton laissé au .directb2s" (backglass left to the .directb2s) and "table sans .directb2s" (table without a .directb2s)). Before each game, the menu itself applies the pack repairs the doctor would offer (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`, `repairPupForPlay`: pack renamed or moved up, option copied or changed, script that switches the pack off, stale Cyber Pinball `.vbs`): without them, a pack stayed off until the doctor was opened. An option chosen on the details screen is kept, the table is never modified, replaced files go to the recycle bin (`trash\… Avant la partie <folder>`), and the menu's log notes each repair ("PuP-Pack (…) : …").

**Playlist order.** A screen whose `screens.pup` gives the playlist without a file plays the first file of the folder. The PUP plugin sorts those files case-sensitively (`PUPPlaylist.cpp`, `std::ranges::sort`), PinUP Player like Windows. Authors often name a transparent image so that it comes first (`aaclear.png`, `blank.png`, `black.png`); for the plugin, `Overlay1 by Dr Nobody TY.png` came first. On *2001*, screen 14 "Callouts" thus showed a whole frame squashed in the middle of the backglass; *Guardians of the Galaxy* had six folders in that case. `playlistRenames` ([puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)) finds, folder by folder, the file PinUP Player would play first and renames it in upper case, extension kept: it then comes first in both orders. The plugin finds a file named by a trigger or by `screens.pup` regardless of case: nothing else changes. This is done on import, by the doctor ("Playlist du PuP-Pack lue dans le mauvais ordre", PuP-Pack playlist read in the wrong order) and before each game.

**The doctor** ([doctor.ts](../packages/core/src/doctor.ts)) checks, without launching VPX:

| Finding | When | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PuP-Pack not found | the script loads a pack (or a `pupvideos` folder exists) and there is no `pupvideos\<nom attendu>` | rename the only folder present; otherwise, add the pack |
| PuP-Pack stored one folder too deep | `pupvideos\X\X\screens.pup` | move the pack up |
| Unused PuP-Pack | another folder in `pupvideos` that no version in the folder loads | recycle bin |
| PuP-Pack switched off by the script | an expected pack is in place, and the script VPX plays (the `.vbs` next to the table if there is one) never starts the pack (`usePUP = False`, or `HasPup = enablePupPack` with `enablePupPack = False`), or, the pack's option taking the DMD window, leaves the PuP-DMD off (`enablePupDMD = False`: "PuP-DMD coupée par le script" (PuP-DMD switched off by the script)) | switch each switch on in `<table>.vbs` (or in the `.vbs` already there); protected script: the values to change |
| PuP-Pack never started | a table without ROM whose script never starts PuP, a pack in place under the expected name (game name for B2S, or only folder), and no `.vbs` that starts it | start it in `<table>.vbs`, at the beginning of `<table>_Init` (also done before the game); protected script: nothing to do without VPX |
| Videos triggered by the DMD: no effect | a pack with `PupCapture`, a table without ROM, active `D<n>` triggers with their image, a script that sends no `D`, and no `.vbs` that matches them up to date | match them in `<table>.vbs` on the FlexDMD frame (also done before the game); colour DMD, no frame timer or protected script: none |
| Script adapted for an older version | Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` was made from another script than the `.vpx`'s (table updated by hand) | rewrite it from the new script, or move it to the recycle bin |
| Option not applied, or modified | options exist, and the root has no `.pup` (or its `.pup` files match no option, `appliedOption`) | copy the chosen option again (remembered, otherwise based on the cabinet) and remember it |
| Option differs from the remembered one, or is incomplete | the option remembered in the `.info` is not the one whose files are in place (`.bat` run by hand, interrupted copy), or is only partly there (a file it deletes came back) | chosen on the details screen, or partly in place: apply it again; chosen automatically at installation: remember the option in place (also done before the game) |
| Option unsuited to the cabinet | an option is applied, and another one scores clearly better for the current cabinet (cabinet changed since installation, older automatic choice, backglass left to the `.directb2s` of a table that has none), unless the option was chosen on the details screen | apply the better one and remember it |
| The PuP-Pack would hide the table's DMD | the pack takes the DMD window (screen 5 or 1), the table draws its DMD (ROM, FlexDMD, UltraDMD) without writing the score on the pack, and the table's `.ini` does not set `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP` | write `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP = 0` in the table's `.ini` (also done before the game) |
| PuP-Pack score on the backglass, DMD screen empty | the cabinet has a DMD screen, the script writes the score on the pack (`LabelNew`), the pack's DMD screen (5, otherwise 1) is placed inside the backglass (CustomPos "2,0,0,100,100": pack made for a single 16:9 screen, like City Hunter) and no option of the pack puts it on the DMD: the PUP plugin draws nothing in the DMD window (`dmdInBackglass`) | rewrite that screen's CustomPos in `screens.pup` (the old file to the trash) so that it becomes a root showing, in the DMD window, the backglass band the other screens leave free, as large as possible without distortion and centred (`dmdBandPlacement`: the screen keeps the 16:9 proportions of the backglass, because the plugin places and sizes labels in % of the width and height; e.g. "1,0,-133.34,100,233.11" for a 1724 × 416 DMD, "1,0,-28.65,100,100" for a 16:9 FullDMD); the original CustomPos and the one written are kept in the `.info`, and the position is computed again when the DMD window changes shape ("Score du PuP-Pack à recadrer sur l'écran DMD", another cabinet setup); a position changed by hand since is left alone (also done before the game) |
| PuP-Pack score to put back on the backglass | the cabinet no longer has a DMD screen and the screen has the position set above | put back the original CustomPos kept in the `.info` (also done before the game) |
| PuP-Pack files the PUP plugin would misread | in a playlist, an image named like a video (`Stark.mp4`, a PNG), or a name with accents | give the image its extension back, make the name ASCII, rewrite every `.pup` in the pack (options included); also done on install and before the game; name mentioned by the script: left |
| PuP-Pack files missing | an active trigger or a lit screen names a file missing from its playlist | none: the pack does not ship it (information) |
| Playlist read in the wrong order | in a folder of the pack, the first file for the PUP plugin (case-sensitive sort) is not PinUP Player's (Windows sort): `aaclear.png` behind `Overlay1….png` | rename the intended file in upper case (extension kept); also done on import and before the game |
| Pack screens missing from the cabinet | a visible screen targets the topper, the DMD or the backglass while that role is not assigned | — (information) |
| Playfield videos not displayed | screen 3 (Playfield) is visible: the plugin does not render it | — |
| PuP-Pack driven by the script | `playlistadd` in the script and no `.pup` | — |
| Pack meant for another ROM | `PupCapture` folder and a ROM different from the pack's original name, or a ROM in another language (suffix `f`, `g`, `i`, `s` after the version, assumed) | — |
| VPX log | the table's last game in `vpinball.log` ("Starting script" or "Startup done" from VPX 10.8.1): pack loaded without `screens.pup`, no pack loaded (unless the script switches the pack off: the previous finding says so), `Not implemented` functions. | — |
| PuP-Pack disabled | the table's pack is in `pupvideos (off)` ("Game parts" > PuP-Pack > "Disable the PuP-Pack") and no pack it loads is in `pupvideos` | — (information): nothing is asked for, neither "not found", nor the "no pack loaded" log, nor an option, nor a script to switch on |
| Script still adapted for the disabled PuP-Pack | Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` switches on or starts the disabled pack (switch, start, DMD frame recognition), or comes from an older version of the table | redo the `.vbs` from the table's script with nothing PuP in it (the `core.vbs` fixes stay), or move it to the trash; also done before every game |

**Unrecognized cases** (the plan then offers the options to pick with the buttons, or the doctor reports it): `.bat` files that ask for a choice (`choice`, `set /p`) or test for files (`if exist`), `.bat` files that run an executable (`PuPDesktopPunch.exe`, installers), options stored outside a folder whose name contains "option" and with no readable `.bat`, packs with no `.pup` at all outside a `pupvideos` folder (old script-driven packs: recognized only if they already arrive inside `pupvideos\`), a pack-specific `PinUpPlayer.ini` (ignored by the plugin), changes made in the PuP-Pack Editor (enabling topper videos, for example) that no longer match any option: reported as information, never overwritten without the button. The VPX FFmpeg DLLs and the `Priority.PUP` keys in `VPinballX.ini` (which put the pack ahead of the directb2s) belong to the VPX sync, not to this module.

Sources for the option rule: the `.bat` example in [dekay's vpinball wiki](https://github-wiki-see.page/m/dekay/vpinball-wiki/wiki/PUP-Packs), [pup-research](https://github.com/francisdb/pup-research) ("copy the .pup files from `PuP-Pack_Options/<option>` to the root"), the [Batocera wiki](https://wiki.batocera.org/systems:vpinball) (Batman 66: `Option 3 - Desktop`), and option names of VPUniverse and vpinball.com packs, from search engine summaries (Tron Legacy, Transformers, Aliens, Pirates of the Caribbean, PinEvent packs: "Option 1 - Backglass and Topper", "2 Screen - 4x3 or 5x4", "Full DMD on Backglass", "Backbox AIO", "PUP Stream"). VPUniverse pages refuse automated reading (403): the rule still needs confirming on real packs.

## PuP-Pack disabled, table by table

To bring a table back to its simplest state, without its videos, and then put them back: table details > "Game parts" > "PuP-Pack" > "Disable the PuP-Pack". The details screen then shows "PuP-Pack · disabled", and the same part offers "Re-enable the PuP-Pack". From a pack's options screen (details > "PuP-Pack"), "Disable the PuP-Pack" leads to the same place.

**What happens.** The pack folder moves from `<table>\pupvideos\<pack>` to `<table>\pupvideos (off)\<pack>`, as it is: a single rename inside the table folder, instant even for several gigabytes, with no copy. The VPX 10.8.1 PUP plugin only looks for the pack under `pupvideos\<name>` (next to the table, then in the PinUP Player folder set in VPX); PinUP Popper only reads `PinUPSystem\PUPVideos`. For the game, it is therefore a table without a pack: the script creates the PuP object, `B2SInit` finds nothing, the game goes on without videos. Re-enabling puts the folder back: same files, same dates, same option remembered in the `.info`.

**The script.** When Cyber Pinball had adapted the table's `.vbs` for the pack (`usePUP` switch on, start added, DMD frame recognition), it puts it back like the table's script: only the `core.vbs` fixes remain, and a `.vbs` that had nothing else goes to the trash. Without a pack, a switch left on would get in the way of the game: a lit PuP-DMD leaves the DMD without a score, and *2001: A Space Odyssey* only plays its music when `usePUP` is false. On re-enabling, the `.vbs` is redone identically. A `.vbs` from elsewhere is not touched.

**Nothing switches it back on.** The doctor says "PuP-Pack disabled" (information) and asks for nothing; the repairs made before every game put the script back without PuP, never the pack; cabinet health and "What's missing to play" count it as done; "Game parts" neither searches nor offers a pack to download for it; sharing with friends does not send it; a pack update installed through the add plan is stored in `pupvideos (off)` and stays disabled.

**Shared pack.** `pupvideos` is shared by the tables of one folder: the other versions that load the same pack (same ROM, same name) also play without it. The confirmation screen names them before disabling, and they say "disabled" too; the pack can be re-enabled from any of them.

**Safety.** The state lives on the disk and nowhere else: a pack is disabled as long as its folder is in `pupvideos (off)` and not in `pupvideos`. If a rename fails (a pack file open in VPX or Explorer), those already done are undone and the message says so: nothing is half disabled. If the application stops between moving the pack and putting the script back, preparing the next game puts the script back. A pack of the same name put back in `pupvideos` meanwhile wins: re-enabling never overwrites it.

**Limit.** If the VPX "PinUP Player folder" setting (`[Plugin.PUP] PUPFolder`) points to a collection that also has this pack, the plugin will load that one: the confirmation screen says so.

## Structure

| Folder | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/core` | Cabinet model, EDID, fingerprints, screen matching, INI, VPX generator, buttons and keys (`buttons.ts`). No Electron, no Windows. |
| `packages/win-displays` | Windows functions through FFI (koffi): screen detection (DisplayConfig, EDID, scaling, ContainerId), audio outputs (`audio.ts`, MMDevices registry read by `registry.ts`), connected USB devices (`usb.ts`, for real DMDs), windows and foreground (`windows.ts`), screen orientation (`orientation.ts`). |
| `packages/installer` | Manifest of third-party components, choice of the root, verified download, extraction, junction to the active version. |
| `app` | Electron application: entry point (`main.ts`), setup wizard for buttons, screens and sound (`wizard.ts`, test sounds in `renderer/sound.js`), table menu (`frontend.ts`), VPX settings (`settings.ts`), table management (`library.ts`), VPX launch and monitoring (`launcher.ts`), `sync` command. |
| `docs/design-system` | Visual language of the redesign (tokens, `.cp-*` components, icons, screen mockups) and its `index.html` page; regenerated by `node docs/design-system/src/construire.mjs`. Applied to the interface (`app/renderer/theme.css`), to the built-in fallbacks (`fallback.css`) and to the icon (`app/renderer/icone.svg`). |

## VPX settings

From the table menu, R key (or Exit then "Réglages de VPX" (VPX settings)); standalone: `npx electron app --reglages [--table=<morceau du nom>]`, launcher action "reglages".

- The list of settings is not hand-coded: `npm run vpx-schema` reads `src/core/Settings_properties.inl` from the VPX source code at the commit of the installed version (`sourceCommit` in the manifest) and writes `packages/installer/vpx-settings/<version>.json`. It adds the settings that plugins declare when they load, read from the `plugins/` folder of the same commit (`packages/core/src/vpx-plugin-settings.ts`): each plugin's `[Plugin.<id>] Enable` (`plugin.cfg`), the `MSGPI_*_SETTING` macros actually registered (`RegisterSetting`), and `[Backglass|ScoreView|Topper] Priority.<moteur>` for each rendering engine of the secondary windows. For 10.8.1-5436: 1,240 settings, 101 of them from plugins. Each plugin setting records the plugin that declares it: it has no effect when that plugin is off. With `-- --ini <VPinballX.ini>`, it checks that every setting in the schema appears in a file written by that version (except those of plugins VPX did not load); with `-- --check`, it fails if the committed file differs from the source code.
- "Essentiels" (Essentials) tab: a translated selection (`packages/core/src/vpx-essentials.ts`), including the groups "Backglass, PuP et DMD réel" (Backglass, PuP and real DMD) and "Dossiers communs à toutes les tables" (Folders shared by all tables). "Avancé" (Advanced) tab: the whole schema by section, with the original labels and descriptions, plus keys outside the schema (keys, options declared by tables) as read-only; an `[Input] Mapping.*` key is shown with the name of the action and of the key.
- Flipper buttons: move and pick a value; Start: edit, confirm; Exit: cancel, go back; Del: default value; mouse and wheel also work, with "‹ Retour aux sections" (Back to sections) or "‹ Fermer" (Close) under the brand.
- Keys written by Cyber Pinball (windows, plugins on or off, plugin priorities on the assigned windows, `SecurityLevel` and `AskToTurnOn`, which prevent VPX from asking questions during play (see [Launching a game](#launching-a-game)), `NudgeStrength`, which keeps the table still on screen (see [Tilt](#tilt)), detected real DMD, button keys once the "Boutons" (Buttons) step is done, and audio outputs once the "Son" (Sound) step is done) are shown but cannot be changed, along with where their value comes from and how to change it.
- Writing: only the setting's line changes (`IniDocument`), with a timestamped backup (`writeWithBackup`). "Valeur par défaut" (Default value) leaves the key empty, which is the VPX convention.
- Per table (`openSettings({ scope: 'table', table })`): the `<table>.ini` file next to the .vpx, found the way VPX does it (table name, otherwise folder name). Only values that differ from the global setting are kept in it, as VPX does; an empty file is deleted. In a sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME`), it is written to `<bac à sable>\sandbox\tables\` and never next to the real .vpx.

## System menu: five sections

The system menu (Exit, Tab or ⚙ from the table menu) shows only five sections, then "Retour aux tables" (Back to tables). Each section opens on its entries; Exit (or "Retour", Back) goes up one level, with the selection on the section being left. A screen opened from an entry returns, through its "Retour", to the section it came from, with the selection on that entry; the gear always reopens the sections.

| Section | Entries |
| --- | --- |
| Tables | All tables, Add a table, Downloads, ROM library |
| Médias (Media) | Find missing media (on VPinMediaDB); Media, sound and attract mode |
| Amis (Friends) | My friends, Game history, Find a player, Friend requests, My badges, Sharing and network (see [Friends: the cabinet network](#friends-the-cabinet-network)) |
| Borne (Cabinet) | Cabinet health, Cabinet setup, VPX settings, Cabinet controls, Windows desktop (Open Windows Explorer on a [dedicated cabinet](#dedicated-cabinet)), Dedicated cabinet, Guest mode |
| Cyber Pinball | Langue / Language, Cyber Pinball update, Recent activity (see [Recent activity](#recent-activity)), Backups (see [Backups](#backups)), Migration, Quit Cyber Pinball, Restart Cyber Pinball, Sleep, Restart the cabinet, Shut down the cabinet |

"Migration" (in Cyber Pinball) gathers what is only used once, when coming from another frontend: "Reprendre les médias" (Import media, from PinUP Popper or PinballX) and "Regrouper les ROM" (Gather ROMs) in the library.

**Menu système > Tables > « Toutes les tables »** (System menu > Tables > All tables): the list of every table on the cabinet, by title (manufacturer · year · version, and "Jouée 12 fois", Played 12 times, or "Jamais jouée", Never played). Start on a table opens **its page, the same as from the wheel** (Play, Play with a friend, Edit information, Game media, Game parts, Table files, settings, diagnosis, removal…); its "Retour à la liste des tables" (Back to the table list, or Exit) goes back to the list, on that table, and every screen opened from the page comes back to it as usual. "Jouer" (Play) launches the game, then goes back to the table menu, the wheel on that table. A table removed from the list goes back to it. Code: `openAllTables` and `sheetReturn` in `app/src/library.ts`.

An entry's red dot (received files to install, update available, DMD screen to check…) goes up to its section, then to the gear; on opening, the selection goes to the first one that carries one.

The tree (sections, order, name and icon of each entry) is in [packages/core/src/systemmenu.ts](../packages/core/src/systemmenu.ts) (`SYSTEM_ROOT`, `SYSTEM_GROUPS`, `SYSTEM_ITEMS`). The application provides what each entry does through its identifier (`library.provideSystemItem`, `app/src/library.ts` and `frontend.ts`); a new entry is placed by adding its identifier to a section, without touching the others. [systemmenu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/systemmenu.test.ts) pins the invariants: each entry placed once, one icon per entry within a section (and not the section's own), every entry provided by the application, and **every "System menu > …" path** written in this manual, its translation, the manual tests, the READMEs and the interface texts names entries that exist, in that order. Demonstrations name an entry (`action:…`): from the system menu, the autopilot finds it in its section.

## Cabinet health

System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Santé de la borne" (Cabinet health) gathers on one page what other screens already know, to see at a glance what prevents playing or deserves a look. Each line has a state — **OK** (tick, green), **À vérifier** (To check: exclamation mark, amber), **Bloquant** (Blocking: cross, red), never colour alone — and opens, with Start or a click, the screen that repairs it; its "Retour" (Back) returns to the page. The summary at the top counts the lines in each state and says how old the check is.

| Check | Where it comes from | States | What the line opens |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Disk space | One drive per root, among those of the tables, the downloads and the data | Blocking under 2 GB free, or a tables drive that no longer responds; To check under 10 GB | Downloads (free up space) |
| Tables | Each table's doctor, on its already-read script (`diagnose`, `playBlockers`) | Blocking: a requested ROM is missing; To check: the table crashed, unreadable script, or script never read; OK: no requested ROM is missing | The table doctor (five lines at most, then "See every table to review"); ROM library (read the scripts) |
| PuP-Packs | The doctor of each table that has a `pupvideos` folder (`pupFacts`, `PUP_HEALTH_FINDINGS`): pack not found or badly placed, switched off by the script, option missing or made for another cabinet, pack that would hide the DMD, VPX log that did not see it loaded | To check: one line per table, with the cause, and "réparé de lui-même avant la prochaine partie" (repaired automatically before the next game) when the menu takes care of it (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`); OK: all ready; nothing without any pack | The table's doctor, where the repair takes one step |
| Media | The "Find missing media" plan on the VPinMediaDB index already kept, offline | To check: VPinMediaDB has media for tables recognised with certainty, or has never been consulted; OK otherwise. Never blocking: media are optional | Missing media |
| Screens | The setup in use against the plugged-in screens (`scoreConfiguration`, `chooseConfiguration`) | Blocking: no setup, none of its screens plugged in, or playfield not found; To check: a screen is missing, another setup would match better, or "VPX confond deux écrans" (VPX mixes up two screens): two roles on two screens with the same name, which VPX 10.8.1 cannot tell apart (`homonymousRoles`, it opens both windows on the first; PuP videos meant for one hide behind the other), "recalé à chaque partie" (realigned at every game, see below), or "pas le playfield" (not the playfield) when the playfield is one of them | Cabinet setup |
| Visual Pinball X | The active version, against the installer manifest's (offline) | Blocking: VPX missing; To check: a newer version is known | Install VPX (the old version stays alongside); VPX settings when all is well |

**Fast to open.** The check runs in the background and yields to the menu between two tables: it never starts VPX, downloads nothing, and takes a few seconds for 200 tables. The page opens at once on the last check, kept from one launch to the next in `<data>\cache\sante.json`, then runs it again ("Vérifié il y a 2 min · nouvelle vérification en cours…", Checked 2 min ago · new check under way…); the last summary stays on screen until the new one is ready. A check also runs 15 seconds after start-up, then every 30 minutes (never during a game), and "Vérifier maintenant" (Check now) starts one.

**No window above VPX.** When a game starts, `closeTopmostWindows` ([frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts)) closes any Cyber Pinball window that is "always on top" (only "Récupérer" is): it would sit in front of the windows where the PUP plugin draws. [pupvisible.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/pupvisible.test.ts) refuses any other always-on-top window.

**VPX windows moved back to their screen.** VPX 10.8.1 recognises a screen by its name only (`Window::GetDisplayConfig`) and takes the first one that bears it. On the "Poste à 3 écrans" development PC, the backglass and the DMD are two PL2294H: VPX opened "Visual Pinball Backglass" and "Visual Pinball Score View" on the same screen (`vpinball.log`: "Window #1" and "Window #2" "on display PL2294H"), where they overlapped, and the PuP videos meant for the DMD were hidden. No `VPinballX.ini` setting avoids it: in windowed mode, `WndX` and `WndY` are relative to the chosen screen and must fit in it. During the game, the loop that reads the window stack every 2 s (`watchOccupied`, [frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts)) therefore moves each VPX ancillary window back to its role's screen. It recognises the window by its title (`VPX_ANCILLARY_WINDOWS`: Backglass, Score View, Topper) and moves it at the same size, at the same place within the screen, without activating it or changing its rank in the stack (`moveWindow`, `SetWindowPos` with `SWP_NOACTIVATE`, `SWP_NOZORDER` and `SWP_ASYNCWINDOWPOS`). This only happens with a VPX version that finds screens by name only (the profile's `name` format) and for roles whose name is shared by another plugged-in screen (`confusableRoles`: another role's screen, or an unused screen of the same model). A window already on its screen is never touched: what is adjusted in the VPX menu stays. These ancillary windows are written windowed at the size of their screen, never fullscreen (`vpxSettingsFor`, `confused` parameter): SDL keeps only one fullscreen window per screen and minimizes the previous one when another goes fullscreen there (`SDL_UpdateFullscreenMode`, `SDL_MinimizeWindow`). Fullscreen on the same PL2294H, the backglass stayed minimized for the whole game, and a minimized window is never moved back: the PuP-Pack no longer showed on the backglass as soon as it also used the DMD (The Matrix, City Hunter). A VPX ancillary window minimized during a game is reported in the menu log ("[avertissement] Partie : « Visual Pinball Backglass » est réduite par Windows…", `minimizedVpxWindows`). A window that VPX puts elsewhere again more than three times is left where it puts it, and the menu log says so. The playfield window, the rendering one, is never moved: when the playfield is one of the mixed-up roles, cabinet health says so. Pure code: `vpxWindowMoves` ([vpxwindows.ts](../packages/core/src/vpxwindows.ts)), pinned by [vpxplacement.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/vpxplacement.test.ts); the menu log notes each window moved back ("Partie : « Visual Pinball Score View » ramenée sur l'écran du rôle dmd…").

**Badge.** A Blocking line in the last check puts the red dot on "Santé de la borne", on the Borne section and on the gear; the entry's details say how many issues are blocking or to check.

Code: the lines and their states in [packages/core/src/health.ts](../packages/core/src/health.ts), the check, the cache and the page in `app/src/health.ts`, the rendering in `app/renderer/library.js` (`renderHealth`). [health.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/health.test.ts) pins every state of every check and that **every line leads to a real screen**: a system menu entry placed in the tree and provided by the application, a table's doctor, the list of tables to review or the VPX installation.

## Shutting down, sleeping, restarting the cabinet

In the system menu's Cyber Pinball section, after "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball) and "Redémarrer Cyber Pinball" (Restart Cyber Pinball; the ways out together, from the lightest to the heaviest): "Mettre en veille" (Sleep), "Redémarrer la borne" (Restart the cabinet), "Éteindre la borne" (Shut down the cabinet). Each opens a confirmation screen that says what is going to happen; Start confirms (the action is preselected, in green like any current choice: going through the confirmation is caution enough), Exit or "Annuler" (Cancel) returns to the section, with the selection on the entry. The module is [app/src/power.ts](../app/src/power.ts); the method, the simulation and the scheduled action are in [packages/core/src/power.ts](../packages/core/src/power.ts), the sleep call in [packages/win-displays/src/power.ts](../packages/win-displays/src/power.ts).

- **Restart Cyber Pinball**: closes and reopens the software alone, the PC stays on (after a ROM or a setting changed outside the menu, a screen or a controller plugged back in, a menu that no longer responds well). No confirmation, like "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball): nothing shuts down. It is the covered relaunch of language and configuration changes (`relaunchMenu`, `relaunchCovered` in [app/src/screen-guard.ts](../app/src/screen-guard.ts)): settings saved, the new process paints its windows before the old one closes, no screen shows the desktop in between, without the startup opening. Dedicated cabinet (Cyber Pinball as the Windows shell): the relaunch goes through Shell Launcher, and "Quitter Cyber Pinball" is not offered (see [Dedicated cabinet](#dedicated-cabinet)).
- **Shut down, restart**: every screen turns plain black, the playfield only showing "Extinction de la borne…" (Shutting the cabinet down…; no brand, media or gear), then the menu saves its settings (state of the menu media), gives back the taskbars a game may have hidden, then runs `shutdown.exe /s /t 0` (`/r` to restart). Its windows and veils stay in place until Windows closes the application, with its own shutdown screen already shown: no screen shows the desktop in between. Full shutdown: Windows Fast Startup only applies with `/hybrid`, so the cabinet starts cold, its USB devices (Pinscape, encoders) enumerated again. Without `/f` (which `/t 0` does not imply): an open application can save, or hold Windows back; after a minute without a shutdown, the menu says so ("La borne ne s'est pas éteinte", The cabinet did not shut down) and Exit brings it back.
- **Sleep**: the same black screen ("Mise en veille…", Going to sleep…), settings saved, then S3 sleep through `powrprof.dll`'s `SetSuspendState(FALSE, FALSE, FALSE)`, called directly through koffi, on a separate thread. Cyber Pinball stays open: on wake, the table menu comes back as it was. Never `rundll32 powrprof.dll,SetSuspendState 0,1,0`: rundll32 passes its own arguments (window, instance, command line), the first one, "hibernate", receives a non-null window handle, and the PC hibernates whenever hibernation is enabled, which is the default on Windows 11 and on the cabinet (`powercfg /a`: S3 sleep and hibernation). The process's shutdown privilege (`SeShutdownPrivilege`) is enabled before the call. Hybrid sleep, if Windows enabled it, is still sleep (memory is also copied to disk). A PC with Modern Standby only (S0 low power idle, no S3) does not allow requesting it: `IsPwrSuspendAllowed` says so beforehand, the entry shows "Indisponible : ce PC n'a que la veille moderne" (Not available: this PC only has Modern Standby) and its screen ("Veille indisponible depuis Cyber Pinball", Sleep not available from Cyber Pinball) points to the Windows Start menu, without asking Windows anything.
- **Scheduled action**: `createPowerControl` performs an action right away (`run`) or at a given time (`schedule(action, at)`, cancelled by `cancel()`, listed by `scheduled()`). The time is read again at least every minute (sleep suspends timers); an action whose time passed more than five minutes ago (cabinet asleep or off at that time) is missed, not caught up on wake (`onMissed`). A scheduled action can wait for the cabinet to be free (`ready`, read every second) until a deadline (`until`), and announce itself with a warning (`notice`) that a button removes. Every action, asked from the menu or scheduled, then waits for Windows to shut down (`settle`). The menu exposes it through `createPowerMenu(…).control`, for the cabinet schedule's automatic stop (see [Cabinet schedule](#cabinet-schedule)).
- **Tests**: nothing ever shuts down. The real executor is only built when no test variable at all is set (`powerSimulated`): offscreen, autopilot, captures, endurance, sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME`), unit tests, even with `CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1`, and on request (`CYBERPINBALL_ALIMENTATION_SIMULEE=1`). The action is only logged there ("Alimentation simulée : shutdown.exe /s /t 0"), the black screen stays 2.5 s, then the menu comes back with the notice "Mode essai : extinction simulée, le PC reste allumé" (Test mode: shutdown simulated, the PC stays on; cleared as soon as another confirmation opens). `CYBERPINBALL_ALIMENTATION_SANS_VEILLE=1` simulates, in tests only, a PC without S3 sleep.
- **Proofs**: [power.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/power.test.ts) pins the invariants (every test simulated, real executor never built in a test, no other path to shutdown in the code: `shutdown.exe`, `SetSuspendState`, `rundll32`, `ExitWindowsEx`, `Stop-Computer`…; methods without `/f` or hibernation; scheduled, missed, cancelled action).

## Guest mode

For a party or the kids: System menu > Borne > Mode invité (Cabinet > Guest mode), then "Activer le mode invité" (Turn on guest mode). The table menu opens locked, and stays so until unlocked, even after a restart (`mode-invite.json` in the data folder).

- **What stays open**: choosing a table (flippers, arrow keys, wheel, click), opening its game page, playing, reading the flyer and the rules, the controls reminder (Help button, H, F1), stopping a stuck game (Ctrl+Shift+K). The game page only offers Play, Flyer, Rules and Back to the menu, without the "Files" section.
- **What is closed**: the system menu (Exit, Tab, gear), VPX settings (R, Settings button), Windows desktop (Ctrl+Shift+D), Ctrl+Q, Alt+F4 on a menu window, the game page actions that edit, delete, repair or download (Edit, Game media, Files, PuP-Pack, Adjust the display, Settings for this table, Diagnose, Delete, What is missing to play, Going further), files dragged onto the window, and Start on a menu message that repairs or installs (wizard, VPX, buttons, VPinballX.ini edited by hand, what is missing to play): Exit closes the message without changing anything. A closed door does nothing but show "Locked · guest mode" for three seconds at the top right, next to the padlock that replaces the gear (with the word "Guest"); the menu footer no longer offers "Exit: menu", and the controls reminder only lists what works.
- **Unlocking**: both flippers and both MagnaSaves held together for 3 seconds, from the table menu, a game page or a message. Each hand holds its flipper and the MagnaSave next to it, the very MagnaSave gesture: doable alone, without letting go of the cabinet. Four buttons held at once never happen by chance in a menu driven one button at a time, and the combination is not listened to during a game or its loading (VPX has the buttons then): it never gets in the way of play. The padlock ring fills up; released before the end, it drops back. At the end, guest mode stops (saved), "Guest mode off" replaces the padlock for a few seconds, then the gear comes back. The combination is only written on the entry's screen, never on the menu nor during a game. It follows the buttons recorded in the "Buttons" step; a MagnaSave that was not recorded is held with a keyboard's Left Ctrl or Right Ctrl (the entry's screen says so), and a keyboard's default keys (Shift and Ctrl, left and right) also unlock as long as no other button has taken them. The first button pressed does what it usually does (a flipper moves the selection by one); the next ones, held with it, nothing else.
- **Troubleshooting**: without the buttons (broken MagnaSave), with Cyber Pinball closed, delete `mode-invite.json` from the data folder.

**Entry points.** Every way of acting on the menu has a name in `GUEST_DOORS` ([packages/core/src/guestmode.ts](../packages/core/src/guestmode.ts)), open or closed to guests: keys and buttons (`handleKey`, `app/src/frontend.ts`), clicks (the `action` channel: gear, table wheel), global shortcuts, closing a window, dragged files (`library.handleAction`), Start on each kind of message (`MESSAGE_DOORS`), table management screens (`GUEST_SCREENS`: the game page and its pages only) and game page actions (`SheetAction`: each one declares its door). The compiler requires a door for every kind of message and every game page action; in guest mode, a screen action without a door is hidden, so a new action stays closed until it is declared. The autopilot goes through the same doors. [guestmode.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/guestmode.test.ts) pins the invariants: the list of open doors (opening one means writing it there with its reason), every declared door is used in `app/src`, the game page and the messages declare their doors, and **every input source in `app/src`** (`ipcMain` channel, global shortcut, `before-input-event`, joystick buttons) is counted file by file: a new one makes the test fail until it says which door it goes through. The lock, the hint, the combination (held buttons, timer, release, game in progress) and the saved setting are tested there too.

**Limits.** During a game, Cyber Pinball's in-game settings (both MagnaSaves: DMD position, VPX menu) are closed to guests (`game.settings` door); VPX keeps its own controls: its menu (F12 on a keyboard) and its settings stay reachable, and Exit leaves the game as usual. Guest mode does not lock Windows (Windows key, Alt+Tab, Ctrl+Alt+Del), nor the wizard started separately from the launcher.

## Backups

Menu système > « Cyber Pinball » > « Sauvegardes » (System menu > Cyber Pinball > Backups) copies the cabinet to a USB drive, an external disk or a network drive, and restores it, on this cabinet (after a failure or a settings mistake) or on another PC. The entry shows the date and level of the last backup.

**Three levels, each containing the previous one** (`LEVEL_PARTS`, [packages/core/src/backup.ts](../packages/core/src/backup.ts)):

| Level | Parts | What it holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Settings and scores (a few MB) | `cabinet`, `settings`, `scores`, `friends` | `data\cabinet.json` and its configurations, `vpx-owned.json`, `config\VPinballX*.ini`; the other JSON files of `data` (menu media, language, DMD positions, guest mode…), each table's `.ini` and `pinmame\cfg`; each table's `pinmame\nvram`, `pinmame\memcard` and `user\VPReg.stg`; `data\social.json` (including the private key of the cabinet's identity) and `data\amis` |
| With the tables | plus `tables` | The table folders (without their `cache`), `pinmame\roms`, `media` |
| The whole installation | plus `vpx` | `emulators` (never the `current` junction), `downloads`, `tools`, `data\enregistrements` |

Never backed up: `app` (the installer puts it back), `trash`, `import`, `parties`, `data\cache`, `data\logs`, `data\backups`, `data\staging`, what friends sent (`data\amis\recus`, `data\amis\tables`), what only applies to this PC (`telechargements.json`, `mise-a-jour.json`, `orientation-ecrans.json`, `borne-dediee\`), the backups' own settings and restores, temporary files. `partOf` sorts each path; junctions and symbolic links are never followed.

**Destination.** A `CyberPinball-Sauvegardes` folder at the root of the chosen drive (USB drive, disk, network drive, or the cabinet disk: “does not protect against a disk failure”), or in a specific folder of that drive: “Choisir un dossier…” (Choose a folder…) opens the Cyber Pinball folder picker on the current destination. The chosen folder is kept from the drive root (`destination.folder`, “Sauvegardes\Borne”); if it is already called `CyberPinball-Sauvegardes`, its parent is taken. A folder inside the Cyber Pinball installation, on no drive, or that cannot be written to (a write test when choosing) is refused with its reason. “Restaurer” (Restore) looks in the chosen folder and at the root of every drive. Drives come from `listDrives` (`Win32_LogicalDisk`, with `FileSystem` and `VolumeSerialNumber`); the chosen drive is recognised by its serial number even if its letter changes (`data\sauvegardes.json`). Before starting, the space needed is worked out (what is already on the destination is deducted, `estimateBackup`) and a level that does not fit is blocked with its reason: missing space, or FAT32 and a file over 4 GB.

**A deduplicated, verified copy.** On the destination, `objets\ab\<SHA-256>` keeps the content of each file once, and `<cabinet>\<date> <level>\sauvegarde.json` the list of one backup. A file unchanged since the previous backup is therefore never copied again, on any file system. Fingerprints already worked out (size, date, SHA-256) are kept in `data\sauvegardes-empreintes.json`. Each object is written to a temporary file, read back and compared with its fingerprint, then stored: a “verified” backup was checked file by file. The list is written after each part: a copy that is stopped or cut off (drive pulled out) keeps what is verified, marked “Incomplète” (Incomplete), and “Reprendre la sauvegarde” (Resume the backup) copies only what is missing. The backup list marks as “Endommagée” (Damaged) one whose object is missing or no longer has its size. During the copy the menu stays usable but games wait (“Une sauvegarde est en cours : les parties attendent sa fin.”, `play` in [frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts)): a game would write its scores during the copy. At the end, “Éjecter la clé” (Eject the drive) asks Windows to eject it and says when it can be unplugged.

**Restoring in two steps.** “Restaurer une sauvegarde” (Restore a backup) lists those on the plugged-in drives and on the cabinet, this cabinet's first. Each cabinet has its own identity (`data\borne.json`); on a fresh installation (no cabinet model), its backups are recognised as this cabinet's. The plan (`planRestore`) gives, part by part, the files replaced, put back or identical; Start includes or excludes a part, and the starting focus is on the first one, never on “Restaurer la sélection” (Restore the selection). A file the backup does not have (a table added since) stays where it is, and without the tables, the settings and scores of a table missing here are skipped. A table's scores (NVRAM, `VPReg.stg`) go back to those of the backup, without merging. Then:

1. the application copies each file of the plan into `data\restauration\`, comparing its fingerprint with the backup's (`prepareRestore`): nothing in the root changes, and if stopped or cut off, the restore simply does not happen;
2. Cyber Pinball restarts and, at the very start, before the language, the cabinet model, the friends or `VPinballX.ini` are read ([app/src/restore-on-start.ts](../app/src/restore-on-start.ts), `applyPendingRestore`), each current file goes to the trash (`<date> Avant restauration`) and the restored file takes its place. The old process, which stays open while the new one covers the screens, can no longer write anything in the root or the data folder, except the log ([app/src/write-freeze.ts](../app/src/write-freeze.ts)): nothing it held in memory can overwrite a restored file. A file it keeps open (a video playing) cannot be put in place straight away: the old one stays, and the file is put in place again 20 s then 60 s after startup (`RESTORE_ATTEMPTS`, in the same report).

The report (`data\restauration-derniere.json`) is announced at startup and shown at the top of Backups. “Annuler la dernière restauration” (Undo the last restore) puts back, the same way, what went to the trash and removes what was added, as long as the trash keeps it.

**Another cabinet.** A backup made on another cabinet does not bring over by default either its screens, buttons and sound (the `cabinet` part, which can be included) or its friends and network identity. “Garder mes amis et l'identité de cette borne” (Keep my friends and this cabinet's identity) is the starting choice; “Remplacer « … » sur le réseau” (Replace “…” on the network) takes its private key and friends, after a confirmation: the old cabinet must not connect again. A backup that holds the identity says so at the end of the copy (“keep it to yourself”).

**Automatic backup.** “Automatique” (Automatic): off (default), every day or every week. Time: “Dès que possible” (As soon as possible, the default: a cabinet is not on all day), the first time the cabinet is free during the day (since midnight) or the week (since Monday midnight); or “À heure fixe” (At a set time), the chosen hour (4 am by default) and, every week, the day (Sunday by default), a missed time (PC off) being caught up as soon as possible. Settings and scores only, on the cabinet (`data\sauvegardes`, the last 10 kept), then copied to the destination as soon as it is plugged in (setting “Copier aussi sur la destination”, Also copy to the destination). Checked every minute, it starts when the cabinet is free: at the menu, no button for 30 s (`backupFree`); a game started during the copy stops it (`yieldToGame`), what was verified stays, and it runs again at the next free moment. After a failure (disk full), no new attempt for 30 min. The screen shows the next one (`lastAutoSlot`, `nextAutoSlot`, `autoBackupDue`). `pruneBackups` removes automatic backups beyond the last 10, incomplete ones caught up by a complete one, then the objects nothing refers to any more; a backup made by hand or another cabinet's is never removed.

Code: the engine in [packages/core/src/backup.ts](../packages/core/src/backup.ts), the screens in [app/src/backups-menu.ts](../app/src/backups-menu.ts), ejection in `app/src/drive-eject.ts`. [sauvegardes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sauvegardes.test.ts) pins the engine: part of each path, nested levels, junctions never followed, deduplicated and read-back copy, estimate and FAT32, backup stopped then resumed, damaged object, two-step restore and its undo, busy file put in place later, data folder outside the root, pruning of automatic backups, due times; [gel-ecritures.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/gel-ecritures.test.ts), the write freeze. [sauvegardes-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sauvegardes-menu.test.ts) pins the screens with simulated drives: nothing writes without its action, starting focus never on an action that writes, unavailable actions greyed out with their reason, game refused during a copy, another cabinet without its identity, confirmation before taking it, automatic backup only when the cabinet is free.

**Limits.** The first launch after a reinstall opens the wizard: the restore is done afterwards, from Backups (the backup's screens and buttons replace those the wizard has just recorded). A folder outside a drive letter (`\\NAS\borne`) cannot be chosen on screen yet: a network drive mapped to a letter works.

## Friends: the cabinet network

System menu > "Amis" (Friends) links cabinets together **with no server**: each finds the others and talks to them directly, over the Internet or on the local network. Sharing is **on by default**; "Partage et réseau" (Sharing and network) turns it off.

| Entry | What it shows |
| --- | --- |
| Mes amis (My friends) | Online, then offline. For each: playing and which table (in real time), in the menu, or seen how long ago; their latest event ("Nouveau meilleur score sur …", New high score on; "Badge reçu : …", Badge earned; "A joué à …", Played). Start opens their page: latest events, their library, "Inviter à une partie en direct" (Invite to a live game; greyed out, "Hors ligne", Offline, when they are not), "Voir sa bibliothèque" (See their library), "Retirer de mes amis" (Remove from my friends), "Bloquer" (Block). At the top, an invitation received or the live game in progress. |
| Mon avatar (My avatar) | The photo or picture your friends see next to your name: "Choisir une image sur ce PC…" (Choose a picture on this PC…), "Chercher une image sur le web…" (Search the web for a picture…), "Recadrer mon avatar" (Crop my avatar), "Retirer mon avatar" (Remove my avatar) (see "Player avatars" below). Without an avatar, your initials on a hue taken from your key. |
| Historique des parties (Game history) | The games with friends finished on this cabinet, newest first, by day: the table, the time, and below it the players with their rank and score. A game opens to review its details (see "Game history" below). |
| Trouver un joueur (Find a player) | The directory: "Chercher un nom…" (Search for a name, ignoring accents and case, "#A1B2" for the tag), cabinets "À proximité (réseau local)" (Nearby, local network) and "Vus récemment" (Seen recently). A player: "Envoyer une demande d'ami" (Send a friend request), or cancel, accept, decline, see their page, block. |
| Demandes d'amis (Friend requests) | Received (to answer, red dot up to the gear) and sent ("En attente : remise dès que sa borne sera en ligne", Waiting: delivered as soon as their cabinet is online). |
| Mes badges (My badges) | Earned and to earn: First game, Ten tables, A hundred games, An hour on one table, Ten hours of play, On the high score table, Grand Champion, Five-time champion. |
| Partage et réseau (Sharing and network) | Sharing on or off, "Votre nom : Alexis #8A12" (Your name), local network, library shared or not, download speed, Windows Firewall, what others see, blocked players. The selection opens on "Retour" (Back): a stray Start does not turn sharing off. |
| Masquer les notifications pendant les parties (Hide notifications during games) | Off by default: a friend coming online, a request to join a game, the news of a game with friends also show during games, with their sound (see "Everywhere, even in a game" in [Tables menu notifications](#tables-menu-notifications)). Start toggles; on, nothing shows or rings during a game, everything waits for the menu. |

**Identity.** Each cabinet has an Ed25519 key, created on first start and kept in `<data>\social.json` (with friends, requests, directory, events and badges; never shared). The name (2 to 24 letters, digits, spaces, `'`, `.`, `_`, `-`) is signed by this key; the `#A1B2` tag comes from the key and tells two players with the same name apart. An unreadable `social.json` is set aside (`social.json.illisible-<date>`), never overwritten.

**Directory with no server.** Every cabinet with sharing on joins the same Hyperswarm topic (`cyberpinball/annuaire/1`, on Holepunch's DHT, bootstrap nodes `node1-3.hyperdht.org`). On each connection, two cabinets exchange their profile and up to 200 profiles they have seen: the directory travels from cabinet to cabinet. A relayed profile only gets in if its signature is valid; the most recent wins; a profile not seen again for 60 days leaves. Two players find each other as well as a thousand: the DHT is shared by every Holepunch user. They only need to be on at the same time.

**Friend request.** It leaves as soon as the other cabinet can be reached, otherwise on its next connection (the requesting cabinet joins it by its key). On each connection, both cabinets restate where they stand (friend: "accept"; pending request: "request"), so an acceptance or removal made while the other was off still gets through. Requested on both sides: friends at once. A decline is remembered for 30 days (the same request gets "no" without coming back on screen). Blocking removes the player from the directory and cuts any connection with their cabinet.

**Presence and events.** Friends receive: online or not, the table being played as soon as VPX shows it (title, manufacturer, year, VPS id, never a path) and the return to the menu; high scores entered at the end of the game (VPX only records the score at the end: the score during a ball cannot be read), games of at least one minute and badges earned. A cabinet that connects receives each friend's last ten events.

**Badges.** Computed from what the tables already record (games and play time in each `.info`, high scores in `VPReg.stg`). On the first computation, those the history deserves are granted without an event: friends only see "Badge reçu" for what happens afterwards ([badges.ts](../packages/core/src/badges.ts)).

**Local network.** A multicast beacon (`239.255.42.99:47999`, TTL 1, every 5 s) announces the cabinet; neighbouring cabinets connect over encrypted TCP (secret-stream, the same Noise as Hyperswarm), even without the Internet. "Nearby" as long as the beacon is less than a minute old. The beacon joins its group network adapter by network adapter (IPv4, loopback excluded), and again at every announcement: `addMembership` without an adapter uses the default route's and throws (`EHOSTUNREACH`, `ENODEV`) when there is none, a cabinet started before its network, an unplugged cable, a VPN taking the route. A refused adapter does not stop the others, and an adapter that shows up later is picked up at the next announcement. **A local network failure is never blocking**: the Internet carries on, "Sharing and network" says "Local network: unavailable" with its reason ("no network adapter connected", or the system error), "Nearby" shows it greyed out, and a local network that could not open (port taken) is retried automatically, 30 s then twice as long after each failure, up to 5 min. A failed start on the Internet side closes everything it had opened and is retried at the same pace; only it shows "Network unavailable" and "Blocking" in "My friends".

**Presence: online, offline, list up to date.** A friend is online as long as at least one greeted link connects them to the cabinet, whatever it is: the Internet, the local network, or one replacing the other. Greeted: their hello arrived on that link, so both cabinets see each other on it; a connection opened then closed before the hello does not count. When their last link drops, they stay online for 15 s (grace): Hyperswarm replaces a duplicate connection by closing the old one before handing over the new one, and a cabinet that reconnects does so within a few seconds. Past that delay with no new link, they go offline, "seen" at the drop of their last link, never at the time it is noticed; nothing is saved when a connection closes. Each side decides on the same links, so both cabinets converge: an Internet link silent for 20 s (crashed cabinet, changed network) is closed, a local link has its TCP probes, and on the local network, if the beacon only gets through one way, the cabinet that does not usually call calls after two beacons without a local link (an Internet link no longer replaces the local one). A followed friend without a link is asked of Hyperswarm again every 5 s for two minutes after startup or a network change, then every 30 s: after four failures (cabinet off, network not ready at startup, brief connections), Hyperswarm waited ten minutes before trying again, and the directory did not give it back. These delays are drawn at random around their value (from half to one and a half times): two cabinets calling each other at the same instant get in each other's way, because Hyperswarm keeps, on the larger key's side, its own call still in progress and cuts the other's, even when that call is going to fail (incoming blocked on the other side); at a fixed pace they fell back together at every redial. An Internet call cut before the hello, with no other link, is retried 0.3 to 1.5 s later (at most six times a minute), without waiting for Hyperswarm's 1 s, 5 s then 15 s. The list says whether it can be trusted ([friendpresence.ts](../packages/core/src/friendpresence.ts)): "Looking for friends…" at startup (30 s, or until every friend is there) and after a network change, "Updating…" during a grace, then "List up to date: 1 friend online", with a bar sweeping back and forth while it cannot be trusted; a friend offline during a search is "Seen 3 h ago · still looking". Everywhere presence shows: "My friends", the system menu ("1 online · still looking"), "Play with a friend", a friend's sheet ("Linked over the Internet and the local network"), the table menu pill (shown even at zero during the search, hollow dot). "Just connected" is not announced during the search: the cabinet is the one arriving. Log (`data\logs\journal.txt`), for friends and requests only: each link opened or closed, over what, its reason ("Duplicate connection", "connection timed out", "fin normale"), its duration and what remains; "X en ligne" with, during the search, the delay since it began ("2.4 s après le début de la recherche"); "X hors ligne (dernière liaison fermée il y a 15 s)"; a friend's local beacon and its address; a failed local call (firewall) once every five minutes; a friend Hyperswarm had given up on.

**Windows Firewall.** Without a rule, Windows would show its "Allow access" prompt on the network's first start, which nobody can answer on a cabinet without a mouse. The installer therefore adds the "Cyber Pinball" rule (inbound, `CyberPinball.exe` only, all profiles), with Windows' permission (UAC), once and only if it is missing ("Partage entre bornes : autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" section); never during an update (`/MAJ`), silently (`/S`, unless `/PAREFEU`) or in a sandbox. Uninstalling removes it. When it is missing (run from source, `npm run deployer`, UAC declined), "Partage et réseau" says so and offers "Autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" (Allow in Windows Firewall). Without it, friends over the Internet still get through (outbound NAT hole punching); the local network and cabinets calling in, less well. Under the same permission, a second rule, "Cyber Pinball VPX", **blocks** inbound traffic for VPX: VPX's inspector, which gives the live state of Stern SAM tables and the DMD image (friends' scores on its score screen) during a game with friends, listens on every address; with this rule, Windows shows no prompt for VPX and the local network cannot get in, while Cyber Pinball reads it through `127.0.0.1`, which the firewall does not filter. As long as it is missing, the inspector is never enabled. It follows VPX's version: VPX is started through the `emulators\vpx\current` junction, and Windows may name the process by its version's real folder; so the rule targets both paths (`vpxFirewallPrograms`). The installer adds it for the version the application will install (`VPX_PAREFEU_REEL` and `VPX_PAREFEU_JONCTION`, from `version` and `executable` in `packages/installer/components.json`); on a cabinet already installed and updated by pre-release, "Partage et réseau" offers "Autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" (Allow in Windows Firewall), one request for both rules. When VPX changes version (new folder, junction moved), the real path no longer matches: the rule must be set again, it is read again before every game with friends, and the inspector stays off until then. "Partage et réseau" always shows the "État en direct des tables Stern SAM" (Live state of Stern SAM tables) line: "Prêt" (Ready), or greyed with its reason (VPX not installed, rule to set, VPX changed version, rule unreadable). Uninstalling also removes the VPX rule if it targets this root, under the same request. The commands: [packages/core/src/firewall.ts](../packages/core/src/firewall.ts), the elevation: `app/src/firewall-rule.ts`, the inspector offered to the game: `app/src/vpx-inspector.ts`.

**Firewall after an update.** After the update to 0.1.5, the cabinet network was blocked, although permission had been given before. The cabinet's firewall event log (`Microsoft-Windows-Windows Firewall With Advanced Security/Firewall`) showed it: the rule added by netsh did not exist; there were only four "Query User" rules, which Windows creates on its own each time its "Allow access" prompt shows (TCP and UDP, for a single network profile, **blocking** until someone clicks). Windows names them after the executable's description, "Cyber Pinball": the same name as ours, so reading by name (`netsh … show rule name="Cyber Pinball"`) took them for a permission. A blocking rule always wins. And the VPX rule, new in 0.1.5, is added by no update: the light update does not run the installer, and the installer in update mode (`/MAJ`) never asks for Windows' permission. The path of `CyberPinball.exe`, for its part, never changes (`<root>\app`, replaced in place). Since then: rules are read **by program** (any inbound rule targeting the executable, whatever its name), through PowerShell, whose values do not depend on Windows' language (`firewallReadScript`, `appFirewallState`): "blocked" (a blocking rule), "allowed on some networks only", "missing", "allowed" on all networks. Adding the rule first removes any inbound rule on the executable, then adds ours for all profiles; for VPX, inbound blocked for each version folder and for the `current` junction. The same Windows permission registers the "Cyber Pinball pare-feu" scheduled task (`firewallSetupScript`): no trigger, started by the application only, outside the session (S4U, administrator rights, no window), on a fixed script encoded in its arguments, which only adds these two rules again. At every startup, and at the one following a full or light update, `app/src/firewall-guard.ts` reads the rules, writes their state to the log ("Pare-feu (après la mise à jour 0.1.5 → 0.1.6) : Cyber Pinball blocked, VPX missing, réparation automatique prête."), and, if one is missing or no longer matches, starts the task: nothing to confirm, even without a mouse. Without the task (never allowed on this machine), or if the repair was not enough, the state stays visible with its reason: "Partage et réseau" (each rule, and "Réparation automatique du pare-feu : prête" or "pas encore autorisée"), "Mes amis" ("Réseau des bornes bloqué par le pare-feu de Windows", friends "Bloqué par le pare-feu" instead of "Hors ligne"), and, at the startup following an update, the "Pare-feu de Windows" screen, opened on the table menu with "Autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" selected: a single Windows prompt, never in the middle of a game. Otherwise, a notice at the top of the menu. The installer also removes any inbound rule on the executable before adding its own, and uninstalling removes the task under the same prompt. The tests (`pare-feu-maj.test.ts`) simulate PowerShell, netsh and schtasks: never the machine's real firewall.

**Shared library.** On by default between friends: two players who became friends each see the other's library without setting anything. "Partage et réseau" > "Ne plus partager ma bibliothèque" (Stop sharing my library) turns it off, "Partager ma bibliothèque avec mes amis" (Share my library with my friends) turns it back on; this choice of the player is kept (`shareLibraryChosen`), and a `social.json` written before this default, which said "no" without anyone choosing it, is read back as "shared". The screen reminds you to only share what you have the right to share (captures of works, ROMs and PuP-Packs remain their rights holders'). Friends see the list of tables and, for each, its own media (those in `medias\`: logo, backglass, videos, rules, music; neither the cabinet's defaults nor the image taken from the `.directb2s`). A friend's page > "Voir sa bibliothèque" (See their library): "Sur votre borne" (On your cabinet: same game by VPS id, ROM, or title and year; "2 manquants", 2 missing) and "Absentes de votre borne" (Not on your cabinet). One of their tables: its media, "Déjà équipé" (Already has one: your table already has this type of media, not necessarily the same file) or "Manquant" (Missing), then "Récupérer les n médias manquants" (Get the n missing media), "Récupérer les n médias" (Get the n media) or "Inviter … à jouer" (Invite … to play). The transfer shows its progress and can be cancelled (Exit); each file arrives in `<data>\amis\recus\` and only leaves it if it has the announced size and SHA-256 fingerprint and first bytes that say media of its type (the import's checks). It then enters the table's "Médias du jeu" (Game media) draft, with its origin ("la borne de Bruno #A1B2", Bruno's cabinet), the cursor on the first media item received ("Afficheur large reçu de Bruno : vérifiez-le, puis enregistrez.", Wide display received from Bruno: check it, then save): you see it, then "Enregistrer" (Save) shows the plan before writing. **A friend's whole table.** A table you do not have: "Récupérer toute la table" (Get the whole table); a table you already have: "Choisir les parties de sa table" (Choose parts of their table). The parts screen asks the friend's cabinet for the list of its files, sorted by game part ("Parties du jeu"): table (the `.vpx`, the DMD content and the extra scripts), backglass, ROM, PuP-Pack, DMD colours, sounds, music, point of view, own settings, menu media; each part with its number of files and its weight, and yours opposite when you have it. The screen opens in the list of parts, the cursor on the first one: each line says in plain words "Cochée" (Ticked) or "Non cochée" (Unticked) (the title counts "3 cochées sur 4", 3 ticked of 4), and Start on a part ticks or unticks it; for a table you already have, ticked, theirs replaces yours, unticked, you keep yours. The flippers go down from the last part to "Récupérer les n parties cochées" (Get the n ticked parts), then to "Retour" (Back); the help strip says so in the list ("Start ou Entrée : cocher ou décocher la partie", Start or Enter: tick or untick the part; `listStart`: any screen whose list is ticked declares it), and MagnaSave goes from the list to the actions and back. By default: all their parts you do not have, except those under rights, unticked with their note: the ROM ("l'œuvre du fabricant : à ne prendre que si vous en avez le droit", the manufacturer's work: only take it if you have the right to) and the PuP-Pack ("vidéos et musiques souvent tirées du jeu : à ne prendre que si vous en avez le droit", videos and music often taken from the game: only take it if you have the right to). One rule for every unticked part: its line says it is there and how to tick it, "+ Disponible : Start pour la cocher" (Available: Start to tick it; "… pour prendre la sienne à la place de la vôtre", to take theirs instead of yours, when you have it), and for the ROM and the PuP-Pack "! Disponible, à cocher vous-même : Start, puis l'attestation de vos droits" (Available, tick it yourself: Start, then the declaration of your rights). Start on one of them opens its legal notice ("La ROM : vos droits", The ROM: your rights; "Le PuP-Pack : vos droits", The PuP-Pack: your rights): what it is and who owns it, where it comes from ("Ce contenu vient directement de la borne de … : Cyber Pinball ne le fournit pas et ne vérifie pas vos droits. Vous en êtes seul responsable.", This content comes straight from …'s cabinet: Cyber Pinball does not provide it and does not check your rights.
