You alone are responsible for it.), then the pledge to tick, "J'atteste avoir le droit de détenir ce contenu (par exemple la machine d'origine ou une licence) et je m'engage à respecter le droit d'auteur et les conditions d'utilisation de ses ayants droit." (I certify that I have the right to hold this content, for example the original machine or a licence, and I agree to respect the copyright and the terms of use of its rights holders; "J'atteste et je m'engage", I certify and agree, "À cocher", To tick, then "Cochée", Ticked). "Prendre la ROM" (Take the ROM) or "Prendre le PuP-Pack" (Take the PuP-Pack) does nothing until it is ticked; once ticked, the cursor moves onto it, then the parts screen comes back on the ROM's line, "Cochée", "✓ Droits attestés" (Rights attested). A part asked for by an invitation is ticked by default only if it is not under rights. The attestation holds for this table while the screen is open; it follows the download (`attested`, `attestedAt` in its queue record), and a file of a rights part that was not attested never enters it (`attestedTableFiles`). Below the list, "Récupérer les n parties cochées" (Get the n ticked parts: the weight; refused if there is not enough room, plus 1 GiB, on the data drive). **A transfer starts only once**: it carries a key, the table and the friend (`dedupe`), and while it runs, waits for its friend, is paused, or is received but not installed, nothing starts it again. It shows where it was started: in the friend's library, the table's line carries its state ("Réception en cours", Transfer in progress; "En attente de Bruno", Waiting for Bruno; "Reçue · à installer", Received · to install), its bar and "42 % · 120 Mo sur 300 Mo · fichier 3 sur 14 · Start : voir la réception" (Start: see the transfer); Start leads to it. On the table's page, "Voir la réception de la table" (See the table transfer, or "Installer la table reçue", Install the received table) replaces "Récupérer toute la table", the cursor on it, with the same bar at the top; likewise "Voir la réception des médias" (See the media transfer). The friend's page adds "1 réception en cours" (1 transfer in progress). The transfer screen says "Lancée à 14:02 : rien d'autre à faire" (Started at 14:02: nothing else to do): it carries on if you leave it, and Start on the table comes back here without starting it again; "Continuer en arrière-plan" goes back to the table, "Annuler la réception" (Cancel the transfer) deletes what was received. Once received, the screen says "Reçue, pas encore installée" (Received, not installed yet) and offers first **"Installer dans ma bibliothèque"** (Install in my library: this table's add plan); likewise the table's page and Downloads. A received table whose plan was cancelled stays "Reçue · à installer" while its folder is there, and "Récupérer de nouveau" (Get it again) is an explicit choice on that screen. A reminder: a table contains a script that VPX runs, to be taken only from friends you trust. The files arrive in `<data>\amis\tables\<date>\<table folder>\` under their relative path, checked on both sides (never "..", nor a drive, nor an absolute path, nor a reserved Windows name, nor a program: `.exe`, `.dll`, `.bat`, `.ps1`, `.lnk`…), each with its size and its SHA-256 fingerprint, computed while sending (a file of several gigabytes is read only once). Saves, high scores (`nvram`, `VPReg.stg`), PinMAME settings and statistics never leave. Then the folder goes through the add plan, like a downloaded table: a new table, or into yours (the parts taken replace yours, to the trash); nothing is put away before "Installer" (Install). A file missing on arrival: nothing is installed. An invitation to a live game on a table you do not have offers "La récupérer chez …" (Get it from …). "Trouver cette table dans le catalogue" (Find this table in the catalogue) remains there to get it from its site. **What is announced is served**: a library media item goes through the import checks as soon as it is announced (`mediaRefusal`, kept per file, size and date), and one that fails them is announced neither in the library nor in its table's files; the log says so ("… n'est pas annoncé (le contenu ne correspond pas à son nom (.png))", … is not announced). If a file is still refused on the way, every file of a table except its `.vpx` is optional: it is skipped with its exact reason ("média refusé par les contrôles : …", media refused by the checks; "introuvable chez votre ami : …", not found at your friend's; "fichier abîmé ou changé : …", file damaged or changed), the rest arrives, and both the transfer screen and Downloads say "1 fichier sauté" (1 file skipped). "Ne le partage plus" is now only said of a friend who turned sharing off. What travels carries no path of the cabinet: each table, media item and file has an opaque id that only the sharing cabinet links to a file; only friends can ask for them.

**Download speed.** "Partage et réseau" > "Limiter le débit des téléchargements…" (Limit download speed…): no limit (by default), 256 KB/s, 512 KB/s, 1, 2, 5, 10 or 20 MB/s (each with its equivalent in Mbit/s, the unit of internet boxes). The limit applies to everything friends send (tables, parts, media), **all transfers together**: shared equally between the transfers in progress, never less than 16 KB/s each. The sending cabinet is the one that slows down: only it pushes the chunks, and slowing down reception would also block presence and messages, which go through the same channel. The receiver gives its speed in the request (`file-get`, field `rate`, in bytes per second), then corrects it on the way (`file-rate`) when the limit changes or another transfer starts or ends; the sender follows this pace on average (`RatePacer`, a late wake-up of Windows timers is made up, with at most 250 ms ahead). A cabinet from before this setting ignores both and sends at full speed; the screen says so. Downloads from sites and VPinMediaDB are not affected. Kept in `<data>\social.json` (`downloadRate`, absent: no limit). Code: [packages/core/src/ratelimit.ts](../packages/core/src/ratelimit.ts), `serveFile` and `fetchOne` in `app/src/friend-content.ts`; tests: [debit-amis.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/debit-amis.test.ts) (including a real 640 KB transfer at 256 KB/s between two cabinets linked in memory, and the limit lifted on the way).

**Live game.** Two entries. A friend's page > "Inviter à une partie en direct" (Invite to a live game): choose a table (those they have in the same version first, see "Choosing the table of a live game" below). A table's page > "Jouer avec un ami" (Play with a friend), right under "Jouer" (Play): choose the friend (online ones first, those who have the table in the same version at the top, offline friends greyed out below). Both stay visible when you cannot invite, greyed out with their reason: "Hors ligne : invitez-le quand il sera connecté" (Offline: invite them once they are connected), "Aucun ami en ligne pour l'instant" (No friend online right now), "Pas encore d'ami : Menu système > Amis > Trouver un joueur" (No friend yet: System menu > Friends > Find a player); a friend in a game can be invited too ("En partie : il recevra une notification discrète sur sa table", In a game: they will get a discreet notification on their table). A room opens, the invitation leaves; up to four players ("Inviter un autre ami", Invite another friend). On the friend's side, the invitation chime rings and "Invitation de …" (Invitation from) opens by itself over the table menu, in the system menu and on the help (over the open screen, which "Répondre plus tard" gives back as it was), the cursor on "Répondre plus tard" (Answer later, with the time left): a button already pressed neither accepts nor declines; otherwise it waits at the top of "Mes amis", two minutes at most. The cabinet checks there that it has the same version as the host, mandatorily (a score to compete on, and tournaments later): the same `.vpx` (SHA-256 fingerprint, kept in `<data>\amis\empreintes.json`) and the same script played. The script played: the table's own, or the `.vbs` next to it when it replaces it (VPX then plays the `.vbs`); a modified `.vbs`, or one from another version, must be the same for everyone, the one Cyber Pinball writes itself (PuP repairs, specific to each cabinet) does not count. Not the same version, no "Accepter" (Accept): "Autre version de cette table" (Another version of this table) (or "Même table, autre script", Same table, another script, a `.vbs` next to the table), and "Récupérer sa version chez …" (Fetch …'s version) opens their table in their shared library, the table ticked, then the add plan; all that is left is to ask for a new invitation. On the host's side: "N'a pas la même version : il peut récupérer la vôtre" (Does not have the same version: they can fetch yours) ([meme-version.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/meme-version.test.ts)). The same `.vpx` is recognised under another name: the invitation gives its size, the cabinet computes the fingerprint of its tables of that size. The log says what it found ("la même version", the same version; "une autre version : partie refusée", another version: game refused; "le même .vpx, mais un autre script", the same .vpx but another script; "reconnue par l'empreinte de son .vpx", recognised by its .vpx fingerprint), or "introuvable ici" (not found here) with the game looked for. A table fetched from a friend keeps the name it has on their cabinet: a `.info` with its title, manufacturer, year and VPS game comes with the received files, and the add plan takes it before the game's name on VPS ([meme-table-autre-nom.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/meme-table-autre-nom.test.ts)). The number of balls per game counts too: known on both sides and different (5 against 3), the score goes out of the ranking, "5 billes au lieu de 3" (5 balls instead of 3) in the invitation, the room and the results ("Autre nombre de billes : score hors classement", Another number of balls: score not ranked); unknown on one side, it judges nothing. It is read, never guessed ([ballcount.ts](../packages/core/src/ballcount.ts), [app/src/ball-count.ts](../app/src/ball-count.ts)): for a ROM table, the "Balls Per Game" adjustment in `<table>\pinmame\nvram\<rom>.nv` (otherwise the shared PinMAME folder), according to that ROM's memory map from the [Pinball Memory Maps](https://github.com/tomlogic/pinball-memory-maps) project (ODbL licence), downloaded on demand and kept in `<data>\cache\cartes-memoire`; without a `.nv`, the table has not run yet and the ROM starts from its factory setting (the map's default); a ROM whose map does not describe this adjustment (Stern SAM: Star Trek, The Walking Dead) stays unknown. Without a ROM: "Const BallsPerGame = 3" outside the script's procedures (GOTG, The Goonies, The Matrix), or the service menu of VPW tables (Game of Thrones: "BALL PER GAME", the value chosen in `VPReg.stg`, otherwise the default). A table that creates its settings on the fly (2001) stays unknown ([nombre-de-billes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/nombre-de-billes.test.ts)). Without the table, only "Refuser" (Decline) remains, with "Trouver cette table dans le catalogue". The host starts the game as soon as a friend has accepted: "Départ commun" (Shared start) opens on every cabinet, a large number from 5 to 1 in the centre and the players below (the cursor on "Retour au menu", Back to menu: a Start pressed to get ready does not leave the game, the start is still on), then the table starts everywhere as if by Start. Each plays their own game on their cabinet: nothing is controlled remotely. Back in the menu, "Résultats" (Results): the players whose score entered the table's high score table, ranked, their score shown large ("Meilleur score connu", Best known score); the others without a rank, "Score non disponible" (Score not available: an unknown score is not ranked); each player's play time; "Autre nombre de billes : score hors classement" (Another number of balls: score not ranked) when that is the case (that score is shown without a rank). The screen fills in as the others come back (fifteen minutes at most after the first). The host can "Rejouer avec les mêmes joueurs" (Play again with the same players). The host runs the game and sends the room to everyone; guests do not need to be friends with each other. **The table's copy**: a live game never touches the player's usual ROM memory, records or settings. VPX plays a copy of the table, made just before launch in `<root>\parties\<game>\` (same drive as the tables) ([tabledouble.ts](../packages/core/src/tabledouble.ts), [app/src/table-double.ts](../app/src/table-double.ts)): linked (hard link, the same file with no extra space) what nothing writes during a game, the `.vpx`, the backglass, the ROMs, images, sounds, videos and fonts; copied everything else, what VPX, PinMAME, PuP or B2S may write (`pinmame\nvram`, `pinmame\cfg`, `user\VPReg.stg`, `.ini`, `.xml`, `.vbs`, PuP scripts); left out `cache\` (VPX rebuilds it) and `medias\`. A table that plays its ROM from the shared PinMAME folder gets its own `pinmame\roms` (links) and a copy of its memory in its copy, otherwise PinMAME would write into the shared folder. The game's records are read in the copy, then it is deleted, never following a link to a folder; a copy left by a crash goes at the next start. No copy, no game, never a fallback to the usual folder: table sharing its folder with others, link or junction in its folder, ROM not found, tables on another drive; the screen says so, "Votre table habituelle reste intacte : rien n'a été lancé" (Your usual table stays untouched: nothing was started). The log compares the usual folder's fingerprint (size and date of each file) before and after: "dossier de la table intact (N fichiers comparés)" (table folder untouched, N files compared), or what changed. The test has a fake VPX write everywhere these programs write, then compares every byte ([double-de-table.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/double-de-table.test.ts)). Measured on a cabinet: Game of Thrones, 1853 links and 19 copies in 3.8 s, none of the 1881 files changed. **The host's settings**: everyone starts from the same machine state, without Cyber Pinball having to read or understand each setting ([hostsettings.ts](../packages/core/src/hostsettings.ts)). As soon as a friend accepts, the host's cabinet sends them, for each ROM of the table, its memory (`pinmame\nvram\<rom>.nv`: balls per game, ball save, tilt warnings, extra balls, high scores) and its DIP switches (`pinmame\cfg\<rom>.cfg`), and the values the table saves (`user\VPReg.stg`: the service menu of VPW tables, their high scores); a file the host does not have goes as "absent". In 96 KiB pieces (a message between cabinets carries 256 KiB at most; a Stern SAM ROM's memory is 128 KiB), each file checked by its SHA-256 fingerprint; the guest tells the host whether it received them whole. At launch, they replace the copies in the guest's copy of the table, and a file absent on the host is removed from it: both cabinets then start from factory settings. The guest's usual folder does not move. In the room: "Prêt, réglages de l'hôte reçus" (Ready, host's settings received); "en route" (on their way) while they travel; "non reçus : score hors classement" (not received: score not ranked) if they arrived damaged, too large (over 1 MiB per file), or if the guest's cabinet runs a version older than this feature; in the results, "Hors classement : réglages de l'hôte non reçus" (Not ranked: host's settings not received). To play with 5 balls, the host sets their own table to 5 before inviting. Settings specific to each cabinet do not travel: PinMAME's `default.cfg`, the table's display and sound options (`.ini`) ([reglages-de-l-hote.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/reglages-de-l-hote.test.ts)). **The group's high score board**: the scores of ranked live games, per exact version of the table (same `.vpx`, same script), kept by each cabinet that played in `<data>\amis\records.json`, twenty places ([groupboard.ts](../packages/core/src/groupboard.ts)). Each ranked player's best score goes in, once per game; out of the ranking (host's settings not received, another number of balls), it does not. The room shows "Record du groupe à battre : …" (Group record to beat) (or "Pas encore de record du groupe sur cette version", No group record on this version yet); the results, "Records du groupe sur cette version" (Group records on this version), the top five. In the game, the table's high score table is the group's: before launch, its records are written into the copy's `VPReg.stg`, in place of the host's, the last place left at zero, so that each player's score always enters it and can be read on return. Only the values are rewritten, in place, within the room already allocated to each: the structure of the file, which VPX reads through Windows, does not change; credits and settings (the host's number of balls) stay. Between friends, the knocker only rings for the group record beaten; these records are not sent to friends like those of a solo game. A ROM table (PinMAME: T2, Star Trek, The Walking Dead) keeps its high score table in the ROM memory: it is read and written in the copy's `<rom>.nv` according to that ROM's map (Pinball Memory Maps, cached in `<data>\cache\cartes-memoire`), mirrored copies and checksums redone (one wrong checksum, and the ROM restores its factory high scores); then everything is read back, and a single wrong checksum cancels the write ([romscores.ts](../packages/core/src/romscores.ts)). Checked on a cabinet's memories: the 134 checksums of T2, 73 of Star Trek and 23 of The Walking Dead, correct according to this computation, stay correct after the high score table, the only thing changed, is written. No map for the ROM, a map without `validation`, a 4-bit memory: nothing is written or read, nothing enters the board ("Aucun score classé pour l'instant", No ranked score yet). Without a `.nv` (the host has none: factory settings), the ROM starts from its factory high scores: a score lower than the last one cannot be read ([tableau-du-groupe.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/tableau-du-groupe.test.ts), [records-rom.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/records-rom.test.ts)). **Live game state**: for original tables built on the common script base (Game of Thrones, GOTG, The Goonies, The Matrix: `Score()`, `CurrentPlayer`, `PlayersPlayingGame`, `BallsRemaining()`, `BallsPerGame`, `bGameInPlay`), the copy's script gets an addition, never the table's ([livestate.ts](../packages/core/src/livestate.ts)). An element the script never uses (a wall, a light; neither its name nor its events appear in it) lends its built-in timer: four times a second, the score, the ball and "game in progress" go to the copy's `cyberpinball-etat.txt`. The `<table>_KeyDown` procedure is wrapped: the coin door is always ignored; Start and the lockbar, outside a game in progress (no other game after ours); during the game, Start goes through (it validates the letters of Game of Thrones' initials), and a player added with Start shows in the state: their score goes out of the ranking. The start is automatic: once the table is ready, on the shared start's “go” (below), the script inserts a credit then presses Start, and tries again if it does not start (four times at most). During the game, Cyber Pinball reads the state twice a second; score and ball go to the others (at most twice a second, at once on a new ball), and the game's strip shows each player, their score and where they are, the best at the top in signal green ([gamenotice.ts](../packages/core/src/gamenotice.ts), `liveBoardView`). **Where it goes**: never at the top of the playfield, and always a visible permanent strip (`liveBoardPlacement`). Automatic takes, in order, the backglass, free or taken by the table (its B2S, its PuP-Pack: the usual case, the strip goes in front, discreetly), otherwise the topper, likewise, otherwise, as a last resort, the bottom of the playfield; the scores on the DMD are a separate setting ("Scores sur le DMD", Scores on the DMD, on by default), in addition, never instead; the log says the place and why ("pourquoi : automatique : fronton (pris par la table, la bande passe devant), et sur le DMD quand il montre l'écran de score"), and "aucune place visible" (no visible place) should it happen. The test [bande-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/bande-partie.test.ts) fixes the invariant on every screen combination (playfield alone, + backglass, + DMD, + backglass and DMD, + topper, DMD under the backglass), each screen taken or not, DMD readable or not, four orientations, one to four players and each choice; `packages\core\ecrans-simules\pincab.json` is a real PinCab's configuration. **On the DMD**, only when it shows the table's score screen. The DMD that VPX or PinMAME draws cannot be written from outside (no window to write to, no script shared by ROM tables), but it can be read: for a game with friends, the VPX inspector is enabled in the double's copy of `VPinballX.ini` (firewall rule closing VPX to the network in place, read through 127.0.0.1), which gives the displays (`/api/tree`: PinMAME's DMD, FlexDMD…) and a frame at each change (`/ws/display`) ([app/src/dmd-watch.ts](../app/src/dmd-watch.ts)). The score screen rule ([dmdscore.ts](../packages/core/src/dmdscore.ts)): a game in progress according to the state (never attract mode, the end of the game or initials entry); a "screen", stable frames (0.4 s at least, within 8% of their ink) linked by transitions of one second at most and changing by no more than 45% of their dots from one to the next; it is the score screen when its frame changes while the score moves (within 1.2 s: the state reading lags), or when it has the layout of a score screen already recognised in the last two minutes (the same one, back after an animation, other digits), or, at rest (score still for 3 s), when it comes back or stays 3.5 s (and, once the score screen has been seen in play, looks like it); quiet for 1.5 s. A mode text, a choice to make (ball held, score frozen), the BONUS neither change with the score nor look like it: nothing is laid over them. Measured off-screen on the cabinet's ROMs with libpinmame: Terminator 2 shows its score screen ~1 s, slides its score ~0.5 s, then shows it again; with the ball in play, its digits change by 15 to 40% of its dots at each hit, an animation, a BONUS or a text by 50 to 160%; Star Trek shows an animated scene in play with the score in a corner. On the bench, with this rule, Terminator 2 played (a hit every 0.4 to 2.5 s) gives 12 passes in 170 s (one every 12 s), all on the score screen, and Star Trek 8, never on "BALL SAVED" nor on an animation. The scores are then laid over it for 4 s, in its place (the FullDMD dressing's, otherwise the DMD zone), same dot font and colour as the menu's DMD; up to three players, "PARTIE ENTRE AMIS" (GAME WITH FRIENDS) on top, one line per player, name and ball ("B2", "END") on the left, score on the right, this cabinet's player at full brightness (`liveBoardDots`). Every 12 s as long as the DMD shows the score screen; sooner, 10 s after the previous one, when one of the player's balls ends, someone else takes the lead or finishes their game; never twice within 10 s; they leave as soon as the DMD shows something else (`LiveBoardFlash`). Without the inspector (VPX firewall rule not allowed) or without a state reading for the table, nothing on the DMD, and the log says so. A table without PinMAME (FlexDMD DMD, The Goonies, The Matrix): its image is never read (the inspector would make its scene render outside the script's thread, which stops VPX); the scores show there at the same pace from the state reading alone, game in progress for 5 s and score moved less than 8 s ago (ball in play: neither ball held nor a choice to make), without seeing an animation, which a pass may cover for 4 s (`DmdSignalWatch`). **Summary in the log**, for each game: "Scores sur le DMD : 9 passages en 112 s de partie (un toutes les 12 s) ; le reste du temps : …" (9 passes in 112 s of play, one every 12 s; the rest of the time: …), or "aucun passage en … ; pourquoi : …" (no pass in …; why: …): setting off, no DMD screen, DMD unreadable and why (inspector unreachable, no DMD in its tree, stream closed), or what the rule saw, as a share of the time. Guard (`LiveDmdTally`): setting on and DMD present, with no pass after 60 s of play, the log says why at once ("Scores sur le DMD : aucun passage après 60 s de partie ; pourquoi : …"); the test [bande-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/bande-partie.test.ts) fixes the invariant (a pass, or the reason) over every combination of setting, screen, reading and DMD. A hardware DMD alone (ZeDMD, PIN2DMD) cannot be covered. **On the backglass**, free or taken by the table (its B2S, its PuP-Pack; the log says which, from `occupiedRoles`, read again every 2 s), permanently: "Partie entre amis" then one card per player, side by side, name, large score, "Bille 2 sur 3" (Ball 2 of 3); at the bottom of the backglass, like an electromechanical machine's score reels, discreetly (a fifth of its height at most, never below the readable size: a short backglass otherwise sent it to the playfield); without a backglass, in the middle of the topper. With neither backglass nor topper, **an inset at the bottom of the playfield**, in the player's left corner, turned with the playfield: on the apron, below the outlane exits, away from the drain and the shooter lane; one line per player (name, score, ball), a tenth of the playfield height at most. **The player's choice**: System menu > Friends > "Scores des amis pendant la partie" (Friends' scores during the game) ([app/src/live-scores-menu.ts](../app/src/live-scores-menu.ts), kept in `<data>\amis\scores-en-partie.json`): Automatic (the above), On the backglass (at the bottom, over its image, its B2S or its PuP-Pack), On the topper (in the middle, likewise), At the bottom of the playfield, On the side of the playfield (in the middle of the player's left edge). Every choice stays visible; one the cabinet does not allow is greyed with its reason (no backglass screen, no topper). Separately, "Scores sur le DMD : activés" (Scores on the DMD: on, the default) or "désactivés" (off: nothing on the DMD any more), Start to switch; greyed with its reason without a DMD screen, or while the VPX firewall rule is not allowed (the DMD cannot be read). The settings apply to every table (the file allows a per-table setting, `tables`, not offered yet); a place a table does not allow falls back to automatic, and the log says why. It is set outside the game: during the game, the buttons belong to VPX, and Cyber Pinball has no menu on top. **Always in front**: PuP, B2S or external DMD windows put themselves back on top during the game; twice a second, the Windows window stack is read again, a window of another process that came in front of the strip is logged ("Bande de la partie entre amis recouverte par "…" (class, process) : remise devant", once; "repasse sans cesse devant" if it insists), and the strip comes back in front (`overlayCoveredBy`), then every 40 ms while the window insists (and 5 s after the last time), never taking the focus. Measured off screen (the real strip on a simulated backglass, covered by another process's always-on-top window):in front of a B2S (always on top set once), 100% of the time; in front of a PuP-Pack putting itself back on top every 100 ms, 83% (before the fast bring-to-front: 11%). No visual setting is offered during the game: the place is set here. Tests: [bande-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/bande-partie.test.ts) (screens from `packages\core\ecrans-simules`, four orientations, one to four players, each choice, covering, pace) and [dmd-ecran-de-score.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dmd-ecran-de-score.test.ts) (drawn sequences). Once the game is over (initials entered), VPX closes by itself after 4 s; the final score comes from the state, it ranks and enters the group board even if nobody entered initials. A game that ran for 20 s at least counts as played, even if VPX crashes or returns an error code when closing ("La partie n'a pas démarré de son côté" showed after several minutes of play). Checked on the real scripts of Game of Thrones, GOTG, The Goonies and The Matrix: completed, they compile (cscript, nothing executed). **ROM tables** (PinMAME): the game state is in the ROM's memory. The live state reads it through `Controller.NVRAM` (the very bytes of the `.nv`), at the addresses of the `game_state` section of the ROM's map (Pinball Memory Maps, cached in `<data>\cache\cartes-memoire`): scores, current player, ball, game over, number of players, and balls per game from its settings (`romLivePlan`). The map and the memory are those of the game PinMAME really loads: the table's alias (`pinmame\alias.txt`) replaces the name the script asks for, for the live state as well as for the group's records, the ball count and the host's settings (`loadedRoms`; Star Trek asks for `st_162h` and loads `st_161h`, its memory is `st_161h.nv`). The keys are wrapped as for original tables. Measured on the cabinet's libpinmame, with no screen or sound, with T2's ROM (`t2_l8`): at boot, the memory shows the previous game for three seconds if VPX was quit in the middle of a game, then the ROM clears it; so a game only counts after the live state's own Start, and the start waits for the table in attract mode (two seconds); the credit goes through `vpmKeyDown` (taken 750 ms later), and Start is held for half a second, two seconds after the credit (again every ten seconds, two minutes at most, if the game does not start). Game over comes before the initials are entered, and the score only enters the high score table afterwards (up to a minute and a half later): as long as a score of the game beats the last place without being in it, the game stays in progress for the live state (Start goes through and confirms the letters), two minutes at most; then VPX closes. The memory is read twice a second (every two seconds above 32 KB: copying 128 KB costs the script about 3 ms, on VPX's thread). Williams WPC: the whole RAM is the NVRAM, everything is read there (Terminator 2). Stern SAM (Star Trek, The Walking Dead): scores, number of players, balls per game and the high score table are in the NVRAM, but the map puts player, ball and game over in main RAM, out of the script's reach; the test bench found no reliable game-in-progress flag in the NVRAM, and a ball counter that keeps the ball of an interrupted game until the following game. These three values go through VPX 10.8.1's inspector: VPX's PinMAME plugin reads a reduced map placed in the double (`pinmame\memmaps\index.json` and `cyberpinball.json`, under the name the script asks for and under the one PinMAME loads), then publishes these values read from the CPU's memory; the inspector serves them over HTTP, and the live state reads them through `127.0.0.1` (`/api/tree` for their number, `/api/states` for their value, 600 ms wait at most: without an answer, that reading is skipped). The values are raw there: the map's mask and inversion (game over: status byte `& 0xFE` zero) are applied in the live state. The inspector is enabled for the game with friends only, through a copy of the cabinet's `VPinballX.ini` placed in the double (`[Plugin.Inspector]`, `Enable = 1`, a free port from 2113 to 2122) and given to VPX with `-Ini`; the usual `VPinballX.ini` is never touched, and whatever VPX changes in the copy during the game leaves with the double (the log says so). It listens on every address, with no command (in this version: `/info`, `/api/tree`, `/api/states`, display images): it is only enabled if the "Cyber Pinball VPX" firewall rule closes VPX to the network for its active version (see Windows Firewall); otherwise the log says "pas de relevé de l'état de jeu (… ; l'inspector de VPX les lirait, s'il pouvait être activé ; règle du pare-feu de VPX absente…)" (no live state: the inspector would read them, the VPX firewall rule is missing) and the game plays as before. A table that reads everything in its NVRAM (T2) never uses it. Main RAM is cleared when the ROM boots: no previous game to discard; the start waits for the table in attract mode, as on WPC. What only a test on the real cabinet will confirm: the addresses of Star Trek's map were measured by its authors on `st_161h` and `st_161hc` (the cabinet plays `st_161h`), those of The Walking Dead on `twd_160h`; the test bench cannot read SAM's main RAM. The Walking Dead's map puts "Balls Per Game" in `game_state` only (`ball_count`): it is read there too, for the live state as well as for the ranking's ball count. Checked: the completed script of both T2 versions on the cabinet compiles (cscript, nothing executed); the live state, run by cscript on the bytes of T2's real memory captured on the bench during a game, gives the start, the live score and ball, keeps the game going during the initials and ends it when the score enters the table ([etat-en-direct.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/etat-en-direct.test.ts)). **The shared start**: everyone starts at the same time; after that, it does not matter who finishes first ([livestart.ts](../packages/core/src/livestart.ts)). Each cabinet starts VPX; when its table has loaded and its state reader answers (“pret=1” in `cyberpinball-etat.txt`: the table on screen for three seconds, or a ROM table waiting for four readings), it tells the host it is ready. The host gives the “go” when every awaited player is ready, and the room carries it to everyone. Each cabinet then writes `cyberpinball-partez.txt` in its copy; the state reader looks for it on every tick (a quarter of a second, half a second on a ROM table), and only then inserts the credit and presses Start. A file rather than a key sent to VPX: no window to aim at and no focus to take, nothing lost if VPX is busy, and the file stays there for a slower reader. Until then, Start and the coin door stay blocked: nobody starts alone. While waiting, a card in the middle of the playfield, in its top third (no ball is in play): “Waiting for players”, the reason (“Waiting for Bruno — Loading table…”), then each player, a green tick once ready, and their status (Starting VPX…, Loading table…, Ready, Left the game, Old version: starts without waiting); at the start, “Go!” for four seconds, and the game strip takes over. Those four seconds count from when “Go!” appears on screen, never from the time of the signal: on a busy cabinet, the card's window can take several seconds to appear. So the card loads, hidden, as soon as VPX is launched, and appears as soon as there is something to say, even for a wait of an instant; a cabinet only says it is ready once its table is on screen. The log says what the player saw: “carte montrée 5.1 s en attente, « Partez ! » 4.0 s”. The live game screen shows the same wait, and “Ready, waiting for the shared start” for each player. Edge cases, each one stated on screen. A player who is never ready: from the first ready player, the others have 90 s (“Starting without Bruno in 45 s if their table is not ready”); then the “go” without them rather than a cancellation: those who are ready do not lose their game, and the latecomer, who has already received the “go”, starts as soon as their table is ready (“The others have already started: your turn!”); their score counts like the others. A player who quits VPX while waiting, or whose game did not start, is no longer awaited (“Left the game”). A cabinet left on a version from before the shared start never says it is ready: it is not awaited, its table starts as soon as it has loaded, and the room says so (“Cyber Pinball … on their cabinet: their table starts without waiting for the others, needs updating”); a guest whose host is in that case starts without waiting, and their table says so. A host who goes silent (cabinet switched off): a ready guest starts on their own two minutes later (“No news from …: your table starts on its own”). A table without a state reader (neither the common script base nor a map for its ROM) is ready as soon as it is on screen; Start is not blocked there: the card says “Wait for “Go!” before pressing Start”, then “Go!” and “Press Start”. The log says “ma table est prête”, “« partez » donné (tous prêts)” or “(sans …, pas prêts à temps)”, then “« partez » posé pour le relevé”. Checked: the state machine (delays, start, old cabinet, silent host), the message between cabinets, two cabinets on a test network, and the completed state reader run by cscript on the common script base and on T2's memory: no credit or Start before the file, not even the player's ([depart-commun.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/depart-commun.test.ts)). **Cabinet versions**: each cabinet announces at the invitation and the answer what it can do (`LIVE_PROTOCOL`: 1 invitation and fingerprint, 0.1.4; 2 host's settings and group board, 0.1.5-essai.8; 3 live state and automatic start, 0.1.5-essai.13; 4 shared start) and its Cyber Pinball version. A cabinet that lags behind shows: in the room, "Cyber Pinball 0.1.5-essai.10 sur sa borne : ni départ automatique ni score en direct, à mettre à jour" (Cyber Pinball 0.1.5-essai.10 on their cabinet: no automatic start or live score, needs updating); in the invitation, "Ancienne version chez …" (Old version on …'s cabinet); in the strip, "Ancienne version : pas de score en direct" (Old version: no live score). In the results, each player has their game score (read live, otherwise entered in the table's high score table) and their rank in the game, with the reason for being out of the ranking (several players in their table, host's settings not received, another number of balls); never their place in their table's high score table, which says nothing about the game ([version-des-bornes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/version-des-bornes.test.ts)). **Invitation during a game**: the cabinet no longer answers "busy", it leaves the choice to the player. The chime rings (one of the interface sounds allowed during a game, see [Interface sounds](#interface-sounds)), the one-line notification "Bruno vous invite à rejoindre une partie" (Bruno invites you to join a game) shows with it (see "Game news"), and a discreet card appears over VPX, in the playfield's top left corner as the player sees it (a recording's indicator holds the right corner): "Bruno vous invite à jouer" (Bruno invites you to play), the table, the time left and "Quittez la partie pour le rejoindre, ou continuez : elle s'efface seule" (Leave the game to join them, or keep playing: it fades away by itself). A window without focus, that clicks go through and that no recording sees ([app/src/game-notice.ts](../app/src/game-notice.ts), [gamenotice.ts](../packages/core/src/gamenotice.ts)): VPX keeps control. After 12 s, it shrinks to a pill "Invitation de Bruno" (Invitation from Bruno) with the time left, until the invitation expires, is cancelled or the game ends. Nothing to do to ignore it; leaving your game brings you back to the menu, where the invitation opens by itself if it is still valid. Only a live game already in progress answers "busy". In the room, the host reads "Invité, en partie : notifié sur sa table, il peut quitter la sienne pour vous rejoindre" (Invited, in a game: notified on their table, they can leave theirs to join you). Seeing the others' score, ball and DMD during the game is the next step, to be decided on a mockup.

**Game news.** Away from the game's screen (on the tables menu, in the system menu, during any game), a one-line notification says right away that a friend invites you to join a game, that a guest accepts or declines, and why, that a player leaves the game, that the host cancels it, or that the game is over for everyone when you finished yours before the others: the winner, your rank, where the results are; a friend coming online too (see "Game with friends" and "Everywhere, even in a game" in [Tables menu notifications](#tables-menu-notifications)). During a game, it sits over VPX, in the playfield's top-left corner for the player, turned with it in the direction VPX really draws the table (`played`, [gameorientation.ts](../packages/core/src/gameorientation.ts); the same rule places the band and the start card), 720 × 76 px on a playfield 1,080 px on its short side, under an invitation's card if there is one, never on the common start's card; a window without focus, that clicks go through and that no recording sees, brought back in front if a VPX window goes over it ([app/src/game-notice.ts](../app/src/game-notice.ts), `line`; `GAME_NOTICE_LINE`). During the game with friends itself, the band keeps what it shows (who finished, who left); an invitation cannot arrive then (the cabinet answers "busy"), a friend coming online can. Back in the menu, results not yet seen open by themselves.

**Invitation endings, on both cabinets**. Every invitation ending is told to both cabinets, and their screens adapt: accepted, declined (with its reason: not this table, not the same version, busy), cancelled by the host, game started without the guest, table changed, expired (two minutes), a friend gone offline (after the presence tolerance). On the host's side, the room puts at the top each answer of the last 30 seconds, "Bruno a refusé votre invitation" (Bruno declined your invitation) · "Sa place est libérée : invitez un autre ami, ou changez de table" (Their seat is free: invite another friend, or change table) (or "Bruno a accepté votre invitation", "Bruno est passé hors ligne : invitation retirée", "Pas de réponse de Bruno en deux minutes"), with the ding, even with the room open; elsewhere, the notification says it. On the guest's side, the decision screen turns to "Invitation annulée par Bruno" (Invitation cancelled by Bruno) (or "Bruno a lancé la partie sans vous", "Invitation expirée", "Bruno est passé hors ligne", "Bruno a changé de table" with "Voir la nouvelle invitation", See the new invitation), with "Retour" (Back); the invitation notification already shown leaves, and another one says the ending ("Bruno a annulé son invitation", ding) when the screen is not open; during a game, the card on the playfield closes and the line says it. A lost message leaves no one waiting: the guest's answer goes again at each resend of the invitation (every 10 s); the ending told by the host (`game-cancel` and its reason, `why`) goes again every 3 s until its acknowledgement (`game-cancel-ok`), two minutes at most, and waits for an offline guest to come back; an acceptance arriving too late gets that ending back (`lateAnswerEnd`), rather than waiting for a start that will never come. Each also notices expiry and the other going offline on its own. An older cabinet still understands the cancellation (without a reason). Invited again after a refusal, in the same room, it is a new invitation: its number (`round`) is in its notification's key. The log tells each ending ("invitation de Bruno (« … ») terminée : cancelled", "fin de l'invitation dite à Poste (expired)", "reçue par Poste", "a répondu « decline »"). Tests: [fins-d-invitation.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/fins-d-invitation.test.ts) (the real service on two cabinets and a test network that loses a message: every ending reaches both sides).

**Reliable shared start, tables without live tracking.** On an unstable connection, "Go!" could stay 90 s on one cabinet and forever on the other, and no score was counted. The waiting card now leaves by itself: "Go!" fades 4 s after it appeared, and no card stays more than 2 min 20 s, even when no message arrives any more (`liveWaitCardEnd`); the service also carries the end of the wait to the screen, on the next tick (`live-game.ts`). A start message lost during a cut is sent again: the host sends the room again (including "go") every 3 s during the game, and at once to a guest back online; the guest sends its state again ("ready", its finished game) as long as the host's room does not show it (`liveStatusBehind`); a lost start message is caught up by the room, which gives the time left (`startIn`). A table without live tracking of the game state (script outside the common base, ROM whose map is missing, VPX inspector impossible) has no automatic start, no live score, and no game over seen by Cyber Pinball: this is said before the game, when choosing the table ("Manual start"), in the room and in the invitation, with the reason (`liveStateSupport`, the same computation as the double); it stays playable. On the table, the card says to press Start at "Go!" and to quit the table when the game is over. The score counts if it entered the table's high score table (read in the double when it closes); otherwise the results say "Score not read: table without live tracking, and the score did not enter its high score table", without a rank. An error after the game (a refused write) no longer loses the result: it is sent to the host anyway. The log gives each start message sent, received or sent again ("Départ commun : …"), each view of the card ("Départ commun, carte : « title — reason »") and why it left ("« Partez ! » lu", "restée trop longtemps", "plus d'attente", "fin de la partie").

**Games in a row, original tables.** A game on Terminator 2, then on The Goonies and The Matrix, could stay on "Loading table…" forever for both players. These original tables (common base) set the player, the number of players and the scores only at Start: before, live tracking wrote them empty (`joueurs=;joueur=;…;score=;pret=1`), the line could not be read, and "ready" was never read; nobody being ready, the delay for the last ones never started. Live tracking now always writes numbers (`CyberPinball_N`, 0 as long as the script has set nothing), and reading it accepts those empty values before the game (`parseLiveState`), never during it. Safeguard: a table on screen for 20 s with no "ready" from live tracking says it is ready anyway, and the log says why ("Départ commun (…) : table à l'écran depuis 20 s, le relevé ne dit pas « prêt » (its last line) : prête quand même"); live tracking keeps its three seconds before the credit and Start. A wait where nobody says ready also ends: "go" at the latest 1 min 50 s after the launch. Everything the service keeps about a game (state sent, resends, manual start, "go" carried to each player) is stored under its identifier and dies with it; the planned launch only applies to its own game, and a start, a room or a state from another game is ignored. Before the start too, nothing depends on a single message: the host sends the invitation again every 10 s as long as the guest has not answered; an invitation already received does not ring twice, and one already answered sends the same answer again (each intended invitation has its number, `round`: a friend invited again after a refusal sees the new one). The log gives each invitation received, sent again, or refused automatically (« déjà dans la partie … »).

**VPX stopped mid-game, FlexDMD DMD.** During a game with friends on The Goonies, VPX stopped during play: "La partie n'a pas pu se dérouler" (The game couldn't run), "VPX exited with code 3221226356" (0xC0000374, heap corruption), after "Actor Ball not found in children of" and "Runtime error on line 9781, col 2: Objet requis: 'FlexDMD.Stage.GetLabel(...)'". The cause was Cyber Pinball: to put the friends' scores on the DMD score screen, it read the DMD through the VPX inspector. The inspector's server runs on its own thread and asks for a frame every 10 ms; for FlexDMD, each frame asked draws the whole scene (`GetRenderFrame` → `FlexDMD::Render`, update and drawing of the elements), on that thread, while the table's script changes the same scene on its own, with no lock (`LockRenderThread` is only a counter; VPX 10.8.1 sources, commit af26b2d93). The scene gets damaged, the script no longer finds its element, VPX closes. The inspector now only reads PinMAME's DMD, whose frame is already computed by the emulation (`inspectorDisplayReadable`): a table without PinMAME no longer turns the inspector on for the DMD, and a FlexDMD or UltraDMD display found in the tree is never read (the log says so; the board stays on the backglass). Neither live tracking (added at the end of the script, timer of a table element), nor the PuP-Pack switches, nor the PupCapture recognition (reading `FlexDMD.DmdPixels` in the script's thread) draw the scene from another thread. For any table, a VPX stop is now read in the end of its log (`diagnoseVpxFailure`): the script error (runtime or compile, line, column, message, the faulty line quoted from the table's script, the FlexDMD element not found just before) and the Windows exit code named (0xC0000374, 0xC0000005…). The failure screen gives that reason ("The table's script stopped", "VPX stopped during the game") and, nothing being missing, Start plays the table again instead of opening "What's missing to play"; in a game with friends, Start opens the game, which the others carry on, and the score counts up to the stop. Cyber Pinball's log keeps one line of it: « VPX arrêté (file) : erreur du script ligne …, VPX fermé (0x…) ». A player whose VPX stops before 20 s of play shows as "VPX stopped during their game", distinct from a game that never started.

**Game history.** System menu > Friends > "Historique des parties" (Game history): every live game finished on this cabinet, newest first, grouped by day ("Vendredi 30 octobre 2026", Friday 30 October 2026); for each, the table as the title, the start time, and below it the players with their rank and score ("1. Bruno 2 841 350 120 · 2. Alexis (vous) 1 000 000 · Chloé 9 999, hors classement", 1. Bruno … · 2. Alexis (you) … · Chloé 9,999, not ranked). A game you won carries a star. Choosing a game opens its details: the table version and its number of balls, the host, then each player, their rank and score ("Meilleur score", Best score), why they were not ranked (host's settings not received, different number of balls, more than one player on their table), the place taken on the table's high score board and the time played; without a score, "Score non disponible" (Score not available) and what happened to them ("La partie n'a pas démarré de son côté", The game did not start on their side; "A quitté la partie", Left the game; "Résultat pas arrivé à temps", Result did not arrive in time; or, for a cabinet that is behind, "Cyber Pinball 0.1.5-essai.10 sur sa borne : ni départ automatique ni score en direct, à mettre à jour", Cyber Pinball 0.1.5-essai.10 on their cabinet: no automatic start or live score, to update: each cabinet's version is kept). Back returns to the list, the cursor on that game. Each cabinet keeps the games it played, as host or guest, once each, in `<données>\amis\parties.json`; games from before this version are not there ([gamehistory.ts](../packages/core/src/gamehistory.ts), [app/src/game-history-menu.ts](../app/src/game-history-menu.ts)). The format prepares the rankings to come (group of friends, then national and worldwide) without building them: each player is identified by their cabinet's public key (their name is only for display), the exact table version is there (same `.vpx`, same script: only those scores compare), each score says whether it counted and why, and a format number (`v`) allows adding fields without losing the games kept; a format newer than the cabinet's is shown, never rewritten. An unreadable file is never overwritten: it is set aside (`parties.illisible-<date>.json`), the history starts empty again and the log says so; the same for `records.json`, the group board ([historique-des-parties.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/historique-des-parties.test.ts)).

**What others see.** Everyone: the name and tag. Friends: presence, the table being played, high scores, badges, games, and the library if it is shared (its tables, their parts and their media; a ROM or a PuP-Pack only if the friend ticks them, attesting they have the right to). Friends and players with a pending friend request: the avatar (never the directory, where others see the initials). Connected cabinets see the IP address, as in any peer-to-peer exchange.

**Tests.** Never the real network during a test: `socialNetMode` turns the network off as soon as a test variable is set (autopilot, captures, stress, `CYBERPINBALL_HOME` sandbox, simulated screens), except `CYBERPINBALL_RESEAU=simule`: an in-process HyperDHT test network and an in-memory local network, on 127.0.0.1 only, with a simulated firewall rule. `CYBERPINBALL_RESEAU=non` also turns it off in normal use.

Code: decisions in [packages/core/src/social.ts](../packages/core/src/social.ts) (profile, messages, directory, friendship, events), [friendlibrary.ts](../packages/core/src/friendlibrary.ts) (shared library, file messages) and [livegame.ts](../packages/core/src/livegame.ts) (invitation, room, start, results), transport in `app/src/social-net.ts` (the only module that opens sockets), services in `app/src/friend-content.ts` and `app/src/live-game.ts`, screens in `app/src/friends-menu.ts`, `app/src/friend-library-menu.ts` and `app/src/live-game-menu.ts`. A player's name always follows their most recent signed profile, wherever it comes from (hello, relayed directory, beacon), never an older one (`learnProfiles`, `renamePeer`). Proofs: [social.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/social.test.ts) (names, signatures, trapped messages, directory, friendship rules on both sides, badges, network mode), [social-net.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/social-net.test.ts) (two cabinets on a test network: directory, request, acceptance even with a cabinet off, presence, high score, removal, local network without the Internet, blocking; local network down at start, the Internet running anyway and the local network retried; beacon that does not throw on a refused or missing adapter; no socket outside 127.0.0.1; no other module loads Hyperswarm, `net` or `dgram`), [firewall.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/firewall.test.ts) (netsh output in every language, same rule in the installer, never during an update, silently or in a sandbox), [friend-services.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/friend-services.test.ts) (library shared by default and refused once turned off, media received identical to the byte, unknown id refused, whole table part by part with no saves, program or untaken ROM, trapped path refused, live game from invitation to results), [friends-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/friends-menu.test.ts) (each screen, sharing by default, each part of a table leading to its own action and the ROM unticked, countdown cursor, invitation greyed out with its reason and "Jouer avec un ami"), [invitation-en-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/invitation-en-partie.test.ts) (invitation received during a game: kept, never "busy"; card then pill, playfield corner in all four orientations, window without focus or capture, opened on return to the menu).

**Limits.** The score during the game is not read (VPX only records it at the end): a live game only compares high scores entered on the table's high score table and play times. One file at a time per friend; an interrupted transfer resumes where it stopped as soon as the friend is back (see "Download manager"). The directory only knows the cabinets seen since sharing started; two players must be on at the same time to find each other, and behind two symmetric NATs (CGNAT, 4G tethering) with no third cabinet to relay, the connection can fail. Adding the firewall rule for real (UAC) has only been tried in principle, never on the development machine's real firewall.

### Choosing the table of a live game

The table choice takes the guests into account **before** the invitation (`packages/core/src/livechoice.ts`).

- **First, "Même version chez tous" (Same version for everyone)**: the tables each guest has in the strictly identical version, judged exactly as at acceptance (`matchLiveTable`): the same `.vpx` (SHA-256 fingerprint) and the same played script. The detail says with whom: "Même version chez Bruno" (Same version at Bruno's).
- **"À vérifier" (To be checked)**: a cabinet that does not tell its versions is flagged, not refused; the table can still be chosen, the check at acceptance decides. Reasons: "Bibliothèque de Bruno inconnue (ancienne version de Cyber Pinball)" (Bruno's library unknown, older version of Cyber Pinball), "… (il ne la partage pas)" (not shared), "Bibliothèque de Bruno demandée à sa borne…" (requested from their cabinet), "… injoignable pour l'instant" (unreachable for now), "Version de Bruno en cours de vérification…" (a fingerprint being computed, at their end or here).
- **"Indisponibles" (Unavailable)**, greyed out, never hidden, with who blocks: "Autre version chez Bruno" (Other version at Bruno's, or "Même table, autre script chez Bruno", same table, other script), then "Bruno n'a pas cette table" (Bruno does not have this table). Start on another version opens the parts of their table in their shared library, the table ticked by default ("Start : récupérer sa version chez Bruno", Start: get Bruno's version); on a missing table, nothing.
- **Several guests**: a table is playable if it is for all of them; the reason names each of those who block. You get there through "Changer de table" (Change table) in the room (the guests get a new invitation, the old room is cancelled and they are told) and through "Autre table, mêmes joueurs" (Another table, same players) on the results.
- **Choosing the friend for a table** ("Jouer avec un ami", "Inviter un autre ami" in the room) follows the same rule: those who have the same version first, the others greyed out with their reason.

**What the friend's cabinet says about its tables.** Their shared library carries, for each table, the size of the `.vpx` and the `versions` announcement: their cabinet can give their strict version. The inviter asks (`table-versions-get`) for the version of only the tables that may be theirs, the same game or a `.vpx` of the same size (the same file under another name); the friend's cabinet computes the fingerprint of those only, once per file (`<data>\amis\empreintes.json`), and answers table by table (`table-versions`). A cabinet running an older version does not announce `versions` and ignores the request: its tables go to "À vérifier". The check at acceptance remains the safety net: a table changed in the meantime is refused there, as before.

### Player avatars

Everyone customises an avatar, visible wherever their name is shown. For now it is always a picture of one's own: a photo or a picture taken from the disk, or in the built-in browser, cropped with the cabinet buttons ([avatar.ts](../packages/core/src/avatar.ts), screen `app/src/avatar-menu.ts`).

- **Where it shows.** "Mes amis" (My friends) (online, the badge's ring is green; offline, it dims), a friend's sheet (in front of their name), "Trouver un joueur" (Find a player) and the results, "Demandes d'amis" (Friend requests), "Depuis un ami" (From a friend), "Partage et réseau" (Sharing and network) (yours), the game history (each game's players as small badges, then each one in front of their rank), the lobby, the invitation and the results of a live game, the invitation card and the score band during the game, and notifications that are about a single player ("Bruno vient de se connecter", Bruno has just connected). Without a picture: the initials ("AM" for "Alexis Martin") on a dark hue taken from the key, the same on every cabinet. The score insert at the bottom of the playfield, too small for readable initials, keeps the hue only.
- **Choosing the picture.** System menu > Friends > "Mon avatar" (My avatar) (or "Partage et réseau" > "Changer d'avatar…", Change avatar…). "Choisir une image sur ce PC…" (Choose a picture on this PC…) opens Cyber Pinball's file picker (.jpg, .jpeg, .png, .webp, .gif, .bmp, .avif, 40 MB at most). "Chercher une image sur le web…" (Search the web for a picture…) opens the built-in browser on an image search: right-click a picture, "Télécharger cette image" (Download this image) (offered on every free search, media included, since image search engines have no download link), and the browser closes onto the cropping screen.
- **Working picture.** The chosen picture is reduced to 1,600 pixels per side at most and redrawn as JPEG by Chromium's engine, in the hidden conversion page (`media-convert.ts`, `fitJpeg`): a photo loses its metadata there (EXIF, GPS position) and follows its orientation. It stays in `<data>\avatars\recadrage\` with its framing (`cadrage.json`): "Recadrer mon avatar" (Crop my avatar) goes back to it, even after a restart; without a working picture, the entry stays visible, greyed out, "Indisponible : choisissez d'abord une image" (Unavailable: choose a picture first).
- **Cropping with the buttons.** The whole picture, the kept square on top (the outside darkened, a green line, the circle the friends will see), and on the right the preview, large and at list size. The flippers choose a move, Start applies it, as many times as needed, the cursor staying on that move: "Valider ce cadrage" (Use this framing), "Zoom avant" (Zoom in), "Zoom arrière" (Zoom out) (one step: a quarter), "Déplacer vers la gauche" (Move left), "… la droite" (right), "… le haut" (up), "… le bas" (down) (one step: a tenth of the square's side), "Recentrer" (Recenter), "Choisir une autre image" (Choose another picture), "Annuler" (Cancel). The square never leaves the picture; at its closest, a tenth of its short side ("Zoom avant" greyed out, "Au plus près", As close as it gets); at its widest, the whole height or width ("Zoom arrière" greyed out). At the start, the largest square, centred; a portrait takes it a little higher, where the face is. With the mouse: drag the frame (it follows the pointer, the move is sent on release), the wheel zooms.
- **Saving.** "Valider ce cadrage" cuts the square into a 256-pixel JPEG (`cropJpeg`, quality 0.86, redone at 0.72 then 0.55 if it exceeds 96 KB), stored under its digest: `<data>\avatars\<sha256>.jpg`. The name changes with the content: nothing to invalidate in the renderer's cache, and two players with the same picture keep it only once. `social.json` keeps the file name (`avatar`), and the friends' ones (`peerAvatars`, by key). Windows read it through the `cyber-media://` protocol, whose folders include `avatars` (the windows laid over the game allow it in their security policy).
- **Exchange between cabinets.** The hello of each link carries the avatar's digest (absent: no avatar; a cabinet from before avatars sends none and counts as having no avatar). A change is announced at once (`avatar`) to connected cabinets. Only friends and players with a pending request, in either direction, ask for it (`avatar-get`) and receive it (`avatar-data`, in base64): the cabinet only answers them, and only for its current avatar. On receipt, the picture must have the announced digest, be a JPEG, PNG or WebP recognised by its bytes (never by its name), weigh 96 KB at most and measure 1,024 pixels per side at most, read from its header without decoding it: otherwise nothing is written. An unanswered request is repeated at most every 30 s.
- **Tidying.** A friend removed, a request declined or cancelled, a player blocked: their avatar leaves `social.json` and its file the folder; a changed avatar replaces the old one. Only files named like an avatar are touched, never the `recadrage` folder.

**Tests.** [avatar.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/avatar.test.ts): type and dimensions read from the bytes, refusals (too heavy, too large, other digest, anything but base64), bounded framing, initials and hue, folder tidying, state read back, sharing rule. [social-net.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/social-net.test.ts): three cabinets on a test network (127.0.0.1); the avatar arrives with a friend request, not at the player who only knows the directory; changed, the old file goes; a declined request forgets it; removed, the initials come back.

**Limits.** A picture of one's own only: no ready-made avatars to choose from yet. No round cropping: the square is sent, the circle is drawn by each cabinet. An animated picture (GIF) becomes still.

## Setup wizard and configuration menu

The setup wizard (`app/src/wizard.ts`, screens `app/renderer/screen.*`) runs at first start, or through "Assistant complet" (Full setup wizard), and goes through: **buttons** (capturing each button), **analog plunger** (sensor plunger, accelerometer), **tilt** (cabinet tilt bob or tilt bob simulated by VPX), **screens and roles** (playfield, backglass, DMD, topper), **playfield orientation and size** (flipper edge, flipping the screen in Windows if VPX needs it, diagonal), **screen layout** (zones), **sound** (audio outputs, speaker test), then the summary and the writing of `cabinet.json` and `VPinballX.ini`. The list of steps, what each one decides and its keys are in [packages/core/src/wizardsteps.ts](../packages/core/src/wizardsteps.ts) (`WIZARD_PHASES`, `WIZARD_STEPS`), tested in [wizardsteps.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/wizardsteps.test.ts).

**System menu (Exit, Tab or ⚙) > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration)** gathers everything that sets up the cabinet. Each step can be redone there on its own and returns to the table menu, saved or not:

| Entry | Option | What is redone | `VPinballX.ini` keys written |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| "Boutons du jeu" (Game buttons) | `--boutons` | game button capture and test | `[Input]` |
| "Boutons de l'interface" (Interface buttons) | `--interface` | capture and test of the buttons that drive Cyber Pinball | none |
| "Lanceur analogique" (Analog plunger) | `--lanceur` | sensor plunger detection, direction, dead zone, speed, accelerometer, test | sensor `[Input]` keys |
| "Tilt" | `--tilt` | cabinet tilt bob or tilt bob simulated by VPX, contact angle, nudge test | `[Player] SimulatedPlumb`, `PlumbThresholdAngle` |
| "Écrans et rôles" (Screens and roles) | `--ecrans` | the screen of each role; flipper edge and diagonal only if the playfield moves to another screen | VPX windows and framing |
| "Orientation et taille du playfield" (Playfield orientation and size) | `--playfield` | flipper edge, screen flip, diagonal | VPX windows and framing |
| "Disposition des écrans" (Screen layout) | `--disposition` | zone of each role on its screen | VPX windows and framing |
| "Son de la borne" (Cabinet sound) | `--son` | backglass and playfield outputs, speaker test | `SoundDevice`, `SoundDeviceBG`, `Sound3D` |
| "Assistant complet" | | all the steps in a row | all managed keys |
| "Touches par défaut des encodeurs" (Default encoder keys) | | immediate reset of both button sets, no wizard | `[Input]` |
| "Configurations de la borne" (Cabinet configurations) | | choose, rename, trash, new configuration | |

A single step starts from the saved model (roles, screens, flipper edge), changes only its own fields in `cabinet.json` (`stepCabinet`) and writes only its own `VPinballX.ini` keys (the `only` option of the sync), even ones changed by hand; the other keys stay as they are until the next sync from the menu. The summary shows only those keys; Exit there restarts the step, Backspace goes back to the last question. Windows and framing: `[Player] Playfield*`, `[Backglass]`, `[ScoreView]`, `[Topper]`, `BGSet`, `ScreenWidth`, `ScreenHeight`, `CabinetAutofitMode`.

**Leaving without saving, at any step** (layout editor, flip countdown and summary included): the "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Leave without saving?) question opens with

- the **"Quitter sans enregistrer"** (Leave without saving) button, always shown at the top right of every screen (also clickable in the layout editor);
- **Ctrl+Q** on a keyboard; Ctrl+Q again answers yes;
- **Esc or Exit held for 2 s** (the button shows "Maintenu : quitter sans enregistrer ? dans 1 s" (Held: leave without saving? in 1 s));
- **during button capture, any key held for 3 s**, which is not captured. At that point the wizard does not yet know which key is Exit: this is the only gesture that works with any encoder. So that it does not get in the way of the capture, a button is captured **on release**: a press on a cabinet button lasts well under a second, the difference is invisible, and a held key is never captured (nothing to undo). Outside button capture, only a held Esc or Exit opens the question: held flippers keep their effect (speeding up a setting in the editor);
- **Exit (or Backspace) on the first question** of a step, when there is nothing before it: the playfield in the full wizard, the flipper edge in orientation alone, the first output in sound alone, the editor in layout alone.

The question says what would be lost ("Boutons : Flipper gauche sur A" (Buttons: Left flipper on A), "DMD : …", "Flippers à gauche de l'image" (Flippers on the left of the image), "Son…" (Sound…), "Écran du playfield : remis dans son orientation d'origine" (Playfield screen: restored to its original orientation), or "Rien n'a été changé" (Nothing was changed)) and where you will end up. **Start (or Enter): yes** · **Exit, Esc or Backspace: no** · the same gesture again (a key held again): yes · with no answer within 20 s, the wizard resumes. During button capture, the Start and Exit already captured also answer. Yes: nothing is written, neither `cabinet.json` nor `VPinballX.ini`; an unconfirmed screen orientation is restored; back to the table menu or, on first start without a model (the menu cannot open), Cyber Pinball closes, as announced on screen ("l'assistant reprendra au prochain démarrage" (the wizard will resume at next start)). A test pins the invariant: the question comes before any step in `handleKey`, the mouse before any other action, every state sent to the screens carries the button, and nothing is written outside the two save functions.

**"Placer librement…"** (Place freely…): at the screens step, after the whole screens and the ready-made spots (bottom of the backglass in 4:1 or 16:9, inset in the playfield), the backglass, DMD and topper offer "Placer librement…". Start opens the layout editor on that role, placed on its saved screen (otherwise a free screen, otherwise the backglass screen), with the cursor on the "Zone" line; "Revenir à l'assistant" (Back to the wizard) keeps the chosen spot and moves on to the next role, Exit returns to the question as it was.

## Interface language

Everything Cyber Pinball displays exists in French and in English: table menu and game pages, system menu, setup wizard, VPX settings, the "Get" window, and the messages and findings of the core (doctor, synchronisation, import, update). Dates and numbers follow the language (`fr-FR` or `en-GB`). Console logs, test reports and files written for other tools stay in French.

- **By default**: the Windows language if it is French, English otherwise (`app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()`).
- **System menu > "Cyber Pinball" > "Language / Langue"**: the entry is named in both languages, so it can be found whatever language is shown, and each language is offered in its own words ("Français", "English"). The choice, made with the cabinet buttons like everything else, is kept in `<data>\language.json` (`{ "language": "en" }`); the menu restarts at once through the screen guard (`relaunchCovered`), as for a change of configuration, and reopens its windows in the new language.
- **`CYBERPINBALL_LANGUE=fr|en`** forces the language (tests, screenshots); a choice made in the menu also replaces it for the restart.

**Method** (`packages/core/src/i18n.ts`): every displayed text goes through `t('texte français', { parameter })`, its French form being the key; the English catalogue, split by part of the interface in `packages/core/src/i18n/en/*.ts`, gives its translation. Parameters go in braces (`t('{role} : aucun écran', { role })`), plurals through `tn(n, '{n} table', '{n} tables')` (0 and 1 are singular in French, only 1 in English), lists through `formatList` ("a, b et c" / "a, b and c"), dates and numbers through `formatDate`, `formatDateTime`, `formatNumber`. The language is set once per process, at start-up (`app/src/language.ts`, imported by `main.ts` before the modules that display anything): no text is translated while a module loads, and the label tables of the core (`ROLES`, `BUTTONS`, `WIZARD_STEPS`…) are getters (`get label()`). Windows receive the language and the catalogue through their preload (`cyberI18n`); `app/renderer/i18n.js`, loaded before the other scripts of each page, gives them the same `t`, `tn` and `uiLocale()`, and translates the fixed texts of the pages, marked `data-i18n` (with a context key, `data-i18n="…"`, when one French text has two meanings).

**Invariant** (`packages/core/src/test/i18n.test.ts`, run by `npm test`): the scan of the interface sources (`i18n-scan.ts`: `app/src`, `app/renderer`, `packages/core/src`, `packages/installer/src`, `packages/win-displays/src`) collects every key passed to `t()`, `tn()` and `data-i18n`, and fails on a key without translation, a key that is not a literal (`${…}` template, variable), different `{parameters}` between a key and its translation, one key translated in two ways, a translation left in French, a key nothing uses any more, and a text that looks French outside `t()` (accents, common words; comments and console logs excepted). A text that must not be translated (file name, search pattern, proper noun) carries `// i18n-ignore` at the end of its line. The test also checks that no text is translated while the core modules load. By hand, on a few files:

```bash
npx tsc -b packages/core && node packages/core/dist/test/i18n-scan.js app/src/wizard.ts app/renderer/screen.js
```

At run time, a text requested without a translation is shown in French and the console reports it once ("Traduction manquante : …").

## Menu icons

Every menu entry carries an icon of its own, and every screen opened from the system menu shows its entry's icon before its title: you can tell at a glance where you are. The icons were drawn for Cyber Pinball, with no icon font and no online resource.

- **A single registry**: [app/renderer/icons.js](../app/renderer/icons.js) draws each icon once (`CYBER_ICONS`, SVG geometry only on a 24 grid) and `cyberIcon(name)` places it in the page. Every window loads it before its scripts; the design system reads it too (`docs/design-system/src/icones.mjs`, "Icônes" sheet). The names known to the code are in [packages/core/src/icons.ts](../packages/core/src/icons.ts) (`ICON_NAMES`, `IconName` type).
- **Declarative**: an entry picks its icon with an `icon` field — required on `SystemItem` (system menu), optional on `Action`, `Row` and `Built` (screen title) in `app/src/library.ts`; `WizardStep.icon` for the steps of "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet setup), `EssentialGroup.icon` for the groups of the VPX settings. A screen opened from the system menu that declares no icon takes its entry's (catalogue, adding a table, ROMs, media), except a table's screens (game page, doctor…), titled with the game's name.
- **Drawing and sizes**: a 2-unit stroke for the whole family (`theme.css`, `.icon`), in the text color. For a 1,080 px short side: 30 px before a menu entry, 24 px from the label and aligned on its first line; 60 px before a title; 38 px for the gear (72 px click area); 22 to 26 px in the tabs and groups of the settings; never under 20 px.
- **States**: the icon follows its label (dark on the green bar, red at rest for a destructive action, dark on the red bar once selected); the "waiting for the user" dot sits at the top right of the icon, as on the gear.
- **No icon** before a value, a choice in a list, a table or a configuration: only before what opens or does something. "Retour" (Back) carries the `back` arrow in menus whose entries have icons.

**Adding an icon**: draw it in the right family of `icons.js` (`path`, `circle`, `rect`… elements and their geometry attributes only, live area 3 to 21), add its name to `ICON_NAMES`, declare it on the entry, rebuild the design system (`node docs/design-system/src/construire.mjs`) and look at it in the sheet, at 22, 30 and 60 px.

**Invariants**: [icons.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/icons.test.ts) (run by `npm test`) checks that the registry and `ICON_NAMES` have exactly the same names, that each drawing is geometry only within the 24 frame, that no two names draw the same icon, that every page drawing an icon loads the registry before its scripts, that no screen draws an SVG itself, and that the entries of "Configuration de la borne" and the settings groups each have their own. On screen, the layout guard (`app/src/layout-check.ts`) reports an unknown icon, one smaller than 20 px, or the same icon for two entries of a menu (`data-icon-menu`).

## Cabinet buttons

**Two independent sets of buttons.** The **game** buttons (flippers, Start, Exit, coin, plunger, MagnaSaves, Extra Ball, pause, tilt, nudges, volume) are what VPX receives during a game: `cabinet.json` > `buttons`, written to `[Input] Mapping.*`. The **interface** buttons (Previous, Next, Confirm, Back and menu; optional: Cheat sheet (Aide-mémoire), VPX settings) drive Cyber Pinball: table menu, details screens, VPX settings, wizard, warnings; `cabinet.json` > `navigation`, which VPX does not read. Each set is captured, summarised and tested on its own, through the same screen and functions (`KeySet`: `GAME_KEYS`, `INTERFACE_KEYS`; `recordKey`, `keyProblems`, `keysToTry`, `packages/core/src/buttons.ts`); keyboard key or joystick button, freely from one button to the next, and one physical button can serve both sets (each set only refuses its own duplicates).

- **By default**, the interface is on the same physical buttons as the game flippers, Start and Exit (`defaultNavigation`). A cabinet saved before the interface buttons existed (no `navigation`) therefore keeps its navigation on its game buttons, with nothing to redo (`navigationButtons`, soft migration). Once the interface buttons are saved, capturing the game differently no longer changes them.
- **Keyboard fallback keys**, always active: Tab (menu and configuration), left / up arrows (previous), right / down (next), Enter (confirm), Esc (back). An interface button cannot take another role's fallback key ("Entrée reste la touche de secours « Valider »", Enter stays the "Confirm" fallback key, `refusedNavigationKey`). H and F1 (cheat sheet) and R (VPX settings) stay active as long as no cabinet button, game or interface, sends them (`interfaceKeys`, `withoutButtonKeys`).
- **The whole interface reads its own buttons**: menu (`interfaceKeys`), VPX settings, "other frontend open" warning, wizard (`navigationKeys`), always given the whole cabinet (`ButtonsSource`), never the game buttons alone (invariant test in `buttons.test.ts`). The "During a game" cheat sheet and the in-game display adjustment stay the game's (`gameKeys`, `displayKeys`).
- **Inconsistent capture** of the interface buttons (`navigationProblems`): the interface falls back to the game flippers, Start and Exit, and the menu offers at startup to redo the step ("Boutons de l'interface à refaire", Interface buttons need redoing; Start: `--interface`).
