This is the first step of the wizard, since everything else is driven with the buttons: the screen asks "Appuyez sur le bouton" (Press the button) for left flipper, right flipper, Start, Exit, coin, plunger, then the optional buttons (left and right MagnaSave, Extra Ball, Pause, tilt bob, slam tilt, nudges to the left, to the right and forward, volume − and +, mute), which Exit skips. The same question is shown on every screen (upright for the player on the previous playfield), above the list of all buttons: those already captured with their key, the current one, those still to come, so you can decide in advance which cabinet button will play which role. A key already captured for another button is refused, as are the Windows key and keys VPX cannot read; a button counts when it is released, only once, and a key held for 3 s opens "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Leave without saving?) instead of being captured.

**Full wizard**: the game buttons first, then the interface buttons, offered on the same buttons as the flippers, Start and Exit just captured: "Valider" (Confirm, Start) keeps them without a test; one button can be redone on its own, or "Tout relever" (Capture all) asks for them one by one (Previous, Next, Confirm, Back and menu, then Cheat sheet and VPX settings, which Back skips). On the interface summary, Previous, Next, Confirm and Back whose key changed must have been pressed before Confirm; on the game summary, the flippers, Start and Exit. The interface keys in effect, the arrows and Enter also choose and run on both summaries, without counting as a test. With no answer on the first step (20 s), both sets keep their keys and the wizard moves on to the screens.

This button-by-button capture is the one for a new cabinet (no keys saved yet); each button still to come shows, dimmed, the key currently in effect, i.e. the encoders' key. On a cabinet that is already configured, and always for the step on its own, the step opens straight on the summary: each button with its current key, nothing to redo. A single button can be changed on its own: click its line, or use the flippers to reach its line (after the actions) then Start; press the new button and you are back at the summary, the other keys kept (Backspace cancels). "Tout relever" (Capture all) starts the full capture over.

A mistake can be fixed without the cabinet buttons, since those are precisely what is being captured: click a line in the list (that button is asked again, then the step resumes at the first unanswered button), Backspace or "◀ Précédent" (◀ Previous) (the previous button; Backspace therefore cannot be captured as a button), "Touches par défaut" (Default keys) and "Tout relever", always shown and clickable (the pointer appears when you move the mouse).

The summary is also a test: the flippers choose the action ("Valider" (Confirm), "Précédent", "Touches par défaut", "Tout relever") or a button to redo, Start runs that choice. A flipper, Start or Exit whose key has just been captured (different from the one in effect) must have been pressed at least once before "Valider" is accepted (check mark in the list, "Essai des touches relevées : Flipper gauche ✓ · …" (Testing captured keys: Left flipper ✓ · …)): a wrong capture therefore cannot be saved without the player noticing. Kept keys and the encoders' default keys are known and need no test: "Touches par défaut" then "Enregistrer" (Save) saves at once. A keyboard's arrows and Enter also choose and run, without counting as a test. "Valider" moves on to the screens (or saves, for the step on its own). In the full wizard, if nobody presses anything for 20 seconds, the current keys are kept (the encoders' keys on a new cabinet): an unattended cabinet thus moves on to the screens as before.

On their own: system menu (Exit) > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Boutons du jeu" (Game buttons; or "Boutons de l'interface", Interface buttons, `npm run assistant -- --interface`, which writes nothing to `VPinballX.ini`: "VPinballX.ini inchangé : VPX ne lit pas les boutons de l'interface."; in that step, "Touches par défaut" gives back the game flippers, Start and Exit in effect), `npm run assistant -- --boutons`, the launcher's `boutons` action, or the "Affecter les boutons de la borne" (Assign the cabinet buttons) action of a key in the VPX settings. The rest of the model is kept; only the `[Input]` keys are taken over.

**Access to configuration without the buttons**: a wrong capture (for example Exit on a button you can no longer find) must never lock you out of the settings. From the table menu and its messages, a discreet gear at the top right (⚙, clickable on top of everything, more visible when you move the mouse) and a keyboard's Tab key open the system menu and its sections (Tables, Médias, Borne, Cyber Pinball): add a table, VPX settings, cabinet configuration (buttons, screens, playfield, layout, sound, full wizard, default encoder keys, configurations), ROM library, quit. In these menus, Tab and Shift+Tab move the cursor whatever buttons have been captured, and every line can be clicked. Tab and Backspace are reserved: the "Boutons" (Buttons) step refuses them, and a capture containing one is treated as inconsistent (default keys).

**Default encoder keys** (left / right Shift, 1, Esc, 5, Enter…), the fallback when a saved capture no longer drives the cabinet: system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" > "Touches par défaut des encodeurs" (Default encoder keys) (with the mouse: click "Exit : menu" (Exit: menu) at the foot of the menu, then the lines), "Remettre les touches par défaut des encodeurs" (Restore the default encoder keys) in the details of a key in the VPX settings ("Avancé" (Advanced) > Input), and, without any button, the launcher's `boutons-defaut` action (`node app/dist/sync-cli.js --boutons-par-defaut`). `cabinet.json` goes back to the default keys, for both sets (the interface on the encoders' flippers, Start and Exit), and `[Input]` is rewritten: VPX actions that had lost their key get it back (`withDefaultButtons`, `app/src/buttons-reset.ts`).

**Inconsistent capture** (required button with no key, the same key for two buttons, a refused key, for example after editing `cabinet.json` by hand): `buttonProblems` detects it, the whole interface falls back to the default keys (`buttonKeys`), the sync stops writing `[Input]` and reports it, and the menu offers at startup to redo the "Boutons" step.

The keys are stored in `cabinet.json` (`buttons` for the game, `navigation` for the interface, `KeyboardEvent.code` codes: `ShiftLeft`, `Digit1`… or joystick tokens): the interface reads `navigation` (or, without it, the flippers, Start and Exit of `buttons`) in the wizard, menu, details screen, table management and VPX settings (`navigationKeys`, `interfaceKeys`, `packages/core/src/buttons.ts`); the in-game key reminder (`gameKeys`) and `[Input]` read `buttons`. A model without `buttons` keeps the default keys and leaves `[Input]` alone.

**What VPX expects** (read from `src/input/InputManager.cpp` and `InputAction.cpp` at commit `af26b2d93` of 10.8.1-5436): VPX no longer stores DirectInput codes (`LFlipKey`, `JoyLFlipKey`… no longer exist). Each action has its own `[Input] Mapping.<action>` key, a list of alternatives separated by ` | `, each one a combination separated by ` & `; a keyboard key is written `Key;<scancode SDL>` (HID usage: A = 4, 1 = 30, Enter = 40, left Shift = 225), a gamepad button `SDLJoy_<GUID SDL>_<n>;<bouton>`. An empty value means VPX's default key. Cyber Pinball translates `KeyboardEvent.code` into an SDL scancode (`SDL_SCANCODES`) and writes:

| Button | `[Input]` keys (VPX default) |
| --- | --- |
| Left / right flipper | `Mapping.LeftFlipper`, `LeftStagedFlipper`, `UILeft` (`Key;225`) / `RightFlipper`, `RightStagedFlipper`, `UIRight` (`Key;229`) |
| Start, Exit | `Mapping.Start` (`Key;30`), `Mapping.ExitGame` (`Key;41`) |
| Coin, plunger | `Mapping.Credit1` (`Key;34`), `Mapping.LaunchBall` (`Key;40`) |
| Left / right MagnaSave | `Mapping.LeftMagna`, `UIUp` (`Key;224`) / `RightMagna`, `UIDown` (`Key;228`) |
| Extra Ball, Pause, tilt bob | `Mapping.ExtraBall` (`Key;5`), `Mapping.Pause` (`Key;19`), `Mapping.Tilt` (`Key;23`) |
| Slam tilt (coin door) | `Mapping.SlamTilt` (`Key;74`) |
| Nudges | `Mapping.LeftNudge` (`Key;29`), `RightNudge` (`Key;56`), `CenterNudge` (`Key;44`) |
| Volume − / + / Mute | none: `Mapping.VolumeDown` and `Mapping.VolumeUp` get `Key;0`, with or without the "Boutons" (Buttons) step; Cyber Pinball reads these buttons itself (see [Cabinet volume](#cabinet-volume)) |

- A skipped optional button keeps VPX's default key.
- **Pause**: VPX's `Pause` action stops the game, and pressing it again resumes it (`SetPlayState(!IsPlaying())`, `InputManager.cpp`). Up to 0.1.3-essai.6, no cabinet button led to it: only the P key of a plugged-in keyboard paused. A capture made before this button does not have it: P stays the pause key, unless another button took it; redo "Boutons du jeu" (Game buttons) to give it to a cabinet button.
- Only single keyboard keys are replaced: a gamepad button or a combination added in VPX's menu (F12, "Input Settings") stays alongside.
- Another VPX action whose default key is that of a button loses it (`Key;0`, no key: an empty value would give it back): otherwise an Extra Ball button on D would also open the debugger. Duplicates that VPX intends are kept (Service 7 keeps `-`, the default key of Volume −). The action gets its key back when the conflict goes away. The summary and the sync's findings name these actions.
- These keys are managed like the others: a key changed by hand in VPX is reported at startup, kept or reset as you choose (`[Input] Mapping.LeftFlipper = Key;4 au lieu de Key;225`). The VPX settings show them in the "Avancé" tab, Input section, with the names of the action and of the key.
- Table scripts do not see the difference: `LeftFlipperKey`, `StartGameKey`… hold the action's identifier, not the key (`ScriptGlobalTable.cpp`). VPX also sends the script the DirectInput code of each key pressed, which an old table can test directly.

**Joystick buttons (Pinscape on a KL25Z, VirtuaPin, encoder in gamepad mode)**: a Pinscape controller sends its buttons as a USB joystick's by default, not as keyboard keys. Cyber Pinball reads them with VPX's own SDL 3 library (`SDL364.dll` next to `VPinballX64.exe`), loaded through koffi and initialised the way VPX initialises it (`SDL_INIT_JOYSTICK | SDL_INIT_GAMEPAD`, no hints; `src/input/SDLInputHandler.h` at commit `af26b2d93`): same GUIDs, same joystick order, same button numbering. VPX names a joystick `SDLJoy_<GUID SDL>_<rank>`, the rank telling apart two joysticks with the same GUID (`joystickDeviceIds`), and a button by its SDL number, from 0. Polled 100 times a second in the main process (`packages/win-displays/src/joystick.ts`, `app/src/joystick-input.ts`), without SDL's event queue or a message pump that would hijack Electron's.

- **The whole interface**: each press reaches the Cyber Pinball window that has focus as a key, whose code (`KeyboardEvent.code`) is VPX's token for the button (`SDLJoy_…_1;3`). The "Boutons" (Buttons) step captures it like a key ("Bouton 4 du joystick", Joystick button 4, numbered from 1 as in Windows' "Game controllers" and the Pinscape configuration tool), refuses it twice, and the menu, the table page, the VPX settings, the "other frontend open" warning and the "Récupérer" (Fetch) window (Exit) follow it. A held button repeats like a key (500 ms, then 30 times a second): a held flipper scrolls the menu. During a game no Cyber Pinball window has focus: presses go nowhere. During capture, a line says what is read: "Joystick lu : mjrnet Pinscape Controller" (Joystick read), "Aucun joystick branché" (No joystick connected), or "Joysticks lus une fois VPX installé" (Joysticks are read once VPX is installed).
- **Keyboard and joystick mixed**: keyboard keys and joystick buttons are captured along the same path (`recordButton`), freely from one button to the next: flippers on the Pinscape, Start and credit on an encoder in keyboard mode, for instance. Windows sends keyboard keys to the foreground window, whereas Cyber Pinball hands joystick buttons to the window Electron considers active; a window can be active without being in the foreground (a relaunched process that Windows refuses the foreground): on the real cabinet (0.1.3-essai.1), joystick buttons were captured, keyboard keys no longer were. Each joystick button press therefore checks that the foreground belongs to Cyber Pinball and takes it back otherwise (`keyboardFollows`, `app/src/foreground.ts`), with a warning in the console ("Clavier : le premier plan de Windows était à …", Keyboard: the Windows foreground was with …) and, in the "Boutons" step, "clavier repris à « window » (process)" (keyboard taken back from …; "clavier retenu par …", keyboard held by …, if Windows refuses) after the joystick line; the wizard takes it when it opens. Focus moving from one wizard screen to another no longer makes it forget a key being held down.
- **Keyboard key releases**: capture counts a button when it is released, and guest mode follows the buttons held down. Yet cancelling a key press in `before-input-event` also makes Chromium drop its release (`RenderWidgetHostImpl`, `suppress_events_until_keydown_`): without a workaround, no keyboard key was captured (the release never arrived, and the key "held" opened "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Quit without saving?) after 3 s), whereas joystick buttons, handed over without going through Chromium, were captured; guest mode considered a released key as still held. Windows that read releases (wizard, menu) therefore go through `onKeyInput` (`app/src/key-input.ts`): the handled press is not cancelled there, the page cancels it (`app/src/preload.ts`, in the capture phase, after asking the main process whether it handled it), and the release arrives. [buttons.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/buttons.test.ts) rejects any `before-input-event` handler that reads releases itself; [clavier-reel.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/clavier-reel.test.ts) captures, off screen, every game and interface button with real keys (`sendInputEvent`), as well as with SDL's virtual joystick, and checks what is saved.
- **`[Input]`**: each of the button's actions gets the joystick token first, and keeps VPX's keyboard key alongside (`SDLJoy_…_1;0 | Key;225`), unless another cabinet button took it. A new capture removes the joystick buttons written the previous time and keeps those added in F12 > "Input Settings" (`withButtonMapping`). The joystick's two MagnaSaves open the in-game settings: Cyber Pinball reads them itself (`watchJoystickButtons`), and `Mapping.InGameUI` keeps F12 only.
- **Check after each game**: VPX writes the joysticks it sees to `vpinball.log` ("Joystick connected: mjrnet Pinscape Controller #1 (… VPX UID: SDLJoy_…_1)"). If VPX saw joysticks but not the one of the captured buttons (`joysticksUnseenByVpx`), Cyber Pinball and VPX do not name it the same way: the menu says so ("Boutons du joystick introuvables dans VPX", Joystick buttons not found by VPX) and Start reopens the "Boutons" step.
- **Outside the capture**: the Pinscape's analog plunger and accelerometer are axes, assigned by the "Lanceur" (Plunger) step (see [Analog plunger and accelerometer](#analog-plunger-and-accelerometer)). Hats and axes assigned to an action in VPX stay alongside the buttons, like a gamepad's.
- **Without VPX installed**, or if the SDL library is missing, joysticks are not read (the step says so): the keyboard, the encoders' default keys and the mouse remain.

### Leaving a long list: the list and the screen actions

A management screen that shows a **content list** (the tables of "Ajouter à un jeu" (Add to a game), the cards of "Médias du jeu" (Game media), the tiles of a file gallery, a friend's library, the files and places of the file picker, the lines of the cabinet health page, the downloads) has two zones: the list, and the screen actions ("Retour" (Back), the other gestures). The core decides by nature, never by length (`screenZones`, [packages/core/src/listzones.ts](../packages/core/src/listzones.ts)): a media card, a line to pick (`Row.pick`) or a health line is in the list; everything else is an action. A list of options (two or three settings to choose from, "Ce que montre cet écran" (What this screen shows)) declares it (`Section.options`) and stays a plain list of actions. A screen without a content list, or with nothing else, does not change.

- **The list is recognisable** by its frame (`.lib-zone`, `app/renderer/library.js`, `zoneFrame`) and its header bar, outside what scrolls: on the left the position of the choice ("17 / 37") or, outside it, the number of items; on the right the marker, a circled key and what it does: "Exit · Sortir de la liste" (Leave the list) while in it, "MagnaSave · Revenir à la liste" (Back to the list) otherwise (a click on it does the same). The zone holding the choice has its frame lit in teal; the other one dims a little. One focus only: green stays on the chosen item.
- **Exit in the list** brings back to the screen actions, on the entry it was entered from, otherwise on the one that does the same as Exit ("Retour", "Annuler" (Cancel)), otherwise on the first one (`leaveList`). A second Exit leaves the screen, as before. The help bar says so: "Exit ou Échap : sortir de la liste".
- **Left or right MagnaSave** (F6 on a keyboard, the Windows key that moves between panes) switches from one zone to the other without an extra step, and finds again the item left in the list (`switchZone`, `ZoneMemory`). The MagnaSave buttons are those of the wizard's "Boutons" (Buttons) step, otherwise the encoders' default keys (left and right Ctrl); a key already taken by an interface button keeps its role (`zoneSwitchKeys`, [buttons.ts](../packages/core/src/buttons.ts)). The switch happens on release, and only if nothing else was pressed meanwhile: the Ctrl of a shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+D) never switches.
- **What Start does in the list** is read on the help strip: "valider" (confirm) usually; a screen whose list is ticked declares it (`listStart`, "Start ou Entrée : cocher ou décocher la partie", Start or Enter: tick or untick the part, for the parts of a friend's table).
- **On the cabinet** (upright screen), the content takes its own height and the actions follow right below it; the gap stays at the bottom (`.lib-body`: `minmax(0, max-content) auto`). A list longer than the room scrolls in its frame, the actions always visible.
- **A flipper press** follows the screen order and goes from one zone to the other at their edge, as before (`zoneStep`): a cabinet without MagnaSave always reaches its actions. **A held flipper** stays in its zone and stops at its end, without wrapping around (`zoneHold`); after one second, it speeds up (`holdMove`): letter by letter on a list sorted by name, section by section (one letter at most every quarter of a second, the initial shown large in the header bar; a table put first, the "probable" one, does not prevent the jump), three at a time otherwise.

Safeguards: [sortie-de-liste.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sortie-de-liste.test.ts) fixes the invariant on every screen with zones (Exit from any item of the list leads to the actions, never out of the screen; the switch goes and comes back to the position left; a held flipper stays in its zone), checks that the renderer puts each content list in its frame with its marker, and lists every scrolling list in `app/renderer`: a new one fails the test until it is classified (content list, action list, or other with its reason; the VPX settings lists have their own navigation, Exit goes up one level there). In the real rendering, the layout safeguard reports "liste de contenu sans cadre ni repère" (content list without frame or marker).

### Reading text longer than the screen

A screen without a content list can carry text longer than itself, with nothing to choose in it: the release notes of an update, a message, an explanation. When such text overflows its frame, the mouse wheel or the scroll bar can reach it, but not the buttons: the flippers only go through the actions. Text that overflows therefore becomes a **reading zone**, one step of the path ([packages/core/src/reading.ts](../packages/core/src/reading.ts)):

- **You enter it** with the left flipper on the first action (the text sits above the actions), with the right flipper on the last one (the path wraps around), or directly with MagnaSave (F6), which also brings you back (`entersReading`).
- **You read it** page by page: right flipper or Start, next page; left flipper, previous page. A page moves 85 % of the view, two lines of the previous one stay so you do not lose your place, smoothly. At the bottom, the right flipper moves to the first action ("Mettre à jour maintenant" (Update now)); at the top, the left one to the last: read, then act, with no extra button (`readingStep`). A held flipper scrolls page after page and stops at the end, without leaving the text.
- **Exit** goes back to the actions, on the one you came from; a second Exit leaves the screen. A keyboard's Page Up and Page Down scroll the text from anywhere; so does the mouse wheel, as before.
- **The text is recognisable** by the frame of a content list (`readingFrame`, [app/renderer/library.js](../app/renderer/library.js)), shown only when it overflows: on the left the part read ("45 % lu" (45 % read)), on the right the marker, "MagnaSave · Faire défiler le texte" (Scroll the text) outside it, "Exit · Revenir aux actions" (Back to the actions) inside. While reading, the frame holds the choice (lit in teal), no action is green and the actions dim slightly; the help strip says "Flippers : faire défiler…" (Flippers: scroll…). Text that fits on the screen gets no frame and no extra step.
- **Who measures what**: the main process does not see the layout. The renderer measures the text (frame hidden, then shown if it overflows: showing it only reduces the space), and tells the main process whether it overflows, whether it is at the top, at the bottom (`reading` action, numbered per screen: a measurement from a previous screen is ignored), only when that changes. Each page asked for by a button is numbered and done once, even if the screen redraws (a progress tick). Scrolling goes through [scroll-keeper.js](../app/renderer/scroll-keeper.js) (`page`, `readingAt`), the only code that scrolls a list. The measurement does not wake the cabinet from attract mode.
- **VPX settings**: the details of the chosen setting (its original VPX description can be long) scroll with the MagnaSave buttons without leaving the list: left goes up, right (or F6) goes down one page ([settings.ts](../app/src/settings.ts), `detailMove`). The help strip says so.

Safeguards: [lecture.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/lecture.test.ts) pins the path (entering, pages, leaving at the end, held flipper stopped at the end) and the real renderer (frame only when the text overflows, measurement sent once per change, one page per press). Every scrolling text meant for reading says so with `data-reading`; in the real rendering of every window, the layout safeguard reports "texte qui défile hors d'atteinte des boutons" (scrolling text out of the buttons' reach) for one that does not (a list of choices with no current choice says so with `data-choices`). The census of scrolling lists ([sortie-de-liste.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sortie-de-liste.test.ts)) gives, for each one, how the buttons reach it.

## Analog plunger and accelerometer

An **analog plunger** (potentiometer or sensor plunger, read by a Pinscape on KL25Z or another controller in joystick mode) is not a button: it is a joystick **axis**, whose value follows the plunger's travel. The wizard's "Lanceur" (Plunger) step assigns and sets it, along with the Pinscape's accelerometer for nudging ([app/src/plunger-step.ts](../app/src/plunger-step.ts), [packages/core/src/plunger.ts](../packages/core/src/plunger.ts)). It follows the interface buttons in the full wizard; on its own: system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Lanceur analogique" (Analog plunger) (`npm run assistant -- --lanceur`, `plunger` action of the launcher).

- **Detection**: "Tirez le lanceur à fond, puis lâchez-le" (Pull the plunger all the way, then let go). Cyber Pinball reads every axis of every joystick through VPX's SDL library (the same numbers as VPX); the axis that moved the most, and came back to rest, becomes the plunger, with its direction (pulling raises or lowers the value). The first readings of a freshly opened joystick don't count, and an axis that jumped without coming back (a gamepad trigger) gives way to the plunger. Meanwhile a gauge shows the axis that is moving.
- **Proposed setting**: a dead zone just above the noise measured at rest; on a six-axis Pinscape ("X/Y/Z + RX/RY/RZ" in its config tool) whose plunger is on Z, the **speed measured by the board** on RZ, more accurate than the one VPX computes; the same Pinscape's **accelerometer** on X and Y.
- **Test**: the gauge shows the position as VPX will compute it (dead zone, direction), from forward to fully pulled, with the furthest pull; each shot (plunger released) shows its strength. Lines: sensor (detect again), direction, dead zone (0 to 30 %, with the flippers), speed, accelerometer; then "Enregistrer" (Save) ("Continuer" (Continue) in the full wizard) or "Pas de lanceur analogique" (No analog plunger). Everything also works with the mouse.
- **Rest far from 0**: VPX has no offset, the plunger would look pulled all the time. The step says so: the rest position is set on the board (Pinscape calibration).
- **Already set**: a cabinet where the step was done opens on its setting; otherwise, a plunger already assigned in VPX (its menu, or its accepted Pinscape layout) is taken as is ("Réglage trouvé dans VPX", Setting found in VPX). In the full wizard, a cabinet with no joystick with axes and no known setting skips the step without writing anything.

Keys written (`vpxAnalogSettings`, format read in `PlungerHandler.cpp`, `PhysicsSensor.cpp`, `NudgeHandler.cpp` and `GamepadNudge.cpp` at commit `af26b2d93`); a sensor value is written `<joystick>;<axis>;<P|V|A>;<dead zone>;<scale>;<limit>`, the axis numbered like VPX (0x200 plus the SDL axis: Z is 514), a negative scale reversing the axis:

| `[Input]` key | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `PlungerSensorCount` | 1, or 0 with no analog plunger |
| `Mapping.Plunger0.Position` | `SDLJoy_…_1;514;P;0.020000;1.000000;1.000000` |
| `Mapping.Plunger0.Velocity` | `SDLJoy_…_1;517;V;0.000000;12.500000;1.000000` (Pinscape speed), otherwise empty |
| `NudgeSensorCount` | 1, or 0 with no accelerometer |
| `Mapping.Nudge0.Type`, `X`, `Y` | 0 (gamepad), `SDLJoy_…_1;512;A;0.000000;2.000000;1.000000`, `SDLJoy_…_1;513;A;…` (the scale is the nudge strength: ×2 proposed for a Pinscape, see [Tilt](#tilt)) |
| `Device.<joystick>.NoAutoLayout` | 1 for each joystick whose buttons or sensors Cyber Pinball writes |

- **VPX's default layout turned off**: VPX recognises a six-axis Pinscape and offers, during a game, to apply its layout (plunger on Z, accelerometer on X and Y). Accepting first erases everything coming from that joystick (`ApplyDefaultDeviceMapping`, `ClearDeviceMappings`), including the buttons mapped by Cyber Pinball. `NoAutoLayout` turns that offer off for the cabinet's joysticks; a Pinscape set to "X/Y/Z" only (three axes) was not recognised by VPX anyway, and its plunger only worked once assigned by hand.
- These keys belong to the "Lanceur" step (`isAnalogKey`) and no longer to the "Boutons" (Buttons) step; `NoAutoLayout` belongs to both. Like the other managed keys, a setting later changed in VPX's menu (the dead zone, for example) is reported, then kept or reset as you choose.
- The "Lanceur" (Launch) button (`Mapping.LaunchBall`) stays: it serves tables with an automatic plunger, and the Pinscape can send it when the plunger is pushed forward ("ZB Launch Ball", set on the board).

## Tilt

What makes the cabinet tilt is decided at the wizard's "Tilt" step ([app/src/tilt-step.ts](../app/src/tilt-step.ts), [packages/core/src/tilt.ts](../packages/core/src/tilt.ts)). It follows the "Lanceur" (Plunger) step in the full wizard, which skips it without writing anything on a cabinet with no accelerometer, no captured tilt bob and no saved tilt (VPX then keeps its simulated tilt bob); on its own: system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Tilt" (`npm run assistant -- --tilt`, `tilt` action of the launcher).

**What VPX does with it** (VPX 10.8.1, commit `af26b2d93`):

- the **Tilt** action (`[Input] Mapping.Tilt`, T by default) reaches the table script as the `MechanicalTilt` key, which PinMAME scripts call `keyBangBack` (`scripts/VPMKeys.vbs`): `vpmKeyDown` calls `vpmNudge.DoMechTilt` (`WPC.vbs`, `s11.vbs`, `sega.vbs`…), which pulses the machine's tilt bob switch (`vpmNudge.TiltSwitch`, `core.vbs`). The ROM counts its warnings, then tilts, like the real machine;
- the **simulated tilt bob** (`physics/cabinet/PlumbHandler.cpp`) is a 10 cm pendulum driven by the cabinet's acceleration: the one VPX derives from the accelerometer's nudges (`GamepadNudge.cpp`: nudge intent, then a 113 kg cabinet on its legs) and from the nudge buttons. When it touches its ring, it presses the Tilt action itself. `[Player]` settings (`Settings_properties.inl`): `SimulatedPlumb` (1 by default), `PlumbThresholdAngle` (contact angle in degrees, 0.15 to 4, 1 by default; smaller, easier tilt), `PlumbDamping` (0 to 2, 1). A table's `.ini` can override them for that table (`PhysicsEngine.cpp` reads the table's settings);
- the **SlamTilt** action (`Mapping.SlamTilt`, Home by default) reaches the script as `keySlamDoorHit`: the machine's slam tilt switch (`swSlamTilt`, 21 on a WPC) stays closed while the button is held, and the ROM ends the game for every player.

The Pinscape guide (mjrnet, "Nudge & Tilt") wires a real tilt bob like a button, on a KL25Z input set as a joystick button (or the T key); with it, the simulated tilt bob is redundant: two tilt bobs, a single bump would count twice.

**The four cases, and what Cyber Pinball writes**:

| Cabinet | "Boutons" (Buttons) step | "Tilt" step | `VPinballX.ini` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wired tilt bob (Pinscape or encoder) | "Balancier de tilt" (Tilt bob) captured (joystick button or T) | "Balancier de la borne" (Cabinet tilt bob) | `[Input] Mapping.Tilt = SDLJoy_…;9 \| Key;23`, `[Player] SimulatedPlumb = 0` |
| Accelerometer only | "Balancier de tilt" skipped (Exit) | "Balancier simulé par VPX" (Tilt bob simulated by VPX), contact angle | `[Player] SimulatedPlumb = 1`, `PlumbThresholdAngle = 0.5` (for example) |
| Coin door slam tilt | "Slam tilt" captured | no effect | `[Input] Mapping.SlamTilt = SDLJoy_…;11 \| Key;74` |
| Neither a tilt bob nor simulation | "Balancier de tilt" skipped | "Pas de tilt" (No tilt) | `[Player] SimulatedPlumb = 0`; a keyboard's T key stays the tilt |

**Capturing the tilt bob** ("Boutons" step): the optional "Balancier de tilt" (Tilt bob) button asks "Faites toucher l'anneau au balancier" (Make the tilt bob touch its ring) and recalls what it is (the pendulum against its ring, wired to a Pinscape or encoder input). It is captured like the others, from the joystick as from the keyboard (`recordButton`). A contact bounces, and a swinging tilt bob touches its ring again: the key just captured no longer counts for the next button for 1 s (`BOUNCE_MS`, `wizard.ts`), except after Exit skips a button (it is pressed several times in a row). "Slam tilt" comes next, optional too.

**The "Tilt" step**:

- **Proposal**: a captured tilt bob wins ("Balancier de la borne", simulated tilt bob off); without one, what VPX does today ("Réglage actuel de VPX : balancier simulé par VPX, contact à 1,00°", Current VPX setting: tilt bob simulated by VPX, contact at 1.00°). A cabinet whose step is done opens on its setting. "Tilt" line: Start (or a click) switches modes: "Balancier de la borne" and "Balancier simulé par VPX" with a captured tilt bob, otherwise "Balancier simulé par VPX" and "Pas de tilt".
- **Contact angle** (simulated tilt bob): Start, then the flippers, in 0.05° steps, from 0.15° to 4°; Start to finish. Smaller, easier tilt: 4° is the hardest.
- **Nudge strength** (with an accelerometer): the scale of the accelerometer's X and Y axes (`[Input] Mapping.Nudge0.X` and `.Y`, `NUDGE_GAIN` in [plunger.ts](../packages/core/src/plunger.ts)), from ×0.50 to ×6, in 0.25 steps; Start, then the flippers. VPX reads the accelerometer like a gamepad stick (`Mapping.Nudge0.Type = 0`, its own default layout for a Pinscape, `SDLInputHandler.h`): a value of 1 is worth g/2 m/s² (`GamepadNudge.cpp`), and any nudge under 1 m/s² is ignored (`NudgeIntentHandler.cpp`). But a Pinscape set to "1G" (its default setting) sends the acceleration in g, full scale at 1 g (`rawToReport`, the firmware's `main.cpp`): at scale 1, VPX ignores everything under 0.2 g and only sees half of the rest. On the real cabinet, the ball barely moved and the simulated tilt bob never tilted, even at 4° (the hardest angle). At ×2, 1 g measured is 1 g in VPX: that is the strength proposed for a Pinscape ("Lanceur" step and "Tilt" step). Under the ring, the test shows the strongest nudge read in the last 3 seconds, as a percentage of the scale, and the threshold under which VPX ignores it (`nudgeFloor`: 20 % at ×1, 10 % at ×2); the tilt bob simulation follows the chosen strength. A strength set in VPX's menu (an axis scale, a reversed direction) is taken over as is. An accelerometer left at strength ×1 is reported at each synchronization ("Secousses : accéléromètre lu à la force ×1,00…", Nudging: accelerometer read at strength ×1.00…).
- **Test**: the contact ring seen from above. With the simulated tilt bob, the dot follows the tilt bob computed as VPX will compute it, one millisecond at a time, from the accelerometer axes read by VPX's SDL library (same axis shaping, same `Mapping.Nudge0.Strength`, same damping): each contact lights the ring, "TILT" under it, and counts as a Tilt press (the tilt bob bounces on the ring: a hard nudge often makes several, as in VPX); the footer gives the share of the contact reached and the nudge in g. With the cabinet tilt bob, the ring lights at each real contact ("Contacts du balancier : n", Tilt bob contacts: n). Without an accelerometer set ("Lanceur" step), the step says so: only the nudge buttons will move the simulated tilt bob.
- **Keys**: `[Player] SimulatedPlumb` and `PlumbThresholdAngle` (`vpxTiltSettings`, `isTiltKey`), only after the step (smooth migration: without it, `[Player]` stays as it is). Damping is left to VPX (F12 > "Nudge Settings"). Like the other managed keys, an angle changed later in a game is reported, kept or reset as you choose.

**Inconsistent settings reported** (`tiltFindings`, at each synchronization: result of the wizard and its steps, `sync` action): tilt bob captured, "Tilt" step never done and simulated tilt bob on (two tilt bobs); "Balancier de la borne" chosen without a captured button; simulated tilt bob without an accelerometer or nudge button; simulated tilt bob chosen but turned off by hand in `VPinballX.ini`.

**Table still on screen**: `[Player] NudgeStrength = 0`, at each synchronization, whether or not the "Tilt" step is done (`VPX_STILL_TABLE`, [tilt.ts](../packages/core/src/tilt.ts)). This VPX setting ("Visual Nudge Strength", from 0 to 2, 1 by default) only shifts the playfield image by as much as the simulated cabinet moves (`Renderer.cpp`: screen offset, or table translation without the static prerender). The ball, the simulated tilt bob and the script do not depend on it. On a cabinet, the real cabinet is what moves: the table stays still on screen and only the ball reacts to nudges (test on a real cabinet). A managed key like the others: set back by hand in VPX, it is reported instead of being overwritten. A table's `.ini` can still override it for that table.

**"Pendant une partie" (During a game) reminder**: a single "Tilt" line (the reminder already fills the screen): the tilt bob's key or button, "secousse trop forte" (nudging too hard) when the simulated tilt bob is chosen, and "slam tilt" with its key if it has one (`gameKeys`).

## Cabinet configurations

The same installation can be used on several machines: three screens besides the main one on one PC, two on another, a cabinet. `data\cabinet.json` holds all the **configurations**; each one is a complete cabinet model (screens by EDID fingerprint, roles, zones, flipper edge, diagonal, sound, buttons, keys left to the user) with its **own `VPinballX.ini`** and its own memory of the keys written. Tables, ROMs, media, per-table settings and statistics stay shared. Code: [packages/core/src/configurations.ts](../packages/core/src/configurations.ts) (computation), [packages/core/src/store.ts](../packages/core/src/store.ts) (files), [app/src/configurations-menu.ts](../app/src/configurations-menu.ts) (screens), tests in [configurations.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/configurations.test.ts).

- **Migration**: a `cabinet.json` from before configurations becomes, the first time it is read, the "Borne" (Cabinet) configuration, taken over as is, which keeps `config\VPinballX.ini` and its memory; the old file is kept as `data\cabinet-v1.json`. Nothing to do.
- **Recognition** (at startup, before each game, `sync`): each configuration gets a score from the connected screens (`scoreConfiguration`): 100 per screen found by its serial number, 60 per screen found by model and port (weak fingerprint), 30 by model and size only (cable moved to another port), −50 per missing screen, −10 per extra connected screen. A configuration is a candidate with at least one screen found, at most one missing (a screen turned off) and no more extra screens than screens found. The only complete candidate is picked automatically; otherwise a choice already made for these same screens; otherwise the best candidate if it is alone with its score. A partly found configuration stays selected with a warning, and the sync warns about the role of the screen that is off (and blocks if it is the playfield), as before. Every choice is explained: "« Second poste » reconnue : 3 écrans sur 3 retrouvés (2 par numéro de série, 1 par modèle et port) — score 260, devant « Borne » (score -110)" ("Second poste" recognized: 3 of 3 screens found (2 by serial number, 1 by model and port), score 260, ahead of "Borne" (score -110)).
- **Ambiguity or no match**: nothing is written. The menu opens on the main screen with "Quelle configuration est branchée ?" (Which configuration is connected?): the known configurations, from closest to farthest with their score, "Nouvelle configuration" (New configuration) (full wizard) and "Plus tard" (Later) (the menu stays usable; a game asks for the configuration again). The choice is remembered for these screens (10 screen sets remembered) and the menu restarts on the right screens. On the command line: exit code 3, and `npm run sync -- --configuration "Nom"` makes the same choice.
- **Configuration change**: a sync finding ("Configuration changée : « Borne » à la place de « Second poste »…" (Configuration changed: "Borne" instead of "Second poste"…)) and a 12 s banner at the top of the menu, with no button to press.
- **System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Configurations de la borne" (Cabinet configurations)** (its details name the active configuration, and say whether it is forced): the list with each one's score for the connected screens; for each one, "Utiliser avec ces écrans" (Use with these screens), "Toujours utiliser celle-ci" (Always use this one) (forced: recognition is suspended until "Reconnaissance automatique" (Automatic recognition)), "Écran du navigateur intégré" (Built-in browser screen) (where the "Récupérer" window opens, see [Screen and pointer](#adding-a-table-the-funnel-the-catalog-récupérer-waiting-list)), "Renommer" (Rename) (on-screen keyboard, or a real keyboard), "Supprimer" (Delete) (configuration trash, restorable with its file; the last one cannot be deleted); "Nouvelle configuration" restarts the wizard with `--nouvelle-configuration`.
- **New configuration**: the wizard creates the configuration ("Poste à 3 écrans" (3-screen PC), to be renamed), remembered for the connected screens. Its `VPinballX-<id>.ini` file starts as a copy of the active configuration's file: hand-made settings that do not depend on the screens (graphics quality, physics, keys) carry over; the wizard then writes the screens, sound and buttons into it. "Assistant complet" (Full setup wizard) redoes the active configuration, and so does the "Boutons" (Buttons) step on its own.
- **One `VPinballX.ini` per configuration, rather than a single one rewritten at each switch**: VPX re-saves its file itself, and the user sets both managed keys (kept in `vpxManual`) and free keys in it. A single file would mean setting aside, then restoring, each machine's own values at every switch, and the detection of hand edits would mistake the other machine's values for tweaks: a value set by hand on one machine would be overwritten or lost on the way back. With one file each, syncing a configuration never touches another one's file, and the memory of keys written stays tied to its own file (the "bascule aller-retour" (round-trip switch) test). The trade-off: a general setting changed later (through "Réglages de VPX", VPX settings) only applies to the active configuration.
- **Safeguard**: all the application code goes through the active configuration's file (`activeVpxIni`, `syncConfiguration` in [app/src/vpx-ini.ts](../app/src/vpx-ini.ts)); a test rejects any other access to `Locations.vpxIni`.

## Table orientation and framing

After the playfield is chosen, the wizard asks where the player stands: the flippers rotate the display until the text is upright, with the "Flippers · vous êtes ici" (Flippers · you are here) band on the player's side. It then asks for the diagonal, suggested from the EDID (the millimeter size from the detailed timing, otherwise the centimeters), and discarded if it does not match the image proportions, as happens with some TVs and with projectors. All of this is stored in `cabinet.json` (`playfield.flipperEdge`, `playfield.manualSizeCm` only if the diagonal differs from the EDID; `packages/core/src/layout.ts`). These questions can be redone on their own through system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Orientation et taille du playfield" (Playfield orientation and size) (`--assistant --playfield`), and with "Écrans et rôles" (Screens and roles) when the playfield moves to another screen.

Keys written to `VPinballX.ini` (`vpxLayoutSettings`, read from `ViewSetup.cpp` and `player.cpp` in VPX 10.8.1):

| Key | Value | Managed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Player] BGSet` | 1 (cabinet) if the image is taller than it is wide for the player, otherwise 0 | yes |
| `[Player] ScreenWidth`, `ScreenHeight` | real size in cm, the long side as the width whatever the orientation | yes |
| `[Player] CabinetAutofitMode` | in cabinet view, 1 ("Fit Table") or 2 ("Fit Screen"): the chosen mode is kept, any other value (absent, 0, `Manual`) becomes 1 | in game: the mode is free, manual is refused |

With automatic framing, VPX recomputes the table's whole cabinet view and derives the rotation from the shape of the window: flippers on the right of a landscape image, at the bottom of a portrait image, with no setting to change it.

#### Direction of tables in play

VPX draws in Windows coordinates: only the shape of the playfield window counts, never the orientation of the screen. What decides the direction (`vpxPlayedFlipperEdge`, `packages/core/src/vpx.ts`, read from `ViewSetup.cpp`, `player.cpp` and `pintable.cpp` of VPX 10.8.1, commit `af26b2d93`):

| Values read by VPX (`VPinballX.ini`, under the table's `.ini`) | View rotation | Flippers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BGSet = 1`, `CabinetAutofitMode` 1 or 2 | `SetWindowAutofit` resets the table's rotation to 0 at every game, `GetRotation` adds 270° for a landscape window, 0° for a portrait window; `ViewCabRotation` has no effect | right (landscape), bottom (portrait) |
| `BGSet = 1`, manual framing (`CabinetAutofitMode` 0, or absent: VPX default) | that of the cabinet view set in the table, often 270° for Windows in landscape | unpredictable: sideways on Windows in portrait |
| `BGSet` 0 or 2 (desktop or FSS view) | that of the table's desktop view | bottom |

A rotation of r degrees puts the flippers at the edge whose `edgeRotation` is r (0 bottom, 90 left, 180 top, 270 right): the same convention as the menu rotation. Without `ScreenWidth`/`ScreenHeight`, VPX still fits the table, with its default size.

Hence the rule: in cabinet view, automatic framing is required, not suggested. Up to 0.1.3-essai.3, `CabinetAutofitMode` was only written when the key was empty: a `CabinetAutofitMode = 0` already there (old `VPinballX.ini`, setting changed in the VPX menu or in "Réglages" (Settings)) stayed, and every table went back to its own cabinet view, rotated for Windows in landscape. On a playfield in portrait in Windows, flippers at the bottom, the menu was upright and the game sideways.

- **Sync** (`requireAutofit`, `orientationFinding`, `packages/core/src/sync.ts`): a value other than 1 and 2 is replaced by 1, with the finding "cadrage automatique de VPX rétabli" (VPX automatic framing restored) when it was written; a cabinet already set up is corrected at the next start, without redoing the wizard. After writing, the direction is computed again from the file: if VPX will not draw the flippers on the player's side (Windows orientation to change, `BGSet` changed by hand, `CabinetAutofitMode` kept by the user in `vpxManual`), a warning says so, with the values at fault.
- **Settings**: the "Manuel" (Manual) choice, and the VPX default that is one, are no longer offered for `CabinetAutofitMode` in the general setting.
- **Doctor**: "Table de travers sur cette borne" (Table sideways on this cabinet, `tableOrientation`) when a table's `.ini`, through `BGSet` or `CabinetAutofitMode`, stops VPX drawing the flippers on the player's side; it then replaces "Cadrage manuel imposé par la table" (Manual view forced by the table). The repair removes these keys only; the file goes to the recycle bin if it holds nothing else. If `VPinballX.ini` itself is at fault, the sync says so.
- **Test**: `packages/core/src/test/orientation.test.ts` goes through the four Windows orientations, of a landscape screen and of a native portrait screen, the four flipper edges and seven starting `VPinballX.ini` files: the game goes the player's and the menu's way, without a warning, and a second sync changes nothing more; screen rotated outside the wizard or flip refused: right direction, or a warning.

For a player on the left or at the top, the wizard rotates the playfield screen itself, for the session only (see [Desktop orientation on exit](#desktop-orientation-on-exit)). At the "Où sont les flippers ?" (Where are the flippers?) step, it announces "Start : retourner l'écran pour VPX" (Start: flip the screen for VPX); Start switches the screen to a flipped orientation (`SessionOrientation.want`, then `setDisplayOrientation`, `packages/win-displays/src/orientation.ts`: `ChangeDisplaySettingsExW` tried as a dry run, applied without `CDS_UPDATEREGISTRY`, read back, and put back as it was if Windows did something else). The wizard runs the detection again, repositions its windows and recomputes the player's edge in the new image (left ↔ right, top ↔ bottom): the player always sees the text upright. Like Windows, it then asks whether to keep this orientation: without Start within 15 seconds, or with Exit, the screen returns to its original orientation, and Start then carries on without flipping (tables upside down). The choice of rotation is in `vpxOrientationFix` (`packages/core/src/vpx.ts`): a flip, which keeps the resolution and the layout of the other screens; a quarter turn only for a Windows portrait screen seen from the side. The sync, on the other hand, does not touch the orientation: it reports the problem and points to the wizard. With another cabinet frontend installed or running (PinUP Popper, PinballX, PinballY), the first Start flips nothing: it warns that the orientation is a Windows setting that the other frontend is subject to as well; Start flips anyway, Exit keeps the Windows orientation (see [Coexisting with another cabinet frontend](#coexisting-with-another-cabinet-frontend)).

The saved edge applies to the orientation Windows had at the time of the wizard, stored with the screen (`lastSeen.rotation`). If it later changes outside the wizard (Settings > Display, driver, waking from sleep), the image rotates under the player: `currentFlipperEdge` (`packages/core/src/layout.ts`) rotates the saved edge by the difference between the two orientations, and this current edge is the one used for framing, for the sync's "à l'envers" (upside down) warning, for rotating Cyber Pinball's windows on the playfield (`playfieldRotation`) and for the edge suggested when the wizard is run again. If no orientation was recorded at save time, the saved edge is kept as is.

A model saved before this step gets no framing key until the wizard has been run again.

#### Desktop orientation on exit

After quitting Cyber Pinball, the desktop and Start menu on the playfield screen stayed upside down. The wizard rotated the screen with `CDS_UPDATEREGISTRY`: the Windows setting changed for good, desktop included, and nothing gave it back on exit. Neither the menu nor VPX touched the orientation; only the wizard did (`WINDOWS_CHANGES`, `packages/core/src/coexistence.ts`).

Windows now keeps its orientation, and Cyber Pinball only rotates the screen while it is open (`SessionOrientation`, `packages/core/src/sessionorientation.ts`; `app/src/display-orientation.ts`):

- **Playing orientation**: the wizard records in the model the Windows orientation in which it configured the playfield, when it had to change it (`playfield.windowsRotation`). Every start (menu, wizard, settings) gives it back to the screen, before opening the windows, through a dynamic change: nothing is written to the Windows setting, and restarting the PC always gives the desktop its orientation back.
- **Origin recorded before touching anything**: before the first rotation, each screen's original orientation (fingerprint, GDI name, origin, orientation given) is written to `<data>\orientation-ecrans.json`, which survives a crash. The file goes away once no screen is rotated any more.
- **Every exit gives it back**: "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball), closing the last window, an update, the dedicated cabinet (exit code 77) go through `will-quit`, before the dedicated cabinet's own exit (`main.ts` imports `display-orientation.ts` first), then through `process.on('exit')` as a last resort (`app.exit`). A relaunch (menu ↔ wizard, language, configuration, Shell Launcher) hands over (`handOverOrientation`): the next process picks up the file, and the screen does not rotate in between.
- **Crash, forced stop**: the file stays; at the next launch, a screen still in the orientation Cyber Pinball gave it (or back to its origin after a restart) keeps its recorded origin; a screen the user has rotated in the meantime keeps its own, which becomes the origin. Shutdown, restart and sleep from the power module: since nothing is written to the Windows setting, the screen comes back in its own; on waking from sleep, if Windows has put its setting back (screens enumerated again), the session takes back the playing orientation (`check`, on `display-added`, `display-metrics-changed` and `resume`).
- **Desktop mode**: "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) gives the Windows orientation back (`suspend`), returning takes back the playing one (`resume`); after a quarter turn, the menu relaunches to reposition its windows.
- **Warning**: an orientation Windows refuses to give back is written to the log ("Orientation : … n'a pas repris son orientation d'origine (0°) : … Paramètres > Affichage > Orientation de l'écran la rétablit.", i.e. did not get its original orientation back; Settings > Display > Display orientation restores it) and to the file; the next start tries again and repeats it in a banner ("À la dernière fermeture de Cyber Pinball : …", when Cyber Pinball last closed).
- **Cabinets configured by an older version** (`legacyOrientation`): a model without `windowsRotation`, a recorded orientation (`lastSeen.rotation`) other than 0° and such that the wizard would have chosen it starting from 0° (`vpxOrientationFix`). If Windows has lost it (restart), the menu records the playing orientation and takes it back without asking. If Windows still keeps it, the menu offers: "Bureau Windows tourné par Cyber Pinball" (Windows desktop rotated by Cyber Pinball); Start gives 0° back to the Windows setting (the only write to that setting, `giveBack`) then puts the playing orientation back for the session, and the screen flickers twice; Exit keeps the desktop orientation as it is. Either way, the question does not come back. Guest mode: Start does nothing there (`message.orientation`).

Simulated screens (`CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS`, unattended run, off screen): the rotation goes to `<data>\ecrans-simules-orientation.json` (`windows`: the setting; `session`: the dynamic change, lost on restart), which `observedDisplays` applies to the screens; no real screen rotates. Evidence: `packages/core/src/test/sortie-orientation.test.ts` (every exit path on the four sets of `packages\core\ecrans-simules`, with a simulated Windows that counts writes to its setting; recovery after a crash, Windows refusing, repair).

#### Direction of tables, checked

A table could show upside down after every restart or session reconnection (seen on a PinCab: 4K playfield lying flat, player at the left edge of the image, Windows flipped by the wizard): Windows had given its own setting back to the playfield screen. The menu, which follows the current orientation (`currentFlipperEdge`), stayed upright; VPX, which only looks at the shape of the window, drew the table upside down, and it had to be turned around in VPX's menu at every startup. The session now gives the game orientation back at startup, on wake and on reconnection (previous section); what follows checks it and shows it.

- **Cabinet health** (`orientationFacts`, `orientationVerdict`, `packages/core/src/gameorientation.ts`): under "Screens", after the recognised configuration, "Tables the right way for you, flippers …" with the Windows orientation, or, blocking, "Tables upside down: VPX will draw them with the flippers …, you stand …", with the cause: the game orientation Windows did not take back, or, with no orientation to take back, "run “Playfield orientation and size” again".
- **Log**: the sync's warnings also go to the console ("Synchronisation : …"), including "Tables will show upside down".
- **Fix made in VPX's menu**: to turn a table the right way, one had to switch to manual framing and rotate the view, which VPX saves in the table's `.ini` (`CabinetAutofitMode = 0`, `ViewCabMode`, `ViewCabRotation`). Cyber Pinball does not overwrite it. When the `.ini` gives the view and its rotation, its direction is known (`manualCabRotation`, `packages/core/src/vpx.ts`, after `ViewSetup::GetRotation`: in "Window" mode the rotation is added to 270° for a landscape window, to 0° for a portrait window; in "Legacy" or "Camera" it stands alone): the doctor does not report "Table de travers" (Table sideways) for a table the right way for the player, and says where VPX will draw the others. A fix made while Windows had lost the game orientation turns the table upside down once the orientation is taken back: the doctor reports it, and its repair removes `BGSet` and `CabinetAutofitMode` from the table's `.ini` (the remaining rotation is ignored by automatic framing).
- **Test**: `packages/core/src/test/gameorientation.test.ts` pins the invariant on a landscape screen and a native portrait screen, for the four Windows orientations and the four player edges: wizard, exit, simulated restart (Windows gives its setting back), session start, then reconnection (`check`); each time, VPX draws the game on the player's side, the menu too, with no warning, and nothing is written to the Windows setting. A cabinet configured by an older version and back at 0° gets its direction back (`legacyOrientation`).

### Table framing, during a game

A table's framing is judged on the running table, not on a list of values. Table details screen > "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game) launches the table with `-PovEdit` instead of `-Play` (VPX 10.8.1, `AppCommands.cpp`): the same game, with VPX's menu open from the start on "Point Of View". The details screen's "Ajuster la vue" (Adjust the view) screen, which picked these values outside a game and then tried them out, was removed, together with its `-TableIni` trial run.

With automatic framing, VPX recomputes the cabinet view after reading the table's overrides: the `[TableOverride] ViewCab*` keys only apply with manual framing. The "Point Of View" page therefore only offers what the framing reads again (according to the VPX 10.8.1 code):

| Mode (`[Player] CabinetAutofitMode`) | Settings on the page | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| "Fit Table" (1) | the mode and `CabinetAutofitPos` (0 to 20 %) | position of the bottom flippers on the screen; higher up, the apron rises into the screen, at the expense of the top of the table |
| "Fit Screen" (2) | the mode only | the full length of the table, stretched or squeezed |
| manual (0) | offsets, scales, glass, rotation, then `ScreenPlayerX/Y/Z` | a hand-set view, which the doctor reports when a table forces it |

- **Save** (Start) > "Save as Table Override": in the table's `.ini`. Back on the details screen, it says "Affichage enregistré pour cette table" (Display saved for this table) if that file changed.
- **Outside a game**, these keys can no longer be changed for a table: in "Réglages de cette table" (This table's settings), they carry the "En partie" (In game) badge and only the table's own value can be removed (`isLiveVisual`, `packages/core/src/vpx-settings.ts`); the table's essentials no longer list them.
- **Doctor**: "Cadrage manuel imposé par la table" (Manual framing forced by the table) when the table's `.ini` (often shipped with it, set up for its author's screen) contains `CabinetAutofitMode = 0`; the repair removes the key, and the old file goes to the trash.

## Sound

VPX 10.8.1 has only two outputs: `[Player] SoundDeviceBG` (backglass: ROM music and voices, AltSound, PuP-Pack, table music) and `[Player] SoundDevice` (playfield: mechanical sounds, mixed according to `Sound3D`). It identifies them by the output's **Windows name** ("Haut-parleurs (USB Audio Device)", i.e. "Speakers (USB Audio Device)" on an English Windows), compared character for character; a name it cannot find silently sends it to the Windows default output. This name is localized, can be renamed, and is sometimes shared by two identical cards: as with screens, Cyber Pinball keeps its own fingerprint and rewrites the current name at every synchronization.

**Detection** (`probeAudioOutputs`, `packages/win-displays/src/audio.ts`): active outputs are read from `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\MMDevices\Audio\Render\{guid}`, read-only and through the Unicode API (`RegGetValueW`), because `reg.exe` mangles accented characters. For each one: the MMDevice identifier (`{0.0.0.00000000}.{guid}`), the name VPX expects ("DeviceDesc (interface)"), bus, form factor, channels and speaker mask set in Windows, ContainerId, and default output (the active output with the highest `Level:0`, cross-checked against Chromium on the development PC). An HDMI output is tied to its screen through the ContainerId of the PnP monitor (`ObservedDisplay.containerId`); the container shared by everything built into the PC (`{00000000-0000-0000-FFFF-FFFFFFFFFFFF}`) ties nothing. `npm run probe` shows all of this.

**The setup wizard's "Son" (Sound) step**, after the screens, or on its own (system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Son de la borne" (Cabinet sound), `--assistant --son`: only `SoundDevice`, `SoundDeviceBG` and `Sound3D` are written). Virtual outputs (Steam, Oculus: bus `ROOT`) and Bluetooth outputs are left out, unless they already had a role. Each output plays, on itself, a sound generated by an oscillator (no file), n beeps for output n, in a loop: `AudioContext.setSinkId` in a wizard window, the output being found by `enumerateDevices` under its Windows name (Chromium appends "(vid:pid)" to the name of USB outputs, which is ignored for the comparison). The player answers with the buttons:

| Button | Output played | Speaker test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Left flipper | backglass | playfield, near the player |
| Right flipper | playfield | playfield, backglass side |
| Start | both (SSF with a single card) | in the backglass |
| Exit | no role, next output | nothing heard |
| Backspace | previous output | previous pair |

The backglass screen's speaker is offered by default for the backglass, and a card set to 7.1 (failing that, the playfield screen's speaker) for the playfield; these outputs are played first, and the screen whose output is the speaker lights up. Running the wizard again starts from the saved outputs. If the playfield output reports 6 or 8 channels, each speaker pair (front, rear, side) beeps on the left, then at a higher pitch on the right (`ChannelMergerNode`, discrete channels in the order of the Windows mask, like SDL), and the player says where they hear it. The answers choose the mix (`playfieldModeFromTest`, `packages/core/src/audio.ts`): front in the backglass and another pair on the playfield gives SSF; front and another pair on the playfield gives surround, in the direction heard; otherwise stereo. Rear / side wiring that is swapped for SSF is flagged. The summary shows the chosen outputs and the `Sound3D` value that will be written.

Permissions: Chromium only gives output names, and only allows `setSinkId`, with the `media` or `speaker-selection` permission; the wizard grants these two to its windows (`setPermissionCheckHandler`) and allows playback without a user gesture (`autoplayPolicy`), since the buttons go through the main process. If the output is not found, or if Chromium opens fewer channels than reported, the screen says so and the player can still answer.

Model (`cabinet.json`, `audio` field): outputs stored by `endpointId`, with the `containerId` as a fallback (for a driver that recreates a screen's HDMI output) and the fingerprint of the screen whose speaker it is; `roles` (backglass, playfield, possibly the same output); `playfieldMode` (`stereo`, `surround`, `ssf`) and `lockbarPair` in surround. At each synchronization, outputs are found again by identifier, then by ContainerId if there is no ambiguity, never by name.

Keys written to `VPinballX.ini` (`vpxAudioSettings`), managed like the others (a value changed by hand is never silently overwritten):

| Key | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `[Player] SoundDeviceBG` | current name of the backglass output; empty if none (Windows default output) |
| `[Player] SoundDevice` | current name of the playfield output; empty if none |
| `[Player] Sound3D` | stereo: 0, or 1 on a 7.1 output (mode 0 cancels the gains there); surround: 3 (front on the lockbar side) or 2 (rear on the lockbar side); SSF: 5 |

A model without a "Son" (Sound) step (saved before that step existed, or with no output to offer) writes none of these keys, and the settings screen leaves them editable. Points to check, reported by the synchronization and the wizard: output not found (the key stays as it is, VPX will play on the default output), two active outputs with the same name (VPX takes the last one), a `Sound3D` too ambitious for the channels set in Windows (surround with fewer than 4 channels, SSF with fewer than 8: VPX falls back to stereo; set the output to 5.1 or 7.1 in the Sound control panel). Cyber Pinball never changes the Windows default output or an output's format by itself; only the cabinet's volume buttons set the Windows volume (see [Cabinet volume](#cabinet-volume)). Volumes (`MusicVolume`, `SoundVolume`) remain player settings.

To be confirmed on a real cabinet: how `Sound3D` modes 2, 3 and 5 actually sound, the expected direction of the rear and side pairs in SSF (Pinscape guide: rear on the player's side), the channels Chromium actually opens on a 7.1 card, the ContainerId of an HDMI output after a port change, and the disappearance of a screen's HDMI output when the screen is in standby.

### Cabinet volume

The **Volume −**, **Volume +** and **Mute** buttons (optional, "Boutons" step) set the sound of **the whole cabinet**: the Windows volume, the one in the taskbar, hence everything that plays (VPX, PinMAME, PuP-Pack, B2S, the Cyber Pinball menu), in the menu as well as during a game.

- **Volume +**: 5% louder, on the 5% grid (16% goes to 20%); muted sound comes back first, as with the Windows keys.
- **Volume −**: 5% quieter. **Held for one second** (`MUTE_HOLD_MS`), it mutes the sound, or restores it if it was muted; the level from before the press is the level of the restored sound.
- **Mute**: mutes or restores the sound. No encoder sends it by default: it has no default key and only exists once captured.
- One step per press, no repeat: holding Volume − is how you mute.
- **Outputs set**: those of the "Son" (Sound) step roles (backglass, playfield; two cards in SSF), each by the same step as the backglass output, keeping their gap, bounded from 0 to 100%; without the "Son" step, the Windows default output (`volumeOutputIds`). An unplugged output is skipped.
- **Badge**: in the middle of the top of the playfield, turned toward the player (`gameNoticeBounds`, `top-center` corner), the icon, "Volume" and the level with its bar; when muted, "Son coupé" (Sound muted) and how to restore it. It stays 1.6 s after the last press, 4 s when muted, and reminds you when you come back from a game. It is the in-game notification window (`overlay`, `app/src/game-notice.ts`): transparent, never focused, clicked through, absent from captures and recordings; it takes control away from neither VPX nor the menu.
- **Reading the buttons** (`app/src/cabinet-volume.ts`): polled every 25 ms, whatever window is in the foreground, keyboard keys through `GetAsyncKeyState` (`keysDown`), joystick buttons through SDL (`watchJoystickButtons`), like the in-game settings combination. A button captured on a keyboard media key (`AudioVolumeUp`, `AudioVolumeDown`, `AudioVolumeMute`) is left to Windows, which already sets the sound when it is pressed; the badge shows the result.
- **Windows volume** (`readEndpointVolumes`, `writeEndpointVolumes`, `packages/win-displays/src/system-volume.ts`): Core Audio, `IMMDeviceEnumerator` then `IAudioEndpointVolume` (`GetMasterVolumeLevelScalar`, `SetMasterVolumeLevelScalar`, `GetMute`, `SetMute`) called through their method table with koffi; outputs are designated by their MMDevice identifier, the one from the "Son" step.
- **`[Input]`**: `Mapping.VolumeDown` and `Mapping.VolumeUp` no longer have a key (`Key;0`), with or without a capture, and a joystick button written there before is removed (`vpxVolumeSettings`); otherwise `-` and `=` would also set VPX, which would show a different volume. A controller added by hand in F12 > "Input Settings" stays.
- **Offscreen** (unattended tests), the Windows volume is never touched: a simulated volume, and presses arrive through the menu windows (the autopilot's `hold:` and `release:` steps). Captures: `volume-borne-niveau.png`, `volume-borne-coupe.png`. Tests: [cabinetvolume.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/cabinetvolume.test.ts).

## VPX plugins: backglass, PuP and DMD

VPX 10.8.1 draws the backglass, PuP packs and the DMD itself, through its plugins ("plugins" mode): `ScreenRes.txt`, `B2STableSettings.xml` and `DmdDevice.ini` are read by nobody in that mode. Everything therefore goes through `VPinballX.ini`. Classic mode (VPX 10.8.0, COM components) will come later; DOF goes through the VPX DOF plugin (see [LED and feedback (DOF)](#led-and-feedback-dof)).

**Who draws on each window.** Several plugins can draw on the Backglass, ScoreView (DMD role) and Topper windows. For each frame, VPX tries their renderers in descending order of `Priority.<moteur>` and keeps the first one that draws (`player.cpp`, `OnAuxRendererChanged`). Without these keys, they are all 0 and the order depends on the order in which plugins load. A PuP pack only draws when it has a video to show: placed in front, it leaves the .directb2s backglass visible the rest of the time.

Keys written (`vpxSettingsFor` and `vpxPluginDefaults` in `packages/core/src/vpx.ts`, `packages/core/src/dmd.ts`):

| Key | Value | Managed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Backglass] Priority.PUP`, `Priority.B2S` | 100, 50; if the backglass role is assigned | yes |
| `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP`, `Priority.B2S`, `Priority.ScoreView` | 100, 50, 10; if the DMD role is assigned. DMD-only screen: `Priority.B2S = 0` ([DMD screen](#dmd-screen-fulldmd-or-dmd-only)) | yes |
| `[Topper] Priority.PUP` | 100; if the topper role is assigned (B2S does not draw on the topper) | yes |
| `[Plugin.B2SLegacy] Enable` | 0 as long as B2S is enabled: both answer tables that request `B2S.Server`, and the last one loaded wins | yes |
| `[Plugin.B2S] ScoreViewDMDOverlay`, `ScoreViewDMDAutoPos` | 1 and 1 if the DMD role exists and is not a DMD only. The FullDMD of a .directb2s goes in front of ScoreView: without the overlay, only its frame would be visible. The DMD's position is searched for in the image | no, offered if the key is empty |
| `[Plugin.DMDUtil] Enable`, and `ZeDMD`, `PIN2DMD` or `Pixelcade` | 1 for the recognized real DMD; nothing without a real DMD | yes |
| `[Plugin.PinMAME] PinMAMEPath` | `<racine>\pinmame`, if it contains `roms` | no, offered if the key is empty |
| `[Plugin.Serum] SerumPath`, `[Plugin.VNI] VniPath` | `<racine>\pinmame\altcolor`, if it contains a `<rom>\<rom>.cRZ` or `.cROMc` (Serum), `.pal` or `.vni` (VNI) | no, offered if the key is empty |

Each table first looks for its ROMs and colorizations in its own folder (`pinmame\roms`, `serum`, `vni`, `pinmame\altcolor`); the shared folders only come after. The synchronization reports "B2S et B2SLegacy activés ensemble" (B2S and B2SLegacy enabled together) when both are enabled (B2SLegacy turned back on by hand, then kept).

**Real DMD.** `probeUsbDevices` (`packages/win-displays/src/usb.ts`) lists the USB devices present through the Windows configuration manager (`CM_Get_Device_ID_ListW`) and reads their name and serial port from the registry, read-only. No port is opened, whereas libdmdutil's detectors send a connection request to every port. Recognized identifiers (`HARDWARE_DMD_IDS`):

| DMD | VID:PID | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ZeDMD | `303A:1001` (ESP32-S3), `1A86:55D3` (CH343); `1A86:7523` (CH340) and `10C4:EA60` (CP210x), common converters flagged "à confirmer" (to be confirmed) | libzedmd's automatic detection filter (`ZeDMDComm.cpp`) |
| PIN2DMD | `0314:E457` | libdmdutil (`PIN2DMD.cpp`) |
| Pixelcade | `1B4F:0008` (IOIO-OTG board) | IOIO firmware; libdmdutil recognizes it by its "IOIO" reply |

DMDUtil finds the port itself: Cyber Pinball writes neither `ZeDMDDevice` nor `PixelcadeDevice`. When the DMD is unplugged, its keys are kept as they are. `npm run probe` shows the recognized DMDs (`npm run probe -- --usb`: all USB devices).

### Several roles on one screen: zones

A two-screen cabinet often puts the backglass and the DMD on the backglass monitor. A cabinet without a DMD screen embeds the DMD in the playfield image. A desktop PC puts everything on one screen: the table on one side, the backglass on the other. In the cabinet model, several roles can therefore point to the same screen, each in its own zone (`zones`), the playfield included. A zone is a rectangle expressed in fractions of the screen image (0 to 1): it survives a resolution change. It can keep an aspect ratio (`aspect`, width over height: 16/9, 4…): it is then resized without changing its proportions. Without a zone, the role fills the whole screen, as before. Code: [packages/core/src/zones.ts](../packages/core/src/zones.ts).

VPX 10.8.1 can do both, without any third-party tool (`src/renderer/Window.cpp`, `Renderer.cpp`):

| The role is… | Keys written | Effect in VPX |
| --- | --- | --- |
| alone on its screen | `<Préfixe>Output = 1`, `FullScreen = 1`, `WndX = WndY = 0`, `Width` and `Height` of the screen | borderless full-screen window |
| in a zone of a screen (playfield included) | `Output = 1` (not for the playfield), `Display`, `FullScreen = 0` ("Windowed"), `WndX`, `WndY`, `Width`, `Height` of the zone, in pixels relative to the screen | borderless window placed over the zone (all VPX windows are borderless) |
| in the playfield zone, on its screen | `Output = 2` ("Embedded in playfield"), `WndX`, `WndY`, `Width`, `Height` of the zone, relative to the playfield window | drawn inside the playfield image, on a black background, after the table: no window to keep in the foreground |

A role on the playfield screen but outside its zone (desktop PC) is a separate window. Table framing follows the playfield window, not the screen: cabinet view when the window is taller than it is wide, `ScreenWidth` and `ScreenHeight` set to the zone's actual size. "Garder" (Keep) on a size changed in VPX brings it back to the whole screen.

**Stacking order.** VPX has no setting for it: embedded displays are drawn backglass, then DMD, then topper (`Renderer.cpp`), and it is Windows that stacks two overlapping windows. Nothing prevents two zones from overlapping (a DMD placed over the bottom of the backglass, for example): this is reported for information, along with what will be in front. Only two roles assigned to the same screen with no zone for either of them give a warning: that is an oversight.

**No per-zone rotation.** VPX 10.8.1 only has rotation settings for the table view (`ViewDTRotation`, `ViewCabRotation`…) and for VR: its Backglass, ScoreView and Topper windows, and their embedded displays, are always drawn in the orientation of the Windows image.

**DMD window: FullDMD and DMD layered.** The DMD role's zone is the ScoreView window's zone. It has two layers: at the back, the FullDMD (the FullDMD image of the .directb2s, or screen 5 of a PuP-Pack, which the PUP plugin takes as the ScoreView root, falling back to screen 1: `PUPManager.cpp`); on top, the ROM's DMD, which the plugins place in the window themselves (B2S: `ScoreViewDMDOverlay` and `ScoreViewDMDAutoPos`, which looks for the DMD's place in the image; PuP: screen 1 placed over screen 5 by `CustomPos`). A zone therefore does not need to target the 128×32 DMD: it gives the FullDMD's place. What the screen shows, FullDMD or DMD only, is said once and for all ([DMD screen](#dmd-screen-fulldmd-or-dmd-only)). `splitBackglassDmd` splits a screen between the backglass at the top and a centered DMD strip at the bottom, at the requested proportions (4:1 for a DMD alone, 16:9 for a FullDMD), taking at most one third of the height.

- **Synchronization**: reports two roles overlapping on one screen (the case of two roles assigned to the same screen without a zone), an invalid zone in `cabinet.json` (the whole screen is then used), a role embedded over the whole playfield, and an embedded display that the player would see sideways or upside down: VPX does not rotate embedded displays.
- **Screen layout** ([app/src/arrange.ts](../app/src/arrange.ts)): a wizard step after the roles, opened on "Terminer" (Finish) (a Start keeps everything as it is), or on its own from the system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Disposition des écrans" (Screen layout), or `--assistant --disposition`; at the screens step, "Placer librement…" (Place freely…) opens it on a role and returns to the wizard afterwards. The panel appears on the playfield screen; each screen shows its zones, the selected one with its corners. Rows: the zone (any role), its screen ("Aucun écran" (No screen) for a secondary role), the kept aspect ratio ("Libre" (Free), 16:9, 16:10, 4:3, 5:4, 21:9, 4:1, 9:16, 3:4), full screen (with a locked ratio: the largest zone at that ratio, centered), horizontal and vertical position, width, height, exact size, exact position, placement above, below, left or right of another role on the same screen (shrunk, ratio kept, if there is not enough room), alignment to an edge or to the center, snapping, and "Annuler les changements" (Undo changes). Position and size are free to the pixel across the whole screen; a zone never leaves the screen and stays at least 16 px on each side. Opened from the menu, the step starts on the "Zone" row; in the wizard, on "Terminer". The panel docks at the top or the bottom, opposite the selected zone when that zone is on its screen.
  - **With the buttons**: flippers to choose a row, Start to open it, flippers to adjust to the pixel (immediate preview; held down, they speed up to 64 px per step), Start to confirm, Exit to cancel.
  - **With the keyboard**: arrows to move the zone (row closed) or to adjust in both directions; digits then Enter for the exact size (width only when a ratio is locked) and for the exact position (X, then Y).
  - **With the mouse**: click a row, or a zone to select it; drag a zone to move it, even onto another screen (it moves there at once, at the same size if it fits, and becomes embedded if it lands in the playfield zone); drag a corner to resize it, the opposite corner staying in place. Each zone shows its corners and its label (role, size, full screen, window or embedded), placed in the first corner of the zone that nothing on the screen covers (top left as a rule; on a playfield rotated by 270°, the panel moves to the left and the playfield label to the right; `placeZoneLabels`, `app/renderer/screen.js`), and each screen without the panel reminds you of the gesture in its hint line, at the bottom, in the page flow: laid as a banner over the screen, it covered the label of the top zone (cabinet backglass). Dragged edges snap within 12 px to the screen's edges and middle and to the edges of the other zones (`snapRect`); the "Aimantation" (Snapping) row turns it off, or hold Alt during the gesture. The playfield cannot be moved to another screen with the mouse: the roles step decides that.
  - **Layout on its own**: saves the model and only rewrites the windows and the framing in `VPinballX.ini`; a role whose screen is merely unplugged keeps its screen.
- **Adjusting in VPX**: a windowed or embedded display moved or resized in the VPX interface (the window's display page, which frames the embedded display) is reported at startup like any key changed by hand. "Garder" (Keep) takes it back into the role's zone, and the key stays managed. A full-screen window that was retouched is left to the user.
