- **PuP-Packs**: choosing the pack option compares the proportions of the zone, not those of the whole screen, and follows the type of the DMD screen (FullDMD or DMD only).
- **Screen wizard**: at the DMD step, the flippers offer the free screens (a wide screen twice: FullDMD on the whole screen, then DMD only as a centered 4:1 strip), then the bottom of the backglass screen (a 4:1 strip for a DMD alone, or 16:9 for a FullDMD with the DMD on top; the backglass keeps the top) and embedding in the playfield (4:1, at the edge opposite the flippers, over 40% of the width). A two-screen cabinet therefore always has a place for the DMD. The offered zone is drawn on the screen, dotted, at the exact place where VPX will show it. The saved layout is offered again by default, unchanged if it was retouched in VPX since then ("comme enregistré" (as saved)). Fine adjustment is done in VPX (see above).
- **Menu**: one window per physical screen, not per role. Roles that share a screen are drawn in the same window, each in its zone; an embedded DMD is drawn on the playfield without rotating, as in VPX. Two windows on the same screen would fight over the full-screen state that covers the taskbar (`coverDisplay`). The playfield page fits within its zone, sizes included (`cqmin`, relative to the zone).

### DMD screen: FullDMD or DMD only

VPX 10.8.1 has only one window for the DMD and the FullDMD (ScoreView). Yet the screen that shows it does not play the same part on every cabinet, and Cyber Pinball has you say it once and for all (`dmdKind` in the cabinet model: `fulldmd` or `dmd`). Code: [packages/core/src/dmdscreen.ts](../packages/core/src/dmdscreen.ts).

| | FullDMD | DMD only |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Screen | a whole wide (16:9) screen, often the speaker panel; or a 16:9 strip under the backglass | a 4:1 strip: real DMD screen, strip under the backglass, 16:9 screen behind the speaker panel's cutout, embedded in the playfield |
| Menu | the `(FullDMD)` media over the whole screen (`(DMD)` shrunk if missing) | the `(DMD)` media in the strip (`(FullDMD)` shrunk if missing), black around it |
| VPX | `[ScoreView]` full screen; `Priority.B2S = 50`: the `.directb2s` FullDMD image, the game DMD inset (`ScoreViewDMDOverlay`, `ScoreViewDMDAutoPos`) | window on the strip; `Priority.B2S = 0`: the game DMD through ScoreView, without the FullDMD image squeezed into the strip |
| PuP-Packs | option that puts its screen 5 (FullDMD) in the DMD window | option that puts its screen 1 (DMD) there, or nothing |

- **Where to say it**: in the wizard, "Écrans et rôles" (Screens and roles) step: a free wide screen is offered twice for the DMD, "FullDMD ?" (the whole screen) then "DMD en bande 4:1 au centre ?" (DMD as a 4:1 strip in the middle?); a screen that is already 4:1 only as "DMD ?"; the bottom of the backglass at 4:1 (DMD only) or 16:9 (FullDMD); embedding in the playfield is always a DMD only. Placed freely, the DMD takes the type of its zone's shape. Or, without redoing the wizard, system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet setup) > "Écran du DMD" (DMD screen): the current state, then "C'est un FullDMD" (It's a FullDMD) and "C'est un DMD seul" (It's a DMD only), each with its sketch. Start saves and relaunches the menu; its windows, `VPinballX.ini` and the PuP-Packs follow. The zone only changes if it does not have the shape of the choice: a FullDMD alone on its screen gets the whole screen; a DMD only, a centered 4:1 strip; under the backglass, the bottom strip at the new shape (`withDmdKind`). A strip that already has the right shape is kept as it is.
- **Screen layout**: the type is kept as long as the DMD zone does not change; dragged to another shape, the shape decides (`dmdKindAfterArrange`, boundary at 8:3). The zone is named after the type ("FullDMD" or "DMD").
- **Configurations from before this choice**: nothing is rewritten. Without `dmdKind`, the type is deduced as before from the zone's proportions (4:1 or 16:9, boundary at 8:3). Only one case is ambiguous: a DMD strip alone in the middle of a wide screen that shows nothing else (`dmdKindQuestion`), perhaps a FullDMD cut down by mistake (for example: a 4.78:1 strip in the middle of a 16:9 screen; the menu showed the FullDMD image shrunk into it). Synchronization reports it ("À vérifier", To check), the menu announces it at startup with a banner, and the "Écran du DMD" entry carries the red dot until it is answered. A type that contradicts the zone's shape (FullDMD in a strip, DMD only on the whole screen) is reported for information.
- **Background of screens without the playfield**: backglass, DMD and topper show their zones on pure black, never the ambient background nor the 9:16 playfield frame of the desktop menu (`#media-bg`, specific to the playfield; `layout-check.ts` rejects it on any other screen).
- **VPX 10.8.1 limit**: in the DMD window, VPX keeps the first renderer that draws. The PUP plugin never draws the table's DMD: for a table that draws its own, the doctor gives the window back to the DMD (`[ScoreView] Priority.PUP = 0`, `pupHidesDmd` rule), and the pack's screen 5 does not show there during play.
- **Tests**: [dmdscreen.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dmdscreen.test.ts).

### FullDMD artwork in game

Case of *The Walking Dead* (16:9 DMD screen): in the menu, the FullDMD screen shows the table's `(FullDMD)` media, artwork at the top and a frame at the bottom with a still DMD in it; in game, only a plain DMD stretched across the whole width was left. Code: [fulldmdframe.ts](../packages/core/src/fulldmdframe.ts), [fulldmdtable.ts](../packages/core/src/fulldmdtable.ts), [app/src/fulldmd.ts](../app/src/fulldmd.ts).

- **What shows in game**: VPX 10.8.1's ScoreView window, on the whole FullDMD screen. Three plugins can fill it, by priority: a PuP-Pack (screen 5, otherwise 1), the B2S plugin (FullDMD image of the `.directb2s`, `DMDImage`, with the DMD inset), then the ScoreView plugin, which draws the ROM, FlexDMD or UltraDMD DMD with its layouts (`plugins\scoreview\layouts\*.scv`: "Fit: Contain", "Glass-Dirty" glass). Neither dmdext nor Freezy: VPX's plugins do their job. On *The Walking Dead*: a `.directb2s` without a FullDMD image (only the backglass image), the PuP-Pack on its "No Topper BG and DMD seperate" option (screens 1 and 5 off): the ScoreView plugin drew alone, on the whole screen.
- **Where the artwork comes from**: the table's `(FullDMD)` media (VPinMediaDB, a 1920 × 1080 image or a video), which only the menu showed. The ScoreView plugin cannot draw a background image: the `- Image` visual of its layouts is rejected ("Not yet supported", `ScoreView.cpp`, commit af26b2d93), and its glass (`Glass`) only covers the DMD rectangle.
- **Options compared**: (a) Cyber Pinball keeps the artwork on the screen and places the DMD window in its frame: faithful to the menu, for every table (ROM, FlexDMD, UltraDMD), nothing to install, and the menu window already stays behind VPX during the game (in-game background); (b) the three-screen `.directb2s` or the PuP-Pack already dress the FullDMD: kept as is when that is the case, but many tables have neither, and the PUP plugin never draws the table's DMD (`pupHidesDmd`); (c) one `.scv` layout per table: impossible in 10.8.1, for lack of a background image. Chosen: (a), and (b) when the table already has it.
- **The frame** (`detectDmdFrame`): a rectangle whose four sides are clean edges, at least 40 % of the image wide, with a DMD's proportions (2.5:1 to 6.5:1); of a frame with several strokes (double neon, gold fillet), the inside is kept. A video is read at 10, 35, 60 and 85 % of its length, off screen, by the converter ([media-convert.ts](../app/src/media-convert.ts)): the frame is the one most of those images show. The result is kept per media (path, size, date) in `data\cache\fulldmd-cadres.json`. On the cabinet: a frame found for *The Walking Dead*, *Game of Thrones*, *2001*, *Terminator 2*, *Star Trek* and *Goonies*; none in the *Guardians of the Galaxy* video.
- **Before each game** (`prepareDressing`): when the DMD screen is a FullDMD (not embedded in the playfield), the table has a `(FullDMD)` media with a frame (its own or the cabinet's, not the `.directb2s` image), it draws a DMD (unknown script: yes), and neither the `.directb2s` (FullDMD image) nor a PuP-Pack (DMD window, non-zero `Priority.PUP`) already dresses it, the table's `.ini` gets `[ScoreView] ScoreViewFullScreen = 0`, `ScoreViewWndX`, `ScoreViewWndY`, `ScoreViewWidth` and `ScoreViewHeight` on the frame (the media is shown whole and centred in the zone, as in the menu). The placement written is remembered in the `.info` (`CyberPinball.fullDmd.window`): Cyber Pinball updates it (other media, other screen) or removes it when there is no artwork any more; the menu log records it ("Habillage FullDMD (…) : …"). Otherwise nothing changes: the DMD window keeps its whole zone.
- **During the game**: the menu window on the DMD screen keeps the artwork (`momentScreen`, `dressed`), still, or animated if "Media during the game" is set to "animated"; also when set to "off", since it is part of the display. The game's DMD sits in the frame, VPX's window on top.
- **Fine tuning**: in game, both MagnaSaves > "Position du DMD" (DMD position, below), or VPX menu > "Display Settings" > "ScoreView Display": position and size to the pixel, then "Save as Table Override". A placement that is no longer the one Cyber Pinball wrote is the user's: it is never touched again. The doctor shows it ("Écrans placés pour cette table", Screens placed for this table); "Revenir à la disposition générale" (Back to the general layout) removes it, and the frame found comes back at the next game.
- **Detection**: the doctor reports "Le DMD cacherait l'habillage FullDMD" (The DMD would hide the FullDMD artwork, `fullDmdHidden`, warning) when the DMD placement set for the table covers at least half of the artwork (`dressCoverage`; `ScoreViewFullScreen = 1`, for instance); the fix puts the DMD in the frame. During the game, the menu reads Windows' window stack again: a VPX window covering the artwork is logged ("Partie : le DMD de VPX recouvre … % de l'habillage FullDMD", `liveDressCoverage`).
- **Diagnosis of every table**: `npm run habillage` (`scripts\diagnostic-habillage.ps1`: Electron on a hidden Windows desktop, nothing appears on screen) lists each table, the frame found, the DMD placement in game, and marks "[À CORRIGER]" (to fix) those whose DMD would cover the artwork. Nothing is changed in the tables.
- **Tests**: [fulldmdframe.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/fulldmdframe.test.ts) (frame, video, placement on the screen, table `.ini`, invariant on simulated screens, doctor) and the `fullDmdHidden` trap in [doctor-traps.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/doctor-traps.test.ts).

### DMD position during a game

Moving the DMD during a game was still complicated, VPX's menu mixing its many settings (the FullDMD's among them) without saying what they are for. During a game, both MagnaSave buttons together (without them, Credit and Start; left Ctrl and right Ctrl on a keyboard) now open Cyber Pinball's **in-game settings**, "Position du DMD" (DMD position) first, before anything else. Code: [dmdplace.ts](../packages/core/src/dmdplace.ts) (positions, table `.ini`, menu), [app/src/in-game-menu.ts](../app/src/in-game-menu.ts) (combination, windows, VPX's window), page [app/renderer/in-game-menu.html](../app/renderer/in-game-menu.html).

- **What moves**: the DMD, that is VPX 10.8.1's "Visual Pinball Score View" window ([ScoreView]), where the ScoreView plugin draws the dots of the ROM, FlexDMD or UltraDMD. During the adjustment, Cyber Pinball moves and resizes it itself (`setWindowBounds`, `SetWindowPos` without activating it): VPX follows an auxiliary window resized from outside (`SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_RESIZED`, `Window::OnResized`, `player.cpp`). You see the real DMD move, on any screen of the cabinet.
- **VPX paused**: the panel's window takes the foreground; VPX loses it and pauses (`Player::OnFocusChanged`, `IsPlaying` requires its game window to have focus), and the buttons, keyboard and joystick alike, go to the menu. On leaving, VPX takes the foreground back, and the game resumes.
- **The combination** is read by Cyber Pinball while VPX is in the foreground: keyboard keys through `GetAsyncKeyState` (`keysDown`, codes from `windowsVk`), joystick buttons through SDL polling (`watchJoystickButtons`, `SDL_JOYSTICK_ALLOW_BACKGROUND_EVENTS`). It is no longer written to `[Input] Mapping.InGameUI` (`inGameMenuSetting` keeps F12 only, and removes the combination written before): VPX no longer opens its menu on top.
- **The menu** (on the playfield screen, in its zone, facing the player; on the side away from the DMD when it is on the same screen): "Écran" (Screen: each screen of the configuration, named after its roles), "Position horizontale", "Position verticale", "Taille" (Size; locked format: the height follows), "Format" (4:1 first, 3:1, 2:1, free, 16:9…), "DMD" (shown or hidden), "Enregistrer pour cette table" (Save for this table), "Revenir à la position générale" (Back to the general position), then "Tous les réglages de VPX" (All VPX settings) and "Reprendre la partie" (Resume the game). An unavailable line stays there, greyed out, its reason given when you select it (DMD hidden, table without a position of its own, only one screen, no key opens VPX's menu).

| Button | In the in-game settings |
| --- | --- |
| Flippers | change line; in an open line, less and more (held: faster) |
| Left, right MagnaSave | change line; in a position line, move up and down |
| Start | open the line, then confirm it |
| Exit | cancel the open line; otherwise resume the game |
| Keyboard | arrows: move the DMD both ways; + and −: its size; Enter, Esc |
| Mouse | drag the DMD frame (even onto another screen, `planDrag`); drag a corner to resize it; Alt: no snapping |

- **Where it is saved**: `<data>\positions-dmd.json`, by cabinet configuration then by table (`DmdPlace`: screen fingerprint, zone in fractions, hidden or not). A table without a position follows the configuration's general position (the wizard's zones, written to `VPinballX.ini`). "Revenir à la position générale" removes the table's position. Leaving with unsaved changes asks: save, discard (the DMD goes back where it was), or keep adjusting.
- **Before each game** (`prepareDmdPlace`, after the FullDMD artwork): the table's position for the connected configuration is written to `[ScoreView]` in the table's `.ini`: `ScoreViewOutput` (1, or 0 hidden), `ScoreViewDisplay`, `ScoreViewFullScreen = 0`, position, size, and `Priority.B2S = 0`. Never during the game: VPX could rewrite that `.ini` when it quits. The keys written are remembered (`written`); a table that no longer has a position (or whose screen is missing) loses them, except those changed since in VPX's menu.
- **On a FullDMD**: the FullDMD picture stays around it, only the DMD's dots are placed where chosen. `Priority.B2S = 0`: the B2S plugin no longer draws the `.directb2s` FullDMD picture in the small window; Cyber Pinball's in-game background shows the FullDMD media behind. A position set this way comes before the FullDMD artwork (`dressUpdate` keeps it, "place set in game"); the doctor reports it neither as "Écrans placés pour cette table" (Screens placed for this table) nor as "Le DMD cacherait l'habillage FullDMD" (The DMD would hide the FullDMD artwork) (`DoctorContext.dmdPlaced`).
- **No DMD window in the game** (DMD off or embedded in the playfield): the menu says so; the chosen position is adjusted and saved, and shows in the next game.
- **On the wheel**: this setting changes nothing on the wheel, which has its own (the table's page > "Gérer la table…" > "DMD sur la roue", DMD on the wheel; see [Menu DMD](#menu-dmd)).
- **Limits**: a PuP-Pack drawing in the DMD window (screen 5 or 1, non-zero `Priority.PUP`) stays there, shrunk to the chosen position. The keyboard's letters and punctuation follow its layout (`windowsVk`); a cabinet's keys (Shift, Ctrl, digits, F1-F24) do not.
- **Tests**: [dmdplace.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dmdplace.test.ts) (positions by configuration and by table, table `.ini`, menu with the buttons, the keyboard and the mouse, position read from VPX's window), [buttons.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/buttons.test.ts) (`Mapping.InGameUI` without the combination, `vpxMenuKeys`, `windowsVk`), [pupvisible.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/pupvisible.test.ts) (windows in the foreground: only on the player's request, closed at the end of the game).

### Adjusting the display during a game

Every visual setting whose result has to be seen is made **during a game**, on the real picture, with VPX 10.8.1's own menu: the table's framing (above), the position and size of the backglass, DMD and topper (`DisplaySettingsPage`, read in the code at commit af26b2d93), and the look of the displays. A PuP-Pack, for example, often draws a frame on the backglass (bottom centre) where the DMD must land, and each pack has its own.

1. **Enter VPX's menu**: from the details screen, "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game) (the table starts with the menu open on framing, and the game does not count in the statistics); or, during any game, both MagnaSave buttons pressed together (Credit and Start on a cabinet without MagnaSave), then "Tous les réglages de VPX" (All VPX settings) in Cyber Pinball's in-game settings (above); F12 on a keyboard directly. `[Input] Mapping.InGameUI` keeps F12 only, **even without recorded buttons**: it is then the only `[Input]` key Cyber Pinball writes (`inGameMenuSetting`, `packages/core/src/buttons.ts`), and the combination that earlier versions wrote there is removed. Exit is never part of it: on its own, it quits the game.
2. **Adjust**: "Point Of View" for framing; Exit goes back to the menu's home page, then "Display Settings" > "Backglass Display", "ScoreView Display" (the DMD) or "Topper Display": position and size to the pixel, applied live; the window can also be dragged with the mouse.
3. **Save** (Start): "Save as Table Override" writes to the table's `.ini`, for that table only; "Save Globally" to `VPinballX.ini`, for every table.

Buttons in VPX's menu (`InputManager.cpp`), listed on the adjustment's loading screen ("Réglage de l'affichage" (Display adjustment), `displayKeys`):

| Button | In VPX's menu |
| --- | --- |
| Left, right MagnaSave | change line |
| Flippers | adjust the value |
| Start | save: for this table or for all |
| Credit | cancel the changes |
| Plunger | default value |
| Exit | previous page, then close the menu; with the menu closed, quit the game |

| The role is… | Adjustable live | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| on a zone of a screen ("Windowed" window) | position and size | the case of the DMD placed on the backglass |
| alone on its screen (full screen) | no | switching to windowed in this menu writes `FullScreen` straight to the general settings, applied at the next launch |
| embedded in the playfield | position and size | outlined in green while the page is open |

- **After the game**: a position saved for the table gives the doctor the finding "Écrans placés pour cette table" (Screens placed for this table) (information, with sizes and positions), and its fix "Revenir à la disposition générale" (Back to the general layout) removes those keys only (`tableWindowsFinding`, `packages/core/src/doctor.ts`). A position saved for every table is a managed key changed by hand: the menu asks at startup whether to keep it (see "Several roles on one screen: zones").
- **Outside a game**: in "Réglages de cette table" (This table's settings), visual settings (framing and camera, windows, `[DMD]` and `[Alpha]` display profiles, the DMD's place in the B2S picture, PuP screen margins) carry the "En partie" (In game) badge and can no longer be changed; only the table's own value can be removed. Cabinet measurements (screen width, lockbar) remain adjustable.
- **Who draws in the DMD window**: the PUP plugin never draws the table's DMD; it only reads its frames to recognize scenes (PuPCapture, `PUPManager::ProcessDmdFrame`). If it takes the ScoreView window (a pack with a screen 5, or a screen 1 not placed on the backglass), the score of the ROM, FlexDMD or UltraDMD does not show there. VPX reads the priority from the table's settings: for a table that draws its DMD (PinMAME ROM, `CreateObject("FlexDMD.FlexDMD")` or `UltraDMD.DMDObject`, DMD folder) and whose script does not write the score on the pack itself (`LabelNew`, `LabelInit`), the doctor writes `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP = 0` in the table's `.ini` ("Le PuP-Pack cacherait le DMD de la table", The PuP-Pack would hide the table's DMD, `pupHidesDmd`), and the menu does it by itself before the game. The pack keeps the backglass and the topper; a value already set for the table in the VPX menu is kept. Case found on *2001* (UltraDMD score, "DMD on Fulldmd" option).
- **Limits**: without MagnaSave, you cannot change line in VPX's menu (`UIUp`, `UIDown`); the stacking order of two overlapping VPX windows (DMD on the backglass) is left to Windows.

## LED and feedback (DOF)

DOF (DirectOutput Framework) drives the cabinet's LEDs, flashers, contactors, knocker and shaker during games, from the configuration made on [configtool.vpuniverse.com](https://configtool.vpuniverse.com). In plugin mode, VPX 10.8.1 ships it: its `DOF` plugin links libdof (vpinball/libdof `eef645d`, pinned by VPX `af26b2d93`). Nothing to install.

**What libdof reads** (libdof sources quoted in [dof.ts](../packages/core/src/dof.ts)):

- `GlobalConfig_B2SServer.xml` in `%APPDATA%\VPinballX\10.8\directoutputconfig\` (the VPX preference folder, whatever `-Ini` Cyber Pinball passes), otherwise in `.\directoutputconfig\` of the VPX folder;
- the configtool's `directoutputconfig<N>.ini` (or `ledcontrol<N>.ini`), searched in `IniFilesPath`, then the table folder, then the GlobalConfig folder, then the VPX folder: **the first folder that has any wins**;
- `Cabinet.xml` only when the GlobalConfig's `CabinetConfigFilePattern` points to it. Without it, libdof finds on its own only Pinscape, Pinscape Pico, LedWiz, DudesCab, UMX, PacLed64, PacDrive, Ultimate I/O, FT245R and PinOne. **Teensy, Wemos D1, PinControl and Art-Net only exist for it when declared in Cabinet.xml**;
- the plugin only starts for a table with a PinMAME ROM: electromechanical and original tables without a ROM get no feedback in plugin mode.

**Addressable LEDs and the KL25Z.** The KL25Z (Pinscape) drives on/off or dimmable outputs, not the data signal of WS2812 strips: strips wired to it never light up. They need their own controller (Teensy with TeensyStripController, Wemos D1 with WemosD1MPStripController, DudesCab, Pinscape Pico), declared in `Cabinet.xml`. The KL25Z keeps the buttons, the plunger and the regular outputs. The screen says so when it sees a KL25Z and no addressable LED controller.

**Controllers recognised without opening anything.** `detectDofControllers` reads the USB IDs from the passive list of `probeUsbDevices` (as for real DMDs):

| Controller | USB ID | `directoutputconfig<N>.ini` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pinscape KL25Z | `FAFA:00F0`–`00FF` (unit = PID & 0xF + 1) or `1209:EAEA` | 50 + unit (51 by default) |
| LedWiz | `FAFA:00F0`–`00FF`, zebsboards `20A0` | unit (1 to 16) |
| Pinscape Pico | product name “Pinscape Pico” | 119 + unit |
| DudesCab | `2E8A:106F` | 90 + unit − 1 |
| PacLed64, PacDrive, Ultimate I/O | `D209:1401`–`1404`, `D209:1500`, `D209:0410`–`0411` | 20–23, 19, 27–28 |
| SainSmart / FT245R | `0403:6001` (common FTDI ID: reported as uncertain) | 40 + rank |
| Teensy (addressable LEDs) | `16C0:0483` (PJRC serial) | the one of the Cabinet.xml `LedWizEquivalent`, 30 for the configtool |

`FAFA:00Fx` is shared by the LedWiz and the Pinscape in LedWiz emulation: since a LedWiz is never a joystick, such a device recorded as a joystick in the “Boutons” (Buttons) step is a Pinscape (`cabinetJoystickHids`, from the IDs carried by the SDL token). Otherwise both numbers are offered. The unit number of a native Pinscape or of a DudesCab is set on the board: 1 is assumed, and said.

**What Cyber Pinball writes.**

- `[Plugin.DOF] Enable = 1` (managed key, `vpxDofSettings`) as soon as a controller is plugged in, declared in Cabinet.xml, or ini files exist. Otherwise nothing: the plugin stays as VPX or the user left it. Changed by hand, it is reported like the other managed keys.
- The configtool's ini files, on import (see below): they replace every previous `directoutputconfig*.ini` and `tablemappings.*`, set aside in `<data>\sauvegardes\dof\<date>\`.
- `Cabinet.xml` and `GlobalConfig_B2SServer.xml` (`IniFilesPath` and `CabinetConfigFilePattern` on `{GlobalConfigDir}`) when addressable LEDs are described, **only if the file is missing or carries the “Written by Cyber Pinball” mark** (`planLedWrite`). A hand-written file is never replaced: the screen says which one and why. A hand-written GlobalConfig that already points to a Cabinet.xml is followed.

The folder is **shared by every VPX 10.8 on the PC** (another cabinet frontend included): it is the same cabinet, the same controllers.

**System menu > “Borne” > “LED et retours (DOF)”** (LED and feedback) ([app/src/dof-menu.ts](../app/src/dof-menu.ts)):

- **Controllers**: those recognised over USB (with the ini number they read) and those only declared in Cabinet.xml;
- **Configuration**: the ini files found and their folder, how many ROMs are configured, Cabinet.xml (written by Cyber Pinball or by hand, number of strips);
- **To check** (`dofFindings`): no ini; a controller without its ini; a Teensy plugged in but not declared; a Cabinet.xml that no GlobalConfig points to; a Cabinet.xml COM port that is missing; a controller type libdof does not handle (SSF, Hue); a strip no output targets; a `LedWizEquivalent` without its ini; `IniFilesPath` not found; an unreadable file;
- **“Ouvrir configtool.vpuniverse.com”** (Open configtool.vpuniverse.com): the page opens in “Récupérer” (Fetch), with the user's VPUniverse account; the ZIP from “Generate Config” comes back to the screen and is imported. The site sits behind a Cloudflare challenge: no automatic download, the user clicks;
- **“Importer la configuration du configtool…”** (Import the configtool configuration…): an already downloaded ZIP (`planDofImport`); a ZIP without `directoutputconfig*.ini` is refused; a Cabinet.xml or GlobalConfig from the ZIP never overwrites a file in place;
- **“Bandes de LED adressables…”** (Addressable LED strips): the board first, in three families (libdof: a `LedStrip` needs a controller that takes values, `ISupportsSetValues`):
  - **Teensy (TeensyStripController) and Wemos D1**: serial port (picked among the plugged-in ports, or typed), outputs of the board;
  - **Art-Net** (WLED on an ESP32, or any other Art-Net node): the node's IP address (empty: broadcast on the whole network) and first universe (0 for WLED); one `ArtNet` controller per universe, each of 512 channels, so 170 LEDs at most (`ArtNet.h`, `DMX_CHANNELS`), numbering starting again from 1 on each universe; a longer strip is split into two strips on two universes. **Alignment with WLED** (`wledSegments`): in “Multi RGB”, WLED lays out 170 LEDs per universe one after the other (LED k: start universe + k ÷ 170, channels 3 × (k mod 170) + 1 to 3). Since a DOF strip never spills over from one universe to the next, it starts in WLED at 170 × (its universe − the first one) + its `FirstLedNumber` − 1: for one strip per universe, “Start” 0, 170, 340, 510 in LED Preferences, with gaps in the numbering. With consecutive “Start” values (0, 120, 240…), the second strip would receive shifted colours. The editor shows, under “Dans WLED (LED Preferences)”, each strip's “Start” and length and the total LED count, and the save notice repeats them. WLED settings recalled on screen: Config > Sync Interfaces > Realtime, “Art-Net” receive, start universe, DMX start address 1, “Multi RGB”; Config > WiFi Setup, “WiFi sleep” unticked; a fast level shifter (74AHCT125, 74HCT245) between the ESP32 (3.3 V) and the strips, bidirectional BSS138 modules being often too slow for WS2812. Art-Net is “ready to test” in libdof, not proven;
  - **DudesCab (MX mode, firmware 1.9 or later) and Pinscape Pico**: strips are described in the board's tool, which libdof reads from the board (`UMXController::UpdateCabinetFromConfig` creates the DudesCab `LedStrip`s itself; Pinscape Pico LEDs are outputs of the board). The editor explains it, and “Ajouter une bande” and “Enregistrer” stay greyed out with that reason: a `Cabinet.xml` would duplicate them.

  Then each strip: name, controller output, columns × rows, wiring (libdof's 16 arrangements, serpentine included), colour order (GRB for most WS2812), brightness, DOF output (1, 4, 7…: the toy's one in the configtool). LEDs are numbered from one end of the controller to the other, output 1 first, strips of the same output one after the other (`stripFirstLeds`). “Enregistrer” (Save) writes the files, backs up those it replaces, and says what to declare in the configtool (controller number 30, each toy on its output). An incomplete description keeps “Enregistrer” greyed out, with the reason. “Ajouter une bande” (Add a strip) starts from a template: vertical strip on the left or right of the playfield (1 × 120, first LED at the top), ring around a backbox speaker (24 × 1), matrix at the back of the playfield (32 × 8 serpentine), or other (1 × 60); each takes the controller's first free output and the next DOF output. The editor reminds how to wire a long data cable up to the backbox: Ethernet cable from the OctoWS2811, each data wire twisted with a ground, 100 to 330 Ω resistor at the start, 5 V brought by its own cable. Test case: a reference cabinet, two strips of more than 100 LEDs on either side of the playfield and two speaker rings, each on its own output (`dof.test.ts`);
- an unavailable function (VPX missing, no built-in browser) stays visible, greyed out, with its reason.

Nothing is sent to the controllers outside a game: no port is opened, no LED is lit for a test. Testing happens in a game, on a table with a PinMAME ROM.

**Table doctor** (`dofFindingsFor`, only on a cabinet that has ini files): `dofLocalIni` (a `directoutputconfig*.ini` in the table folder hides the cabinet's when `IniFilesPath` is not set; fix: move it to the recycle bin), `dofNoPinmame` (table without a ROM: no feedback in plugin mode), `dofNoConfig` (the configuration has nothing for its ROM, by libdof's three rules: case-insensitive match, then `<rom>_…`, then prefix). Never blocking.

**Cabinet health**: a “LED et retours (DOF)” check (`dofLines`), absent on a cabinet with no controller and no configuration; one line per point to check, otherwise “DOF prêt” (DOF ready), each leading to the screen.

**VPX settings**: the “Essentiels” (Essentials) tab has a “Retours de la borne (DOF)” (Cabinet feedback) group: `[Plugin.DOF] Enable` (managed), and `[Controller] DOFContactors`, `DOFKnocker`, `DOFChimes`, `DOFBell`, `DOFGear`, `DOFShaker`, `DOFFlippers`, `DOFTargets`, `DOFDropTargets`: table sound, DOF, or both (VPX default). Only scripts that use `controller.vbs` read them.

Tests: [dof.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dof.test.ts) (IDs, ROM rules, folder reading, Cabinet.xml written then read back, hand-written files never replaced, findings from the real case to the fixed one, import, `[Plugin.DOF] Enable`) and three trap tables in [doctor-traps.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/doctor-traps.test.ts). In a sandbox, the DOF folder is `<sandbox>\sandbox\directoutputconfig` (`CYBERPINBALL_DOF_DIR` for another one); `CYBERPINBALL_USB_DEVICES` simulates the controllers.

## Game media

Each game can have its own presentation media: logo, playfield video, backglass, display, topper, loading video, music, launch sound, flyer and rules. The menu shows each one on the intended screen at the right moment. When a media item is missing, the menu shows a fallback drawn by Cyber Pinball in its place: never an empty screen, never the Windows desktop.

A game's media go in its table's folder, in a `medias\` subfolder, and are named after the folder, `(Type) <nom du dossier>.ext`, as VPX 10.8.1 suggests: `medias\(Backglass) Terminator 2 (Williams 1991).mp4`. The `.vpx` name works too, for media specific to one version. The flyer and the rules can have several pages: `(GameHelp) <nom>.png`, then `(GameHelp) <nom> 2.png`, `… 3.png`. No per-screen setting: each media item's screen, proportions and rotation come from the cabinet model.

| Type (file) | In the menu | What it is for | Where and when it is shown | Formats | Without a file |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `(Wheel)` | "Logo" | Identifies the table in the menu list, on a transparent background. | Playfield: table list; attract mode | png, apng, webp, gif | the title as stylized text |
| `(Playfield)` | "Plateau" (Playfield) | The table in play, in the background behind the list. | Playfield, dimmed under the list; attract mode | mp4, webm, m4v, or an image | discreet teal smoked glass, no fake table visual |
| `(Backglass)` | "Fronton" (Backglass) | The game's backglass image or animation. | Backglass: selected table, loading, attract mode; during play if VPX leaves the screen free | mp4, webm, m4v, or an image | the lit image of the `.directb2s`, otherwise a card: title, manufacturer, year |
| `(DMD)` | "Afficheur" (Display) | Animation for the 4:1 display. | Narrow display (4:1), at the same moments as the backglass | same as the backglass | the "Afficheur large" media scaled down, otherwise the title in dots on a 128 × 32 grid |
| `(FullDMD)` | "Afficheur large" (Wide display) | 16:9 animation for a wide display. | Wide display (16:9), at the same moments | same as the backglass | the "Afficheur" media centered, otherwise the title in dots |
| `(Topper)` | "Topper" | The animated banner above the backglass. | Topper, at the same moments | same as the backglass | animated logo and Cyber Pinball brand, manufacturer and year |
| `(Loading)` | "Chargement" (Loading) | Video played while VPX loads the table. | Playfield, from the Start press until the game appears: full screen on a cabinet, in the 9:16 frame on a desktop | mp4, webm, m4v | backup loading screen: brand, title, manufacturer, year, progress bar |
| `(Audio)` | "Musique du jeu" (Game music) | The music of the selected table. | Backglass speakers, as long as the table is selected ("Musique des jeux" (Game music), on by default); in attract mode if attract-mode sound is set to reduced | mp3, ogg, m4a, flac, wav | the cabinet music, otherwise silence |
| `(AudioLaunch)` | "Son de lancement" (Launch sound) | A short sound at launch. | Backglass speakers, once, on Start | same as the music | nothing (or the loading video's sound) |
| `(GameInfo)` | "Prospectus" (Flyer) | One or more pages presenting the game. | Playfield, on request: details screen > "Prospectus", page by page | png, apng, webp, jpg, jpeg, gif, mp4 | a card generated from the `.info`: title, manufacturer, year, authors |
| `(GameHelp)` | "Règles" (Rules) | One or more rule cards. | Playfield, on request: details screen > "Règles", page by page | same as the flyer | the rules found automatically (card and text from the `.vpx`, rulesheet, video), then the game controls |

`(Other)`, `(RealDMD)` and `(RealColorDMD)` (Popper, PinballX) have no screen in Cyber Pinball: they are read, kept and ignored. Popper's old formats (`flv`, `f4v`, `avi`, `mov`, `wmv`, `mpg`, `mpeg`) cannot be played: the media doctor reports them, and the import leaves them aside.

For each screen, the menu takes the first media found: `(Type) <nom du .vpx>` (the `.vpx` name), `(Type) <nom du dossier>` (the folder name), VPinFE's fixed names (`bg.png`, `wheel.png`, `table.mp4`…), the `.directb2s` image (backglass only), the **cabinet default** `<racine>\media\(Type).ext`, and finally the built-in fallback.

### Adding media

- **Details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media)** (the simplest way, with the buttons or the mouse): each media item with its role, the screen and the moment it appears, a preview, and what the menu shows today. You take a file copied into `<racine>\import` (from a USB stick), pick it on this PC or drag it onto the window; you replace, remove, set the playfield half-turn and the game's volume. Nothing is written before "Enregistrer" (Save), which shows the plan first. See the Manager: "Médias du jeu" section.
- **With the table**: an archive containing media laid out the PinUP Popper way (`POPMedia\…\Wheel\<jeu>.png`), the PinballX way or the VPinFE way is recognized when the table is added ("Ajouter une table" (Add a table)); the media are renamed `(Type) <dossier>` in `medias\`. A media pack on its own, without a `.vpx`, completes an installed table. See "Importing media".
- **From a PinUP Popper or PinballX installation**: system menu > "Cyber Pinball" > "Migration" > "Reprendre les médias" (Import media, from PinUP Popper / PinballX). Everything is copied; the original installation is not modified.
- **By hand**: copy the file into `medias\` under the name `(Type) <nom du dossier>.ext`. The menu shows it immediately on every screen, without restarting (see "Live changes"). The doctor (details screen > "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and repair)) reports a name that does not match the game, a file stored outside `medias\`, an unreadable format, proportions that do not fit the screen, and offers the rename.
- **Cabinet defaults**: a file `<racine>\media\(Type).ext`, with no game name, is used for every game that lacks that media type: a topper in your game room's colors, ambient music, a background video. It takes precedence over the built-in fallback.

- **Online, on demand**: "Chercher les médias" (Search for media) takes everything VPinMediaDB has for the game, with a plan to approve ("Media from VPinMediaDB"). For one specific media item, "Voir les propositions" (See suggestions) shows every version found, online and on the cabinet, and you choose from the previews ("Suggestions for each media item and deep search").

### Flyers and rules

Details screen > "Prospectus" (Flyer) or "Règles" (Rules) (the number of pages is written under the action): the page fills the playfield screen, rotated for the player, whole and undistorted. The flipper buttons go to the previous or next page, Start to the next one and then back to the details screen after the last page, Exit back to the details screen; with the mouse, the buttons under the page do the same. A video page (`mp4`) loops, muted. Without a file, the flyer is a sheet generated from the `.info` (a backglass-style card with the title, then manufacturer, year, authors, version, description). Code: `sheetPages` ([packages/core/src/mediaview.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaview.ts)), the `pages` screen in `app/src/library.ts`, rendering by `renderPages` in `app/renderer/library.js`.

**Rules found automatically**. The rules are no longer limited to the cards the user stored: Cyber Pinball looks for what explains the game by itself, and shows it before the controls, which are almost always the same. Page order:

1. the table's rules cards (its `(GameHelp)` media); without them, the card the author stored in the `.vpx`, read without writing anything to the table's folder;
2. the rules text the author stored in the `.vpx` (`TableRules`), without the card it points to;
3. a public rulesheet: the one from The Pinball Primer that the VPS catalogue gives for the game (`tutorialFiles` field), otherwise the one from Pinball Rule Sheets (pinballrulesheets.com), matched in the site's list by manufacturer and full title (edition ignored; Stern before 1999 is Stern Electronics, another manufacturer). The text is split into pages of about 1,100 characters, each part under its heading ("(suite)" (continued) when it goes on); it shrinks to fit, never below 20 px at 1080; 80 pages at most, the rest is read on the site;
4. a tutorial video on YouTube known to VPS (a tutorial comes before an overview): its thumbnail, title, authors and a QR code to watch it on the phone;
5. the game controls, always last, with what each source gave: found, searching, or missing and greyed out with its reason (none for this game, no network, game not recognised in the VPS catalogue, catalogue not downloaded yet).

**Game guide**. For a few games, a guide written for Cyber Pinball comes before everything else: a "Le but" (The goal) page, then one objective per page, and "Bonus et astuces" (Bonuses and tips). Each page shows the playfield seen from above (the flat image stored in the `.vpx`, otherwise a playfield drawn from its walls, ramps, bumpers, targets and holes), the numbered shots in place, the shots from the flippers or the plunger, and in the margins the label of each shot on the page; the text below says what to do and what it is worth. Shots are named after their element in the `.vpx`: their positions are read from the player's table (`readVpxPlayfield`). The guide is matched by the VPS game; another recreation of the same game, where fewer than three quarters of the named elements exist, does not get it (badly placed markers would be worse than nothing). The guide screen is drawn at a fixed size, portrait (1,080 × 1,860) or landscape (1,760 × 960) depending on the room, then scaled; it takes the whole booklet screen, the actions stay below. Ten guides: Back to the Future, Terminator 2, Game of Thrones, Guardians of the Galaxy, Star Trek, The Walking Dead, The Goonies, The Matrix, City Hunter, 2001 - A Space Odyssey; written by Claude from each table's script, rulesheet and card, to be reviewed (each guide's "À vérifier", in the research notes). Code: [packages/core/src/tableguide.ts](../packages/core/src/tableguide.ts), guides in `packages/core/src/guides`, rendering in [app/renderer/guide.js](../app/renderer/guide.js). Tests: [tableguide.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/tableguide.test.ts).

Each page states its source under the text: the table, or the site, its address, and "en anglais" (in English) when the interface is not in English. The sheets are written by communities and state no licence: Cyber Pinball ships none of them. They are read on the player's machine when the game page opens, kept in `<data>\cache\regles` (read again after 30 days, the pinballrulesheets.com list after 7; without a network, the kept copy is used; a missing page is not asked for again before the delay). The video thumbnail goes to the media cache (`regles-video-<id>.jpg`). The search runs in the background when the game page opens, once per table (again if the `.vpx` changes); the booklet fills in when it returns. The VPS game is the one the table names, otherwise the deep search when it is certain (`tableGame`), or, between tied leads, the only one with the manufacturer and year the table gives (Williams's 1991 Terminator 2 rather than "JP's Terminator 2", which has the same ROM); only the VPS database already downloaded is used, opening the rules never starts a download of the database. With the mouse, "Commandes de la partie" (Game controls) jumps to the last page. Code: [packages/core/src/tablerules.ts](../packages/core/src/tablerules.ts) (reading pages, matching, cache), [app/src/table-rules.ts](../app/src/table-rules.ts) (page order, sources), `renderHelp` in `app/renderer/library.js`. Tests: [tablerules.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/tablerules.test.ts).


### In the menu windows

On the table menu, each menu window shows the media of the selected table (elsewhere, see "Outside the table menu: the default display"). The lookup follows this order (`resolveMedia`, [packages/core/src/medias.ts](../packages/core/src/medias.ts)): `.vpx` name, folder name, VPinFE names, `.directb2s` image (backglass), cabinet defaults, then the built-in fallback.

| Screen | Media | Display |
| --- | --- | --- |
| playfield, behind the menu | `(Playfield)` | `cover`, darkened under the list; rotated so the media's flippers land on the cabinet's flipper edge (a portrait file has its flippers at the bottom, a landscape file on the right, as in Popper and VPinMediaDB) |
| menu list | `(Wheel)` | logo above the title of the selected table and in a fixed-size box in front of each title in the list (the title's initial when there is no logo); in the wheel, each table's medallion. Without a logo, the centre medallion writes the title (three lines at most) and the others carry the game's initial, large: shrunk to a neighbour's place, a name fell below the 20 px floor (13.6 px on a cabinet). Name and initial are never seen together: when a medallion leaves the centre, the name goes out in 60 ms, before being shrunk below 20 px, then the initial lights up (`fillWheelItem` and `placeWheelItem`, `app/renderer/frontend.js`; `.wheel-initial`, `frontend.css`) |
| backglass | `(Backglass)`, otherwise the lit image of the `.directb2s` | `contain` on black |
| DMD | `(FullDMD)` on a FullDMD screen, `(DMD)` on a DMD only ([DMD screen](#dmd-screen-fulldmd-or-dmd-only); without an answer, from the proportions of its zone), otherwise the other one | `contain` on black; around a strip, black |
| topper | `(Topper)` | `contain` on black |

- **Zones**: each role of a shared screen (backglass and DMD strip, DMD embedded in the playfield) has its own media in its zone.
- **Playback** (`app/renderer/media.js`): videos loop, muted, except the playfield background video when the "Son des vidéos du menu" (Menu video sound) setting asks for it (see "Menu sound"); 280 ms crossfade on table change; the media of the two neighboring tables on each side are preloaded, and a video you just left is kept ready for a while (eight items at most, four of them videos); the playfield background is paused under an opaque overlay (help, details screen, message); all videos are paused when the window is hidden. Fast navigation stacks nothing up: a layer still waiting for its first frame is dropped.
- **Fallback** when there is no media or on a playback error: the components of `app/renderer/fallback.js` (task T3) as soon as `frontend.html` has loaded them (`window.CyberFallback`), otherwise a minimal fallback: title, manufacturer and year on black, shrunk until they fit their zone, and nothing on the playfield background. The drawn topper shows the brand and the **game's title** (two lines at most), not the manufacturer and year, which are already on the backglass and in the menu.
- **Background during play**, the "Médias pendant la partie" (Media during play) setting on the "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode) screen (system menu; Start moves to the next choice, saved in `<données>\medias.json`): **frozen** by default (first frame, drawn once on a canvas, with no video decoder open during play), **animated** (the playfield and the screens VPX occupies stay frozen), or **off** (black, with a discreet title). See "Loading and play".
- **`cyber-media://` protocol** ([app/src/media-protocol.ts](../app/src/media-protocol.ts), rules in [packages/core/src/mediaview.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaview.ts)): the only path from the windows to the files (the CSP of `frontend.html` allows nothing else for images and videos). It only serves a media file (an extension from the catalog) placed directly in the `medias\` folder of a known table, in `<racine>\media\` or in the `.directb2s` image cache (`<données>\cache\medias\`), plus, one at a time, the files that "Médias du jeu" (Game media) previews before storing them (import folder, file picked on this PC, `allowPreview`); built-in fallbacks are drawn by the window, with no file. Subfolders, escaping with `..`, alternate data streams, symbolic links and any other extension are refused (403). It answers range requests (206), which video playback needs. The address changes with the file's date and size: a media file replaced under the same name is not served from the cache.
- **Live changes** ([packages/core/src/mediawatch.ts](../packages/core/src/mediawatch.ts)): a media file added, replaced or removed (any type: wheel logo, playfield, backglass, DMD, FullDMD, topper, loading video, music, launch sound, `.directb2s`) and a table added, renamed or deleted show up immediately on every screen, without restarting, whatever the path: "Médias du jeu" (Game media), "Chercher les médias" (Find media), suggestions, adding through "Récupérer" (Fetch), a file copied by hand. One single mechanism: the menu watches `<racine>\tables` and `<racine>\media` (recursive `fs.watch`, native on Windows); 400 ms after the last write (a copy produces a burst of them), it rescans the tables (`.vpx`, folder added or renamed) or forgets its resolved media (`MenuMedias.invalidate`; the `.directb2s` is read again while the previous image stays on screen, with no fallback in between), then sends its state to every window again. What VPX writes next to the tables during a game (`.ini`, logs) triggers nothing. In the wheel, a table already on screen keeps its place: only its content changes, the new logo replacing the old one (or the title) once loaded; the selection stays on the same table. Since file addresses change with their date and size, no screen keeps the old image: the `.directb2s` image cache is stamped the same way and the dimensions cache checks date and size, so there is nothing to repair at startup. A folder missing at startup (`<racine>\media`) is watched as soon as it exists (when the tables are rescanned); where watching is impossible (network drive), resolved media expire after 10 s.
- **`.directb2s` image**: extracted in the background, one table at a time, the first time the table comes up; the fallback shows in the meantime, then the image fades in to replace it.
- **Playback errors** (undecodable video, damaged image): reported by the window to the main process with the error code, written to the console and to the single log `<données>\logs\medias-erreurs.json` (format described in "The media doctor"), one entry per file; an entry disappears when the file changes. The doctor reports them.
- **Tests**: [mediaview.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/mediaview.test.ts) (addresses, allowed folders, rotation for each flipper edge, DMD or FullDMD, `.directb2s`, setting, error log, CSP and rendering hookups), [mediawatch.ts](../packages/core/src/test/mediawatch.test.ts) (folder watch, new file after a change of each type, `.directb2s` re-read without a fallback, the real renderer's wheel showing a logo added to a table already on screen, without moving).

### The table menu on a desktop, from 5:4 to 16:9 and wider

On a desktop (playfield zone wider than tall, `html.menu-desktop`), the table's media sits in a 9:16 frame on the left, full height; the wheel or the list, and the information, are on the right. Dimensions in reference pixels (`--u`, the zone height is 1080); the playfield frame never changes (x 64 → 627). With a band wheel (Médaillons, Bandeau, Minimaliste, Arche), the game's backglass is on the right, the wheel at the bottom, and the layout follows the screen width (`app/renderer/frontend.css`, `html.menu-desktop[data-menu-band]`):

- **16:9 and wider**: a band 1236 wide (`--b` = 1236 / 1080 `--u`), 34 from the right edge; a 16:9 backglass above it, aligned with the top of the playfield, the wheel overlapping its bottom; the help bar is aligned with the bottom of the playfield.
- **From 16:10 to 16:9**: the whole group shrinks to the room left on the right of the playfield (a band of `min(1236, W − 684)`, its left edge at 650). The alignments stay: top of the backglass on the playfield's top, help bar on its bottom; the wheel overlaps the backglass less.
- **Narrower than 16:10** (3:2, 4:3, 5:4): **compact layout**, two zones. On the right of the playfield, a column from 675 to 64 from the edge; the 16:9 backglass at the top, the whole band at the bottom, both as wide as the column; between them, the empty space stays dark (from 26 in 3:2 to 313 in 5:4). Before, the backglass and the band kept their 16:9 dimensions and covered almost all of the playfield frame; only a strip of the playfield stayed visible (16:10 overlapped too).
- **Texts of a shrunk band**: each font size has a floor (`max()`), larger than drawn when the band shrinks: 20 px from 16:10; in the compact layout, 26 for the title (23 on the Médaillons plate, so that it fits there with manufacturer · year), 23 for a long title, manufacturer · year and the counter, 22 for the help (20 px are only 14 pixels on a screen 768 high). Manufacturer · year stack above the centered counter, the title above them, without touching. In the compact layout, the title fits on one line ("…"), across the column for Bandeau and Minimaliste (the Arche and the Médaillons keep their drawn slot: widened, the title crossed the medallions sliding along the Arche's legs); the name in the center medallion, on two lines at most; the Médaillons counter never wraps. The medallions' name and initial have their floor too (`--floor-text`, computed outside the wheel), including while the name leaves or reaches the center.
- **Nothing moves** from one table to the next: all these dimensions depend only on the screen size. At 16:9 and wider, and for the title list, the rendering is the same as before, pixel for pixel.
- **Proof**: [cadres-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/cadres-menu.test.ts) checks that each menu frame belongs to its zone and that the guard sees two zones overlapping; [plancher-textes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/plancher-textes.test.ts) redoes the computation of the wheel's floors.

### Outside the table menu: the default display

The cabinet screens (backglass, DMD or FullDMD display, topper, and the playfield background) only show a table's media while it is the subject. Outside the table menu, nothing of the last table on the wheel stays on screen or keeps playing: every screen switches to the **Cyber Pinball default display**, silently, and going back to the wheel gives the selected table its media and music back.

| Open screen | Cabinet screens | Sound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Table menu, attract mode | media of the selected (or shown) table | its music, or the sound of its video |
| Loading, game | media of the table being played (see "Loading and game") | launch sound, then VPX |
| A table's details screen and what opens from it ("Médias du jeu" (Game media) and its proposals, flyer, rules, "Fichiers" (Files), "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What is missing to play), "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and repair), "Modifier les informations" (Edit details), that table's VPX settings, the file picker and on-screen keyboard opened from these screens) | media of **that** table: it is the subject, and you see what you are adjusting | its music |
| Failed game ("La partie n'a pas pu se dérouler" (The game could not run)) | media of the table that was played | its music |
| System menu and everything that opens from it (adding, catalogue, downloads, ROMs, settings, cabinet configuration, language, updates…), global VPX settings, controls help, messages, quit confirmation | default display | nothing |

- **Default display** (`defaultMediaView`, [packages/core/src/mediaview.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaview.ts)): each screen's built-in fallback with no game title, which draws the brand in the art direction (`app/renderer/fallback.js`, teal black background, signal green rules): backglass with the brand's cover ("CYBER" above "PINBALL" in signal green, light, wide and widely spaced Saira, the art direction's illustration on the right, see [Brand backglass and DMD](#brand-backglass-and-dmd)); DMD display with the stacked brand in its dot font; FullDMD with the stacked brand inside its lamp frame (live menu DMD: the brand, PRESS START and the clock, see [Menu DMD](#menu-dmd)); topper with the logo and the brand; playfield background in smoked glass. No table file is read, no video runs; the display follows its screen (DMD only or FullDMD). Dark from the first frame: never white at the switch.
- **Rule** (`screensTable`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts)): each menu phase states which table it is about, in an exhaustive table (a new phase does not compile until it is declared there). The system menu only keeps a table for a screen that declares it: a table's own screens (details, flyer, editing, doctor, removal) carry it, a described screen gives it when it opens (`openCustom(build, at, table)` in `app/src/library.ts`), an input or a notice keeps the one of the screen it opens from. Any new screen that says nothing gets the default display.
- **Trials**: the layer report (`cyberEtat()`) says "marque" (brand) for the default display, "repli" (fallback) for a table that lacks that media.
- **Tests**: [ecrans-par-defaut.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/ecrans-par-defaut.test.ts) (each phase, each screen, a renderer component for each, `frontend.ts` wiring).

### Menu sound

The menu sound comes from a single window, the playfield one (`app/renderer/menu-audio.js`). The main process decides what to play at each moment (`menuSound`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts)); everything is set in **System menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode)** (`app/src/menu-settings.ts`), saved in `<données>\medias.json` and applied immediately.

| Moment | You hear | Setting (default) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Navigation, the table's details screen, failed game | the selected game's music, `(Audio)` (otherwise the cabinet's, `<racine>\media\(Audio).*`), looping | "Musique des jeux" (Game music): **on** by default, so the wheel is never silent after installation (turn it off for a silent cabinet in a living room); "Volume de la musique" (Music volume) (60%) |
| Navigation, game with no music or music off | the sound of the playfield background video | "Son des vidéos du menu" (Menu video sound) (40%; 0%: muted) |
| Loading | the launch sound, `(AudioLaunch)`, once; without it, the sound of the loading video | "Volume du lancement" (Launch volume) (80%) |
| Play | nothing: everything fades out as soon as VPX's game window appears | — |
| Attract mode | as during navigation, for the table being shown | "Son en attente" (Attract mode sound): **off** by default, or reduced (30% of the volume) |

- **One sound at a time**: the game's music, otherwise the sound of the playfield video; never both. A screen that lets you listen to sounds (music suggestions, [Table sounds](#table-sounds)) silences the menu while it is open. The backglass, display (DMD or FullDMD) and topper videos are **always muted**, on purpose: several videos with sound at once would be a cacophony, and it is also PinUP Popper's usual setting. VPinMediaDB videos have no audio track anyway (`table.mp4` and `dmd.mp4` checked on the cabinet): a game's sound there is a separate file, its music (`audio`, about 650 games out of 1,150), stored as `(Audio)`. A game with no music whose playfield video has no sound therefore stays **silent in the menu**, whatever "Son des vidéos du menu" (Menu video sound) says, even if its display is animated (the case of *Guardians of the Galaxy* on the reference cabinet: no `(Audio)`, no sound in `(Playfield)` or `(FullDMD)`, and no music on VPinMediaDB). To hear it: give it music (details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > "Musique du jeu" (Game music): "Sons de la table" (Table sounds) to take one from its PuP-Pack, see [Table sounds](#table-sounds), or a `medias\(Audio) <dossier>.mp3` file), or add cabinet music `<racine>\media\(Audio).mp3`, played for every game that has none; "Musique des jeux" (Game music) must be "jouée" (on).
- **Per-game volume**: each volume is multiplied by the game's relative volume (details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > "Volume de ce jeu" (This game's volume), `CyberPinball.medias.volume` in the `.info`), without exceeding the maximum. A game set to 50% plays its music at 30% when the setting is at 60%.
- **Transitions**: on table change, the old music fades out (700 ms) and the new one only starts if the table stays selected for 350 ms: scrolling through the list plays nothing. A video's sound follows its crossfade. At launch, the music stops and the launch sound starts.
- **Output**: the backglass output in the cabinet model (the setup wizard's "Son" (Sound) step; it is also the output for VPX music, `SoundDeviceBG`), otherwise the playfield one, otherwise the Windows default output. It is found among Chromium's outputs by its Windows name (the "(vid:pid)" suffix of USB outputs is ignored), then music, launch sound and videos are sent to it (`setSinkId`). Output not found: the default output. For this, the menu windows have the `media` and `speaker-selection` permissions and playback without a user gesture (`autoplayPolicy`), like the wizard. **All of Cyber Pinball's sound** uses this output, not just the menu's: the Windows default output is not necessarily connected to the cabinet (on a PinCab, often an HDMI screen with no speakers; for example, the wheel and the launch played on the cabinet, but not the sounds offered in "Médias du jeu" (Game media)). The sounds and videos of the library screens are sent to it as soon as they are created (`CyberMenuAudio.route`, `app/renderer/library.js`), and any audio or video element of the window that starts playing without going through it is sent there (`play` event, in the capture phase). So are the web pages of the "Récupérer" (Get) window (YouTube, previews), frame by frame (`audioSinkScript`, `packages/core/src/audiosink.ts`, run at each `did-frame-finish-load`): Electron has no per-window output. Its hidden views, and those of media conversion, stay muted.
- **Windows desktop** (windows minimized): the music goes silent, then resumes on return.
- **Off screen**, every application window is muted (`createWindow`, `app/src/offscreen.ts`): an unattended test makes no noise. After each step, the autopilot records what would have played (`CyberMenuAudio.status()`: file, volume, playback, output, video sound) and what each screen shows (`cyberEtat()`: phase, position, table shown in attract mode, and for each media layer the displayed item, video playing or paused, image, frozen image or fallback) in `son.txt`, next to the screenshots.
- **Tests**: [menumoments.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/menumoments.test.ts) (settings and hand-written values, volumes, sound for each moment, menu output, sound media resolved on disk, rendering hookups, muted windows off screen), [sortie-audio.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sortie-audio.test.ts) (invariant: every rendering script that creates a sound routes it, every web page view is muted or routed; sound offered in "Médias du jeu" (Game media) on the cabinet output in the real rendering; web page script).

### Interface sounds

Seven sounds punctuate the menu, on top of the music: four pinball noises, a chime, a ding and a signal, never cutting or lowering it (`app/renderer/ui-sounds.js`). The registry, what triggers each sound and the rule deciding whether it plays are in [packages/core/src/uisounds.ts](../packages/core/src/uisounds.ts) (`UI_SOUNDS`, `uiSoundCue`).

| Sound | When | Source and licence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Solenoid click (`solenoid`) | a neighbouring table in the table menu (left and right buttons, or a click on the wheel) | WebAudio synthesis written for Cyber Pinball (filtered metallic strike, dull body of the coil, plunger return); Cyber Pinball's code licence, no recording |
| Coin drop (`coin`) | Start on "Jouer" (Play): launching a game (not the display adjustment) | WebAudio synthesis (bounces of a metal disc, inharmonic modes, drop into the cash box); same licence |
| Knocker (`knocker`) | back from a game in which a score entered the table's high-score list (`VPReg.stg`, see "High scores") | WebAudio synthesis (hammer on the wooden cabinet, cabinet resonance); same licence |
| Coil thump (`coil`) | cinematic launch: the moment VPX takes over (its game window holds the foreground, see [Cinematic launch](#cinematic-launch)) | WebAudio synthesis (a dry, dull 125 ms "thunk": a filtered noise contact, a muffled body falling from 145 to 95 Hz, a hint of metal around 650 Hz); same licence |
| Invitation chime (`invite`) | a friend invites you to a live game, in the menu as well as during a game (see "Live game" in [Friends](#friends-the-cabinet-network)) | WebAudio synthesis (three rising bell notes, A, C sharp, E, 110 ms apart, with their bell partials; 0.6 s, no reverb): the only musical sound of the registry, far from the tables' mechanical knocks, it can be recognised over VPX's music; same licence |
| Game-with-friends ding (`news`) | news of a game with friends, in the menu as well as during a game: a guest accepts or declines, a player leaves, the game is over (see "Game with friends" in [Tables menu notifications](#tables-menu-notifications)) | WebAudio synthesis (two soft bell notes rising a fourth, E then A, 120 ms apart, half as loud as the chime and without its metal attack; 0.45 s, no reverb): from the chime's family, but news, not a call; same licence |
| Friend signal (`friend`) | a friend just came online, in the menu as well as during a game; once per friend in a quarter of an hour, never for a reconnection within the presence tolerance (see "Everywhere, even in a game" in [Tables menu notifications](#tables-menu-notifications)) | WebAudio synthesis (a single soft note gliding up, from 520 to 780 Hz in 70 ms, with a hint of octave; 0.25 s, no reverb): neither bell nor knock, it is mistaken neither for the chime nor for the ding; same licence |

- **Settings**: **System menu > Médias (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode)**: "Sons de l'interface" (Interface sounds; on by default; Start turns them off or back on, and plays the click) and "Volume des sons de l'interface" (Interface sound volume; 50% by default; choosing plays the coin). Saved in `<données>\medias.json` (`uiSounds`, `uiSoundsVolume`).
- **Never during a game**: the main process requests no sound during a game or in attract mode (`uiSoundCue`); the window, for its part, refuses any sound while the state says "game in progress", silences anything still ringing and closes its audio context: no stream is open on the output while VPX plays. The coin drops during loading, before VPX takes the screens. The one exception is the **handover** sound (`handover` in `UI_SOUNDS`: the coil thump alone): requested at the very moment VPX takes over, it starts at the beginning of the game and rings out there (125 ms), then the audio context closes; the window only accepts it for `coil`, even if another sound claimed to be a handover sound. The second exception is a **call** sound (`call` in `UI_SOUNDS`: the invitation chime alone): a friend's invitation awaits an answer, it rings once during the game; the window only accepts it for `invite`. The third exception is the sounds of **notifications that also show during a game** (`notice` in `UI_SOUNDS`: the ding and the friend signal): each rings with its notification, once, unless "Masquer les notifications pendant les parties" (Hide notifications during games, System menu > Friends) is on; the window only accepts `invite`, `news` and `friend`. All follow the interface sounds setting and volume: turned off, they are silent too.
- **Output**: the menu sound's output (see [Menu sound](#menu-sound)), passed on by `menu-audio.js`; the audio context closes four seconds after the last sound.
- **Fast scrolling**: the same sound does not start again within 45 ms; each table passed clicks, without a machine-gun effect.
- **Licence**: no excerpt from a real pinball machine and no sound of uncertain rights; each sound is computed on the fly by WebAudio, with no file. An added recipe must be too, or come from a CC0 sound whose source is recorded in `UI_SOUNDS`.
- **Adding a sound**: its identifier in `UI_SOUND_IDS`, its entry (name, trigger, source, licence) in `UI_SOUNDS`, its recipe in `RECIPES` in `ui-sounds.js`, the `playUiSound('<id>')` call in `app/src/frontend.ts`, and its row in this table. The test rejects a sound with no recipe, no trigger, or missing from the manual.
- **High scores**: the knocker sounds back from a game when a score has entered the table's high-score list. High scores are read before launch and on return (`readTableRecords`, `newRecords`, [packages/core/src/highscores.ts](../packages/core/src/highscores.ts), the same source as the menu DMD) from what VPX 10.8.1 keeps for the table: `<dossier de la table>\user\VPReg.stg`, where VPW tables and most original tables save `HighScore1..N` and their initials. **Not covered**: PinMAME tables whose ROM keeps its own high scores (`nvram\<rom>.nv`, a raw image of the game's memory, to be read with each ROM's map); for them, the knocker does not sound. Without a reading before the game (missing or unreadable file, or several tables in the same file with no name to tell them apart), nothing is compared and nothing sounds: a high score is never assumed.
- **Off screen** the windows are muted; `CyberUiSounds.status()` reports what would have played (sound, volume) and whether the window is muted, recorded in `son.txt` by the autopilot (`interface` field).
- **Tests**: [uisounds.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/uisounds.test.ts) (nothing plays when off, at 0%, in attract mode or during a game, on both sides; recipe, trigger and manual row for each sound; knocker wired to the high scores before and after the game; each sub-screen of "Médias, son et attente" returns to its own row).

### Loading and play

What each screen shows from launch to the end of the game (`momentScreen`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts); rendering in `app/renderer/frontend.js`):

| Moment | Playfield | Other screens |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loading (from Start until VPX's game window appears) | on a cabinet, the game's `(Loading)` video alone, full screen and rotated according to the flipper edge; on a desktop, the same video in the menu's 9:16 frame on the left, with the panel holding the title and the game's controls on its right; without it, the backup loading screen in its place (`fallback.js`: brand, title, manufacturer, year, progress bar) | the game's media, animated, until VPX's windows cover them |
| Play | first frame of the playfield background, under VPX | a screen VPX **does not occupy**: according to "Médias pendant la partie" (Media during play) (frozen by default, animated, off); a screen it **does occupy** (`.directb2s` backglass, PinMAME DMD, PuP): frozen, since it cannot be seen and must cost nothing |

- **Loading screen** (`loadingScreen`, `app/renderer/frontend.js`). **On a cabinet** (playfield zone taller than wide, `html.menu-cab`), the playfield suits the loading media: it fills the whole screen, alone, with no title, no "Chargement de la table…" (Loading table…) and no controls. Without media, the backup loading screen replaces it, full screen, without its label (`fallback.js`, option `label: false`). The one exception, the in-game display adjustment (details screen > "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust the display during a game)) keeps its reminder, the buttons of VPX's menu (`loadingKeys.display`), as a banner over the media or under the fallback's progress bar. **On a desktop** (`html.menu-desktop`), the loading media (or its fallback) takes the place of the menu's media, in the 9:16 frame on the left (531 × 944 at x 80, y 92, for a short side of 1080); on the right, a single panel (`loadingCard`) uses the frame's measurements: same top, same height, same edge, 80 from the screen's edge, and a gap of 60 from edge to edge, the frame's own distance to the screen's edge. It holds, in the flow, the title (64, or 48 for a long title; two lines at most), manufacturer · year, "Chargement de la table…" (Loading table…) and its signal-green progress bar, then, after a rule, the game's controls; whatever space is left stays below them. The controls (`fitLoadingCard`) take the most readable layout that fits without cutting anything off: two columns (at least 440 wide each) with no key split over two lines, otherwise one column, otherwise two columns with split keys, at a size of 24, 22 then 20 (the design system's floor); then a compact panel (32 margins, title at 48); only as a last resort, a size below the floor rather than a hidden control (a dozen joystick buttons in a 5:4 window). The brand stays in its place, top left. The layout is rebuilt if the window changes shape. Layout guard (`app/src/layout-check.ts`, `html.loading-phase`): on a cabinet, no interface text over the media; on a desktop, the panel does not cover the frame, edges included, and the fallback sits exactly in the frame. Tests: [chargement.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/chargement.test.ts) (the real renderer under linkedom, measurements from `frontend.css`).
- **Slow loading** ([packages/core/src/loadingprogress.ts](../packages/core/src/loadingprogress.ts)). 8 s after Start (`LOADING_SLOW_MS`) without the table on screen, the desk PC loading screen says so, in its panel: the **step actually observed** ("Cyber Pinball prépare la partie" (Cyber Pinball is preparing the game), "VPX est lancé : il charge la table" (VPX is running: it is loading the table) once the process has started, "VPX a ouvert ses fenêtres : la table finit de charger" (VPX has opened its windows: the table is finishing loading) as soon as a VPX window appears before the game's, `onWindows` in `launcher.ts`), the elapsed time ("depuis 12 s" (for 12 s), counted by the renderer every second) and how to stop ("Ctrl+Maj+K ou Exit : arrêter" (Ctrl+Shift+K or Exit: stop)). No percentage, no bar moving with time: nothing is estimated. The step replaces "Chargement de la table…" (Loading the table…) in the panel, the time and the stop under the bar. **On a cabinet, nothing**: the playfield keeps the loading media alone, with no controls or step (the layout guard still refuses any text on the cabinet loading screen). Exit, once the 8 s have passed, and Ctrl+Shift+K at any time during loading stop the launch, through the forced stop door (`game.force-stop`, open to guests): before VPX is launched, it is not launched; afterwards, it closes (polite close, then forced after 5 s); the wheel comes back, with no failure message and no game counted. The log says "Lancement : la table n'est pas à l'écran 8 s après le Start (…)" and "Lancement : arrêté pendant le chargement (…)". Offscreen, `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_SIMULEE_CHARGEMENT_MS` makes the simulated game slow. Tests: [chargement-lisible.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/chargement-lisible.test.ts).
- **Occupied screens**: as soon as the game window appears, then every 2 s, the Windows window stack is read again (`occupiedRoles`): a role is occupied when a window from another process covers at least half of its zone (the whole screen, or its share of a shared screen: DMD strip under the backglass, embedded DMD) above the Cyber Pinball window. The console says so: "Partie : écrans occupés par VPX : backglass, dmd." (Play: screens occupied by VPX: backglass, dmd.)
- **Focus**: none of this shows a window or brings one to the foreground; only the state sent to the menu windows changes, and they stay behind VPX's windows (screen guard).
- **Simulated game** (off-screen tests only): `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_SIMULEE=<rôles occupés>` ("backglass,dmd", or empty) replaces VPX with a fake game (`simulateVpx`, `app/src/launcher.ts`): loading, a 10 s game with the autopilot, return to the menu, without launching anything or grabbing a global shortcut. On screen, the variable is ignored.

### Startup intro

When Cyber Pinball opens, each screen of the cabinet plays its intro animation, once, then **holds its last frame** (the playfield keeps going in a very subtle loop); the first button (or a click) shows the wheel. A button during playback skips the intro. Setting **System menu > Médias (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode) > "Ouverture au démarrage" (Startup intro)**: played by default, or off (the menu opens straight on the wheel). Saved in `<données>\medias.json`, field `intro`. Code: [packages/core/src/intro.ts](../packages/core/src/intro.ts) (settings, files, timing, views), [app/renderer/intro.js](../app/renderer/intro.js) and `intro.css` (the animations), [app/src/intro-menu.ts](../app/src/intro-menu.ts) (the settings screen).

| Time since the start | Playfield | DMD / FullDMD | Backglass | Topper |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 250 – 1,700 ms | a spark rises from the bottom of the screen along a thread of light; at 1,250 ms, ignition at the core: flash, anamorphic streak, ripple, and some thirty threads of light unfold into a spindle | power-on: random dots (DMD) or one round of the lamp frame (FullDMD), then the ball crosses the display from bottom to top (950 ms) | black | black |
| 1,250 – 3,100 ms | pulses run along the threads, which slow down without stopping; the brand takes shape in luminous dust, from the middle outwards (1,950 ms) | the brand is drawn behind a sweeping line (1,350 ms); FullDMD: PRESS START (2,500 ms) | the artwork flickers on like its lamps, then the brand letter by letter | a streak of light crosses it (2,300 ms) and leaves the logo and the brand |
| 3,400 – 4,100 ms | a gleam passes over the brand | a gleam passes over the brand's dots | a gleam passes over the brand | a gleam passes |
| then | the loop, with no jump from the last frame: the threads of the spindle keep turning, very slowly, and the glowing dust drifts on three depths (the far ones finer, blurrier and slower), with a slight sway; "Appuyez sur un bouton pour choisir une table" (Press a button to choose a table) appears (4,300 ms), pulses twice and stays | last frame | last frame | last frame |

- **When**: at startup only, when the menu lands on the wheel (`introAtStartup`); not on a message to deal with (VPX missing, buttons to redo, no table), nor when choosing a configuration, nor when the menu relaunches itself (language, configuration: `--sans-ouverture` option), nor under the test autopilot (unless `CYBERPINBALL_OUVERTURE=1`). The animations start together once every menu window is ready (at most 5 s after they open): until then, black.
- **The Cyber Pinball animations are part of the application**: they are drawn by the renderer (CSS, SVG, dots; the playfield by a WebGL2 shader, `app/renderer/intro-shaders.js`, at most 1,920 pixels high during the animation, less if the graphics card cannot keep up, the last frame at full resolution; without WebGL2, the brand alone), in each screen, at any aspect ratio, turned for the player on the playfield; no file carries them, nothing can delete them. The cabinet's DMD plays the 4:1 display one or the FullDMD one, depending on its screen. On a desktop PC (playfield wider than tall), the playfield plays the backglass intro, full screen. Timing written once (`INTRO_TIMELINE`), sent to the windows with their state; at the end, only the playfield keeps going: its loop, at the animation's canvas size (no size change, so no jump in sharpness), at 20 frames per second at most, 15 then 10 if the graphics card cannot keep up, stopped under the night veil and when the window is hidden; on a screen larger than 1,920 pixels, the brand letters fade in sharp at the end, in a full-resolution 2D canvas laid over the shader, which keeps their glow.
