The download happens in a hidden view; **VPUniverse's timer runs in the page**, as for a member who clicks: the page asks the server for the wait, counts down, then starts the file, and the card shows the seconds left ("Attente du site · 31 s" (Site wait · 31 s)). Cyber Pinball waits and shortens nothing. Start on a queued card removes it; "Arrêter la file" (Stop the queue) stops everything. A download cap stops that site's queue; a page that asks for something (several files to choose from, terms, a check) opens in the visible window, and the file downloaded there goes back to the media item.
- **Receiving**: the file is stored in `<téléchargements>\sites\<site>-<entry>\fichiers`, the archive unpacked; a pack laid out the Popper or PinballX way gives each file's type by its folder. Received files are recorded in `<téléchargements>\sites\recus.json` and suggested on later visits, for this media item as for a pack's other screens ("Reçu de VPForums" (Received from VPForums)). The file awaited by the last card chosen for a media item goes into the draft by itself; another choice made in the meantime is never replaced. Nothing is written to `medias\` before "Enregistrer" (Save); the source (site, entry address) goes into the `.info`.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/mediasites.ts` (categories, addresses, results page scripts, matching, count per media, web search, received files), `sitesessions.ts` (timer, `WAIT_PROBES`), `app/src/media-sites.ts` (search, thumbnails, queue, received files), `app/src/acquire-window.ts` (hidden view, download recipient), `app/src/media-proposals.ts` (cards); tests in `packages/core/src/test/mediasites.test.ts`. The test bench calls the garbage collector during every wait: a hidden view nothing holds any more would vanish at the whim of a GC, and closing it afterwards threw "Object has been destroyed"; `acquire-window.ts` therefore keeps each hidden view until it is closed (`hiddenViews`, `closeHidden`, invariant test in `offscreen.test.ts`).

### Free search: "Chercher moi-même sur le web…" (Search the web myself...)

When the connected sources do not have what you need (game music for Guardians of the Galaxy, when the suggestions only have toppers), you search yourself in the built-in browser, and Cyber Pinball sees what you download arrive.

- **Entry**: on each media item's screen (table sheet > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > a media item), right after "Voir les propositions" (See suggestions), **"Chercher moi-même sur le web…" (Search the web myself...)** opens the "Récupérer" (Get) window without waiting for the suggestions search. Its detail gives the search text ("Built-in browser, free search: “Guardians of the Galaxy table music vpinball”"), "Resumes the page where you left off" once the window is closed, or "Downloading: 1.2 MB of 3.4 MB…" during a download.
- **Bar**: the window opens on a pre-filled DuckDuckGo search (table title without manufacturer or year, screen words in English, "vpinball": `webSearchQuery`, `packages/core/src/mediasites.ts`). Under the usual line, a search line: the **Web**, **Images**, **Videos** (DuckDuckGo) and **YouTube** engines, the field and **"Chercher" (Search)** (`webEngineUrl`, `packages/core/src/webmedia.ts`). The field keeps the text of the last results page, even on the pages after it; switching engine runs the same text again. While you type in the field, keys are not cabinet buttons; Esc always closes. The "Retour à la fiche" (Back to the entry) button becomes "Retour à la recherche" (Back to the search). Closing (Esc, Exit, Alt+F4) keeps the page and its history; the window reopened for the same media item resumes where you left off, with the search text. The status line is a how-to ("the file is checked, then offered for “Musique du jeu”"), not a warning.
- **Video pages**: every page loaded, or changed without reloading (YouTube), is read by a probe (`VIDEO_PAGE_PROBE`): a YouTube video (watch, shorts, youtu.be), Vimeo, Dailymotion, or a `<video>` element on another page. One more line describes it ("YouTube video: “…” · 2:47"). When the page plays an ordinary video file (http(s) address), **"Télécharger cette vidéo" (Download this video)** downloads it like a link and it comes back to the media item, sound included (for music, it is extracted). YouTube, Vimeo and Dailymotion play a segmented stream (`blob:`): the line offers **"Récupérer le son" (Get the sound)** and **"Récupérer la vidéo" (Get the video)** through yt-dlp (see below), the move expected for this media item lit first (the sound for music and the launch sound, the video for a screen). The title is read from the live page: YouTube does not update its meta tags from one video to the next.
- **yt-dlp**: the tool that gets the video or the sound of a YouTube, Vimeo, Dailymotion… page, **alone, without ffmpeg**. It does not ship with Cyber Pinball: the **first** request downloads it from the official repository's GitHub releases (`yt-dlp.exe`, about 18 MB, the bar's line says so before the click), checks it against **two SHA-256 hashes** that must agree (the one GitHub's API gives for the file, and its line in the same release's `SHA2-256SUMS`; a file that differs is deleted, never run) and stores it in `<racine>\tools\yt-dlp` with `version.json` (`packages/core/src/ytdlp.ts`, `app/src/ytdlp-tool.ts`). A new release is looked for at most once a week, on use, and replaces the old one with the same checks; with no network, the installed tool is used as is. yt-dlp never updates itself (`--no-update`). Without ffmpeg, only single-file formats: **the sound alone as M4A** (accepted as is by the music, can be trimmed), otherwise the best sound (WebM Opus, converted to WAV); **the video alone as MP4** H.264 up to 1080p (silent, which is enough for a menu screen). A single file, never a playlist (`--no-playlist`), no user configuration read (`--ignore-config`). For YouTube, yt-dlp needs a JavaScript engine: Cyber Pinball's executable stands in for it (`--js-runtimes node:<executable>`, `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, Node 24; yt-dlp needs Node 22 at least). The retrieval has its line in the bar, like a download (installation, then percentage, amount, speed), and "Annuler" (Cancel), or Start then Start on the cabinet, stops yt-dlp and what it started, keeping nothing. The file received goes through the same check and the same "Received" screen as a download. A failure gives its reason ("private video", "YouTube asks to prove you are not a robot…", "network unreachable"…). yt-dlp is only offered for a search started from a media item. YouTube's terms only allow downloading where the service permits it: what you get is your own call.
- **Detection**: every file downloaded in the window comes back to it (`will-download`, the page's recipient); during the search, **Windows Downloads** is watched too (`watchDownloads`) for what you get in your own browser ("Ouvrir dans mon navigateur", Open in my browser): only media and archives that arrive after opening count (`isMediaDownload`), copied (the original stays where it is), a file still being written (`.crdownload`, `.part`) ignored. Watching stops when you leave the table's media, at most two hours later.
- **Check** (`judgeWebFile`, `packages/core/src/webmedia.ts`): each file, archive unpacked, is judged for the media item from its **content** (first bytes, `sniffMedia`), not only its name: **accepted** as is (a WebM video named `.mp4` is stored as `.webm`), **to convert**, or **refused** with its reason ("web page saved under a file name: the site sent back a page instead of the file", "an image, whereas “Musique du jeu” needs: mp3, ogg, m4a, flac, wav", ".mkv video the menu does not play; converting it would need an external tool (ffmpeg)", "unknown format (.txt)"). A file of a pack laid out for another screen goes to it. Everything is noted in `<téléchargements>\sites\web.json` (`files`, `convert`, `refused`, `windows`); what is of no use is not kept.
- **Conversions with no external tool** (`app/src/media-convert.ts`): Chromium's engine decodes, in a hidden page (a view with no window, in a separate session whose every address is served by the application, with no network): an image (JPEG for the logo, BMP, AVIF, ICO) is redrawn as **PNG**; a sound the menu does not play (Opus, AAC, WebM) or the **sound of a video** (MP4, WebM, MKV, MOV) is decoded, trimmed and written as 16-bit 44.1 kHz **WAV** (`wavFile`). An MP4 or MOV video can also give its sound track **as is**, as `.m4a`, with no re-encoding ("Take its sound as is (.m4a) and assign", `extractMp4Sound`, the same means as "Sons de la table" (Table sounds)). A video in a format the menu does not play is not converted.
- **"Reçu pour « … »" (Received for “…”) screen**: it opens by itself behind the window as soon as the file arrives, if the table's media are still open. It shows the file with its preview (a sound plays when its card is selected), its state ("Accepted as is", "To convert to PNG", "Video sound, to extract to WAV"), **what will be written** ("Stored as “(Audio) Guardians of the Galaxy (Stern 2017).wav”") and what goes to the trash, then the refused files and those that go to other screens. Actions: **"Affecter à « … » et enregistrer" (Assign to “…” and save)** (or "Convert to PNG, then assign"), "Put in the draft" (writes nothing, with the other changes), "Back to the search" (resumes the page), "Keep for later" (stays in the suggestions). Several useful files: one screen shows them all, you choose. Nothing usable: "Received: nothing usable for “…”", with the reasons.
- **Trimming a sound** (game music, launch sound): "Trim, then assign…" shows two cards, **the excerpt** (it plays when the card is selected, on "Assign this excerpt") and **the waveform** of the whole sound (`waveformPng`), the kept part in signal green on a lit band, the rest in dimmed teal, the fade-out darker. Actions: "Start later / earlier", "End later / earlier", "Step: 1 s / 5 s / 15 s", "Fade-out: yes / no" (2 s), "The whole sound". Offered first: the first ten seconds for the launch sound; the whole sound, up to three minutes, for the music (with a fade if it is cut). The excerpt is redone in the background on each change (the decoded sound stays in the hidden page).
- **Immediate assignment**: "Assign … and save" writes **this media item only** at once (`planSingleMedia`, `packages/core/src/mediaedit.ts`, same plan and same trash as "Enregistrer" (Save)): the file copied into `medias\` under its name, the old one to the trash, the origin (host and address, or "Windows Downloads") in the `.info`; the table is read again for the menu. The draft's other changes stay to be saved; this media item's, replaced, is dropped. It is the only write that skips the plan screen: the "Received" screen has shown what would be written.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/webmedia.ts` (engines, `sniffMedia`, `judgeWebFile`, WAV, trimming, waveform, video page probe), `app/src/media-web.ts` (entry, "Received" and trim screens), `app/src/media-convert.ts` (conversions), `app/src/media-sites.ts` (reception, check, Windows Downloads), `app/src/acquire-window.ts` and `app/renderer/acquire.*` (search bar, video page); tests in `packages/core/src/test/webmedia.test.ts` and `ytdlp.test.ts` (including the probe on its sample pages `youtube-video.html` and `video-directe.html`, as a visitor and as a member, detection with a fake "Récupérer" (Get) window and a fake Downloads folder, assigning a single media item).

### Recording a game for the wheel

A table's presentation video on the wheel (the playfield full screen, on a cabinet) is first of all the table in real conditions. Cyber Pinball films it while you play, with nothing to download or install: Chromium's screen capture (DXGI or Windows Graphics Capture) and the MediaRecorder encoder (MP4, H.264, AAC sound), inside the application's Electron.

- **Entry**: table sheet > **"Jouer et enregistrer"** (Play and record) (door `sheet.record`, closed in guest mode). The preparation screen says what will be filmed and starts it: "Lancer et enregistrer" (Launch and record). Its settings, kept for next time in `<data>\enregistrement.json`: **screens filmed** (always the playfield; the backglass and the display as you choose, when the cabinet has them), **start** (0, 5, 10, 20, 30 or 60 s after the table appears; 10 by default), **length** (15, 30, 45, 60 or 90 s; 30 by default), **game sound** with the playfield video (yes by default).
- **Why no gesture during the game** (options weighed): 1. a button combination in game, read by Cyber Pinball while VPX has the foreground: every cabinet button already belongs to VPX (both MagnaSaves open its menu, Start launches a ball, Exit quits), one more gesture would also trigger the VPX action; 2. a keyboard shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+…): useless on a cabinet without a keyboard; 3. **recording armed from the sheet, automatic start and end**: chosen, no button is hijacked, nothing can fire by mistake during a real game, and the player knows in advance when it starts and when it stops.
- **The two events** (`advanceRecording`, [packages/core/src/gamerecording.ts](../packages/core/src/gamerecording.ts)): **`recording-start`**, when the VPX game window has held the foreground (the launcher's `onForeground`) for the chosen delay, to skip the VPX fade-in; **`recording-end`**, at the first of: the length reached (clean end: 0.4 s fade to black, the wheel loop restarts without a jolt; a fade-in too), the table left (VPX closed: the last half second, held back before being written, is dropped, so what comes before the closing is never in the video), a capture failure. An end under 5 s keeps nothing. The log says "Enregistrement (…) : événement de début." and "… : événement de fin (duration, game-end or failed).".
- **Witness**: a discreet pill in the corner of the playfield, top right for the player (far from the flippers and the ball), turned like the menu (`indicatorBounds`): green ring and "Enregistrement dans 7 s" (Recording in 7 s) during the delay, a slowly beating red dot, "REC" and "0:12 / 0:30" while recording, then "✓ Enregistré · 0:30" (Recorded) for three seconds (or, in amber, "Trop court : pas gardé", Too short: not kept). Transparent window, no focus nor click, above VPX, **excluded from capture** (`setContentProtection`, WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE since Windows 10 2004): the video never contains it. It is the only Cyber Pinball window left on top during a game (exception bounded by `pupvisible.test.ts` and `buttons.test.ts`: one corner of the playfield, never the focus, removed three seconds after the end). Page `app/renderer/recording.html` (a single red accent, a low-amplitude beat, still when Windows reduces animations).
- **Capture** (`app/src/game-recording.ts`, `app/renderer/recorder.js`, a window never shown): one capture per filmed screen, at the size just needed (a 4K playfield is captured at 1920 × 1080); each role takes **the zone where VPX draws it** there (backglass and DMD sharing a screen: each its band). The playfield is **always rendered in portrait, flippers at the bottom** (`uprightRotation` from the edge where VPX draws the flippers, `orientationFacts`), 1080 × 1920 at most: that is the standard the wheel knows how to turn (`mediaFlipperEdge`), and it shows it exactly as the player saw it, in every screen orientation; a desktop playfield (landscape, flippers already at the bottom) is cropped in the centre to 9:16. Backglass and DMD keep their orientation, scaled to 1280 wide at most. The sound is Windows' (output loopback), on the playfield video only.
- **Back from the game**: the **"Enregistrement de la partie"** (Game recording) screen previews each video, what it would replace, its size and length, then: **"Utiliser pour la roue"** (Use for the wheel) first opens the plan (`planMediaChanges`: the video stored in `medias\` under the game's name, the old media to Cyber Pinball's recycle bin, the old one's forced half-turn removed); nothing is written before **"Confirmer"** (Confirm), then the wheel shows the new video. **"Garder de côté"** (Keep aside) (and Exit) changes nothing on the wheel: the file waits in `<data>\enregistrements\<.vpx name>\(Playfield) YYYY-MM-DD HH-MM-SS.mp4` and comes first among that media's proposals ("Médias du jeu" > the media > "Voir les propositions", source "Enregistré en partie sur cette borne", Recorded in game on this cabinet). **"Rejouer et enregistrer"** (Play and record again) starts over (the first stays aside). **"Jeter l'enregistrement"** (Discard the recording) sends it to the recycle bin.
- **Simulated game** (off-screen tests): no screen is captured; the recorder draws a fake table the way VPX would draw it (red band at the top, green flippers at the bottom), the rest of the path is the real one. Unattended, the recorded game lasts the delay and the length, plus 3 s; `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_MS` shortens it (end by leaving the table).
- **Proof**: [enregistrement-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/enregistrement-partie.test.ts) (events, delay, length, leaving, failure, witness, fade, settings, playfield orientation in the four orientations round trip with the wheel, desktop crop, shared zones, files, proposals).

## Launching a game

`app/src/launcher.ts` launches the table the way a frontend does: `VPinballX_BGFX64.exe -Ini <VPinballX.ini> -Play <table.vpx>`, then watches the process's windows every 250 ms until it ends. The game starts when the "Visual Pinball Player" window appears (it takes the foreground back if needed, `onPlaying`); VPX has taken over when Windows says that window holds the foreground (`onForeground`, read on every pass: the cinematic launch's coil thump). Closing that window shuts VPX down cleanly (exit code 0). Ctrl+Shift+K stops a stuck game (polite close, then forced after 5 s).

**No screen ever shows the Windows desktop.** The menu windows are no longer hidden during a game: they stay behind VPX's windows, without taking focus from it. The playfield keeps the loading screen (the game's loading video, or a fallback screen; on a desktop, the controls panel) until the game window appears, then each screen switches to its **in-game background**: the game's media, frozen by default, visible only on a screen VPX does not use (topper without a window, backglass without a `.directb2s`, no DMD); see "Loading and play". For this, each window receives a `game` state (table, media, playback of each role), rendered by `renderGame` (`app/renderer/frontend.js`). On return, the menu takes the foreground back as before.

A single rule covers every moment, the **screen guard** (`app/src/screen-guard.ts`, started by `main.ts` before any other window, except the waiting screen for another running frontend):

- **one black cover per screen**, present from startup to shutdown, which follows screens being added, removed and rotated; it never takes focus (`focusable: false`) and only appears once painted black. It covers screens without a Cyber Pinball window: a screen with no role, and screens other than the main one with `--reglages`;
- **screen unplugged and plugged back in by Windows**, even during a game (a graphics card that detects its outputs again can do so every twenty seconds; seen during a game in the headset): Windows moves the windows of the vanishing screen to the main screen, and the screen comes back with another identifier. Its new cover goes at once under the Cyber Pinball windows (`tuckVeil`), never in front of the window of the screen's role; screen names in the reports follow their place, not their identifier. Outside a launch or a game, once the screens have settled, each menu window moved off its screen takes it back (`recoverScreens`, `recoverCovered` in `app/src/cover-display.ts`); during a launch or a game nothing moves (the foreground goes to VPX), and the recovery happens on return, at once then 1.5 s later. A cabinet screen left black under its cover (`veiledScreens`, `packages/core/src/screencover.ts`) is reported ("Écran « FullDMD » : resté noir sous son voile (retour d’une partie) ; la fenêtre du menu repasse devant.", FullDMD screen left black under its cover (return from a game); the menu window comes back in front), counted in the endurance report and written to the screens log;
- **gap-free relaunch** (`relaunchCovered`, the only way to relaunch the application: menu → wizard, wizard → menu, settings → wizard): the new process opens its covers and windows on top of the old one's, which only closes 400 ms after the new one has reported its windows painted (marker file `CYBERPINBALL_RELEVE`, created by `paintedOnScreen` in `app/src/offscreen.ts`; 15 s wait at most): being visible to Windows is not enough, since a window is transparent until its first frame;
- **never a light screen**: when a window appears, Windows 11 paints it in a light color before showing its page, even when already painted (`ready-to-show`) and despite `backgroundColor`. Two distinct flashes, measured on real screens (a probe reading the screen every 8 ms): the light theme's system backdrop (#F3F3F3, "Mica"), as long as the window has received no frame, always for the process's first window and for one to three seconds when the menu starts, on all four screens; and a pale blue fill (#D3E3FD) of about 25 ms each time a window appears already full screen (shown, or restored from the taskbar). `createWindow` therefore creates every window without a system backdrop (`backgroundMaterial: 'none'`): before its first frame, it lets what it covers show through (the black cover). And `coverDisplay` only makes a window full screen once it is shown: not yet shown, it takes the size of its screen and goes full screen when it appears; `minimizeCovered` ("Bureau Windows", Windows desktop) leaves full screen before minimizing it. Tried without enough effect: DWM transitions disabled, Electron's dark theme (gray #202020), appearing transparent then opaque (`setOpacity`: while transparent, the window does not present its frames to Windows, and the flash comes back when it turns opaque);
- **taskbars hidden during a game**: when VPX takes the foreground, before its game window goes full screen, Windows briefly brings the taskbar back in front of the screens it does not occupy (seen on the DMD screen). They come back at the end of the game, when the application closes, and at the next startup if the application stopped in the middle of a game (marker `barres-des-taches-masquees` in the data folder): only the ones Cyber Pinball hid, a taskbar already hidden by another frontend stays hidden;
- **taskbars always behind the menu** (taskbar guard, `packages/core/src/taskbarguard.ts`): Explorer only puts a screen's taskbar behind a full-screen window when that window takes the foreground, and brings it back in front as soon as another window takes it or the taskbar is shown again. At startup, each menu window takes the foreground as it appears; after a game, only the playfield takes it back, and the DMD and backglass never have it: their taskbar, shown again, stayed in front (at times on the DMD, sometimes on the playfield). Now each taskbar is put under the menu window of its screen before being shown again (`placeWindowBelow`, without activating anything), then the taskbar guard runs again when coming back from a game, from the desktop, at every screen or work area change, every time a menu window takes the foreground back, and at every screen guard check: a taskbar in front of a menu window is put back behind (« Barre des tâches (Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd) devant « Cyber Pinball — DMD » sur l’écran « DMD » (retour d’une partie) : remise derrière. »). Never while another program has the foreground (VPX in game, Start menu). A taskbar that Windows brings back in front five times within ten seconds is hidden as during a game, with the same marker, until desktop mode or until the application closes. Nothing is set in Windows: only the window order changes, as Explorer does itself;
- **screens log** (`<data>\logs\ecrans.txt`, 512 KB at most, oldest entries removed): at every switch (menu startup, game start, VPX game window shown, back from a game, Windows desktop and back, screen change, taskbar put back behind), the Windows foreground window, for each cabinet screen the Cyber Pinball window that covers it and everything above it (« BARRE DES TÂCHES DEVANT », taskbar in front; « DÉCOUVERT », uncovered), and the rectangle of each taskbar, shown or hidden, permanently on top or not. "Santé de la borne" (Cabinet health), "Écrans" (Screens) group, says how many times the guard put a taskbar back behind since startup, and flags as to check a taskbar it had to hide;
- **console report**: twice a second, the Windows window stack is read again (`screenCover`, `packages/core/src/screencover.ts`); a screen that stays for more than a second without a Cyber Pinball window covering it, or under a taskbar, produces "Écran « Topper » : aucune fenêtre de Cyber Pinball ne le couvre, le bureau Windows se voit." (Screen "Topper": no Cyber Pinball window covers it, the Windows desktop shows through.), then "de nouveau couvert" (covered again). The endurance test counts these reports per launch ("écrans découverts" (uncovered screens)). "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) in the system menu suspends the rule until you return;
- **desktop mode**, the reverse rule (`app/src/desktop-mode.ts`, system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) or Ctrl+Shift+D): every screen of the PC becomes usable again, however many there are. Every application window leaves its screen, whatever it is (`BrowserWindow.getAllWindows`): the menu windows are minimized, the black covers hidden (`hideCovered`, taken out of full screen first so they do not come back pale blue), and taskbars a game may have left hidden come back. Without this, often only one screen came back, because only the menu windows were minimized and the covers, which have no taskbar button, stayed full screen on every screen. On the desktop, twice a second, a Cyber Pinball window back on a screen (cover of a screen plugged in meanwhile, reopened window) is put away again ("Mode bureau : « … » était revenue sur son écran, de nouveau rangée." (Desktop mode: "…" was back on its screen, put away again.)), and the guard reports a screen still covered, even partly ("Mode bureau : écran « Topper » encore couvert par « … »." (Desktop mode: screen "Topper" still covered by "…".), `desktopBlockers`). Ctrl+Shift+D becomes a global shortcut for the duration of desktop mode (released on return; if another program already holds it, the console says so and the taskbar remains the way back). On return, each screen first gets its cover back (a screen plugged in during desktop mode gets its own), then the windows put away come back in front of it, and the playfield takes the foreground again.
- **complete shutdown, single instance** (`app/src/app-lifetime.ts`, `app/src/single-instance.ts`, rules in `packages/core/src/lifetime.ts`): a menu or wizard window closed from Windows (Cyber Pinball's taskbar button, Alt+F4) closes the whole application, its other windows and covers included, as does the last window with a taskbar button (VPX settings on their own). Before, only the playfield closed: the backglass, DMD, topper and covers stayed open, invisible in desktop mode, and "Cyber Pinball" processes stayed in the Task Manager (reported on 08/11/2026). Guest mode still refuses Alt+F4. A requested shutdown that does not complete is forced after 15 s ("Fermeture : Cyber Pinball tourne encore 15 s après la demande (…) ; sortie forcée." (Shutdown: Cyber Pinball still running 15 s after the request (…); forced exit.), with the windows left open). A second launch does not start next to the first: the open instance leaves desktop mode or takes the foreground again (not during a game), and the second launch closes; it waits up to 8 s for an instance that is closing (dedicated cabinet relaunch, double-click right after quitting). The lock is Electron's, tied to the profile: each test sandbox has its own; the new process of a handover starts at once and takes the lock once the old one has exited. Tests: `packages/core/src/test/fermeture-complete.test.ts`.

Tests pin down the rule: no window is hidden with `hide()` (except the covers, through `hideCovered`, in desktop mode only), the application only relaunches through `relaunchCovered`, and the guard starts before the other windows (`packages/core/src/test/screencover.test.ts`); every window is created without a system backdrop, and full screen and minimizing only go through `cover-display.ts` (`packages/core/src/test/offscreen.test.ts`); in desktop mode, with the simulated screens of `packages\core\ecrans-simules` (1 to 4 screens), no Cyber Pinball window covers anything any more, then every screen is covered again on return, its cover put back before its window (`packages/core/src/test/mode-bureau.test.ts`).

**No editor or audit window.** With `-Play`, VPX 10.8.1 does not create its editor (`PlayTableCommand`, `src/core/AppCommands.cpp`): no table audit (the "Table audit" box only exists in the editor, when opening a table with errors), no script editor (a script error goes to the log and to an in-game notification), no progress window, debugger or table picker. Checked on the development PC with three tables, including Terminator 2 1.25, whose audit reports 18 errors: only the "Visual Pinball Player", "Visual Pinball Backglass" and "Visual Pinball Score View" windows (class `SDL_app`) are shown; "Visual Pinball Topper" is created hidden when the topper is not assigned; nothing more on exit.

Two VPinballX.ini settings still make VPX ask a question in the middle of loading; Cyber Pinball manages them (written at every sync, with manual changes detected and never silently overwritten, like the other managed keys):

| Setting | Value written | Otherwise |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Player] SecurityLevel` | `0`: all ActiveX objects in the script allowed (VPX default) | from 1 to 3, "This table is attempting to create an object of type …" (Yes / No) for each object, even `Scripting.Dictionary`; at 4, all refused and the script stops |
| `[PlayerVR] AskToTurnOn` | `2`: VR off (VPX default); a game in VR has its own INI ("Virtual reality", below) | at 0, "Connection to VR headset failed / Retry connection ?" (Yes / No) or "VR mode activated but OpenXR initialization failed."; the Windows build of VPX includes OpenXR |

Every other visible VPX window is classified (`classifyVpxWindow`, `packages/core/src/vpxwindows.ts`):

- **dialog box** (`#32770`): the game stops and its text becomes the on-screen message, without the buttons. The ActiveX prompt and the VR headset prompt are recognized: the message names the setting to restore. The text is read with `WM_GETTEXT` (`windowTexts`): it is the only way to read an edit box in another process, where VPX puts the audit report and script errors;
- **editor window**, if another version of VPX opened one: the editor (class `VPinball`), the script editor (`CVFrame`), "Preparing Table" and the debugger are hidden (closing them would quit VPX or ask a question), the table audit ("Dialog" box whose text starts with "Table audit:" or "This table contains error(s)") is closed; the game goes on, and the game window takes the foreground back;
- **unknown window**: left on screen.

The last two cases leave a one-sentence report (`LaunchResult.notices`): written to the console at the end of the game, added to the message of a failed game and to the endurance test summary ("autres fenêtres de VPX" (other VPX windows)).

**Script written for an older core.vbs.** *Back to the Future (Data East 1990)* stopped at every launch on "Table script bug: cvpmFlips2.Init is called twice. Maybe 'vpmInit Me' is duplicated in the code ?": its script calls `vpmInit Me` twice in `bttf_Init`. Older core.vbs versions let it through (a second `vpmInit` does nothing more: events already created, same PinMAME settings); the one in VPX 10.8.1-5436 (`cvpmFlips2.Init`) shows that message and quits the game (`QuitPlayer 2`). The scripts VPX provides to tables thus refuse slips of older tables: each known case is a rule in [scriptcompat.ts](../packages/core/src/scriptcompat.ts), which spots it in the script's text (without VPX), fixes it without moving a line, and recognises the message VPX stops on. For `vpmInit`: from two calls in the code on (comments, strings, `object.vpmInit` and a `vpmInit` defined by the script left out), Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` (`scriptpatch.ts`, as for PuP-Packs) replaces each call with `CyberPinball_VpmInit`, defined after the last line, which calls `vpmInit` only the first time; its last line keeps `"compat": ["vpmInitOnce"]`, which the other `.vbs` repairs (PuP-Pack, updated table) keep. The table is not modified; a `.vbs` from elsewhere is fixed itself. Doctor finding "Script écrit pour un ancien core.vbs" (Script written for an older core.vbs) (`scriptCoreCompat`), repaired at installation and before each game (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`); script readings move to version 8, so tables already installed are read again. When the text shows nothing (a single `vpmInit`, in a Sub called twice), the game stopped by that message gets the `.vbs` fixed right away (`repairScriptAfterStop`), and the failure screen says "Cyber Pinball a corrigé le script de la table (…) : relancez-la." (Cyber Pinball fixed the table's script (…): start it again.). Tests: `scriptcompat.test.ts` (detection, lines in place, run by `cscript` on a fake core.vbs: a single call to `vpmInit`, doctor, PuP-Pack, updated table, `.vbs` from elsewhere, stop message) and the `scriptCoreCompat` trap table.

`node scripts/sonde-fenetres.mjs --exe … --ini … --table …` (after `npm run build`) lists every window of a game, hidden ones included, along with what the launcher would do with them; `--commande Edit` opens the table in the editor to check that the editor and the audit are recognized on real windows. Always on copies of the table and of VPinballX.ini.

### Virtual reality

System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Réalité virtuelle" (Virtual reality) (`app/src/vr.ts`, rules in `packages/core/src/vr.ts`): off by default, Start toggles it. The entry carries a "BETA" badge in all its states, as the feature is still being tested: a pill after the label (`badge: 'beta'`, `MenuBadge` type in `app/src/library.ts`, reusable by any menu entry; `LigneMenu` component of the design system). The entry reads the active OpenXR runtime without waking the headset: the `XR_RUNTIME_JSON` variable, otherwise `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Khronos\OpenXR\1`, value `ActiveRuntime` (`packages/win-displays/src/openxr.ts`), written by Meta Quest Link, SteamVR or Virtual Desktop. Its detail names the runtime; with no runtime, or if its manifest is gone, the entry stays visible, greyed out, with the reason. The choice is kept in `<données>\vr\reglage.json`.

When it is on, each game (not "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game)) gets its own VPinballX.ini, `<données>\vr\VPinballX.ini`, or the double of a game with friends, rewritten in place. It is a copy of the cabinet's, which keeps `[PlayerVR] AskToTurnOn = 2`, with:

- `[PlayerVR] AskToTurnOn = 1` ("Autodetect"): VPX 10.8.1 plays in the headset if the runtime answers and the headset is ready, otherwise on the screens, without any question (`Player::Player`). The value 0 is never written: without a headset, it would block the cabinet on "Retry connection ?";
- `[PlayerVR] AddBackglass`: at 1, VPX places a panel above the playfield that shows what the backglass window would draw (B2S and PUP plugins). At 0 for a table that has its own VR room, and so its own backglass already: it is recognised by its script (`tableHasVrRoom`: `RenderingMode = 2`, a `VRRoom` or `VR_Room` option, or at least three `VR_…` objects, comments left out; 7 tables out of 11 on the development PC);
- `[PlayerVR] PreviewDisplay` and `PreviewFullScreen` (`vrPreviewSettings`): the preview of what the headset sees takes the place of the game window, which does not exist in VR: playfield screen (`[Player] PlayfieldDisplay`) and same mode (`PlayfieldFullScreen`, full screen or windowed). The backglass, DMD and topper keep their windows in VR (VPX log of 9 November 2026): putting the preview there would stack them. Without this setting, VPX opens a square window in the middle of the main screen. Nothing is written when the cabinet's VPinballX.ini already picks the preview screen. Worked out again for every game: a screen plugged back in changes its name. If VPX mixes up two screens with the same name, the window is brought back onto the playfield screen during the game (`VPX_ANCILLARY_WINDOWS`);
- the VR settings of the previous game: the `[PlayerVR]` section (table orientation and position, which VPX saves when it quits, `LockFeetToGround`…), except the preview screen, and `[Player] VRPreview`. After a game with friends, they are taken from the double before it goes (`rememberVrSettings`).

In VR, VPX does not open "Visual Pinball Player", but "Visual Pinball VR Preview" (what one eye sees). It also creates the backglass, DMD and topper windows, but never draws them in VR ("Ancillary windows are not yet supported while in VR mode"): the game's INI turns them off (`[Backglass] BackglassOutput`, `[ScoreView] ScoreViewOutput`, `[Topper] TopperOutput` at 0, `VR_NO_ANCILLARY`), and those screens keep the Cyber Pinball background. The backglass in the headset does not depend on them: VPX draws it through the plugins. VPX 10.8.1 freezes when quitting a game in the headset (three games out of three on 9 November 2026, nothing in its log after the last frame): when Windows declares the preview hung (5 s without responding) and it stays so 3 s more, after at least 20 s of play, Cyber Pinball closes it (`isHung`); exit code 0xCFFFFFFF ("Close the program" chosen in Windows' "not responding" window, `VPX_HUNG_EXIT_CODE`) is no longer taken as a failed game. The launcher takes the preview as the game window (`LaunchResult.vr`), and the log says "partie dans le casque" (game in the headset) or "casque pas prêt, partie sur les écrans" (headset not ready, game on the screens). **One pose, fixed.** The place of the headset cabinet (`<données>\vr\casque.json`) is the only one: for every game in the headset it is written into the game's INI (`placeVpxSettings`: `[PlayerVR] Orientation`, `TableX`, `TableY`, `TableZ`, and the lockbar if the cabinet is aligned), so the VPX table is exactly where the menu cabinet was. Nothing moves it by itself: neither the companion (in the Quest room, a space change announced by the Quest is only reported) nor Cyber Pinball. It only moves on request: "Caler la borne" (Align the cabinet), or keypad 5 (at the menu, the cabinet comes in front of the player; in game, VPX recentres the table for that game only). Only if nothing gives a place (neither casque.json nor a place saved by VPX, `vrHasSavedPlace`) would the table be put at the origin of the headset space, often around the player; Cyber Pinball then waits for "Startup done" in `vpinball.log`, and presses VPX's recentring key for it, if VPX has the foreground (`VRCenter`, keypad 5: `vrCenterKey`, `centerWhenStarted`): the table comes in front of the player, lockbar at `[Player] LockbarHeight` (85 cm by default). Keypad 5 recentres it by hand, 8 and 2 raise and lower it; VPX keeps this place when it quits. During a game in the headset, both MagnaSave buttons open the VPX menu directly (its key, F12 by default), which shows in the headset: its "Virtual Reality Settings" page recentres and moves the table. The Cyber Pinball menu, on the screens, would not be seen there. The VPX menu is drawn in the headset only, over the picture of both eyes ("LiveUI" pass after `ApplyStereo`, `Renderer.cpp`): the preview does not show it. Closing the preview window stops the game like Escape (`SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_CLOSE_REQUESTED`, `CS_STOP_PLAY`): with `-Play`, VPX saves the VR settings and closes, and Cyber Pinball goes back to the menu as after any game.

**Load during the game.** During a game, in the headset or not, the menu stays at rest: its windows keep a still image behind VPX, the headset companion has stopped after handing over, and its screens rendered off screen are closed. To check it on the cabinet, every 5 s the menu reads what its processes take (`app.getAppMetrics`: main, Chromium GPU, screen pages, others) and what VPX takes (`GetProcessTimes`, `processCpuMs` in `packages/win-displays/src/processes.ts`), as a percentage of one core, without starting or waking anything; at the end, a log line sums it up ("Charge pendant la partie (dans le casque) : Cyber Pinball 1,2 % d’un cœur en moyenne, 3 % au plus (principal 0,4 %, GPU de Chromium 0,3 %…) ; VPX 95 % d’un cœur en moyenne…", Load during the game (in the headset): Cyber Pinball 1.2% of one core on average, 3% at most (main 0.4%, Chromium GPU 0.3%…); VPX 95% of one core on average…). It is used to compare before and after a change (Link settings, driver, VPX version). Code: `app/src/game-load.ts`, `packages/core/src/game-load.ts`, test [game-load.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/game-load.test.ts).

Limits: the Cyber Pinball menu stays on the screens (Virtual Desktop shows the desktop in the headset); no DMD in the headset for a table without a VR room (VPX only adds the backglass there, and its plugins cannot place an object in the scene); a setting other than the VR ones, changed during a game in the headset, is kept only if it is saved for the table ("Réglages de VPX" (VPX settings) sets the others for all games). Tests: `packages/core/src/test/vr.test.ts`.

#### Menu in the headset

With virtual reality on and the headset ready, the Cyber Pinball menu also shows in the headset, laid out as a cabinet: the playfield lying in front of the player, tilted by 6.5°, the flipper edge towards them, the lockbar 85 cm above the floor; at the far end of the playfield, upright, the DMD (or FullDMD), the backglass above it, then the topper. On a real aligned cabinet, each screen keeps its real size (EDID or size entered in the wizard; otherwise a default width). At a desk, or without an aligned cabinet, the head takes a cabinet's proportions (`STANDARD_HEAD`): the backglass slightly wider than the playfield (1.05 times), the DMD half as wide as the backglass (a quarter of its area), centred below, the topper very small (0.4 times the backglass), sitting on it; each keeps its picture's proportions. In every case, the playfield at least that of a 42-inch cabinet (`MIN_PLAYFIELD_M`, 93 cm: a 17-inch desktop playfield would make a toy cabinet). You navigate with the cabinet buttons as usual: the screens do not change, and Cyber Pinball keeps the foreground.

The menu is drawn separately for the headset (`app/src/casque-vues.ts`): one Cyber Pinball window rendered off screen per cabinet screen, the same page as on the screens, the playfield in the cabinet layout (portrait), whatever the PC screen, which keeps its menu in landscape. Windows shrinks a window, even a hidden one, to the work area of the main screen (1920 × 1032 on a 1080 screen): the page keeps its screen's proportions and a zoom gives it its layout (`headsetViewSize`). Their pictures are GPU textures (`webPreferences.offscreen.useSharedTexture`): their NT HANDLE is duplicated into the companion (`openHandleTarget`, `packages/win-displays/src/handles.ts`), a new one for every picture (Electron opens one per picture and Windows recycles their numbers), and Electron takes its texture back when the companion answers "libre". These windows hear nothing, receive no button, and what they send on their own is ignored (`isHeadsetView`). The native companion `cyberpinball-casque.exe` (`packages/casque-vr`, C++, Direct3D 11 and OpenXR) shows each picture as an OpenXR panel ("quad" layer); without those windows, it duplicates the PC screens (DXGI desktop duplication). It opens no window. Switching between the PC and the headset: with the headset on, the menu is drawn for it alone, and the PC screens rest ("Dans le casque" (In the headset), nothing else to draw); taken off, or the Meta menu open (OpenXR session no longer "FOCUSED", "porte 0"), nothing is rendered for the headset any more, but the PC screens stay on "Dans le casque": no back and forth every time the headset is lifted. They only take the menu back when the headset is switched off or Link is cut (see below). The OpenXR loader is `openxr_loader64.dll` from the VPX copy, loaded at run time: nothing is installed in Windows. Cyber Pinball (`app/src/casque.ts`) starts it when virtual reality is on and the runtime is present, describes the panels to it (`packages/core/src/casque.ts`, `headsetPanels`, one line per screen on its standard input), and starts it again every 15 s while the headset is not ready. Before a game, Cyber Pinball hands over to it ("releve"): it keeps the headset, loading screen included, until VPX takes the player's gaze (its OpenXR session is no longer "FOCUSED"), then stands by, drawing nothing ("releve … veille"); never the Meta home between the menu and the table. When the game ends, the companion starts a fresh session (`HeadsetMenu.restart`): a fresh session takes the headset by itself (about 1 s), whereas the session standing by waited for the player to pick it again in the Link home (5 to 11 s, test of 10 November 2026). A session stopped by the runtime ("session-arretee": Link home, application picked again) starts a fresh session at once; it is not a lost headset. Two minutes at most if VPX does not take the headset; game on the screens (headset not ready for VPX): stopped at once. In the headset, the playfield is in portrait, as on a cabinet, even with a landscape PC screen: its page rendered for the headset is turned a quarter (`headsetTurn`) and takes the cabinet layout. Once per startup, a few seconds after the first pictures, the companion saves each panel's picture as it sends it to the headset (`<données>\logs\casque\<role>.bmp`): a defect seen in the headset can be compared with it. The cabinet stays where it is in the room, like an object: its floor position and heading are kept in `<données>\vr\casque.json` and given back to the companion at each start ("ancre"), after a game as at the next startup. Without a kept place, it settles in front of the player at the first gaze the headset really tracks ("TRACKED" position and orientation: lying down or not worn yet, the headset only gives a frozen pose). Keypad 5 (VPX's recentring key) and the headset's recentring (Meta button held) bring it back in front of the player. A screen is only copied to the headset when its picture changes (a pointer move alone does not count): a still menu costs nothing to the GPU or to Link. **Smoothness** (test of 10 November 2026: "it moves a little too much, it is not very smooth"): the companion's frame loop never waits for the GPU any more; each copied Electron texture is given back ("libre") when an event query issued after the copy has completed, read without waiting on every turn (before, `gpuFinish` waited there for all the GPU work, mipmaps included, on every picture received, enough to miss headset frames and let its reprojection make the screens shake). With the headset on, the companion logs its cadence every 10 s: headset frequency, frames per second, skipped frames (gap between predicted display times), longest copy and render, pictures received ("cadence casque …"). The scene never moves by itself: no animated halo, nothing else. `cyberpinball-casque.exe --diagnostic` lists each graphics card, its screens and what their duplication gives, without showing anything in the headset.

The detail of the "Réalité virtuelle" (Virtual reality) entry says where it stands: "menu de Cyber Pinball dans le casque" (Cyber Pinball menu in the headset), "menu dans le casque dès qu'il est prêt" (menu in the headset as soon as it is ready), or "compagnon absent de cette version" (companion missing from this version). The companion is built with the application (`npm run deployer`, or alone: `npm run casque-vr`) when CMake and the Visual Studio 2022 C++ tools are installed; it only depends on Windows DLLs.

The screens are placed where those of the real cabinet are, without a cabinet body (the real cabinet is there; aligned with the controllers, they fall onto it, see "Aligning the cabinet"): no border, body, legs, frames or wall. Around them, only a matte floor without a grid, slightly lighter under the cabinet, and the chosen table's tint as a 4 cm halo around the head screens (otherwise the brand teal), seen as in the mock-ups despite the headset's linear-light blending (`packages/core/src/casque-scene.ts`, `headsetScene`). The companion draws the screens in the projection layer, with depth (`scene::ImageRenderer`, `packages/casque-vr/src/scene.cpp`), rather than as "quad" layers, which the headset always puts in front of everything: the alignment markers and the controller tips pass in front of the playfield as in the room. Each screen image keeps its mipmaps (`scene::makeImage`) and is read with anisotropic filtering: the lying playfield stays sharp at an angle without shimmering. "Quad" layers are only used if Direct3D refuses the projection layer. When a table starts, everything fades to black in 300 ms (`XR_KHR_composition_layer_color_scale_bias` when the runtime has it). `--rendu-essai <scène> <image.bmp>` renders the scene off screen, the screens carrying an oriented test image (top-left corner red, top-right green, bottom-left blue).

**Controllers: one rule, in the menu as in game** (`packages/core/src/manettes.ts`, `headsetControls`, pinned by [manettes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/manettes.test.ts)). A = confirm, always; B = back or cancel, always; the thumbsticks (both) move the selection, repeated while held; the triggers are the flippers in game, previous and next in the menu (like the cabinet flippers, repeated too); the grips are the MagnaSaves, in game only; X goes to the next collection in the menu (like the cabinet's right MagnaSave on the wheel; elsewhere, between the list and its actions), the credit in game; ≡ on the left controller opens the headset menu (in the Cyber Pinball menu) or the VPX menu (in game). In the menu, the companion reads the OpenXR actions (`oculus/touch_controller` profile) on every frame while the menu has the player's gaze, and sends "bouton <nom> 1|0" on every change: `start`, `exit`, `menu`, `collection` (X), the triggers, and the directions of both thumbsticks (`haut`, `bas`, `gauche`, `droite`: the dominant axis, pressed beyond 0.6, released below 0.4). Cyber Pinball hands them to the menu through the path of the cabinet buttons (`HEADSET_BUTTONS`, `headsetButton`, `headsetKey` in `app/src/frontend.ts`). Headset taken off, Meta menu open or game launched, nothing arrives any more, and any held button is released. **In game**, VPX 10.8.1's OpenXR bindings apply (`XRInputHandler.h`), and its defaults did not follow this rule: B gave a credit, X opened the VPX menu, and the left thumbstick click aligned the table on the controllers, continuously (`VRControllerCalibration`, `m_controllerViewCentering`: the table then follows the middle of the two controllers, at their height). So the INI of every game in the headset receives the rule's bindings (`vpxHeadsetInput`, `VPX_HEADSET_MAPPINGS`): A = Start, and "increase / confirm" in the VPX menu (`UIRight`); ≡ = the VPX menu, and back in that menu (`InGameUI`); B = pause and resume (`Pause`: the VPX menu, stuck to the eyes in VR, no longer opens by accident, decision of 10 November 2026); thumbsticks = up, down, left, right in that menu (the left one also nudges the table, the right one pulled back cocks the plunger, as by default); triggers = flippers (staged at half travel); grips = MagnaSave; X = credit; Y = quit the game (VPX quits on the press, like Escape); right thumbstick click = launch the ball; left thumbstick click = nothing. Each action keeps its keyboard keys and cabinet buttons; VPX's automatic layout is turned off for the controllers (`[Input] Device.OpenXR.NoAutoLayout = 1`), otherwise it would put all its bindings back as soon as one is missing (`InputManager::SetDeviceDefaultMapping`); the plunger and nudging on the thumbsticks are added as sensors if they are not there (`PlungerSensorCount`, `NudgeSensorCount`). The headset menu shows this legend, for the menu and for the game. **Controllers wizard** (for every headset and every configuration): the companion reads each button and axis of the catalogue (`CONTROLLER_ELEMENTS`: triggers, grips, A, B, X, Y, left and right ≡, thumbstick and trackpad clicks, thumbsticks, trackpads), bound for each OpenXR interaction profile that has it (Quest Touch, Valve Index, HTC Vive, Windows Mixed Reality), and reports the profile plugged in ("profil touch"). Each profile has its default setting (`defaultControllerSetting`); the Touch one is the rule above. The first time a headset without a setting for the active configuration is worn, the wizard opens by itself (before alignment): the buttons suggested for these controllers, then confirm to keep them, or back to set them one by one, like the cabinet's "Lanceur" (Plunger) step. One function after another (flippers, confirm and Start, back, headset menu, credit, quit, MagnaSave, launch the ball, plunger, move the selection), "press the button you want"; a button already taken is refused with the function it holds; the back button, once chosen, skips an optional function or returns to the previous step; at the summary, confirm keeps it. The setting is kept per headset and per configuration (`<données>\vr\manettes-<configuration>-<profil>.json`, `readControllerSetting`: an inconsistent file gives the default back) and serves the menu (`menuActionOf`) as well as each game's INI (`vpxHeadsetInput`: buttons, plunger and nudging on their axes). It can be reopened through ≡ > "Manettes" (Controllers) and System menu > Cabinet > "Manettes du casque" (Headset controllers). In the VPX menu, a thumbstick tilted to 0.6 moves the selection (VPX asked for 0.9: the thumbstick pushed all the way). **The VPX menu in VR** is a flat picture attached to the head, drawn straight into each eye's image (`vs_imgui.sc`: position at z = 0, `LiveUI.cpp`), with a fixed crossed offset of 15% of an eye's width to fake depth (`LiveUI.cpp`, line 440) and a fixed scale (line 208): it looks stuck to the eyes, and no INI setting moves it back or places it.

**Headset menu** (≡ on the left controller, in the Cyber Pinball menu): "Caler la borne" (Align the cabinet), "Recentrer la borne" (Recentre the cabinet: in front of the player, facing where they look; its lockbar keeps its width and height, the place is kept), "Retour" (Back), and the controllers legend. It opens over the current screen, which comes back as it was (`openHeadsetMenu`, `library.interrupt`).

**Headset switched off or Link cut.** The companion says "casque-perdu" when its session is lost (LOSS_PENDING, instance lost, xrWaitFrame failing with a session error), or stops without anyone asking outside a handover: it stops, and the PC screens take the menu back by themselves; it only restarts 15 s later. During a game in the headset, it stands by (see the handover): VPX 10.8.1 does nothing about losing its session (`VRDevice::PollEvents` sets `m_applicationRunning` to false and nothing reads it), the game would go on with nobody; so Cyber Pinball closes VPX (`headsetLost`, `vrGameStop`) and the menu comes back on the PC screens. A headset merely taken off the head switches nothing: once worn, the menu stays in the headset (`HeadsetMenu.inUse`), the PC screens on "Dans le casque" (In the headset); only what is drawn for the headset stops until it is put back on.

**Aligning the cabinet.** System menu > Cabinet > "Caler la borne" (Align the cabinet, right after "Réalité virtuelle"), or in the headset ≡ on the left controller > "Caler la borne". Aligning guides step by step, in few words ("Étape 1 sur 3 : coin gauche", Step 1 of 3: left corner…): the player touches the real lockbar with the tip of the controller (a small green cube is drawn there), trigger pressed, left corner (a green marker shows it) then right corner; the headset cabinet comes onto it at once, two green markers on the corners of its lockbar and a third in the middle, the point to touch to check: the screen says how many centimetres from the aligned middle it falls. A keeps, B cancels (the cabinet goes back to its place). Kept: the place of the headset cabinet, lockbar height included (`<données>\vr\casque.json`), and that of the VPX table in the VR INI (`[PlayerVR] Orientation`, `TableX`, `TableY`, `TableZ`, `[Player] LockbarWidth`, `LockbarHeight`), carried into every game in the headset. The calculation is VPX 10.8.1's own when it aligns the table on its controllers (`VRDevice`, `m_controllerViewCentering`), in the room space VPX and the companion share: the table falls on the real lockbar. Corners less than 40 or more than 120 cm apart, a lockbar less than 50 or more than 130 cm above the floor (headset floor wrong) or a corner 5 cm higher than the other are refused, and aligning starts again at the left corner. Without a Quest boundary (no room space) it is refused: it would not last from one session to the next. The Quest keeps this alignment as long as its boundary and floor do not change; after "Adjust floor" or a new boundary it must be done again, and the entry says so. **One alignment per configuration** of Cyber Pinball (`<données>\vr\casque-<configuration>.json`, `cabinetPlaceFileName`): a desk setup and a cabinet have neither the same room nor the same Quest boundary, and the alignment of one never serves the other. **At a desk** (playfield flat, landscape: `calibrationSetting`), there is no lockbar to touch: the alignment screen offers to place the cabinet where the player is looking (Start: its lockbar 30 cm in front of them, 88 cm above the floor, `freePlace`), or on the left then right edge of the desk touched with the tip of the controller; then it never moves, exactly as on a cabinet. As long as the cabinet is not aligned, alignment opens by itself in the headset, once per session, before navigation (`offerCalibration`). The Quest boundary (the room rectangle, the companion's "piece w d") is kept with the alignment: if it has changed at the next start (boundary redone, another stationary boundary: the room origin has moved), or if the Quest announces during the session that it moved its room ("piece-changee"), the log says so and alignment is offered again, with the reason. The companion also reports the eye height each time the headset is put on ("yeux h"): outside 1.0 to 2.1 m, the Quest floor is wrong (the cabinet would be at the player's feet), and the alignment screen gives the path to the setting (Quick settings > Settings > Environment setup > Boundary > "Set floor level"; names vary with the Quest version). A recentring (keypad 5, or "Recentrer la borne" in the headset menu) keeps the aligned lockbar, its width and height. **The game table, exactly on the cabinet**: [pose-vpx.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/pose-vpx.test.ts) replays VPX 10.8.1's calculation (`VRDevice::UpdateVR`, matrices from `src/math/matrix.h`) on the settings written into the INI, for nine orientations and both configurations (touched corners, desk placement), and on the alignment from the 10 November 2026 test: the bottom of the playfield falls in front of the touched lockbar, at its height, the playfield running away from the player. VPX clamps `TableX` and `TableY` to ±100 cm (`Settings_properties.inl`): a lockbar more than about one metre from the centre of the Quest boundary cannot receive the table; the alignment screen and the log say so (`vpxPlaceProblem`). After a game in the headset quit cleanly, Cyber Pinball compares the place VPX writes back on exit (`SaveVRSettings`) with the cabinet's: a table moved during the game (keypad 5, VPX menu) is logged, with the gap (`checkVrPlaceAfterGame`); the next game starts from the cabinet again. **Playfield height**: VPX lays the playfield at "lockbar − bottom glass × scale", read from the table (`readVpxGlass`: `GLAB`, or `GLAS` before format 1080, when the glass was horizontal), 21.6 cm on most installed tables, 10.8 cm on Back to the Future: up to 20 cm below the lockbar, while the menu lays its own 5 cm below (`GLASS_DROP_M`). Everywhere, real aligned cabinet included, VPX's playfield falls exactly on the menu's: `TableZ` and `TableY` put its bottom in line with the lockbar, 5 cm below it, and `LockbarWidth` gives it the width of the menu's playfield (`menuPlayfield`), VPX setting the table's scale to "LockbarWidth / (table width + 2 1/4")" (`placeVpxSettings` with the table and that width; checked for four glasses, two widths, five orientations at a desk and an aligned cabinet). **Two rooms for one Quest** (test of 10 November 2026: table "behind me and below the floor" from the first frame). When the Quest redoes its room under a session already open ("piece-changee": headset put back on, boundary or floor redone), that session switches to the new one, but an application started afterwards, like VPX, may get the other: that day, the same desk edge touched before and after was 1.25 m lower and turned by 131°, and the place aligned in the new room, laid by VPX in the old one, fell behind the player and below the floor (test "l'ancienne pièce du Quest" in pose-vpx.test.ts). In the afternoon, after "Reset view" in Link, the open session measured the eyes at 0.5 m, and every fresh session at 1.6 m again: fresh sessions, VPX's included, keep the original room. On every room change ("piece-changee", reported with its pose: the new origin in the old one), the companion therefore starts a fresh session, at once in the menu, at the end of the game while standing by (`roomRestart`): the cabinet stays where it is in the real room, without aligning again, and VPX gets it at the same place. A Link recentring no longer puts it behind the player. If the fresh session still measures eyes outside 1.0 to 2.1 m, the Quest gives the old room to applications that start: the alignment screen asks to stop then restart Link. During a game, the companion standing by logs the player's head and the middle of the aligned lockbar seen from it ("tête du joueur pendant la partie…"); after a game where VPX recentred the table, the head it saw (`vpxRecenteredHead`) is logged next to the companion's: a gap says the two do not share the same room. On 9 November 2026, they matched to the millimetre (test "VPX et le compagnon voyaient la tête au même endroit"). Code: `packages/core/src/calage.ts` (`lockbarPlace`, `lockbarVpxSettings`, `calibrationProblem`, `roomToCabinet` for the markers), `app/src/frontend.ts` (`openCalibration`, `calibrationMarks`), companion: "calage 1|0", "pointe gauche|droite x y z"; test [calage.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/calage.test.ts).

Limits: a screen rotated in Windows (portrait) stays black in the headset; so does a screen plugged into another graphics card than the headset; an HDR screen (format other than 8-bit BGRA) is not copied. Tests: `packages/core/src/test/casque.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/test/casque-scene.test.ts`.

### 3D vision

System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Vision 3D" (3D vision) (`app/src/vision3d.ts`, rules in `packages/core/src/vision3d.ts`): the table perspective follows the player's head, like a window opened onto the playfield ("head tracking"). Off by default, Start toggles it; "BETA" badge. The setting is kept in `<données>\vision3d\reglage.json`, with the head's playing position for each cabinet configuration.

Sensors, recognised among the USB devices present without opening anything (`vision3dHardware`; the "Camera" or "Image" driver class is read by `packages/win-displays/src/usb.ts`):

- a Kinect for Xbox 360 (VID 045E), plugged in with its power adapter, comes first. Without its driver (`Kinect10.dll` in System32) or, for a Kinect for Xbox 360 (camera 045E:02AE), without SDK 1.8 registered (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Kinect` `SDKInstallPath`, or `KINECTSDK10_DIR`), the entry becomes "pilote Kinect à installer" (Kinect driver to install). The driver alone (runtime) indeed refuses a Kinect for Xbox 360 (`NuiInitialize`: `E_NUI_NOTSUPPORTED`, 0x83010008); only the Kinect for Windows makes do with it. Choosing it downloads from Microsoft the SDK 1.8 installer, which also holds the drivers and runtime (`kinect-sdk-1.8` component in `packages/installer/components.json`, `on-demand` distribution, SHA-256 checked), checks its Authenticode signature (exact Microsoft Corporation subject, `signer` field). The installer itself is never run: its interface ("RedistUX", .NET) shows its licence checkbox despite `/quiet`, and a full-screen menu covers it. Its packages (`bundlePackages`: Visual C++ x64, x64 drivers, runtime and SDK, with the SDK's external `redist.cab` archive placed next to it) are taken from its attached archive (the largest CAB archive in the file, `attachedCabinet`) by Windows' `expand.exe`, each checked as signed by Microsoft, then installed without any window (`msiexec /qn`) by an elevated script: Windows asks for consent once. The entry says that installing means accepting Microsoft's licence. Once the driver is in, 3D vision turns on. Cyber Pinball never ships it. The 2013 driver (`kinectcamera.sys`) is refused by Windows 11 memory integrity (Windows Security > Device security > Core isolation; read from `HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\DeviceGuard\Scenarios\HypervisorEnforcedCodeIntegrity`, `packages/win-displays/src/device-guard.ts`). When it is on, the installation is not offered; a plugged-in webcam takes the Kinect's place (the detail says so), otherwise the "Kinect bloquée par Windows" (Kinect blocked by Windows) entry frees the screens and opens the "Core isolation" page. Cyber Pinball never changes this security setting: the player decides. A driver failure is stated in plain words from its code (`nuiErrorReason`, codes from `NuiApi.h`): Kinect not supported without the SDK, no power, already in use, not enough USB bandwidth…;
- otherwise, a webcam. A window that is never shown (`renderer/vision3d-webcam.html`, `vision3d` partition) opens the camera alone (no microphone) and finds the face in every frame with MediaPipe Tasks Vision (full-range BlazeFace, `app/ressources/vision3d/blaze_face_full_range.tflite`, on the CPU). The short-range model does not find a face that fills only a tenth of the picture, which is what it does in front of a cabinet, more than a metre away. Frames are read through `MediaStreamTrackProcessor`: a hidden window may stop decoding a video. The distance comes from the gap between the eyes (6.3 cm) and the camera's field of view (70° by default, `webcamFovDeg`). This window has no network access: pictures never leave the PC;
- a Kinect for Xbox One is recognised, but not supported yet: the entry says so.

Without a sensor, the entry stays visible, greyed out with the reason. An application built without the C++ tools has no plugin: the entry says so too.

The entry opens the "Vision 3D" (3D vision) screen (`openVision3dScreen`, `app/src/frontend.ts`): where the sensor stands, in plain words, then "Activer" or "Désactiver" (Turn on or off), "Tester le capteur" (Test the sensor), "Installer" or "Réinstaller le pilote de la Kinect" (Install or Reinstall the Kinect driver), "Ouvrir « Isolation du noyau »" (Open "Core isolation"). The Kinect state is read from Windows without opening it: its camera (PID 02AE or 02BF) only shows up when powered by the power adapter ("Kinect sans courant" (Kinect without power)), and its driver state comes from the configuration manager (`deviceStatus`, `packages/win-displays/src/usb.ts`: driver loaded, otherwise the Device Manager code; 28: no driver, to install; any other code: driver present but not loaded, restart needed, for instance right after turning memory integrity off). A Kinect that is not ready gives way to a plugged-in webcam, and the detail says why. "Tester le capteur" (Test the sensor) turns it on outside a game for 20 s (`HeadTracker.test`): the screen shows the head's distance and offset live, nothing is sent to VPX nor saved; the test stops by itself, on going back, or when a table starts. After each game with 3D vision, a report is written to the log and shown on the screen (`vision3dGameReport`): what `vpinball.log` says about the plugin since the launch ("Plugin Vision3D loaded", "was found but is disabled", a load failure, or nothing: `vision3dPluginLog`), the number of positions sent and the sensor failure if there was one. It tells where to look when the view does not move.

Three settings, on the "Vision 3D" (3D vision) screen, kept in `reglage.json` and applied at once to the running sensor (`HeadTracker.configure`):

- "Calibrer la position de jeu" (Calibrate the playing position): the sensor on (test), 50 measurements, about two seconds, the player still in playing position; their median becomes the playing position of the cabinet configuration. Safer than the measurement taken on the fly at the start of a game;
- "Intensité" (Intensity) (`intensity`, 25 % to 150 %, 75 % by default): the head shift is multiplied by it before being sent to the plugin. At 100 %, the table moves as behind a real window; lower, it moves less, against "seasickness". Also adjustable in game: the MagnaSave buttons, then "Intensité de la vue 3D" (3D view intensity), each Start moves to the next one;
- "Réactivité" (Responsiveness) (`smoothing`, `SMOOTHING_PRESETS`): the "1 €" filter and an anticipation from the head's speed (4 cm at most), which hides part of the sensor's lag (the Kinect measures 30 times a second and delivers the head 60 to 100 ms late). Gentle: 0.7 Hz, 0.05, no anticipation (the previous setting, about 45 ms of lag added by smoothing on a clear movement); normal: 1 Hz, 0.3, 35 ms (about 12 ms); quick: 1.5 Hz, 0.8, 60 ms (almost nothing).

During a game (not "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game), not a game in the headset), the sensor turns on when the table is on screen and off at the end (`HeadTracker`). Kinect: `Kinect10.dll` is read through koffi, skeleton in seated mode (the upper body is enough behind the cabinet body), "head" joint of the first tracked skeleton, levelled from the floor the sensor sees (`kinectHead`). Once per game, the Kinect motor aims at the head if it is more than 6° off centre (`kinectAim`); the frames that follow, while it turns, are ignored. The position is smoothed ("1 €" filter, `OneEuroFilter`), compared with the playing position, capped at 60 cm, then sent to the VPX plugin over local UDP (127.0.0.1, port 4242, opentrack format: six doubles, x, y, z in centimetres). The playing position is the median of the first 24 measurements, saved for the cabinet configuration; "Recentrer la vue 3D" (Recenter the 3D view), in the in-game settings (MagnaSave), measures it again.

The "Vision3D" VPX plugin (`packages/vpx-vision3d`, C++) is copied into `plugins\vision3d` of the active VPX version before each game if it changed, and enabled like the other plugins (`enabledPlugins` in the manifest). It stays inert as long as it receives nothing: a game without 3D vision pays nothing for it. On the first packet, it turns off VPX's static pre-render (the scenery must follow the head), then, on every frame (`OnPrepareFrame`), moves the point of view (`SetActiveViewSetup`, `viewX`, `viewY`, `viewZ`). In "Window" mode (cabinet, `ViewCabMode` = 2), the shift gets the same rotation VPX applies to the player position (`ViewSetup::SetViewPosFromPlayerPosition`): the perspective is that of a real window. In "Camera" mode (desktop), the camera follows the head, an approximate effect. Adjustable gain (`[Plugin.Vision3D] Gain`, 1 = real movement). Silent sensor: gradual return to the neutral view within a second. At the end of the game: original view and pre-render restored. The opentrack format also lets opentrack itself plug in (its axis directions are set in opentrack). Build: `node scripts/vision3d.mjs` (with `--banc`: test bench without VPX); `npm run deployer` does it too when CMake and the Visual Studio 2022 C++ tools are there.

Limits: the effect only works for the tracked player, and a camera filming the screen does not show it; the webcam needs some light; the Kinect for Xbox One is not supported. Tests: `packages/core/src/test/vision3d.test.ts`, `vision3d-webcam.test.ts` (Chromium fake camera and test portrait), `vision3d-kinect.test.ts` (fake `Kinect10.dll`, `packages/vpx-vision3d/test/kinect-factice.cpp`), plugin bench (`packages/vpx-vision3d/test/banc.cpp`).

## Coexisting with another cabinet frontend

Cyber Pinball installs on a cabinet that already has PinUP Popper, PinballX or PinballY without changing anything in their installation. Code: [packages/core/src/coexistence.ts](../packages/core/src/coexistence.ts) (known frontends, taskbars, list of the Windows settings it changes) and [app/src/frontend-gate.ts](../app/src/frontend-gate.ts) (waiting screen); the invariant is pinned down by [coexistence.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/coexistence.test.ts).

**What stays separate.**

- Everything lives in the Cyber Pinball root folder: its own copy of VPX (`emulators\vpx\`), its tables, ROMs and media.
- VPX is launched with `-Ini <root>\config\VPinballX.ini`: the other frontend's `VPinballX.ini` is neither read nor written. Only the log `%APPDATA%\VPinballX\10.8\vpinball.log` is shared if the other frontend also uses VPX 10.8: each game rewrites it.
- VPX plugin mode: no component is registered in Windows (VPinMAME, B2S and PinUP Player stay the other frontend's); `B2STableSettings.xml`, `DmdDevice.ini` and `PUPDatabase.db` are not touched. The DOF folder (`%APPDATA%\VPinballX\10.8\directoutputconfig`) is shared by every VPX 10.8 on the PC: Cyber Pinball only writes there on request (configtool import, previous configuration backed up; addressable LEDs described), and never in place of a hand-written `Cabinet.xml` or GlobalConfig (see [LED and feedback (DOF)](#led-and-feedback-dof)).
- The other frontend's files are never moved: "Reprendre les médias de PinUP Popper / PinballX" (Import media from PinUP Popper / PinballX) copies them, and a table picked outside `import\` is copied.
- ROMs and NVRAM are stored separately: high scores and game settings are not shared.

**What is shared: a few Windows settings.** Their full list is `WINDOWS_CHANGES`. The test rejects a declared call made outside its file, and any call that would change something else (`FORBIDDEN_WINDOWS_CALLS`: registry outside the installer, default audio output, the application starting itself at sign-in, system parameters, processes killed by name).

| Setting | When | What Cyber Pinball does for the other frontend |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Orientation of the playfield screen, for the session only, given back on exit | wizard, "Où sont les flippers ?" (Where are the flippers?) step, only if VPX cannot put the flippers on the player's side; given again at every startup | Another frontend installed or running: the first Start warns ("Retourner l'écran dans Windows ?" (Flip the screen in Windows?): its menu and tables would be upside down too). Start flips anyway, then asks for confirmation within 15 s as usual; Exit keeps the Windows orientation, tables upside down in Cyber Pinball. |
| Taskbars | during a game | Only the ones Cyber Pinball hid come back (`taskbarsToRestore`): a taskbar already hidden by the other frontend stays hidden. The `barres-des-taches-masquees` marker keeps the HWND of each one. |
| Ctrl+Shift+K shortcut | during a game | Released at the end of the game. |
| Ctrl+Shift+D shortcut | in desktop mode | Released on return to the menu. |
| Start at sign-in, desktop replaced or hidden | installer option, and System menu > "Borne" > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet) or "Lancement au démarrage" (Launch at startup), off by default | Another frontend installed: the screen flags it before turning anything on (if it also starts with Windows, the two would fight over the screens). Each Windows setting is recorded before being written and put back as it was when turned off (`Sys-Set`, `windowssettings.ts`). |
| "Programs and Features" entry | installation (`HKCU`) | Removed on uninstall. |
| "Cyber Pinball pare-feu" scheduled task | "Autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" (Allow in Windows Firewall), under the same permission | No trigger: started by Cyber Pinball only, when one of its firewall rules is missing, to add it again (see "Firewall after an update"). Touches nothing else; removed on uninstall. |

**Another frontend already running.** The two frontends would fight over the screens, the DMD display and the cabinet buttons. At startup, before covering any screen (`otherFrontendsGate`, called by `main.ts` before the screen guard), Cyber Pinball looks for the processes of the known frontends (`OTHER_FRONTENDS`: `PinUpMenu.exe` and `PinUpDisplay.exe` for PinUP Popper, `PinballX.exe`, `PinballY.exe`; `PinUpPlayer.exe`, the PuP-Pack player, is not one of them). If it finds one, only the main screen shows "PinUP Popper est ouvert" (PinUP Popper is running): Cyber Pinball starts on its own as soon as it is closed (checked every second); Start or "Continuer quand même" (Continue anyway) starts right away; Exit or "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball) quits. Nothing is closed or changed in the other frontend. Before each game, the menu checks again: a frontend opened in the meantime gives "PinUP Popper est ouvert" (Start: play anyway · Exit: back to the menu). A frontend accepted once is no longer reported until Cyber Pinball closes, menu ↔ wizard relaunches included (`CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS_ACCEPTES`, passed on to the new process).

**Installed frontends** (`installedFrontends`, for the orientation warning): the executable, or the `POPMedia` or `Media` folder, in the default locations (`vPinball\PinUPSystem`, `PinUPSystem`, `PinballX`, `PinballY`…, on C:, D: and E:).

**Simulated frontends**: offscreen, only simulated frontends count: `CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS` (running, executable names separated by commas; with `CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS_FICHIER`, closed when that file disappears) and `CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS_INSTALLES` (installed).

## Dedicated cabinet

An option, off by default: until it is turned on, Cyber Pinball writes nothing to Windows. When on, it makes Cyber Pinball the Windows *shell* for the cabinet account, instead of Explorer: at sign-in it opens instead of the desktop and the taskbar, and the desktop never shows again, not even during the 1 to 2 s of startup (see [Known limitations of the prototype](#known-limitations-of-the-prototype)). Other accounts, the administrator included, keep Explorer. Entry: System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet).

**Chosen means: Shell Launcher**, the Windows feature made for kiosks (optional features `Client-DeviceLockdown` and `Client-EmbeddedShellLauncher`, WMI class `WESL_UserSetting`): one shell per account (SID), and an action when the application exits, depending on its exit code (0 restart it, 1 restart the PC, 2 shut it down, 3 do nothing). It only exists in the Enterprise, Education and IoT Enterprise editions (Windows 11 IoT Enterprise LTSC 2024 included), not in Pro or Home: there, the screen uses the account's "Interface utilisateur personnalisée" (Custom User Interface) setting and a supervisor (see below, "Without Shell Launcher").
