Ruled out: the `Shell` value under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion\Winlogon`, per account and without elevation, but described by no current Microsoft documentation (on the development PC, `IniFileMapping\system.ini\boot` only has `Shell` read from HKLM), with no restart if Cyber Pinball closes or crashes (black screen), and without the startup programs that Explorer launches.

Code: [packages/core/src/dedicated.ts](../packages/core/src/dedicated.ts) (elevated script, simulated Windows, judging the results), [app/src/dedicated-menu.ts](../app/src/dedicated-menu.ts) (screen), [app/src/dedicated-system.ts](../app/src/dedicated-system.ts) (executor: the real one, and the simulated one of offscreen runs and sandboxes), [app/src/dedicated-session.ts](../app/src/dedicated-session.ts) (session where Cyber Pinball is the shell). Tests: [dedicated.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dedicated.test.ts) runs the logic of the real script in PowerShell on a simulated system layer (no call to Windows) and compares it with the simulator; [dedicated-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dedicated-menu.test.ts) walks through the screen and pins the invariant: **no write to Windows starts anywhere but the "Turn on" and "Turn off" actions**.

### Turning on

1. First create another administrator account, with a password: it is the way back in, as a rescue. Without it, the screen refuses to turn on ("Blocking").
2. Sign in with the cabinet account, with Cyber Pinball installed (`<root>\app\CyberPinball.exe`; from the sources, turning on is refused).
3. System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet): the screen says what changes and checks the Windows edition and the rescue account.
4. "Turn on for this account": Windows asks for permission (UAC; an administrator's password if the cabinet account is a standard account). A single script, `<data>\borne-dediee\activer.ps1`, reads the state of Windows, enables the optional features, makes Explorer the shell of other accounts if it was not, gives this account the command `"<root>\app\CyberPinball.exe" --borne-dediee`, starts Shell Launcher, then reads the state again; the screen checks every point. If turning on fails midway, the script puts the previous state back by itself.
5. Restart the PC: the session opens on Cyber Pinball. If Windows asks for a restart to enable the features, the screen says so; after the restart, "Finish turning on".

Kept in `<data>\borne-dediee`: `etat.json` (the previous state of Windows, reference for turning off) and `desactiver.ps1` (the rescue script).

### Going back

System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet) > "Turn off and bring back the desktop" (Windows permission). The script puts every point back in its previous state, reads it again, then disables the optional features if they were disabled; the screen compares point by point and marks each one "Checked". A difference stays on screen ("Blocking"), and the previous state is kept to try again. The desktop comes back at the next sign-in; the features are fully removed at the next restart.

| Point | Turn on | Turn off |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Startup of the cabinet account (`SetCustomShell`) | Cyber Pinball, restarted on any exit except code 77 | the previous setting, or none |
| Shell Launcher running (`SetEnabled`) | yes | the previous value |
| Startup of other accounts (`SetDefaultShell`) | `explorer.exe`, if it was not | the previous value |
| `Shell` value under `HKLM\…\Winlogon` (rewritten by `SetEnabled`) | not written by Cyber Pinball | the previous value, checked |
| Optional features | enabled | disabled, if they were |

### Rescue

- **On the cabinet**: Ctrl+Alt+Del, then "Task Manager"; File > Run new task: `explorer.exe` (a file window) or any other program. Or Ctrl+Alt+Del, "Sign out", then sign in with the administrator account, which keeps the desktop. Shell Launcher does not block Ctrl+Alt+Del (the Windows keyboard filter would, but Cyber Pinball does not enable it).
- **From the administrator account, without Cyber Pinball**: in PowerShell started as administrator, the rescue script puts the previous state back, like "Turn off", and prints its result; the next time the "Borne dédiée" screen opens, Cyber Pinball reads it, checks it and forgets the activation.

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "D:\CyberPinball\data\borne-dediee\desactiver.ps1"
```

- **Cyber Pinball keeps restarting** (it crashes at startup, Shell Launcher restarts it): Ctrl+Alt+Del, "Sign out", administrator account, the script above, then restart; the cause is written to `<data>\logs\erreurs.txt`.
- **As a last resort**, without Cyber Pinball's files, in PowerShell as administrator (`Borne`: the name of the cabinet account):

```powershell
$sid = (New-Object System.Security.Principal.NTAccount('Borne')).Translate([System.Security.Principal.SecurityIdentifier]).Value
$sl = [wmiclass]'\\localhost\root\standardcimv2\embedded:WESL_UserSetting'
$sl.RemoveCustomShell($sid)
$sl.SetEnabled($false)
```

### Without Shell Launcher: Windows Pro and Home

On Windows 11 Pro, Shell Launcher does not exist, and the screen offered nothing. It now offers, on every edition, two ways to choose from, always for the cabinet's account only; on Enterprise, Education and IoT Enterprise, "Activer pour ce compte" (Turn on for this account) is still Shell Launcher.

- **"Remplacer le bureau pour ce compte" (Replace the desktop for this account)**: the "Custom User Interface" policy (the one `gpedit.msc` sets under User Configuration > Administrative Templates > System), the `Shell` value of `HKEY_USERS\<SID>\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Policies\System`, documented by Microsoft and read at sign-in. It is set to `"<root>\app\CyberPinball.exe" --borne-compte`. A policy can only be changed as an administrator: Windows asks for permission (UAC), when turning on and when turning off. Same conditions as Shell Launcher: another administrator account, an installation.
- **"Lancer au démarrage de Windows" (Launch at Windows startup)**: the `Cyber Pinball.lnk` shortcut in the account's Startup folder (the same as the installer checkbox), and the desktop hidden behind it: empty wallpaper (`Control Panel\Desktop\WallPaper`), black background color (`Control Panel\Colors\Background` = `0 0 0`), hidden icons (`Explorer\Advanced\HideIcons` = 1). No UAC. The black desktop shows for 1 to 2 s before Cyber Pinball covers the screens. Also under System menu > "Borne" > "Lancement au démarrage" (Launch at startup), which opens the same page and says in its label whether Cyber Pinball already launches (installer shortcut included).

**The supervisor.** Windows does not restart a shell that closes; Cyber Pinball does it itself, like Shell Launcher: `--borne-compte` starts a windowless process (`app/src/supervisor.ts`, called by `main.ts` before anything else) that launches `CyberPinball.exe --borne-dediee` and restarts it on every exit, crashes included. Everything "What the dedicated cabinet changes elsewhere" describes therefore holds as is (relaunches, exit code 77 of an update, after which the supervisor stops). If Cyber Pinball exits 5 times within 2 minutes, the supervisor opens Explorer and stops, rather than leave a black screen (`supervisorVerdict`). It records its start in `<data>\borne-dediee\surveillant.json`.

**The backup.** Before any write, the script (`<data>\borne-dediee\reglages-ecrire.ps1`, generated) records each setting it is about to change, missing value included; this record is saved in `reglages-windows.json` and the screen shows it ("Sauvegarde du … : « Désactiver » remet", Backup of …: "Turn off" puts back, before and now, setting by setting). "Désactiver et rétablir le bureau" (Turn off and bring back the desktop) or "Ne plus lancer au démarrage" (Stop launching at startup) puts the backup back as it was, then checks it setting by setting ("Vérifié", Checked). A write that fails midway puts the previous state back by itself. The rescue script `retablir-reglages.ps1` does the same outside Cyber Pinball; the screen then reads its result and forgets the activation:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "D:\CyberPinball\data\borne-dediee\retablir-reglages.ps1"
```

On a stuck cabinet: Ctrl+Alt+Del, "Task Manager", File > Run new task, this command, ticking "Create this task with administrative privileges" if the desktop is replaced.

**Diagnosis.** Desktop replaced: if the PC has restarted since turning it on and the session still opens with the desktop, the screen says so in red, "Windows n'a pas appliqué le réglage" (Windows did not apply the setting: the supervisor never started) or "Le surveillant a rendu la main à l'Explorateur" (The supervisor handed over to Explorer: Cyber Pinball kept closing, cause in `erreurs.txt`). In the first case, fall back to "Lancer au démarrage de Windows".

Code: [packages/core/src/windowssettings.ts](../packages/core/src/windowssettings.ts) (script, simulated Windows, verdict, supervisor rule), [app/src/supervisor.ts](../app/src/supervisor.ts), the screen and the executor above. Tests: [windowssettings.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/windowssettings.test.ts) runs the real script on a simulated layer and compares it with the simulator, for each way and each starting point, turning off and failure midway included; [dedicated-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dedicated-menu.test.ts) goes through both ways on Windows Pro and pins the invariant: a write only comes from the screen's actions.

### What the dedicated cabinet changes elsewhere

- **Restarts** (menu ↔ wizard, language, cabinet setup): Shell Launcher only watches the process it started. That process writes down its arguments (`<data>\borne-dediee\relance.json`, valid for one minute), closes, and Shell Launcher starts it again; the new one picks them up. In between, the screen is black for a moment (without Explorer, there is no desktop to see).
- **Closing or crashing**: Shell Launcher restarts Cyber Pinball. "Quit Cyber Pinball" disappears from the menu (it would only relaunch); "Restart Cyber Pinball" stays.
- **Update**: Cyber Pinball closes with code 77, for which Shell Launcher does nothing; the installer (or `maj-legere.js`) restarts it, as usual. That Cyber Pinball is no longer watched by Shell Launcher until the next sign-in: if it closes, it restarts itself, but a crash is no longer caught. Restarting the PC after an update brings the watch back.
- **"Windows desktop" becomes "Open Windows Explorer"**: without Explorer there is neither desktop nor taskbar. The entry opens an Explorer window (under Shell Launcher, `explorer.exe` started on its own only opens a file window: it only becomes the shell, with desktop and taskbar, when the Winlogon `Shell` value is its own; behaviour reported by third-party sources, to be confirmed on the cabinet), then frees every screen like desktop mode; Ctrl+Shift+D or Alt+Tab brings back the menu.
- **Taskbars**: without Explorer there are none; hiding them during a game (screen guard) finds nothing to hide, and still only restores what it hid.
- **Taskbars when the cabinet starts with Windows**: when "Lancer au démarrage de Windows" (Start with Windows) or "Remplacer le bureau" (Replace the desktop) is on (`reglages-windows.json`, `etat.json`), when the `Cyber Pinball.lnk` shortcut is in the account's Startup folder (the installer's box included) or when Cyber Pinball is the shell, the taskbars are hidden **for the whole session**, not only during games (`cabinetHidesTaskbars`, `packages/core/src/coexistence.ts`). Those Explorer creates after Cyber Pinball when Windows starts, or shows again, are hidden as soon as they appear (checked every 50 ms). They come back when going back to Windows: "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) in the menu or Ctrl+Shift+D (hidden again on returning to the menu), and "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball) (`restoresTaskbarsAt`: only `desktop` and `quit`). A handover (menu ↔ wizard, language) passes them to the new process without them showing. If Cyber Pinball stops without giving them back (crash, forced stop), a hidden PowerShell started at the first hiding (`taskbarRescueScript`) waits for the process to end, then 8 s, and, if no Cyber Pinball took over and the `<données>\barres-des-taches-masquees` marker is still there, shows the taskbars it lists and removes it. Started by hand without this setting, Cyber Pinball hides the taskbars only during games, as before. Tests: `coexistence.test.ts`.
- **Wizard at first startup**: it does not offer the dedicated cabinet (off by default, Windows permission needed). On a dedicated cabinet, the wizard runs like the menu, and quitting it restarts Cyber Pinball.
- **Installer "at Windows startup" shortcut**: no effect on a dedicated cabinet (the Startup folder is launched by Explorer). It is the same one as "Lancer au démarrage de Windows" (Launch at Windows startup) makes (`Cyber Pinball.lnk`); the screen flags it and offers "Retirer le lancement au démarrage" (Remove the launch at startup).
- **Uninstalling**: if `etat.json` or `reglages-windows.json` is still there, the uninstaller warns (without Cyber Pinball, the session could open on a black screen) and only goes on if asked to: it lacks the rights to undo these settings.

## Unattended end-to-end tests

**Rule, for every test and every development session: off-screen by default.** An unattended test must never open a window on the machine's screens, steal its focus or depend on the screens plugged in: the machine is used for work while the tests run in the background.

- **Off-screen** (`app/src/offscreen.ts`, rules in `packages/core/src/offscreen.ts`): automatic as soon as `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT`, `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` or `CYBERPINBALL_STRESS` is set, or forced with `CYBERPINBALL_HORS_ECRAN=1`. Every window is created through `createWindow` and rendered in memory (Electron off-screen rendering, `webPreferences.offscreen`) at the size of its screen, with a scale factor of 1: never shown, never in the foreground, absent from the taskbar, sound muted. `coverDisplay` only sizes them (simulated rotation included); `show`, `focus`, full screen, minimizing and taking back the foreground have no effect. The rendering is that of a real window: captures (`capturePage`), the layout guard, replayed keys and clicks (`click:<selector>`, `sendInputEvent`) all work the same way.
- **Simulated screens**: `CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS=<file.json>` replaces screen detection everywhere (setup wizard, menu, settings, `sync`, button reset: `observedDisplays`, `app/src/displays.ts`), in the same shape as `npm run probe -- --json` (`displays` field), EDID included; a malformed file is rejected, naming the faulty screen and field. Screen sets versioned in `packages\core\ecrans-simules\`: `pc-dev` (the four screens of the development PC, those of the cabinet model in `D:\CyberPinball`), `borne` (4K playfield lying flat, 1080p backglass, 4:1 DMD, topper), `poste-deux-ecrans` (the current machine: main screen and two screens), `un-ecran`. With simulated screens, orientation is never applied to a real screen.
- **Watch**: during an off-screen test, any application window that is visible or in the foreground (`isVisible`, `isFocused`, native windows of the process, dialog boxes included) is reported in the console ("Hors écran : …" (Off-screen: …)) and in `<captures>\hors-ecran.txt` ("Aucune fenêtre visible ni au premier plan" (No window visible or in the foreground) otherwise). The demo scripts fail if a window appeared; `npm test` replays the wizard off-screen on the simulated cabinet and checks this.
- **Application log**: everything it writes to its console (a table's copy, the host's settings, the group records, an invitation, a VPX launch…) also goes to `<racine>\data\logs\journal.txt`, one dated line per message, `[avertissement]` (warning) or `[erreur]` (error) when it is not plain information; beyond 2 MB it becomes `journal-ancien.txt` and a new one starts. What happened on a cabinet during a test can be read afterwards (`app/src/journal.ts`, `packages/core/src/journal.ts`, tests in `journal.test.ts`). It also records Chromium processes that stopped (a page's renderer, the GPU: `journalProcessesGone`, `app/src/uncaught.ts`) and any window shown without having received `ready-to-show`: Electron never emits it for a window already visible at its first frame, nor for a page that has painted nothing meaningful, and, at the restart after an update on a busy PC, the menu could stay invisible this way, Cyber Pinball running behind its black veils. Every window therefore waits through `whenReadyToShow` (`app/src/offscreen.ts`), which shows it after 10 s at the latest and records the state of its page (“pas de ready-to-show après 10 s (page en chargement : non, rendu planté : non…)”); `offscreen.test.ts` rejects any other wait for `ready-to-show`.
- **Uncaught exception**: on screen, on the cabinet, it is written to `<racine>\data\logs\erreurs.txt` (date, kind, stack; the oldest entries go beyond 512 KB) and to the console, and the application carries on: never Electron's modal box, which would freeze the menu until OK is clicked, often hidden under its full-screen windows. An unhandled promise rejection is logged the same way (`app/src/uncaught.ts`, `packages/core/src/errorlog.ts`, tests in `errorlog.test.ts`). Since 10/11/2026 it also holds **caught incidents** ("incident rattrapé": a phone gesture not delivered, a built-in browser page that crashed and was reloaded, a refused request, a failed sign-in import; one line, never what was typed nor a page's full address) and, at the next start, **native crashes** that Node does not see: Crashpad keeps their report in `%APPDATA%\CyberPinball\Crashpad\reports\*.dmp`, sending nothing, and each new report becomes a "plantage natif" (native crash) entry with its path (`journalNativeCrashes`, on screen only). Off-screen, it stops the test as failed, with the error in the console ("Hors écran : exception non rattrapée, essai arrêté." (Off-screen: uncaught exception, test stopped.)), instead of Electron's modal box ("A JavaScript error occurred in the main process"): on the hidden desktop nobody would close it and the main process would stay frozen, timers and safeguards included (`app/src/offscreen.ts`, invariant test in `offscreen.test.ts`).
- **Hidden desktop** (`scripts\bureau-cache.ps1`, loaded for example by `scripts\diagnostic-habillage.ps1`): a test script relaunches itself entirely (PowerShell, node, Electron, tools) on a separate Windows desktop, `CyberPinballEssais` (`Enter-BureauCache`, `CreateDesktop` then `CreateProcess` with that desktop), which the user never sees; its output comes back to the original console. Even if a window were shown by mistake, nothing would appear on the machine's screens. The application applies the same rule to any unattended test, whatever launched it (`npx electron app` by hand, another script, another session): when off-screen and outside the hidden desktop, it relaunches itself there with the same arguments (`relaunchOnHiddenDesktop`, `app/src/offscreen.ts`; `packages/win-displays/src/desktop.ts`), copies back its output and returns its exit code; `npm test` checks this. The relay started on the hidden desktop is placed in a Windows job object (`JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, created suspended and placed before it starts) held only by its launcher: when the launcher dies, even killed outright at a test's timeout, the whole hidden-desktop tree dies with it; it does not inherit the launcher's console, only the log is handed to it. On the test side, every Electron goes through `runElectron` (`packages/core/src/test/electron-run.ts`): output to files, timeout, then any process of the test still alive (recognised by its `--user-data-dir`) is killed, tree included, and the test fails naming it; firewall commands (PowerShell, netsh, schtasks) have their input closed, are returned when the process ends, killed tree included at their timeout (30 s, 5 min for the Windows permission) and when the application closes (tests in `enfants-vivants.test.ts`). **Only known gap: a new console.** Windows 11 hands it to Terminal, which opens on the user's desktop and takes the focus, even from the hidden desktop: the scripts run their tools in their own console (`Start-Process … -NoNewWindow`, never `start`), and the application runs them with `windowsHide` (invariant tests in `offscreen.test.ts`).
- **Reserved for the machine's screens**: a VPX game ("Jouer" (Play), "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game)), the endurance test and captures of the whole desktop are refused off-screen with the message "… nécessite les écrans du poste" (… requires the machine's screens), and only run with `CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1` (real windows, full screen, focus taken). The "Choisir des fichiers" (Choose files) dialog box is also refused off-screen. Reading a table's script (`VPinballX -ExtractVBS`) is still allowed: VPX then opens no visible window.
- **Simulated screens for a test by hand**: the screen sets in `packages\core\ecrans-simules\` (`pc-dev.json`, `borne.json`, `poste-deux-ecrans.json`, `un-ecran.json`, `pincab.json`) are passed to `CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS`. A test by hand:

  ```powershell
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS = "$PWD\packages\core\ecrans-simules\borne.json"
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_HOME = "$PWD\tmp\essai\home"; $env:CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE = "$PWD\tmp\essai\captures"
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT = 'SansBoutons,Digit1'
  npx electron app --assistant
  ```

**Layout guard** (`app/src/layout-check.ts`): at each key replayed by the autopilot (setup wizard, table menu, VPX settings), every window is inspected in its actual rendering. It reports two texts or controls that overlap (a layer with an opaque background hiding another one, like the help over the menu, does not count), anything that goes outside the window, and text clipped by a container that hides its overflow, itself included. An intended cut ("…", lines limited by `-webkit-line-clamp`) only excuses the element that makes it: the lines it keeps must not be clipped by any other container (a title limited to two lines in a zone one line high lost its first line without being reported). A scrolling list must carry no fade (`mask-image`: the line at the edge would look disabled), and the selected entry of a list (action, row, card) must be whole within it: neither clipped nor hidden by what stays pinned at the top of the list, except in a list the user moved away from it themselves with the wheel (`data-user-scroll`, set by `scroll-keeper.js` until the next change of selection). A list that scrolls without a user gesture (wheel, pointer, keyboard in the page) or a render from `scroll-keeper.js` is reported ("défilement sans geste de l'utilisateur" (scrolling without a user gesture), also in the page's console); the autopilot, which brings a row into view before clicking it, declares that as a gesture. Proofs: `defilement.test.ts` (progress ticks and change of selection in the real renderer of the menu and of the VPX settings, no `scrollIntoView` or `scrollTop` set outside `scroll-keeper.js`). A test checks that the injected scripts compile (`packages/core/src/test/layout-check.test.ts`): a syntax error there made the guard silent. The height used for a text is that of its font, in the right direction for a rotated view. **20 px floor** (design system): a text or an icon smaller than 20 px at the scale of a 1,080 px short side, as it is seen, is reported ("texte plus petit que 20 px" (text smaller than 20 px)): the font size is multiplied by the scale of every `transform` above the text, and an animation that loops counts at its smallest step. The names in the wheel's small medallions, shrunk by `scale(--s)`, went down to 13.6 px without being reported: the guard only measured icons, and the stylesheet test only read sizes in `cqmin`. A font size in fixed px does not follow the screen: the screens' stylesheets have none (only the developer's sheet, `fallback-demo.css`, keeps some); the "Récupérer" banner had fifteen, from 12 to 16 px. What a `clip-path: inset()` cuts off (the wheel brings its medallions in through its edges) does not count as outside the window. **Menu zones**: each frame of the table menu states its zone (`data-frame`: "plateau" for the media's 9:16 frame, "menu" for the backglass, the wheel, the information and the help bar); two different zones that overlap are reported ("cadres qui se recouvrent", frames overlapping), as is a frame that leaves the playfield zone. Two frames of the same zone may overlap (the wheel overlaps the backglass), and a frame that covers the whole zone is a background (the playfield media on a cabinet). At 4:3, the backglass and the wheel covered the playfield frame without anything saying so: no text overlapped there (see "The table menu on a desktop, from 5:4 to 16:9 and wider"). A deliberate last resort below the floor is declared with `data-below-floor` (the loading card's controls at their smallest size); the cinema launch, which carries the medallion off the screen, is set apart, as are the fallbacks (`.cp-fb`, `fallback.js`): media drawn in place of an image or a video of the game, their texts follow the size of their zone like a media's (the loading fallback in the desktop's 9:16 frame). Proofs: [plancher-textes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/plancher-textes.test.ts) (wheel geometry, gliding included; no size in fixed px, nor written below 20 px, in the stylesheets, the menu's `--u`, `--w` and `--b` units included; reference cases in a real off-screen Chromium). **Layers, style-sheet text, icons**: an opaque layer only excuses an overlap if it hides the text underneath entirely; if it hides only part of it (a banner over the end of a label), it is reported ("caché en partie", partly hidden). An element that lets the mouse through (`pointer-events: none`) counts like the others: the point check used to fall through it. Text written by the style sheet (`content:` of a `::before` or `::after`) is reported: with no text node, it escaped the check as well as translation; the interface text scan also reads the style sheets (`i18n-scan.ts`, `scanCss`). An icon pushed alone above or below its text by a line break (running text, or a wrapping flex row; rotated views included) is reported ("icône seule sur sa ligne", icon alone on its line); a menu row keeps its icon in front of its text (`.lib-row-icon + .lib-row-text`). Proof: [disposition-ecrans.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/disposition-ecrans.test.ts) (control cases in a real off-screen Chromium, then the real page of the layout editor, in the state rendered by `arrange.ts` and `wizard.ts`, on the cabinet, the shared backglass screen, the two-screen desk, the development PC and a single screen at 1366 × 768, 1920 × 1080 and 4K, in French and English). Problems go to the console ("Mise en page : …" (Layout: …)) and to `<captures>\mise-en-page.txt` ("Aucun problème de mise en page." (No layout problems.) otherwise).

The wizard screen is built as a flow (`app/renderer/screen.*`): header, content centered in the remaining space, footer (message, step, help) and flipper strip, with no absolute positioning; content that is too tall pushes the footer down instead of sliding under it. The summary panel, which does not scroll, shrinks its body by one or two steps as long as it overflows. The button list switches to two columns when the screen is taller than it is wide.

**Sandbox guard** (`packages/core/src/sandbox.ts`, `app/src/sandbox-guard.ts`): as soon as `CYBERPINBALL_HOME` is set together with `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT`, `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` or `CYBERPINBALL_STRESS`, the application and `sync` refuse any file write outside `CYBERPINBALL_HOME`, `CYBERPINBALL_ROOT`, the project's `tmp\` folder and the Windows temporary folder; the offending path is written to the console and the operation fails. It protects the real root from a test that would target it by mistake (an ini path copied from a real cabinet model, an installer parameter that was ignored). A deliberate test on the real root is done without `CYBERPINBALL_HOME`.

- **Setup wizard**: `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT` replays a sequence of keys, `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` saves a capture of each screen after each key. The `SansBoutons` pseudo-code acts as if nobody had touched anything during the "Boutons" step (current keys kept, without waiting 20 s). Each key is pressed then released (button capture counts on release); `Maintenir3:KeyA` holds it for 3 s, `CtrlQ` sends Ctrl+Q. These keys go straight to key handling; `Clavier:KeyA`, on the other hand, goes along the path of a real key (`webContents.sendInputEvent` on the primary screen, `before-input-event`, Chromium, the page) and reports a key whose press or release did not arrive; `Joy3` presses button 3 of an SDL virtual joystick. Each capture leaves a line in the console ("Assistant : relevé game, credit sur Digit5 : gardé", or refused, or skipped).

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=SansBoutons,ShiftLeft,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,ShiftRight,Digit1,Escape,Digit1 CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/captures npx electron app --assistant
  ```

  When unattended, screen orientation is simulated (the geometry changes in `ecrans-simules-orientation.json`, no screen rotates). The `Attendre16` pseudo-code lets 16 seconds pass without a key, to see the countdown expire. Proposing, canceling, letting expire, then confirming the flip:

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/flip/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=SansBoutons,Digit1,ShiftRight,Digit1,Escape,Digit1,Escape,ShiftRight,Digit1,Attendre16,ShiftLeft,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Escape,Escape,Escape,Digit1 CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/flip/captures npx electron app --assistant
  ```

  The "Son" (Sound) step really plays its beeps (short ones: each key arrives 400 ms after the previous one) and writes a report of each sound to the console (output found, channels opened). `CYBERPINBALL_AUDIO_OUTPUTS=<file.json>` replaces the detected outputs with a list in the format of the `audio` field of `npm run probe -- --json`, for example to add a fake 7.1 card and capture the speaker test (Electron cannot find it: no sound, message on screen). With four screens, a fake backglass HDMI output and a fake 7.1 card:

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_AUDIO_OUTPUTS=tmp/son/sorties-simulees.json CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/son/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=SansBoutons,ShiftRight,ShiftRight,ShiftRight,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Escape,Escape,ShiftLeft,ShiftRight,Escape,Escape,Digit1,ShiftLeft,ShiftRight,Digit1 CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/son/captures npx electron app --assistant
  ```

- **Launcher endurance** (milestone 2 criterion, **requires the machine's screens**: `CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1`): `CYBERPINBALL_STRESS="N,S"` launches the table chosen by `CYBERPINBALL_TABLE` N times, stops each game after S seconds and checks that the menu takes back the foreground. The report is displayed and written to `data\logs\stress-*.json`; these games do not count in the statistics.

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1 CYBERPINBALL_STRESS=50,10 CYBERPINBALL_TABLE=1.25 npx electron app
  ```

- **Menu rotation**: `CYBERPINBALL_FLIPPERS=bottom|left|top|right` forces the flipper edge without modifying `cabinet.json` (left: 90°, right: 270°, top: 180°). With `--reglages`, the screen alone only rotates if this variable is set. The autopilot's `drag` step: the file drop frame.

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_FLIPPERS=left CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=Digit1,Escape,Escape CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/captures npx electron app
  ```

- **VPX settings**: `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT` and `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` also work with `--reglages`, with `click:<selector>` (`click:.back`: the back button).

- **Installer**: in a sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME` set), the installer puts `root.json` and the shortcuts (`Menu Démarrer`, `Bureau`, `Démarrage`) in that folder and writes nothing to the registry. In silent mode, `/D=` is mandatory there (failing that, only the root remembered in that sandbox is accepted): never a fallback to the real root. The uninstaller reads this choice back from `installation.ini`. Installation, first launch (`--user-data-dir` also keeps Chromium's data in `tmp\`), then uninstallation:

  ```powershell
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_HOME = "$PWD\tmp\essai\home"
  Start-Process release\CyberPinball-Setup-complet.exe -ArgumentList '/S', "/D=$PWD\tmp\essai\racine" -Wait
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT = 'wait:500'; $env:CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE = "$PWD\tmp\essai\captures"
  Start-Process tmp\essai\racine\app\CyberPinball.exe -ArgumentList "--user-data-dir=$PWD\tmp\essai\electron" -Wait
  Start-Process 'tmp\essai\racine\app\Désinstaller Cyber Pinball.exe' -ArgumentList '/S'
  ```

## Known limitations of the prototype

- Application startup: between its launch and its first black covers (about 1 to 2 s), the desktop stays visible; no window can exist any earlier. Except on a [dedicated cabinet](#dedicated-cabinet) (Windows Enterprise, Education or IoT Enterprise): Cyber Pinball replaces Explorer there, so there is no desktop left to see. If the application is force-stopped in the middle of a game, it leaves the taskbars hidden until its next startup (or until Explorer restarts). Before its first frame, a window is transparent (no Windows system backdrop): if nothing from Cyber Pinball is underneath, the desktop shows through until then; this is the case of the black cover at startup, hence the desktop staying visible a little longer. A screen that is plugged back in or wakes up while the menu is running (monitors put to sleep by the Windows lock screen, renumbered on wake-up) gets its black cover again, but the menu windows do not follow this change yet: the screen guard reports the uncovered or badly covered screen in the console, and restarting Cyber Pinball puts everything back in place.
- VPX 10.8.1 keys (`Player`, `Backglass`, `ScoreView`, `Topper` windows, `[Plugin.<id>] Enable`, priorities and settings of the B2S, PUP and DMDUtil plugins). The format of screen names and the plugins to enable change with the VPX version: they are described per version in the manifest (`adapterSettings`).
- VPX log for diagnostics: `%APPDATA%\VPinballX\10.8\vpinball.log` ("The selected display … is not available" lines).
- Executable and installers unsigned as long as no certificate is provided (configuration ready, see [Code signing](#code-signing)): SmartScreen warns when the installer is launched.
- Updates on request only (system menu, see [Updating from the cabinet](#updating-from-the-cabinet)): no background check. During installation (about a minute), the Windows desktop and the installer's progress page are visible on the main screen. Rolling back to the previous version when the installer fails has not yet been triggered in a test.
- VPX locates screens in logical coordinates: with Windows scaling other than 100%, the position written may not match. The wizard reports it.
- Model stored as JSON. The spec calls for SQLite.
- Cabinet configurations: per-table settings (including the framing set during a game, even though it depends on the playfield screen) are shared by all configurations. A general VPX setting changed after a configuration was created only applies to the active configuration. The selection screen, configuration management and the banner have not been captured yet (waiting for the off-screen test mode, so as not to occupy the machine's screens).
- VPX 10.8.1 plugin mode only: no `ScreenRes.txt`, `DmdDevice.ini` or `B2STableSettings.xml` yet for classic mode; DOF through the VPX plugin only (no classic DirectOutput.dll), with no LED test outside a game.
- Zones: the stacking order of two overlapping zones and the rotation of a zone cannot be set (VPX has no setting for them). The actual rendering in VPX still has to be checked on a cabinet, in particular the windowed playfield of a desktop PC.
- Buttons: keyboard keys and joystick buttons (Pinscape, encoder in gamepad mode; see "Cabinet buttons"). A gamepad's hats and axes do not serve as cabinet buttons; an encoder button that sends a key combination is not supported.
- Sound: videos and menu music still play on the Windows default output, as do COM components outside plugins (standalone VPinMAME, B2S, PinUP Player). Nothing checks yet that the default output is the backglass one.
